# Theme — Semicap / WFE (Wafer-Fab Equipment)

_Wiki · generated 2026-06-19 · cross-company theme · sources: equity calls (`../../../equity_calls_transcripts/Semis`), briefing roll-ups (`../_briefings/by-ticker`), earnings transcripts, research library (`E:\research_library`). Company pages: [../00_INDEX.md](../00_INDEX.md) · themes index: [00_THEMES.md](00_THEMES.md)._

## 🆕🔴🔴 2026-09-20 (/wiki-ingest 21h) — **CHINA SPE IMPORTS TURNED THE CORNER IN AUGUST AND THE LITHO LINE IS LEADING: $3.3bn TOTAL (+24% y/y) WITH LITHO AT ITS HIGHEST LEVEL OF THE YEAR AND A FOURTH STRAIGHT MONTH OF GROWTH — WHILE THE BUY-SIDE'S 2028 WFE BOGEY CONVERGES ON ~$300bn FROM THREE INDEPENDENT SURVEYS, AND TWO DESKS CALL SEMICAP A *FUNDING SHORT* IN THE SAME WEEK.**

_Sources: **UBS · Jimmy Yu / Nicolas Gaudois / Timothy Arcuri / Yongwei Lai / Francois-Xavier Bouvignies / Kenji Yasui / Randy Abrams / Sunny Lin / Xinlei Li / Qing Luo**, *"China Semi Equipment: SPE import analysis — Solid YoY growth with broadening demand across provinces in August"*, published 21-Sep-2026 (received 09-20); **Jefferies · William Beavington**, Global SEMIS Daily 2026-09-20 carrying the US Semis Silicon Valley Bus Tour (⚠️ desk relay of private management meetings); **J.P. Morgan TMT spec sales · Scott Silver**, *"Silver Linings Euro TMT Weekly"*, 2026-09-20 (⚠️ sales commentary); **UBS Sector Sales · Robert Ruple**, *"Tech Musings"*, 2026-09-20 (⚠️ sales commentary)._

🔴🔴🔴 **(1) THE CHINA IMPORT DATA, AND THE INFLECTION IS IN THE DERIVATIVE.** August SPE imports **$3.3bn (+24% y/y, −5% m/m)**. **8M26 cumulative $20.4bn, −4% y/y — narrowing from −8% in 7M26, and EX-GUANGDONG the same series is +28% y/y.** By tool: **litho $989mn (+32% y/y, 30% of total), *"their highest level of the year, marking the fourth consecutive month of growth"***; non-litho **$2.3bn (+21% y/y)**, of which **dry etch $721mn (22%)** and **deposition $741mn (23%)**. By origin: **Netherlands $919mn** (largest, on the litho strength), **Japan $723mn (−7% y/y**, mostly *"other"* equipment which UBS believes is largely **track systems**), **Singapore $502mn (+74%)**, **Malaysia $417mn (+17%)**, **Taiwan $281mn (+175%)** — UBS reads the latter three as *"read-throughs to US vendors."* ➤ **For [[LRCX]] / [[AMAT]] specifically: Taiwan, Malaysia and Japan are the top three dry-etch exporters; Singapore, Malaysia and Korea the top three for deposition.**

🔴🔴 **(2) THE GEOGRAPHIC BROADENING IS THE PART THAT IS GENUINELY NEW, AND IT COMES WITH A PER-UNIT ASP.** **Shanghai $1.4bn (+71% y/y, 44% of total)**, supported by capacity expansion in **both memory and leading logic**; **Sichuan $626mn (more than doubling y/y, 19% of total)**, likely **Huali's Chengdu fab** — and **Sichuan took THREE litho units at an ASP of US$100mn EACH**, which UBS reads as *"likely indicating a capacity expansion at relatively advanced nodes."* **Jilin took one unit at $63mn; Chongqing, Anhui, Beijing, Hubei, Jiangsu and Shaanxi took eight units for $114mn in total.** Beijing 10% / Shaanxi 5% / Liaoning 4% / Jiangsu 4% / Guangdong 3% of the monthly total, with **Shaanxi (Samsung Xi'an) and Liaoning (SK Hynix Dalian) attributed to overseas memory makers' expansion.** ➤➤ **A $100mn average selling price per litho unit in a tier-2 province is the single most informative number in the release: it is an immersion-class ASP, not a mature-node one, and it is landing outside the traditional clusters.**

⚠️⚠️ **(3) THE LOCALISATION WARNING SITTING INSIDE THE GROWTH:** UBS notes import demand from **Hubei and Anhui remained MUTED** even though *"recent tender announcements from YMTC and CXMT and updates in YMTC's latest prospectus suggest that a new round of capacity expansion could begin in the near term"*, and concludes *"the discrepancy may reflect an **accelerating pace of equipment LOCALIZATION**."* ➤ **Read that as a share risk inside a growing pool — exactly the shape that makes aggregate China WFE a poor proxy for Western vendors' China revenue. UBS raised its **China WFE forecast to US$49bn / 63bn / 70bn for 2026-28E (+9% / +29% / +11% y/y)** and its top picks in the note are **Naura and AMEC**, not the Western names.** ⚠️ **And CXMT's G5 announcement the same day — mass production at 11.95nm active-area half-pitch, ≥50% gross DPW gain, *"ahead of our previous expectation that G5 would begin contributing only around 2028"* (MS · Charlie Chan) — says the localisation is producing working nodes, not just tools.**

🔴🔴🔴 **(4) THE 2028 WFE BOGEY IS CONVERGING, AND THAT IS ITSELF A FINDING.** JPM's Asia marketing found *"WFE expectations already running at ~$260-270bn for 2027 and close to **~$300bn for 2028** among some investors I met"* ⚠️ (the note prints a euro symbol on a series the industry quotes in USD — read as dollars). UBS's desk: *"investors remain **firmly anchored to $300b in WFE**."* Both sit on top of the **Barclays desk survey logged 09-16 (modal CY28 bucket $290-300bn, up from $240-250bn in mid-June)**. ➤➤ **Three independent buy-side surveys inside five weeks landing on the same number. The ~$35-40bn one-quarter re-rating this wiki flagged on 09-16 is now consensus rather than a survey artifact — which means the bogey is no longer a source of upside surprise. Jefferies' bus tour reaches exactly that conclusion: *"the Semi-caps look like a FUNDING SHORT tactically, given the LACK OF POTENTIAL UPSIDE ON '28 WFE right now."***

🔴🔴 **(5) THE ROTATION INSIDE SEMICAP, REPORTED BY A DESK FOR THE FIRST TIME — AWAY FROM THE MEMORY-LEVERED NAMES.** UBS: *"we are starting to sense a **subtle shift in favor of FOUNDRY/LOGIC exposure** given the anticipated capex ramp at [[TSM]] and [[INTC]] that is beginning to **tilt positioning in favor of [[KLAC]] or [[ASML]] over [[LRCX]] and [[AMAT]] on a tactical basis**, where crowding had previously existed."* And the transmission mechanism: the guarded memory view *"has overlayed to a more guarded position on Semicaps as well **given the historical correlations to Memory**."* **Later-cycle back-end names getting incremental interest: UCTT, MKSI, ENTG and [[TER]].** ➤ **Second independent desk in a week describing semicap as trading off memory SENTIMENT rather than its own order book — the [[MU]] page's 09-16 Capstone call called it *"a second derivative."* ⚠️ Note the two desks agree semicap is a funding short while disagreeing on which names to own inside it, which is what a positioning trade looks like rather than a fundamental call.**

🔴🔴 **(6) THE COMPANY-LEVEL SUPPLY MARK FROM THE BUS TOUR, AND IT NAMES THE SAME CONSTRAINT THREE OTHER SOURCES HAVE: [[AMAT]] expects *"very strong growth in C27, although **CUSTOMER CLEANROOM AVAILABILITY REMAINS A CONSTRAINT**, with fab purchases and conversions of existing lines providing incremental space"* — and plans to **double its own systems manufacturing capacity from current levels by C28**, including via its new Singapore facility, *"while continuing to pursue gradual pricing improvements."* Advanced-packaging revenue **$2.4bn this year**, HBM the largest growth driver, panel-level packaging *"more meaningful over the medium term"*; integrated solutions **20% → 30% of systems revenue**.** ➤➤ **Cleanroom — not tools, not capital, not capacity intent — is now named as the binding constraint by THREE independent sources: AMAT management here, the 09-14 Capstone call (60-80K per-company annual memory ramp ceiling), and JPM's Fall Series deck. That is the most corroborated supply-side fact on this theme and it caps every WFE forecast above, including the $300bn one.**

## 🆕🔴🔴 2026-09-18 (/wiki-ingest 21h) — **THE EUV CAPACITY LADDER IS ABOUT TO BE RE-GUIDED UPWARDS AND MORGAN STANLEY SAYS THE MARKET ALREADY KNOWS: 90+ LOW-NA TOOLS FOR FY27 (FROM ~85), 2028 REAFFIRMED AT ≥110 — AND MS ITSELF MODELS **98 / 103** SHIPPED. THE OPEN QUESTION IS EXPLICITLY DEMAND, NOT SUPPLY.**

- 🔴🔴 **THE EVENT IS DATED: [[ASML]] reports **3Q26 on Wednesday 14 October**. MS (Lee Simpson / Shawn Kim / Nigel van Putten / Amelia Scicluna, *"Expect Earnings to Tick Up"*, **2026-09-18**, **OW, PT €1,700, reiterated Top Pick**) models **Q3 sales €12.3bn (~5% above consensus €11.7bn and ABOVE the top of management's guide)** and **EPS €11.75 vs Street €10.84**, with a **Q4 guide above €14bn** — but **no further material FY26 revision** after the raise to €43-45bn (from €36-40bn). Estimate changes: **2026 Sales/EPS +4%/+7%; 2027 +9%/+14%.****
- 🔴🔴🔴 **THE CAPACITY NUMBERS, WHICH ARE THE ONES THIS THEME TRACKS: MS expects management to **raise FY27 low-NA EUV capacity guidance to 90+ tools** (vs ~85 guided on the 07-15 call) and to **reaffirm 2028 at or above 110 low-NA**; MS's own shipment model is **98 EUV in FY27 and 103 in FY28**. Their NDR framing: the July guidance should be read as *"a **FLOOR**, rather than a ceiling,"* with *"recently achieved manufacturing efficiencies"* providing more flexibility than the market recognises. Beyond 2029, the **Brainport Industries Campus (BIC)** build-out in 2028-29 supports DUV first, then transitions to EUV.**
- ⚠️⚠️ **READ THE ASYMMETRY CAREFULLY, BECAUSE IT IS THE WHOLE POINT: MS says the re-guide is **already expected** (*"only a few investors concerned that ASML cannot deliver more than 85 low-NA EUV tools next year"*), so a raise to 90+ is not the surprise. What MS explicitly does NOT underwrite is that demand fills it — *"**whether demand ultimately reaches that level remains to be seen**."* ➤ **That is the same seam the Capstone EUV model sits on (65 / 85 / 110 units, DRAM at 50% of 2028 EUV): 110 in 2028 is now corroborated as a CAPACITY commitment by the covering bull, and left open as a DEMAND question by the same note. Supply and demand have to be scored separately here.****
- ✅ **CHINA FLIPS FROM RISK TO UN-PRICED TAILWIND IN THIS NOTE, AND THE MECHANISM IS DUV: MS argues the market is *"overlooking potential upside from new memory entrants"* — their Asia team models **CXMT surpassing [[MU]] in DRAM CAPACITY by 2028** at **~500kwpm = 15% of a global 3,374kwpm DRAM base**, and **YMTC becoming the global #2/3 NAND player** → a **DUV demand tailwind in FY27 and FY28 respectively** that is *"yet to be appreciated."* Competition + export-restriction fears are conceded to *"constrain the multiple for now"*; the stock has traded **down ~20% this quarter** on capacity and China concerns MS calls **overdone**.**
- 🔭 **CORROBORATING FOOTNOTE FROM THE CORPUS, SAME 48 HOURS: **CXMT is preparing a NAND R&D/production line at its new Beijing fab** and is already in purchase-intent discussions with customers (@QQ_Timmy, 2026-09-18) — i.e. the China entrant MS is underwriting on DRAM is simultaneously widening into NAND. ⚠️ *Press relay of unnamed industry sources; logged as an event, not as capacity.***

### 🆕🔴🔴🔴 2026-09-18 (/run-inbox 23h — added to this date's block) — **JPM'S FALL-SERIES DECK PUTS THE WFE LADDER AT $163bn / $225bn / $263bn FOR 2026-28E ON A BASIS THAT *EXCLUDES* WAFER BONDERS AND WAFER-LEVEL PACKAGING — SO THE HIGHEST 2027 NUMBER ON THIS PAGE IS ALSO THE NARROWEST ONE. CLEANROOM IS NAMED THE PRIMARY BOTTLENECK FOR A THIRD TIME, AND KLAC IS THE HOUSE'S BEST RISK/REWARD.**

- 🔴🔴🔴 **THE LADDER, AND THE BASIS CAVEAT THAT HAS TO TRAVEL WITH IT.** JPM: **WFE +31% y/y in 2026E and +38% y/y in 2027E**, with spending at **US$163bn (2026E) → US$225bn (2027E) → US$263bn (2028E)** against history of **US$112bn (2024) and US$124bn (2025)** — and the series is stated to **EXCLUDE wafer bonders and other wafer-level packaging equipment** (J.P. Morgan · Harlan Sur / Mayur Ramdhani / Apoorva Kumar / Akanksh Chauhan — "US Semiconductors and Semiconductor Capital Equipment: Fall Series 2026 Update", 2026-09-17, 30pp deck). ➤➤ **Against what this page already carries: MS (08-25) at $223bn 2027 / $254bn 2028, BofA (09-15) at $210bn 2027 / $270bn+ 2028, GS (08-24) at $281bn 2028. JPM's 2027 is effectively ON TOP OF MS and ~7% above BofA — but on a NARROWER definition, because the advanced-packaging bonder line every other house has been adding is carved out. Like-for-like, JPM is the most aggressive 2027 mark on this page, not merely a co-leader. Its 2028 ($263bn) then sits BELOW GS ($281bn) on that same narrower basis, which makes JPM's implied 2028 growth (+17%) the deceleration year.** ⚠️ **Do NOT stack this series against any WFE number on this page that includes hybrid-bonding / wafer-bonder spend — the exclusion is JPM's own and it is exactly the line item [cowos-packaging](cowos-packaging.md) is growing fastest.**
- 🔴🔴🔴 **CLEANROOM IS NOW THE THIRD INDEPENDENT NAMING OF THE SAME BINDING CONSTRAINT, AND THE MEMORY SIDE FINALLY SUPPLIES THE PHYSICAL REASON.** JPM: **"cleanroom constraints remain the primary bottleneck"** (2026-09-17). This page already carries [[AMAT]] IR naming *customer cleanroom space* as the cap on upside (08-20) and [[LRCX]] naming clean-room space as the binding constraint through 2027 (09-09). ✅ **The mechanism arrives from the memory model the same week: Deutsche Bank's rebuilt DRAM supply model states that producing an incremental HBM bit takes ~3x the silicon of a standard DRAM bit today, climbing toward 4:1 with HBM4/HBM4e, because of multi-die stacking, advanced bonding and TSVs — *"all of which require more cleanroom capacity"*** (Deutsche Bank · Melissa Weathers / Joe Schwab, "Global Memory: Chipping away at industry supply imbalance", 2026-09-16). ➤ **Three equipment-side observations of a constraint plus one supply-side model of WHY it binds: HBM does not just consume wafers, it consumes FLOOR AREA per bit at a rising rate. That is the cleanest available argument that the cleanroom bottleneck gets WORSE as the memory mix shifts, independent of capex budgets.**
- 🔴🔴 **THE FRONT-END / LEADING-EDGE LEG, WITH A DATED ORDER INFLECTION.** JPM anchors F/L spend on **TSMC's N2/N3 buildout plus incremental [[INTC]] upside**, and says its **Asia checks point to a more aggressive TSMC roadmap — multiple 1H26 fab SHELL starts and rising equipment orders from 2Q26 onward** (JPM, 2026-09-17). ➤ **"Shells first, tools later" is the same sequencing [[SAMSUNG]] IR described on 08-25 and which this page initially mis-read as caution before the primary showed it was an option strategy. JPM now dates the tool leg off the shell leg: shells in 1H26, orders from 2Q26. That converts a qualitative shell observation into a checkable order timeline.**
- 🔴🔴 **THE MEMORY LEG SPLITS DRAM FROM NAND, AND IT RANKS THE SAME WAY LAM DID.** JPM: **memory WFE is broadening, "led by DRAM capacity additions", while NAND stays conversion/migration-led until potential greenfield upside in 2H28+** (2026-09-17). ✅ **Confirms [[LRCX]]'s own ranking on 09-14 (DRAM first, ahead of leading-edge logic) and is consistent with [[SAMSUNG]] management the following day saying NAND is limited to process migration at Xi'an *"due to limited space in fabs in Korea"*** (Goldman Sachs · Giuni Lee / Taeyong Lee / Daiki Takayama, Samsung management virtual meeting, 2026-09-18). ➤ **The NAND greenfield date (2H28+) is the one to hold: it is the first dated window on this page for when the NAND wafer-start response could actually begin, and it sits AFTER the 2027-28 shortage the memory theme is trading.**
- 🔴🔴 **THE HOUSE'S PICK, AND ITS REASONING IS A 2027 EXPOSURE ARGUMENT RATHER THAN A CYCLE CALL.** JPM's **most attractive risk/reward in the group is [[KLAC]]** — YTD underperformance plus **underappreciated 2027 foundry/logic WFE exposure**, with **upside to CY27 consensus as [[INTC]] increases engagement and capex around yield and cost improvement** (2026-09-17). ➤ **Note what the argument is NOT: it is not "process control intensity rises", the usual KLAC bull leg. It is a customer-specific claim about Intel spending on yield, which is falsifiable against Intel's own capex commentary and which this page can track directly.**
- 🔴🔴🔴 **THE BOTTOM-UP DRAM FAB PIPELINE UNDERNEATH "DRAM LEADS 2027 WFE" IS NOW ON DISK, FAB BY FAB — AND IT IS BIGGER THAN THE SAME HOUSE'S PRIOR VERSION.** DB's supply model (a bottoms-up build of 10+ upcoming DRAM fabs) takes **total DRAM WSPM from 1,921k (2025) to 3,796k (2030E), i.e. ~1,875k of wafer starts ADDED**, with incremental capacity of **+180k / +360k / +483k / +533k / +345k WSPM in 2026-30E**; wafer-start growth is revised UP to **+9% / +17% / +20% / +17% / +10% y/y (from +7% / +15% / +17% / +14% / +8%)**. Named ramps include **SK Hynix Yongin Fab 1 — six cleanrooms, first cleanroom HVM in 1Q27, ~+360k WSPM by 1H30**, with all four Yongin fabs *"expected to be completed by 2033, 12 years ahead of original 2045 target"*; **Samsung Pyeongtaek P5 beginning HVM in 2028 and P5 Fab 2 construction started in 2H26 (triple-fab design, +150k)**; **[[MU]] ID1 HVM in 2027 and ID2 by end-2028 at ~120k WSPM each**; and **CXMT raised by +275k WSPM versus DB's prior forecast** on accelerated Shanghai expansion plus a new Beijing fab (DB, 2026-09-16). ➤➤ **This is the single most useful WFE-demand artefact to arrive this month: the DRAM leg of the 2027-28 raise is no longer an intensity assumption, it is a named list of cleanrooms with dates. It is also where the 2029-30 risk lives — DB's own off-ramp is *"risk of oversupply can be mitigated by DRAM makers slowing the pace of tooling projects if needed"*, which is a WFE event a year before it is a price event.** ✅ **The CXMT raise is directionally the same call MS made in the 21h block above (CXMT ~500kwpm by 2028, 15% of a 3,374kwpm global base) — two houses independently marking the China DRAM entrant UP inside a week, one via a DUV tailwind and one via wafer starts.** ⚠️ *DB's capacity base (3,796k WSPM in 2030E) and MS's 3,374kwpm 2028 base are different years and different constructions — do not treat the CXMT shares as comparable.*
- 🔴🔴 **NET-NEW MECHANISM: HBM IS NOW PULLING *LEADING-EDGE LOGIC* CAPACITY, NOT ONLY DRAM CAPACITY.** Samsung management: **4nm foundry capacity "remains very tight due to demand from HBM4 base die"**, the company is **considering adding 4nm capacity in Korea**, and **new line additions of 2nm nodes for HBM5 base dies are also being reviewed**; separately it **confirmed the start of equipment set-up at the Taylor fab** dedicated to orders from a US auto customer, and is considering further US fab expansion (GS/Samsung management, 2026-09-18). ➤ **This page has modelled HBM as a DRAM-WFE driver and a packaging driver. The base die makes it a FOUNDRY-WFE driver too — and at the leading edge, where tool intensity per wafer is highest. A 2nm line stood up for HBM5 base dies is logic WFE that no memory-mix model on this page currently attributes to HBM.** ⚠️ *These are company statements of intent ("considering", "being reviewed"), not committed capacity — logged as a demand vector, not as a fab.*
- 🔭 **AND THE BACK-END GETS ONE LINE FROM THE KOREA SIDE, WHICH IS WHERE THE TCB DEMAND SITS.** BofA's Korea overview: the memory super-cycle *"should also create long-term growth opportunities for equipment vendors (not only front-end but also backend including TCB)"* and for material suppliers, on the basis that **next-generation HBM needs more DRAM dies stacked (16-hi, 20-hi) with a logic die**, making **TCB — or eventually hybrid bonding — "increasingly more critical" for new AI datacenter builds** (BofA Securities / Merrill Lynch Seoul · Simon Woo, CFA — "Korea super-cycle continues into 2030, driven by AI and globalization", 2026-09-18, 66pp). ⚠️⚠️ **CARRY THE CONTRADICTION WITH IT: SemiAnalysis, the same week, argues the opposite direction on stack height — *"4-hi HBM provides the best $/bandwidth and therefore lowest cost per token"* (2026-09-18). Bonder demand per HBM bit is a direct function of dies per stack, so the 4-hi-vs-20-hi fight logged on [hbm-memory](hbm-memory.md) is an unresolved input to back-end tool TAM. Nothing adopted.**

## 🆕🔴 2026-09-17 — **JPM PUTS THE SUBSYSTEM LAYER AT *2-3x WFE IN UPCYCLES* AND SEMI REVENUE *ACCELERATING TO >50% Y/Y* AT MKSI — CORROBORATING BofA'S 20-POINT SUBSYSTEM-OVER-WFE SPREAD FROM A SECOND HOUSE, AT A MATERIALLY LOWER PRICE TARGET.**

_Source: J.P. Morgan · **Harlan Sur** — **Fall Series 2026 Webinar — Semis & Semi Cap Equipment**, 2026-09-17 10:30am ET (w/ Mayur Ramdhani) (pasted exhibit; broker/analyst confirmed by Felipe 2026-09-17, deck identified from the Fall Series webinar invitations in Felipe's Outlook, 2026-09-15 / 09-16; deck PDF not on disk, NetRoadshow-gated), slide *"SMID Cap Semis: Selective on Opportunities Within The Group"*. ⚠️⚠️ **MKSI IS NOT A COVERED NAME ON THIS WIKI — no page, no house model. Filed as a SUBSYSTEM-LAYER read-through; the rating and PT are recorded as JPM's, not adopted.**_

- 🔴 **THE MARKS: JPM rates **MKSI OW, PT $380**, on *"the high-beta way to play the WFE upcycle, with a secular AI packaging kicker."* Segment detail: *"**Semi accelerating to >50% Y/Y growth** on etch/dep subsystem demand"*; *"**E&P inflecting** on AI advanced-packaging and PCB chemistry, with **equipment orders at all-time highs and visibility through 2027**"*; *"operating leverage is materializing and deleveraging is ahead of plan."*** ✅✅ **SECOND-HOUSE CORROBORATION OF THIS PAGE'S OWN SUBSYSTEM FINDING.** The 08-07 block here carries **BofA (Michael Mani / Vivek Arya, Buy): the subsystem layer outgrowing WFE by more than 20 points with visibility through CY27.** **JPM's *">50% y/y semi growth"* and *"visibility through 2027"* are the same claim from a different house — and the mechanism JPM supplies is the useful addition: *"Semi has historically grown **2-3x WFE** in upcycles."*** ➤ **That is a reusable heuristic for this page: in an upcycle the subsystem suppliers are a LEVERED derivative of the WFE number, so a WFE forecast implies a subsystem forecast at 2-3x the growth rate. ⚠️ It is a historical regularity asserted without a data window — no cycles named, no r², no definition of "upcycle". Use it as a sizing prior, never as a model input.**
- 🔴 **THE ADVANCED-SUBSTRATE NUMBER IS THE ONE WORTH KEEPING:** *"E&P's **laser + chemistry + plating stack addresses ~70% of critical advanced-substrate steps**."* ➤ **This page tracks advanced substrates as a named bottleneck in the AI supply chain but has had no process-coverage figure for it. A single supplier addressing ~70% of the critical step count is a concentration datapoint, and it is the substrate-layer analogue of the CoWoS and hybrid-bonding chokepoints on [cowos-packaging](cowos-packaging.md).** ⚠️ **"Critical steps" is undefined on the slide — a STEP-COUNT share, not a revenue or value share, and the two are not interchangeable.**
- ⚠️ **AND THE PRICE-TARGET DISPERSION, RECORDED BECAUSE THIS PAGE ALREADY CARRIES THE OTHER SIDE: JPM OW **$380** against BofA Buy **$450** (cut from $500 on 2026-08-07, on a method change to 17x CY28E PE from 22x CY28E EV/EBITDA, *"due to recent sector multiple contraction"*).** ➤ **Two OW/Buy ratings ~18% apart on target. Both houses agree on the fundamental (subsystems levered to WFE, visibility into 2027); the gap is entirely multiple. Neither adopted — MKSI is not covered here.**

## 🆕🔴🔴🔴 2026-09-16 (/wiki-ingest 21h — Barclays' quarterly buy-side survey + BofA's European semicap note) — **THE BUY-SIDE'S OWN CY28 WFE NUMBER MOVED ~$40-50bn IN THREE MONTHS, AND IT IS NOW ABOVE MOST PUBLISHED HOUSE FORECASTS — WHICH IS EXACTLY WHY THE DESK SAYS THE GROUP STOPPED WORKING ON EARNINGS**

_(**Barclays desk · Jeffrey Rand** — "Barclays TMT Desk Commentary: RESULTS Semis Sentiment Survey", 2026-09-16 ⚠️ **DESK ANALYST PRODUCT, EXPLICITLY "NOT RESEARCH"; a survey of Barclays' institutional client base, n≈38 on most questions** · and **BofA · Didier Scemama** — "ASML & ASM: Industry-Leading Growth at Near-Trough Multiples", 2026-09-16, Buy on both, [[ASML]] Top Pick)_

- 🔴🔴🔴 **THE DISTRIBUTION, AND ITS DRIFT — the single most useful number this page has on where the MARGINAL BUYER sits rather than where the houses sit. "WFE in CY28 will be?", September 2026 versus the same desk's mid-June survey:**

| CY28 WFE | Sep-2026 | Jun-2026 |
|---|--:|--:|
| <$250bn | 3% | 60% (all buckets <$250bn) |
| $250-260bn | 11% | 21% |
| $260-270bn | 13% | 5% |
| $270-280bn | 18% | 10% (>$270bn) |
| $280-290bn | 16% | 0% |
| $290-300bn | **26%** | 0% |
| $300-310bn | 8% | 0% |
| $310-320bn | 5% | 0% |
| >$320bn | 0% | 0% |

- 🔴🔴 **WHAT THE TABLE SAYS: the modal answer moved from **$240-250bn (26% of June respondents)** to **$290-300bn (26%)**; **84% are now at ≥$260bn against 15% in June**; and **not one June respondent was above $270bn while 55% now are.** Call it **~$280bn central versus ~$245bn in June — a ~$35-40bn / ~15% re-rating of the buy-side's own forecast inside one quarter**, which is a larger move than any published house made over the same period.**
- 🔴🔴 **AND THE CONSEQUENCE THE DESK DRAWS, which is the part that matters for positioning: *"investors are still positive on the fundamentals of the space, but are not sure how much more upside there is to buyside WFE numbers that are already close to $300b for CY28… there is more appetite for investors to own these names INTRAQUARTER where headlines are more likely to drive stock moves compared to earnings when investors do not see a lot of upside to buyside numbers."* **This is the mechanism behind the de-rating that [[themes/macro-cycle](macro-cycle.md) has been describing: the sell-side keeps raising into a buy-side that has already raised further, so beats stop being surprises.** Semicap is simultaneously the **#3 preferred LONG sector (20%) and the #2 preferred SHORT (24%, from 8% in June)** — the widest straddle in the survey.**
- 🔴 **LARGE-CAP PREFERENCE ORDER, unchanged in shape from prior surveys but now explicit: **[[LRCX]] > [[AMAT]] > [[ASML]] > [[KLAC]]**. On the SMID side **ONTO, ENTG and AMKR** are named as recently more positive.** ⚠️ Note this sits against [[BESI]]/[[AEIS]]-type SMID preferences carried elsewhere on this page from MS (09-09) — different houses, different SMID lists, no reconciliation attempted.
- 🔴🔴 **THE OTHER SIDE OF THE ARGUMENT, PUBLISHED THE SAME DAY: BofA's Scemama says the European de-rating *"has become increasingly difficult to justify. Both companies now trade at or close to trough CY27 valuation multiples despite some of the strongest growth profiles in the global semiconductor equipment sector"*, stays **ABOVE consensus** on both, and argues **consensus is too low because ASML and ASM will OUTPERFORM WFE through CY27** — with **visibility into capacity over the next 18 months** and China/MATCH Act *"more than reflected in the share price."* **So: the buy-side has raised its WFE number to ~$280bn and de-rated the stocks anyway; BofA's answer is that the equipment names' growth is a SHARE-OF-WFE story that the WFE number alone cannot settle.
- 🔴 **NET-NEW ANGLE WORTH CARRYING SEPARATELY — AI REGULATION AS A SEMICAP POSITIVE: BofA argues the policy debate now dominating the tape is *"neutral to potentially positive"* for semicaps: *"Increased governance and monitoring requirements are likely to add incremental compute demand, while shifting spending toward custom ASICs, CPUs, networking and memory."* **That is the mirror image of the de-rating-on-policy-risk argument, and it rhymes with [[OPENAI]]'s own framing the same week that alignment work is compute-ADDITIVE (see [themes/tokenmaxxing](tokenmaxxing.md)). NOT ADOPTED as a house view — logged as the first sell-side attempt to price AI governance as demand rather than as risk.
- ⚠️ **METHOD CAVEAT, STATED SO IT TRAVELS WITH THE NUMBERS: this is a self-selected client survey run by a trading desk, not a research forecast; the June and September panels are not guaranteed to be the same respondents; and the buckets changed between surveys (June's top bucket was ">$270bn"), so the cross-survey comparison above is bucket-aligned, not respondent-matched.**


## 🆕🔴🔴🔴 2026-09-15 (/wiki-ingest 21h — BofA's two desks raise the whole stack, plus company meetings at [[ASML]] and [[BESI]]) — **THE WFE LADDER GETS ANOTHER RAISE AND THE FIRST ONE THAT COMES WITH AN EUV-vs-EX-EUV SPLIT: BofA TAKES CY26 $144bn → $156bn, CY27 $190bn → $210bn, CY28 $250bn → $270bn+, CY30 $292bn → $360bn, AND SAYS EUV WFE COMPOUNDS FASTER THAN EX-EUV (+28% vs +25% CAGR) WITH WFE INTENSITY REACHING 11.2% IN 2030E. SEPARATELY [[ASML]] TELLS TWO HOUSES THE 65/85/110 EUV LADDER IS "NOT A SUPPLY CAP" AND THAT THE PRICE RISE LANDS IN IMMERSION DUV AND LOW-NA EUV, MOSTLY IN THE 2028 P&L.**

_(**BofA Securities · Vivek Arya** — "US Semiconductors: State of the Union: raising estimates, industry TAM doubling to $3.2tn CY30", 2026-09-15, ⚠️ **relayed in BofA TMT sales commentary by Brian Fenske — a SALES RELAY of a research note, the note itself not read**. **BofA Securities · Didier Scemama**, Head of European IT Hardware Research — two notes emailed direct, 2026-09-15. **Bernstein · David Dai, CFA / Carmine Milano, CFA** — ASML and BESI "SDC Key Takeaways" from Bernstein's 23rd Strategic Decisions Conference, London, 2026-09-15, plus the Bernstein tech daily relay by Tyler Seidman. **J.P. Morgan · Sandeep Deshpande** — ASML and BE Semiconductor company visit notes, 2026-09-15 (meetings held 2026-09-11). **J.P. Morgan · Duncan Wagner**, APAC Hardware/Semis specialist sales — "Silver Sunrise Euro TMT Daily", 2026-09-15, ⚠️ **desk commentary, not research**.)_

- 🔴🔴🔴 **THE RAISE, AND THE DRIFT IT CAUSES ON THIS PAGE. OLD (carried here since 06-29 as "BofA/Arya $144bn / $190bn / $250bn for CY26-28", with FY30 $292bn): SUPERSEDED. NEW: CY26 $156bn (+33% YoY), CY27 $210bn, CY28 $270bn+, CY30 $360bn.** Upside comes **largely from higher Memory WFE expectations, especially DRAM**, plus an incrementally higher auto/industrial recovery. ➤ **Placement in this page's own ladder: CY27 $210bn is the LOW mark of the raised cluster** — below UBS $225bn (Arcuri 09-01), MS $223bn and GS $218bn — which narrows the four-way 2027 dispersion this page has tracked from ~$33bn to ~$15bn. **CY28 $270bn+ sits between MS $254bn and GS $281bn, on top of UBS $275bn**, and remains **~18-21% BELOW the $330-340bn 2028 figure [[AMAT]] management DISOWNED on 08-20** — so the gap is still un-taken upside, not a contradiction. **CY26 $156bn is above [[LRCX]]'s own "low-$150B" and above the ~$150bn shipped-WFE market [[AMAT]] said demand was running above.**
- 🔴🔴 **⚠️ BASIS GUARD, STATED BEFORE ANYONE REUSES THE HEADLINE: BofA also raises the TOTAL SEMICONDUCTOR INDUSTRY TAM by 19% to $3.2tn in CY30 from $2.7tn, an 18% CAGR CY26-30 from 14% — "the industry took 50 years to reach the $1T sales milestone, and is already set to double TAM in 4 years from $1.7T today." THAT $3.2tn IS THE TOTAL INDUSTRY TAM. It is NOT the AI-data-centre-systems TAM (~$1.7tn CY30) that [ai-tam-bofa.md](ai-tam-bofa.md) carries from the May-2026 exhibit, and the AI-DC sub-line was NOT restated in today's work. Do not net the two, do not compare them, and do not treat the $3.2tn as an upgrade to the AI-DC number.** The one thing that is directly comparable is the WFE ladder above, because it is the same series this page has tracked all year.
- 🔴🔴 **NET-NEW STRUCTURE, AND IT IS THE FIRST TIME THIS PAGE HAS HAD THE SPLIT: EUV WFE IS FORECAST TO GROW FASTER THAN EX-EUV WFE — +28% CAGR vs +25% — as EUV adoption expands across sub-5nm logic and advanced DRAM, with WFE INTENSITY AT 11.2% IN 2030E.** The EPS-power marks BofA hangs on it: **[[ASML]] EPS CAGR of 35% 25-30E, implying €110 EPS power in 2030E**, and **ASMI €70 EPS power in 2030E on a 40% CAGR**, with the higher intensity named as what strengthens the ASMI base case. Positive read-across to ASML, ASMI, VAT and COTN, and to preferred semis IFX and STM. ⚠️ **Both EPS-power figures are 2030E and must not be lined up against the 2028 EPS marks elsewhere on this wiki** (Bernstein has ASML FY28 at €67; the JPM desk describes buy-side 2028 at €75 with bulls nearer €80). ⚠️ The intensity number is a 2030E ratio with no stated denominator in the email — treat 11.2% as BofA's own construction, not a cross-checkable figure.
- 🔴 **THE NAND LEG FINALLY GETS A DISCRETE DOLLAR: NAND WFE reaching $15bn (+38% YoY) in 26E on a stronger NAND investment cycle; automotive raised to $60bn (+12% YoY) in 26E.** ➤ Two cross-checks this page owes itself. (1) It does NOT overturn the standing objection that **NAND greenfield is absent until CY28** — a $15bn 26E at +38% is a migration/conversion cycle, exactly the shape argued here on 07-26 and 08-27. (2) ⚠️ **It is NOT the same basis as the 08-15 [[AMAT]] callback mark of a ~$20bn NAND run-rate going "way, way above the peak" (Arcuri)** — a calendar-year spend and an exit run-rate are different measures and must not be netted or read as a cut.
- 🔴🔴 **THE LITHO CEILING IS EXPLICITLY REMOVED, WHICH CHANGES HOW EVERY 110-BASED CONVERSION ON THIS PAGE SHOULD BE READ.** From Bernstein's SDC meeting with [[ASML]] IR: **"The EUV shipment numbers are not supply caps. The previously communicated EUV LNA capacity plans are 65 systems in 2026, 85 in 2027, and 110 in 2028, based on the current footprint, sustainable demand assumptions, and expected improvements in next-generation LNA productivity. Capacity is therefore not physically capped at these levels."** From JPM's Veldhoven visit with CFO Roger Dassen: management is **"looking into whether they can do more than 110 EUV tools in '28 but had nothing to announce"**, and **driving down EUV cycle time is the key capacity driver**. ➤ **This page has used 110 as a physical ceiling in its GW-to-WFE conversions (e.g. UBS's "~110 EUV slots ≈ ~$225bn WFE before ASML must add floor space"). That framing is now wrong in one direction: the 110 is a DEMAND/commitment count, not a supply ceiling** — consistent with the 09-09 Capstone IR mark (the 110 is "bottoms up from our customers", above-110 needs POs and down payments "we're not getting") and with the 09-14 unit-basis flag already on this page that **the 110 is LOW-NA ONLY**. ⚠️ JPM's behavioural read is the counterweight to anyone modelling a spike: ASML wants to shave peaks and declines so "volumes will stay high for longer rather than have a high peak and then a trough" — **a vendor managing for cycle EXTENSION, which caps any single-year unit maximum**.
- 🔴 **AND THE ASML PRICE LEG GETS A PRODUCT AND A YEAR, WHICH THIS PAGE'S 09-14 "BOTH VENDORS PUT PRICE ON THE TABLE" BLOCK DID NOT HAVE: the increases will be seen in IMMERSION DUV AND LOW-NA EUV, and "the lion's share of these increases will start in the P&L in 2028", directly accretive to margins, with the EXISTING BACKLOG CONTRACTUALLY EXCLUDED.** Bernstein's version from the value side: ASML "should capture a larger share of the value created by higher throughput, overlay and accuracy, especially given customer very high profitability (mostly a 2027/28 story and will not impact the existing backlog due to contractual agreements)". Capacity plumbing to match: the new **Brainport campus builds modules and systems for DUV and DUV immersion with volume production starting in '29**, freeing Veldhoven for EUV — a third independent confirmation of the 09-09 IR line. ➤ **Scoring note: with [[ADVANTEST]] at +10-15% tester ASP per year (09-14) and [[ASML]] at a one-time 5-10% step-up landing in 2028, the price leg of this cycle now has THREE vendors and TWO different shapes — an annual rate at test, a one-time step at litho. Do not chain them into a single industry ASP series.**
- 🔴 **THE ARGUMENT THAT MATTERS IF THE AI-SPEND MIX SHIFTS: BofA's companion regulation note says "Regulation may reduce the frequency of frontier training runs, but production AI agents will require monitoring, verification, containment and auditability across prompts, outputs and tool calls" — broadening compute spending toward custom silicon, CPUs, networking, optics, power and advanced packaging — and concludes that "equally we see Semicaps as largely agnostic to such shift in spending with a potential longer-term benefit if custom ASICs and connectivity silicon adopt the most leading-edge nodes".** ➤ This is the cleanest published statement of the semicap-layer hedge this page has implicitly assumed: a mix shift INSIDE AI compute is a wafer-mix question, not a WFE-dollar question, and the direction of any error is positive if the custom-ASIC pool migrates to leading-edge nodes.
- 🔴 **READ-THROUGH TO THE INSPECTION LAYER — A NEW ACTINIC INSERTION AT A14, WITH A DATE ON THE PURCHASE ORDERS. Lasertec (NOT a wiki page — colour only) believes it can see an additional ~3 EUV LAYERS NEEDING ACTINIC INSPECTION TOOLS ONCE QUALIFIED AT TSMC's A14 PROCESS, WITH POs EXPECTED CY27.** Its FY6/27 order expectations were reset (~¥400bn hoped into the F4Q26 print vs a ¥350bn actual midpoint). ➤ Two implications for this page. (1) It is a **content/intensity** datapoint, not a capacity one: more actinic layers per node is the mask-shop analogue of rising litho intensity, and it lands on the A14 node this page already tracks as the High-NA insertion battleground. (2) The **order reset against the layer expansion is the same shape as the rest of the sector** — near-term orders disappointing while the structural insertion count goes up. (J.P. Morgan · Duncan Wagner, specialist sales — desk commentary, not research, 2026-09-15)
- ⚠️ **THE BEAR SIDE, LOGGED IN THE SAME BREATH BECAUSE IT IS THE TRANSMISSION MECHANISM FOR EVERY MULTIPLE ON THIS PAGE: memory remains the most questioned sub-sector, with investors split between "higher-for-longer" pricing and MEMORY DE-SPEC CONCERNS ACROSS HBM AND NOW EVEN NAND** (DeepSeek V4.1's significant KV-cache reduction, plus YMTC supply). JPM's **Gokul Hariharan**: **bandwidth and interconnect have replaced pure memory capacity as the critical bottleneck**. And the structural observation: **SPE historically trades as a SECOND DERIVATIVE of peaking memory names and memory pricing**. ➤ Set against BofA's own demand check the same day — **no slowdown in customer orders, LTAs, capacity commitments or semis pricing, memory pricing flattish week-on-week, B200 GPU rental pricing only -10% off the March peak, and "2027 remains much a fully booked/contracted year across all compute/networking/memory vendors"** — and against [[LRCX]]'s CFO calling DRAM de-speccing "a red herring" (09-09). **The de-spec debate is now in its third week on this page and has still not moved a single WFE forecast down.**
- ⚠️ **MARKET CONTEXT FOR 2026-09-14, THE AI-"PACING" SELL-OFF — FOUR HOUSES, FOUR DIFFERENT BASKETS AND WINDOWS. QUOTE THE BASKET, NEVER MERGE THEM: Rothschild** measured **SOX -5.9% / SMH -4.8% against IGV +5.0%** and called it "the largest spread between the IGV and SMH we have ever seen"; **J.P. Morgan's US semis specialist desk (Josh Meyers)** called it "the biggest single day SOXX-IGV spread ever"; **Morgan Stanley** recorded the first **+10% single-session close in its Software-vs-Semis pair (MSZZSFSE +11.3%) since 2006**; **Susquehanna** called it "the largest software-versus-semis performance gap in more than five years". ➤ Four superlatives, four different constructions — SOX vs SOXX vs SMH vs a proprietary MS pair, and "ever" vs "since 2006" vs "five years". They are not corroborating each other and must not be stacked. What they agree on is direction: **the de-rating on 09-14 was a SOFTWARE-vs-SEMIS rotation, not a semis-fundamental event** — the same distinction MS's "7 Debates" made on 09-07 (WFE numbers keep going up, the market refuses to pay for them). [[BESI]] printing a 3-month relative LOW on that screen is the single-name expression of it.
- 🔴🔴🔴 **THE BERNSTEIN SDC NOTE READ IN FULL — AND THE ONE GENUINELY NEW *MIX* NUMBER IS A MEMORY-SHARE-OF-EUV FIGURE, WHICH THIS PAGE HAS NEVER CARRIED IN ANY FORM: [[ASML]] "believes MEMORY COULD ACCOUNT FOR ROUGHLY 50% OF FUTURE EUV DEMAND AND POTENTIALLY MAINTAIN THAT SHARE, given the LARGE WAFER REQUIREMENTS associated with AI-RELATED MEMORY PRODUCTION RELATIVE TO LOGIC."** ➤ **Where it lands on this page's own arithmetic: the canonical Bernstein WFE decomposition (07-30) puts MEMORY AT 40% OF WFE (DRAM 32 / NAND 8) with NAND at 0% EUV — so a ~50% memory share of EUV is ABOVE memory's share of the WFE dollar and has to be carried almost entirely by DRAM.** It is the litho-side confirmation of [[LRCX]]'s 09-10 ranking (**DRAM the LARGEST driver of 2027 WFE growth, ahead of leading-edge foundry/logic**) and of [[KLAC]]'s same-week non-decelerating 2027, and it says the litho layer is now as memory-levered as the dep/etch layer that this page's DRAM-vs-logic debate has been fought on. ⚠️ **"Future" is UNDATED and the metric is UNDEFINED (units vs revenue vs exposures) — a directional vendor claim, NOT a modellable split. Do NOT reconcile it against the 40%-of-WFE segment weights: different denominators.** (Bernstein · David Dai, CFA / Carmine Milano, CFA — "ASML: SDC Key Takeaways - 110 LNA EUV units is not a supply cap in 2028", Outperform, PT EUR 2,500, ASML.NA close 2026-09-14 EUR 1,387.80, 2026-09-15)
- 🔴🔴 **AND THE VENDOR PUTS A FLOOR UNDER LITHO INTENSITY EXACTLY WHERE THE DRAM BEAR CASE ATTACKS IT: ASML "CONTINUES TO DISAGREE WITH SUGGESTIONS THAT LITHOGRAPHY INTENSITY COULD FALL DURING THE TRANSITION FROM 6F² TO 4F² ARCHITECTURES", expecting instead ADDITIONAL EUV LAYERS TO BE USED IN CRITICAL DRAM PROCESSES and ONGOING MIGRATION FROM DUV MULTI-PATTERNING TOWARDS EUV SINGLE EXPOSURE.** ➤ **First direct rebuttal on this page of the 4F²-kills-litho-intensity argument, and it arrives from the OPPOSITE END OF THE TOOL CHAIN to [[LRCX]]'s "DRAM de-speccing is a red herring" (09-09). The two largest single-step vendors — litho and etch — now defend the same DRAM content leg from opposite sides, which is the strongest form this page's de-spec counter-chain has taken.** ⚠️ **Interested-party assertion, unquantified, NOT ADOPTED. And note it sits ON TOP of, not against, [[AMAT]]'s own 6F2→4F2 read already on this page (SAM +10% moving 6F2→4F2, +30% at 3D DRAM, Cantor 06-25): AMAT sizes the etch/dep GAIN, ASML denies the litho LOSS, and nobody has published the NET.** (Bernstein · Dai / Milano, "ASML: SDC Key Takeaways", 2026-09-15)
- 🔴🔴 **THE PRODUCTIVITY CEILING MOVES UP, AND IT IS MECHANICALLY BEARISH FOR TOOL *UNITS* EVEN AS IT IS BULLISH FOR ASML'S P&L: ASML "sees a PATHWAY FROM ROUGHLY 330 WAFERS PER HOUR ON CURRENT LNA SYSTEMS TOWARDS 400-500 WAFERS PER HOUR IN FUTURE SYSTEMS", combined with broader platform improvements and the larger mask format.** ➤ **This page's Low-NA throughput ladder topped out at 300 wph (NXE:4200G) and 330 wph (NXE:4200H, post-2030). 400-500 wph is a NEW CEILING ABOVE EVERYTHING ON FILE.** ✅ **It also retroactively corroborates a mark this page explicitly did NOT ADOPT nine lines below** — ASML IR's *"new high productivity platform… 500 [wafers] an hour"* from the 09-09 Communacopia-week transcript, left unreconciled against the 300 wph G figure given minutes earlier. **A second, independent, WRITTEN source now carries the same magnitude, so the 500 was not an ASR garble. The 09-14 "NOT ADOPTED" status is upgraded to CORROBORATED, NOT ADOPTED AS A DATED MODEL INPUT (no system name and no year is attached to the 400-500 in either source).** ➤➤ **The consequence for this page's own standing argument: wafer capacity compounding faster than units (+45% vs +30%) is precisely why the 110 is not a binding capacity number — and EVERY further productivity step widens that wedge, i.e. each one REDUCES the units needed to deliver a given kwpm. A 330 → 400-500 wph step is a further ~21-52% of capacity per tool the customer never has to buy as a unit.** ⚠️⚠️ **VINTAGE/BASIS CONFLICT, FLAGGED AND NOT RESOLVED: Bernstein calls 330 wph "CURRENT LNA systems", while ASML IR's OWN ladder six days earlier puts current at 220-260 wph (F ≈ 260), 300 (G) only "towards the end of the decade", and this page dates 330 (NXE:4200H) to POST-2030. Both cannot be "current". NOTHING ON THIS PAGE IS SUPERSEDED — establish which system the 330 refers to before either figure enters a model.** (Bernstein · Dai / Milano, "ASML: SDC Key Takeaways", 2026-09-15)
- 🔴🔴 **ORDER-BOOK QUALITY GETS THE TWO THINGS A CYCLE THIS HOT ACTUALLY NEEDS — CASH COMMITMENT AND A DOUBLE-BOOKING DENIAL, THE FIRST ON THIS PAGE FROM ANY VENDOR: 2026 revenue is "EFFECTIVELY COVERED", 2027 EUV PRODUCTION IS "LARGELY BOOKED" and DUV CAPACITY "CLOSE TO FULLY ALLOCATED", ORDERS FOR 2028 HAVE ALREADY BEEN RECEIVED — and "2028 ORDERS INCLUDE CUSTOMER DOWN-PAYMENTS, providing a STRONGER LEVEL OF COMMITMENT THAN IN PREVIOUS CYCLES". Asked the obvious question, ASML "DOES NOT CURRENTLY SEE SIGNS OF DOUBLE-BOOKING IN THE BACKLOG", and says it is monitoring customer requirements and shipment schedules "to ensure BACKLOG QUALITY REMAINS HIGH."** ➤ **Double-booking is the standard falsification of every "fully booked" claim this page carries — including BofA's own demand check in the bullets above ("2027 remains much a fully booked/contracted year across all compute/networking/memory vendors"), which has no such test attached to it. The DOWN-PAYMENT leg is the harder of the two pieces of evidence, because it is cash rather than opinion: it extends to 2028 ORDERS the prepayment discipline the 09-09 Capstone transcript described for 2027 (85 prepaid, plus many of the 110).** ⚠️ **A vendor's own assessment of its own backlog is the weakest available form of this evidence and is NOT ADOPTED as fact — it is logged because the question was PUT and the answer is ON THE RECORD, which makes it falsifiable at the next order print.** And the supply side is being built to match: **ASML is coordinating expansion "across its ENTIRE SUPPLY CHAIN, including key suppliers such as ZEISS, WHICH HAS AGREED TO INCREASE CAPACITY"** (Zeiss has no wiki page) — which matters because this page's binding-constraint thread has landed on NON-TOOL inputs every time (clean rooms through end-2027, construction labour, the sub-system tier). (Bernstein · Dai / Milano, "ASML: SDC Key Takeaways", 2026-09-15)
- 🔴 **CHINA — A SIZED GAP AND AN ARGUMENT THAT THE POLICY WORKS IN REVERSE, BOTH NET-NEW AGAINST THE MATCH-ACT MATERIAL ALREADY ON THIS PAGE: ASML "estimates that CHINESE EUV CAPABILITIES REMAIN MORE THAN A DECADE BEHIND CURRENT INDUSTRY LEADERS", and on the recent Chinese immersion-lithography announcements sees "a SUBSTANTIAL GAP BETWEEN PROTOTYPE DISCLOSURE AND HIGH-VOLUME MANUFACTURING". On policy, management was "PARTICULARLY CAUTIOUS regarding the potential impact of the MATCH ACT, arguing that ADDITIONAL RESTRICTIONS COULD ENCOURAGE EVEN GREATER INVESTMENT IN DOMESTIC CHINESE LITHOGRAPHY R&D" — because "China is willing to accept SLOWER technological progress but is UNLIKELY TO ACCEPT BEING COMPLETELY BLOCKED from accessing advanced technologies."** ➤ **The monopolist arguing that tighter export controls ACCELERATE the substitute is the mirror image of the localisation bull case this page carries from Bernstein's China tour (localisation rates "from 20-30% today to 80%+ for new projects") — same conclusion, opposite motive, which is what makes it worth logging. Detail on [china-export](china-export.md).** ⚠️ **BASIS GUARD: ">a decade behind" is an EUV-capability estimate by the incumbent and is NOT comparable with this page's 08-04 "~25 YEARS BEHIND" mark, which is Gavin Baker's learning-curve verdict on the Chinese IMMERSION DUV tool. Different technology, different metric, different source — do not net them or read the gap as narrowing.** (Bernstein · Dai / Milano, "ASML: SDC Key Takeaways", 2026-09-15)
- 🔴 **TWO LOWER-ORDER ITEMS, BOTH LANDING ON MARKS THIS PAGE ALREADY HOLDS. ① THE 12-INCH MASK CONSORTIUM GETS ITS FIRST PRODUCTIVITY NUMBER: the larger mask format "could provide 40% PRODUCTIVITY INCREASE through more efficient die layouts while reducing some of the limitations and productivity penalties associated with STITCHING" — and [[INTC]] "DOES NOT CONSIDER STITCHING A TECHNOLOGICAL SHOW-STOPPER", although it becomes LESS ATTRACTIVE FOR VERY LARGE DIES because RETICLE EXCHANGES can negatively affect productivity**, with the required reticle-stage modifications "technically challenging but viewed as manageable". ➤ **This page already dates the 12'' photomask ecosystem (pilot line 2031, production readiness 2033) and already carries Intel as the format's "strongest advocate" because it is the only path that removes stitching entirely. The 40% is the first QUANTIFICATION of the prize; the "not a show-stopper" line is a mild tension with "strongest advocate" — Intel WANTS the format but does not claim to NEED it, which weakens any 2031/2033 date as a forcing event.** ② **HIGH-NA ECONOMICS, STATED BY THE SELLER: ASML is "LARGELY AGNOSTIC regarding the TIMING of High-NA adoption because the ECONOMIC DIFFERENCE between High-NA and Low-NA is RELATIVELY LIMITED from its perspective", and High-NA "COULD EVEN BE SLIGHTLY DILUTIVE compared with HIGHLY PROFITABLE Low-NA tools" — though profitability should "improve considerably over the next few years as volumes increase" — and it "DOES NOT INTEND TO LOWER PRICES TO ACCELERATE ADOPTION" because "REDUCING PRICES TODAY WOULD MAKE FUTURE PRICE DISCIPLINE FAR MORE DIFFICULT."** ➤ **First-party confirmation of the conclusion this page ADOPTED from Bernstein's own cost modelling — *"slower HNA adoption is probably even better for [[ASML]]… much higher margin for LNA over HNA EUV"*. The vendor now says it in its own words, adds the word DILUTIVE, and supplies the pricing-discipline reason a cost model could not: the High-NA list price is being held as a precedent for the whole future price book, not as an adoption lever. ➤ It also re-frames the [[TSM]]-2030 / [[SAMSUNG]]-2028 adoption split already on this page as LOW-STAKES FOR ASML's P&L and high-stakes only for the customers' yield.** (Bernstein · Dai / Milano, "ASML: SDC Key Takeaways", 2026-09-15)

## 🆕🔴🔴🔴 2026-09-14 (/run-inbox — GS Communacopia 2026 MANAGEMENT compiles, dated 2026-09-13/14) — **THE TEST LAYER GETS ITS OWN PRICING LEG AND ITS OWN DEMAND-POOL INVERSION: [[ADVANTEST]] SHORTENS THE ASIC-vs-GPU TESTER CROSSOVER FROM "2-3 YEARS" TO "ROUGHLY ONE YEAR" AND MARKS TESTER ASP AT +10-15% PER YEAR, WHILE [[TER]] PUTS ~70% SHARE ON ONE HYPERSCALER AND ~$1bn OF ANNUAL ATE TAM ON EACH. THAT MAKES *THREE* VENDORS PUTTING PRICE ON THE TABLE IN ONE WEEK — AND ADVANTEST'S IS THE LARGEST ANNUAL RATE OF THE THREE.**

_(**MANAGEMENT SOURCES, NOT GOLDMAN SACHS RESEARCH** — the analyst's own small-group and broadcast notes from **Goldman Sachs' Communacopia + Technology Conference 2026, San Francisco**; the speakers are company management. ⚠️⚠️ **There is NO rating, NO price target and NO house estimate anywhere in either document — nothing below may be attributed to GS as a call.** Advantest (§1) and Teradyne (§13) sit in the [FULL SUMMARY - UPDATED compile, 2026-09-13](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/GS_Conference_2026_FULL_SUMMARY_UPDATED_2026-09-13.html); the richer [SMALL GROUPS + BROADCAST compile, 2026-09-14](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/GS_Conference_2026_MY_NOTES_SUMMARY_2026-09-14.html) covers [[TSM]], [[ASML]] and [[AMAT]] but carries **no Advantest and no Teradyne section**.)_ ⚠️ **DEDUP, STATED UP FRONT: the [[TSM]] / [[ASML]] / [[AMAT]] content in these compiles is the SAME set of Communacopia-week meetings this page already folded in from the Capstone Notion transcripts (the 09-14 block immediately below). NOT ONE NUMBER from those three was re-inserted. What follows is the TEST layer, which those blocks did not cover.**

- 🔴🔴🔴 **THE DEMAND-POOL INVERSION, AND IT IS THE MOST CONSEQUENTIAL TEST-LAYER LINE THIS PAGE HAS CARRIED: [[ADVANTEST]] MANAGEMENT *"shortened the expected crossover versus GPU from 2-3 years to roughly one year"* for ASIC test demand** — *"ASIC demand has accelerated enough"*, and *"ASIC exposure is increasingly important because hyperscaler custom silicon may become the largest incremental tester pool"* (GS Communacopia 2026 — Advantest meeting, 2026-09-13). ➤ **This page has tracked the ASIC-vs-merchant-GPU debate through third parties all quarter — Hon Precision's "ASICs to SURPASS GPUs in 2027" (JPM 07-30) and the mask-blank 25% CAGR driven *because of* ASIC proliferation (08-27). The INCUMBENT of merchant-GPU test now says the crossover is ~12 months away, having previously said 2-3 years. A 1-2 year compression, stated by the vendor with the most to lose from it, is the strongest form this signal can take — and it lands on the one account structure where the incumbency inverts, because [[TER]] says in the SAME WEEK it holds ~70% at one hyperscaler.** ⚠️ **"Crossover" is a demand-pool comparison, not a revenue-mix disclosure.**

## 🆕🔴🔴🔴 2026-09-14 (/wiki-ingest — Communacopia-week Capstone meeting notes, sources dated 2026-09-09) — **[[ASML]] RE-LOCATES ITS OWN "+45% CAPACITY" NUMBER AND IT IS NOT WHAT THIS PAGE LOGGED ON 09-01/04: THE 45% IS THE *SHIPPED FLEET* (UNITS × THROUGHPUT), THE INSTALL-BASE UPGRADES ARE *ON TOP* (~20 TOOL-EQUIVALENTS FROM THE D FLEET ALONE), AND THE 110 IS *LOW-NA ONLY*. _(**COMPANY PRIMARY, CAPSTONE IN THE ROOM** — [[ASML]] IR executive and [[AMAT]] management/IR at separate investor group meetings during GS Communacopia + Technology week, San Francisco, both **2026-09-09**, recorded via Notion AI · plus the semicap leg of a same-day **sell-side analyst call, BROKER UNCONFIRMED** (speakers 'Natalia'/'Gianmarco'; corpus name-matches point to two different houses — **never attribute to a named house**).)_ ⚠️⚠️ **All three are machine transcriptions of unlabeled multi-speaker rooms. - 🔴🔴🔴 **CORRECTION-GRADE AGAINST THIS PAGE'S OWN 09-01/04 BLOCK — THE 45% IS NOT AN INSTALL-BASE EFFECT.** This page carries BofA's relay of the ASML CFO: *"productivity upgrades driving c.45% MORE WAFER CAPACITY."* **ASML IR's own decomposition, given first-hand nine days later, locates the number somewhere else and makes the two legs ADDITIVE:** *"we will go from **44 units last year, 65, 85, 110**… we're going from models that are **160 wafers an hour and 220** … next year it's going to be **220 and 260** … towards the end of the decade… the newer **G model, 300** wafers an hour. So although we're growing **30% of the units** year over year, **the capacity of those units to deliver is growing by 45% year-over-year**."* **THEN, separately:** *"**On top of this**, we're also adding a lot to the install base… the older D model. We have **over 100 of those in the field** and we've already found ways to… take them from their existing 160 wafers an hour up to 190… **it's over 20 tools worth of capacity we could add just on that one model in the install base**"* (ASML IR @ Communacopia-week investor group mtg, 2026-09-09 — Capstone Notion transcript). ➤➤ **THE 45% IS UNITS × THROUGHPUT ON THE *NEW-BUILD* FLEET — IT ALREADY CONTAINS THE 30% UNIT GROWTH. It is therefore NOT the "45% of a tool order a customer does not have to place" that this page's 09-01/04 bullet inferred: the units and the capacity are the same statement counted two ways, and the install-base upgrade is the ONLY genuinely unit-displacing leg — sized here for the first time at **>20 tool-equivalents from one model**. The 09-01/04 bearish-for-units read is NOT deleted, it is RE-SIZED downward to the install-base leg alone.** ⚠️ **AND A UNIT-BASIS TRAP THIS PAGE HAS NEVER STATED: asked whether the 110 splits between Low-NA and High-NA, IR answered *"That number is only low NA."* Every 110-based conversion on this page and in `../_meta/assumptions.md` is a LOW-NA count; High-NA sits outside it.** ⚠️ The transcript separately has IR describing a post-G *"new high productivity platform… 500 [wafers] an hour"* — **not reconciled with the 300 wph G figure given minutes earlier and NOT ADOPTED.**
- 🔴🔴 **READ-ACROSS FLAGGED, NOT RE-DERIVED — THIS LANDS ON THE CANONICAL `euv-fab-intensity` CONSTANT.** The ledger's canonical conversion is **~11 EUV tools per 25kwpm at N2** on a **fixed** real-throughput basis (2,430 wpm/tool, ~34% OEE, Bristlemoon Jun-2026). ASML has now said on the record that nameplate throughput per shipped tool rises **160 → 220 → 260 → 300 wph** across the D/E/F/G ladder *and* that the installed fleet is being re-rated in place (**D: 160 → 190 wph on 100+ tools**). ➤ **A tools-per-kwpm constant held flat therefore OVER-COUNTS tools for a given capacity in the later years of any 2027-30 model. The canonical entry is NOT amended here — this is logged as a dated vendor statement bearing on it, for the assumptions pass to adjudicate.**
- 🔴🔴 **BOTH OF THE TWO LARGEST VENDORS PUT PRICE ON THE TABLE IN THE SAME ROOM-WEEK, WHICH IS THE ASP LEG UBS SAID ON 09-01 IS *"NOT IN CONSENSUS AT ALL"*.** **[[ASML]]:** organic ASP tracks throughput roughly **1:1** (*"the tool that we shipped at 160 [wafers] an hour the ASP was about 160… the 220 tool, the ASP was about 220"* — so the F at 260 wph implies **~€260M+**), **plus a ONE-TIME structural step-up of 5-10% on NEW EUV and immersion POs** (*"5% to 10% increase on these special product, EUV and immersion, where we have real differentiation"*), entering backlog *"now-ish"*, shipping *"maybe late next year, but mostly 2028"*, with existing POs untouched — *"Those POs are sacred."* An investor's sum of the three legs — 2027 organic + 2028 organic + the step-up — put a 2028 EUV tool *"like 20% higher than it is today"*, and IR answered *"Yeah, right on EUV"* (⚠️ **the ~20% is the INVESTOR's arithmetic accepted by IR, not an ASML disclosure**). **[[AMAT]], same week:** *"we've been consistently increasing margins… in the last year we've increased our semi-equipment **190 basis points**… to the **mid-50s**. We've increased **AGS margins 180 basis points**"*, with the downside clause stated explicitly — *"if things slow down somewhere in our customer base, **we're not going to lower our system prices**… once the price point is established, we're not going to discount"* — and an admission that the pass-through is lagging the customer (*"we're not going up as fast as them"*, against customers taking 1,000-4,000bps of GM expansion). ➤ **For a page that models WFE in DOLLARS, this is the leg that makes the dollar TAM grow faster than the unit TAM, and it is now stated by both vendors rather than modelled. It is also the mechanism behind the 07-17 Jefferies *"tool inflation ~10%"* mark already on this page — which the ASML step-up now puts ABOVE 10% on EUV/immersion specifically.**
- 🔴🔴 **AND THE CEILING ON TOOL-VENDOR PRICING POWER GETS NAMED, WITH A HISTORICAL PRECEDENT — THE FIRST TIME ANY SOURCE ON THIS PAGE HAS BOUNDED IT:** asked why only 5-10% when *"memory is raising prices by a billion percent"*, ASML IR: *"More than that would be greedy… **although there's no other EUV supplier, customers… pursue Moore's Law by integrating many different things.** Litho… but then materials, etch and… architecture changes… 3D integration… **if one leg gets too expensive, then we rely more on another leg. So if litho doubles in price, they might say, ah, I'm going to find an architecture that's less litho-intensive.**"* The precedent he volunteers is **NAND**: *"NAND stopped shrinking horizontally, and that's what we're good at. They stopped. **They don't even buy [immersion] tools.** Then they increased density by going vertical."* ➤ **The monopolist's own stated pricing model is that its pricing power is capped by ARCHITECTURAL SUBSTITUTION, not by competition — and it has one worked example where a whole segment walked away from its highest-value product. That is the single most useful frame on this page for pricing any tool vendor's ASP leg, and it also puts an upper bound on the litho-intensity bull case. Litho intensity itself was put at *"mid 20s or so"* (ASML's own framing: litho spend ÷ litho + everything else), consistent with the 24-26% range Bernstein carries here.**
- 🔴 **THE MARGIN LADDER AND THE SERVICE-INTENSITY RATIO, BOTH OF WHICH SIT ON THIS PAGE'S EXISTING SERVICE-VS-EQUIPMENT BLOCK (08-02):** D→E was *"37% faster, 30% more ASP"* and took GM *"from 52-ish to 58-ish"*; E→F is *"less dramatic"* but directionally the same; and the CMD frame is now disowned upward — *"we talked about a gross margin trajectory at a capital market[s] day going to **as high as 60% by the end of the decade**. I think that's **looking a little rusty** right now"*, with new targets at the **2027 CMD** (⚠️ *"rusty"* is an ASR garble; the following sentence is *"things are looking a little rosier"*). **The service ratio is the transferable number: on mature DUV ASML recovers *"1% or 2% of the ASP on a recurring basis"* (break-fix); on EUV it is *"more like 5% of the price on a recurring basis"* — i.e. ~5% of a €250M+ tool, not ~1-2% of *"a few million"*.** Install-Base Management GM swings *"low 40s"* in weak periods to **60%** in strong ones, and the lumpiness is real: last quarter *"we forecasted 2.5 billion. We did 2.8 billion. There's **300 million euros** of money that fell into our lap within the quarter."* ➤ **Slots into the long-term service-target ranking this page carries (LAM 10%+ · ASM 12%+ · KLA 13-15% · [[AMAT]] mid-teens): litho's version is an ASP-linked annuity that re-bases upward every time the tool price does — so the 5-10% step-up lifts the service line mechanically too.**
- 🔴 **THE DUV COMPANION RATIO, WHICH IS THE MIX POINT BEHIND THE BRAINPORT ANNOUNCEMENT AND WHICH THIS PAGE HAS NEVER CARRIED:** *"if you look at the number of layer stacks at customers, **a DRAM might have 80 to 100 layers. Currently, only five to seven of those are EUV.** … if you need 110 EUV tools, you need a lot more of the other tools to be the companion of that… **For dozens more EUV, you need hundreds more** [DUV]."* Hence **Brainport/Eindhoven from 2029 is a DEEP-UV capacity project**, freeing Veldhoven for EUV — and asked directly whether the supply mix is shifting toward DUV, IR: *"**The composition is not changing.** It's just how we choose to find the most efficient way to make it."* ➤ **Two consequences: (1) the 2029 Brainport headline is NOT an incremental EUV signal and should not be read as one; (2) any EUV-unit forecast on this page implies a DUV-unit forecast an order of magnitude larger, which is where the immersion step-up also applies.**
- 🔴🔴 **[[AMAT]] SUPPLIES THE ONE RATIO THAT PRICES THE UPGRADE-VS-GREENFIELD DEBATE THIS PAGE HAS RUN QUALITATIVELY SINCE 08-25:** *"The biggest difference in DRAM is **if you're just upgrading, you're probably spending 25% of the CapEx as if you were doing greenfield**. That's the difference. So it's **the amount of spending, not the share**"* — and NAND is the segment where it bites, *"way less capital intensive in [NAND] than it is in DRAM or [foundry]… it's really all upgrade driven today."* Around it: the growth ladder said out loud (*"we said in February it's **over 20%**, in May we said **over 30%**, now we're **approaching 40%**"*, ⚠️ verbal, the last PRINTED figure is >30%); **ICAPS at *"mid to high single digits over time"***; **over 100 new fabs tracked, 10 announced in the last quarter alone**; and the structural mix statement this page should keep — **foundry wafer starts: smartphones were *"four times larger than data center"* a few years ago, data center has already passed PCs and is crossing smartphones, and *"in a couple of years, it's 2x data center versus smartphones."*** ➤ **The 25% ratio is directly usable: a DRAM year delivered by upgrade rather than greenfield is ~¼ the WFE dollars for the same bit growth. That is the quantified version of the de-spec / "shells not tools" argument — and it cuts BOTH ways, because it also says the greenfield years are 4x.** ⚠️ **AND CLEAN ROOM IS RE-CONFIRMED AS THE 2027 GATE FROM A THIRD SEAT IN ONE WEEK: *"how fast is clean room capacity coming online? … that's got to really gate more than anything how fast we ramp at 27"* — alongside [[LRCX]] (09-09, 09-10) and [[MSFT]]'s Amy Hood on datacentres.**
- ⚠️🔴 **THE "$300bn 2028 WFE" LINE — HELD TO CANONICAL DISCIPLINE, BECAUSE TWO DIFFERENT THINGS ARE CIRCULATING UNDER ONE NUMBER.** ① **[[AMAT]]'s version is a SUPPLY-CHAIN STRESS TEST, not a forecast:** asked whether it plans for a $300bn WFE market, management answered *"we're definitely running all those scenarios all the time"* and *"you can imagine that there are **scenarios above the $300 billion that we're driving through our supply chain**"* — consistent with the 08-20 disavowal already on this page (the 2x capacity plan is *"demand preparedness, not an implicit WFE forecast"*) and with [[KLAC]]'s identical $300bn supplier-prep framing (08-01). ② **The analyst call's version is UNATTRIBUTED MARKET CHATTER** — the Capstone side said *"I get to around **250** of WFE spending, but I'm starting to hear numbers higher now"*, answered *"**Way higher, yeah**"*, and *"starting to hear for 2028… people are getting to **300 billion dollars** or something like this."* ⚠️⚠️ **NOT A HOUSE MARK, NOT ADOPTED, AND THE BROKER IS UNCONFIRMED. Verify against UCTT's own disclosure before it is quoted anywhere.**

That is the most important structural agreement on this page.** **Lam also held CY26 WFE in the LOW $150BN RANGE, called 2027 "A GROWTH YEAR" and 2028 "PROBABLY A PRETTY DECENT YEAR", put its SERVED MARKET ABOVE 36% OF WFE THIS YEAR AND AHEAD OF PLAN, printed GM AT 52% — A 20-YEAR HIGH — and put NAND WAFER STARTS ENDING 2026 ABOUT 20% BELOW PEAK.**
- 🔴🔴 **AND LAM FLATLY CONTRADICTS THIS WIKI'S 09-09 HEADLINE FINDING: Bettinger calls "DRAM DE-SPECCING 'A RED HERRING'" — against the three-layer HBM de-spec chain logged the same day ([[NVDA]]'s Rubin Ultra HBM4E downticked 16-Hi → 8-Hi/12-Hi; [[SKHYNIX]]'s 8Hi mix at 55-60%, HIGHER than 12Hi; [[BESI]]'s 2027/28E hybrid-bonding shipments CUT to 115/190 and ONTO's 2027 advanced-packaging growth cut to ~50% from ~60% explicitly "on NVDA / HBM DE-SPEC RISK").** ⚠️ **NEITHER SIDE ADOPTED. Note they may not even be discussing the same thing — Bettinger says DRAM, the chain logged is HBM STACK HEIGHT, and de-speccing a stack is not de-speccing a die. That distinction is the first thing to pin down. THE FALSIFIABLE TEST ALREADY ON FILE STANDS: BESI's 2027 hybrid-bonding tool shipments against 115.**
- 🔴🔴 **APPLIED'S MIX STATEMENT IS THE STRUCTURAL ONE AND IT IS HELD FORWARD, NOT TREATED AS A PEAK: "leading-edge logic, advanced packaging, and memory [will] account for ~80% OF WFE IN 2026 AND SIMILAR MIX GOING AHEAD", with the ~40% 2026 REVENUE GROWTH GUIDE REITERATED, "STRONG GROWTH CONTINUING INTO 2027 AND 2028 given INCREASING ORDERS AND BACKLOG WITH LONGER-DATED VISIBILITY", and PROCESS CONTROL "EXPECTED TO GROW MORE THAN 50% IN 2026."** ➤ **An ~80% AI-adjacent mix that does NOT mean-revert says the trailing-edge share never comes back — the opposite of every prior WFE cycle on this page, and directly relevant to the China read-through, because the domestic build is concentrated in exactly the ~20% residual. And Process Control at >50% against ~40% company-level growth means the metrology/inspection attach is compounding ~10pp faster than the corporate average, on a mechanism (yield at advanced nodes) that gets HARDER with stack height and hybrid bonding — the same signal [[KLAC]] carries from the other side.** ⚠️ **Note the confidence gap on 2028: Dickerson says "strong growth… into 2027 AND 2028"; Bettinger says 2028 is "PROBABLY a pretty decent year." Same industry, same 24 hours, audibly different conviction. NEITHER ADOPTED.**
- 🔴 **TEST GETS A MANAGEMENT FLOOR RATHER THAN A SHARE STORY: [[TER]]'s CEO — "the TEST EQUIPMENT MARKET [will] AT LEAST GROW IN LINE WITH OVERALL SEMICONDUCTOR CAPEX", with AI-driven growth "TO ACCELERATE THROUGH 2027" on RISING SHARE AT A MAJOR MERCHANT COMPUTE CUSTOMER, hyperscale custom silicon and ASIC testing, and hyperscaler requirements across CHIP, SYSTEM AND DATACENTER test creating "REINFORCING DEMAND ACROSS ALL THREE BUSINESSES."** ⚠️ Nothing quantified; the "major merchant compute customer" is unnamed (only [[NVDA]] is of that scale — INFERRED, NOT STATED).
- 🔴🔴 **THE LOCALISATION BEAR CASE GOT ITS OWN CAPEX NUMBER FROM MORGAN STANLEY, AND IT NAMES THIS PAGE'S CATEGORIES AS THE TARGETS: the China BLUEPAPER puts "~US$12tn, or Rmb80tn, of INCREMENTAL industrial capex (out of US$50tn cumulative) for industrial IRL during 2026-35", accelerating to a 6-7% CAGR over 2028-35 — and labels as "SOVEREIGN BOTTLENECKS" precisely "WAFER FAB EQUIPMENT (WFE), ELECTRONIC DESIGN AUTOMATION, CNC MACHINE TOOLS, METROLOGY AND ADVANCED MATERIALS", with "UPSTREAM SOFTWARE/EQUIPMENT PROFIT MIGRATION" listed as NOT PRICED IN.** ➤ **Read as a Chinese bull case it is, item for item, the Western semicap and EDA localisation bear case. Recorded in full on [china-export](china-export.md).** ⚠️ MS estimate over a 10-year horizon, no share assumptions — **NOT ADOPTED.**
- 🔴 **AND THE NEAR-TERM ORDER SIGNAL FROM SEMICON TAIWAN THAT IS NOT ABOUT FRONT-END AT ALL: US brokers' six takeaways (via @QQ_Timmy) put the bottleneck in PHOTONIC-IC COUPLING AND TEST equipment — "PIC must catch up to a semiconductor/EIC automation system that has matured over decades", and ROBOTECHNIK/FICONTEC SAYS ITS TARGET SHIPMENTS OVER THE NEXT YEAR WILL EXCEED 50% OF ITS CUMULATIVE SHIPMENTS OF THE PAST 25 YEARS. "TEST IS THE EARLIEST CPO BENEFICIARY" — double-sided wafer-level Insertion 2 still considered indispensable, hyperspectral KGD screening pulling test forward to Insertion 1 or 0, and dual-FA active alignment for 1.6T modules CUTTING COUPLING TIME BY ~50%.** ⚠️ **The brokers' own framing is the investable point: "it is not about a single machine — the WHOLE AUTOMATED LINE has to be filled in within 1-2 YEARS, and whoever can supply BOTH hardware and software automation captures this expansion." Chinese-language relay of broker checks; the vendors named are Taiwanese small caps not covered here. Detail on [optical-cpo](optical-cpo.md).**

## 🆕🔴🔴 2026-09-09 (/wiki-ingest) — **WFE PER GIGAWATT GETS A RATIO AND A CROSS-CHECK: $7BN OF WFE PER $100BN OF AI CAPEX, WHICH ON MS'S OWN $1.2TN 2027 HYPERSCALE CAPEX / 29GW IMPLIES 2027 WFE OF $204-242BN AGAINST MSe $223BN. PLUS THE HIGH-NA ROADMAP GETS DATES — AND THE TWO HOUSES' TOOL RAMPS DIFFER BY ~4x.**
_(Morgan Stanley · **Shane Brett** (seven core debates; prefers **[[AEIS]], MKSI, ONTO**), **2026-09-08**; Morgan Stanley · **Lee Simpson** (European semis), **2026-09-08**; BofA · **Didier Scemama**, **2026-09-08**; Barclays · **Simon Coles**, **2026-09-08**; Bernstein · Qingyuan (China tour), **2026-09-09**)_
- 🔴🔴 **THE RATIO, AND THE FACT THAT IT TIES: "WFE PER GW: Shane sees $7BN OF WFE PER $100BN OF AI CAPEX. Morgan Stanley Internet estimate of $1.2T HYPERSCALE CAPEX IN 2027 / 29GW OF COMPUTE implies 2027 WFE $204-242BN vs MSe $223BN."** ➤ **A top-down ratio that reproduces the bottom-up house number is the most useful WFE sanity check this page has had. It also means the 2027 WFE forecast is now hostage to the hyperscale capex forecast in a way that can be tracked directly.** ⚠️ **$/GW HAZARD: "$7bn of WFE per $100bn of AI capex" is a WFE-TO-CAPEX RATIO and "$1.2tn / 29GW" is a ~$41bn CAPEX-PER-GW basis. Neither is a build cost, a revenue-per-GW figure, or a financing figure — six mutually non-comparable per-GW units now circulate on this wiki (see [themes/ai-datacenter-power](ai-datacenter-power.md)).**
- 🔴 **THE SEVEN DEBATES, WITH THE TWO CONTRARIAN CALLS AND THE VALUATION ARGUMENT: "Bull cases have MOSTLY COME THROUGH: [[INTC]], NAND, DRAM. However, Shane thinks TERAFAB ($2BN WFE IN 2027/2028) and MATURE LOGIC (+18% IN 2027) ARE UNDERAPPRECIATED."** Plus **"DRAM TAM OF $979BN on +10% ASP GROWTH AND +38% SUPPLY GROWTH"**; **"[[INTC]] market share: would NOT RULE OUT A 'SUPPLIER RESHUFFLE'"**; **"HBM DE-SPEC: PROCESS CONTROL MOST VULNERABLE"**; concerns on **AI ROIC and "overly full expectations"** with **[[AEIS]] and ONTO "now BELOW historic fwd PE range"**; and the dislocation call — **"SUB-SYSTEM VS OEM DISLOCATION: MKSI and [[AEIS]] are MISPRICED, TRADING LIKE 'EARLY-CYCLE' STOCKS VALUED ON CY27 VS OEMs ON CY28."**
- 🔴🔴 **HIGH-NA STOPPED BEING A "WHETHER" QUESTION AND THE UNIT RAMPS NOW DISAGREE BY ~4x, WHICH IS THE LIVE EDGE ON THIS PAGE. The roadmap: [[INTC]] already in HVM with >1 MILLION WAFERS processed across qualification, R&D and production including select Panther Lake layers on 18A, performing at or better than 0.33-NA EUV; [[TSM]] into advanced-node manufacturing FROM 2030; [[SAMSUNG]] into DRAM production FROM 2028; [[SKHYNIX]] committed with NO date; [[MU]] ABSENT and "far behind in EUV adoption"; and a 12'' PHOTOMASK ecosystem targeting a PILOT LINE BY 2031 and PRODUCTION READINESS BY 2033.** The forecasts: **BofA models 5 High-NA tools this year, 6 next and 20 BY 2030** with contribution "likely modest through 2030"; **BARCLAYS' "EUV high NA shipped units DON'T START GROWING FROM 5 PER YEAR UNTIL 2029."** ➤ **Same 2030 TSMC date, ~4x different tool count in between. Falsifiable against ASML's own High-NA shipment disclosure through 2027-29.**
- 🔴 **AND THE NARRATIVE CONSEQUENCE BARCLAYS NAMES, WHICH IS THE REASON THIS MATTERS BEYOND UNIT COUNTS: "across global semicap, [[ASML]] has been PERCEIVED AS A LAGGARD with respect to WFE growth given the view that DEPOSITION AND ETCHING WOULD GROW FASTER THAN LITHOGRAPHY in the coming years as architectures become more complicated and 3D. HIGH NA ADOPTION WOULD MATERIALLY CHANGE THAT NARRATIVE although THE DEBATE NOW WILL BE DOES ASML LAG UNTIL 2030 ONWARDS when high NA adoption fully ramps."** Plus BofA's second-order call: litho intensity rising further post-High-NA **"likely PUSHES OUT the adoption of 3D DRAM to BEYOND 2036."** And the capacity decision: ASML **"has a significant decision ahead on whether to FURTHER EXPAND EUV CAPACITY than the recently expanded targets"**, with High-NA certainty possibly unlocking **increased shareholder returns "at the CMD next year."**
- 🔴⚠️ **MS'S OWN EUROPEAN TEAM SUPPLIES THE BEAR CASE FOR MEMORY-EXPOSED SEMICAP: "DRAM pricing appears CLOSE TO A SECOND-DERIVATIVE PEAK, and our memory analysts expect the cycle to TRANSITION TO LATE-CYCLE BY 4Q26. While we think this could WEIGH ON SENTIMENT TOWARDS MEMORY-EXPOSED SEMI-CAP EQUIPMENT NAMES, structural AI tailwinds should provide some cushion."** Its three semi-cap debates are **"(i) [[ASML]]'s EUV CAPACITY INTO 2028, (ii) EXPORT RESTRICTIONS, (iii) 2028 WFE GROWTH"**, and it cut **[[ASML]]'s PT to EUR 1,700 from EUR 1,930 on a 30x P/E (from 35x)** and **[[BESI]]'s to EUR 220 from EUR 260 on 31x FY27 (from 35x)** while cutting BESI's **2027/28E hybrid-bonding tool shipments to 115/190.**
- ⚠️ **CHINA LOCALISATION, TWO READS: Bernstein's 20-company China tour found "supply shortages now THE DOMINANT industry concern and A KEY CATALYST FOR ACCELERATING LOCALIZATION", with LOGIC FABS the biggest beneficiaries and localisation rates expected to rise "FROM 20-30% TODAY TO 80%+ FOR NEW PROJECTS, driving MULTI-YEAR ORDER GROWTH."** Barclays' read-across is more measured: Chinese semicap progress in **etching, deposition, hybrid bonding and compound-semiconductor equipment** is real, but **"the incremental updates on newer products remain largely focused on CUSTOMER QUALIFICATION, PILOT PRODUCTION, INITIAL VOLUME DEPLOYMENT AND REPEATED ORDERS"** — with **ASMI** facing multiple domestic vendors advancing thermal ALD, PEALD and batch furnace "particularly in memory" while ASM's stronghold stays logic/foundry/analog; **[[AIXA]]** seeing Naura and AMEC progress in **InP MOCVD** (Naura's batch system ahead in client verification) but with "high customer switching and qualification barriers"; and **[[BESI]]** seeing Chinese die-to-wafer hybrid bonding still only being qualified while wafer-to-wafer has repeat orders. ➤ **The two are not contradictory: demand-side urgency is accelerating localisation intent, while the tool-side evidence is still at qualification rather than displacement.**

## 🆕🔴🔴 2026-09-07 — **TSMC PUTS A NUMBER ON ITS OWN TOOL ORDERS AT SEMICON: 1.0x (END-2025) → 1.5x (1Q26) → 1.9x (JULY). BLOOMBERG CONFIRMS IT INDEPENDENTLY AND NAMES A DIFFERENT BINDING CONSTRAINT — CONSTRUCTION LABOUR.**
- 🔴🔴 **"[[TSM]]'s comment at SEMICON that stronger demand has led to A RISE IN THE EQUIPMENT ORDERS IT NEEDS TO PLACE WHICH HAS GROWN FROM 1x BY THE END OF 2025 TO 1.5x IN 1Q26 AND FURTHER TO 1.9x BY JULY"** (BofA · **Haas Liu**, "Semicon Taiwan: AI drives innovation across the semiconductor stack", 2026-09-07/08, Estimate Change).
- ✅ **INDEPENDENT CONFIRMATION, SAME DAY: "TSMC's QUARTERLY CHIPMAKING TOOL NEEDS ALMOST DOUBLED THIS YEAR as it expands production to meet AI demand. TSMC is grappling with A LACK OF EXPERIENCED CONSTRUCTION WORKERS IN TAIWAN, and will work hard 'WELL INTO NEXT YEAR' to close the supply-demand gap"** (Bloomberg, via JPM Tech HW/Semis Weekly, 2026-09-07). **Two sources, one of them management, same magnitude.**
- ➤➤ **THE BLOOMBERG VERSION ADDS THE THING THE BofA VERSION OMITS, AND IT CHANGES THE TRADE: the constraint TSMC names is CONSTRUCTION LABOUR, not tool lead time. An order index that nearly doubles into a labour-constrained fab shell means the ORDERS are real and the RECOGNITION TIMING is the risk. This page has spent two weeks logging tool and material lead times as the binding constraint (ABF laminator 6 months → 2 years, die bonder at 12 months, 09-06). Today the largest buyer says the wall is CIVIL WORKS. Both can be true; they bind in sequence, and the civil-works constraint binds LATER and is harder to relieve with capex.**
- 🔴 **BofA raises industry capex on the back of it: LOGIC FOUNDRY 2027 +23% y/y to US$105bn; PACKAGING & TESTING +36% y/y to US$33bn — "spending strength continues to be led by TSMC but now SUPPLEMENTED BY COMMITMENT FROM OSATs" — with utilisation staying high in '27 across advanced nodes AND packaging "EVEN WITH AGGRESSIVE EXPANSION", and ADVANCED-TECHNOLOGY PRICING RISING 10-15%+ Y/Y IN '27.** BofA lifts PO and estimates on **TSMC, ASE, All Ring and [[MEDIATEK]]**, staying positive on **Chroma and GUC.**
- ⚠️ **AND THE COUNTER-DATAPOINT IN THE SAME NOTE, WHICH SHOULD NOT BE DROPPED: "TSMC noted the importance of higher-level BACK-END TOOL AUTOMATION AND MODULARIZATION as it could REDUCE TIME FOR RE-VALIDATION and provide the supply chain with HIGHER LEVEL OF FUNGIBILITY FOR DIFFERENT GENERATIONS OF TECHNOLOGIES." Cross-generation tool fungibility is structurally NEGATIVE for the tools-per-node bull case, and it comes from the same paragraph as the 1.9x. Carry both.**

**🆕🔴 2026-09-07 — MORGAN STANLEY: "WFE CONTINUES TO REWRITE THE BULL CASE", AND THAT IS WHY IT STAYS IN-LINE.** (Morgan Stanley · **Shane Brett, Joseph Moore**, Cate Folan, Mason Wayne, Ella Tulchinsky, Nicole Kozhukhov — "Semiconductor Capital Equipment: 7 Debates on SPE", 2026-09-07, 17pp, **Industry View IN-LINE**)
- 🔴 **"WFE continues to REWRITE THE BULL CASE as the industry BLOWS PAST CONSTRAINTS. The market is increasingly UNWILLING TO PAY FOR INCREMENTAL UPSIDE WITHOUT CLEARER EVIDENCE OF DURATION (AI ROIC). We favor names priced as though 2027 MARKS THE PEAK ([[AEIS]], MKS, ONTO)."**
- ➤ **This is a VALUATION call, not a demand downgrade, and the distinction matters for how this page files it: MS agrees the numbers keep going up and argues the de-rating is rational until ROIC duration is proven — so it expresses the sector through names where a 2027 peak is already in the multiple. ⚠️ The seven debates are not enumerated in the summary and the PDF was not retrieved.**
- 🔭 **Desk counterpoint the same day: "SEMICAPS. [[AMAT]], [[LRCX]], [[KLAC]], [[ASML]], [[TER]], AMKR… THEY BOTTOMED A FEW DAYS AGO.
