STOCKS 30% OFF HIGHS FOR LITERALLY NO REASON"** (BofA TMT spec sales · Brian Fenske, 2026-09-07 — *sales commentary, not research*). In the tape WFE still trailed: **"only KLAC +5.7% stood out really positively"** (JPM, 2026-09-07).
- **China: J.P. Morgan's Billy Feng, from CSEAC in Wuxi, RAISES '26/'27 China capex forecasts +10%/+9% and INTRODUCES A 2028 ESTIMATE OF US$54.7bn, on localisation and a local AI supply chain, "HELPED BY MEMORY STOCK LISTINGS."** ➤ *IPO-funded tool buying is a capital-markets channel that can close faster than end demand.*
- **Test and packaging content, from Jefferies/Fubon's SEMICON booth tour (Sherman Shang / Daniel Yang, 2026-09-06): "(1) ADVANCED PACKAGING BECOMES CORE ENGINE OF AI SYSTEM SCALING; (2) TEST, METROLOGY, OPTICAL I/O GAIN CONTENT AS AI COMPLEXITY RISES; (3) AI EXPANDS SEMICONDUCTOR PYRAMID BEYOND LEADING-EDGE NODES."** Corroborated by the Taiwanese monthlies: **Chroma August +156% y/y with QTD at 75% of MSe and 3Q tracking >20% q/q on SLT, metrology, CPO and FT handlers/burn-in ovens (MS · Derrick Yang, OW, PT NT$2,800); WinWay August +233% y/y with implied 3Q26 at +42% q/q vs guidance of +35% and MSe/Street at +37%/+28%, on AI GPU/ASIC/CPU socket ramps, but GM guided to 38-40% in 2H on higher depreciation and MEMS mix (MS · Tiffany Yeh, OW, PT NT$12,000).** ➤ *Two test-chain proxies printing +156% and +233% y/y is the strongest independent corroboration available for the "test gains content" leg — and WinWay's margin caveat is the first sign that socket capacity is being added faster than it can be absorbed at current margins.*
- 🔭 **ACCESS CALENDAR: BofA SEMICON WEST GROUP MEETINGS, OCT 13-14, San Francisco — [[TER]], MKSI, CAMT, PLAB, [[AEIS]], NVMI, ACLS, hosted by Vivek Arya. Bernstein is also holding meetings at SEMICON West Oct 13-14, and runs its 8th Annual Semis bus tour in San Jose SEPT 15-16 (ALAB, [[AMAT]], AMBA, [[AVGO]], LSCC, MPWR, [[NVDA]], [[NXPI]]).**

## 🆕🔴🔴 2026-09-06 — **FOUR HOUSES AND THE LARGEST BUYER'S OWN COO DESCRIBE THE SAME BINDING CONSTRAINT IN ONE DAY, AND IT IS *TOOL AND MATERIAL LEAD TIME*, NOT CAPITAL BUDGETS: AN ABF LAMINATOR AT **6 MONTHS → TWO YEARS** SINCE APRIL/MAY, A DIE BONDER AT **12 MONTHS** WHOSE SLOTS [[INTC]] IS PERSONALLY ALLOCATING THROUGH END-2027, TSMC SAYING *"TOOL DELIVERY FROM WFE PARTNERS ARE STILL NOT ENOUGH"*, AND ANNOUNCED **JAN-2027 PRICE HIKES OF 10-15% AT FOUNDRY AND >20% AT OSAT**.**

_All items below dated 2026-09-06 unless stated, all sourced from **SEMICON Taiwan 2026** (held 31 Aug – 4 Sept 2026). ⚠️ **CoWoS/CoPoS capacity numbers and the T-glass debate from the same conference are NOT on this page — they belong on [themes/cowos-packaging](cowos-packaging.md).**_

- 🔴🔴 **THE FIRST-PARTY QUOTE, AND IT IS FROM THE BIGGEST BUYER OF WFE ON EARTH.** At the SEMICON Taiwan CEO Forum fireside chat, **TSMC Deputy Co-COO Mr. Hou** said he had ***"never seen demand growing and changing this frequently as it has over the past six months, while the tool delivery from WFE partners are still not enough."*** MS's own frame around it: ***"Taiwan is still one of the bottlenecks of AI infrastructure buildout."*** (Morgan Stanley · Charlie Chan et al., "Greater China Semiconductors: SEMICON Taiwan 2026 key takeaways", 2026-09-06 — **MANAGEMENT PRIMARY QUOTE relayed by a house; the quote is TSMC's, the framing is MS's**.) ➤ **This is the cleanest statement this page has that the constraint sits on the SUPPLY side of the tool transaction, from the counterparty with the most bargaining power in the industry. It corroborates the DIRECTION of the relayed trade-press headline logged the day before (*"TSMC Fab Equipment Demand Nearly Doubles in Six Months… Amid Tool Shortages"*, Susquehanna · Mehdi Hosseini weekly relaying tomshardware, 2026-09-05 — **still headline-level, article UNREAD, NOT ADOPTED**) — but note the corroboration is of the *shortage*, not of the *"$64bn"* or the *"doubles"*, neither of which is adopted here.**
- 🔴🔴 **THE HARDEST NUMBER OF THE DAY, AND IT IS A MATERIALS TOOL RATHER THAN A WFE TOOL: *"the lead time of **Nikko-Materials' ABF vacuum laminator has risen to TWO YEARS, vs SIX MONTHS in April/May**."*** ➤ **A 4x extension in roughly four months, on a tool where *"Nikko-Materials' laminators have a near monopolistic position in high-end ABF substrates"* — and the reason it does not self-correct is stated in the same breath: *"its tool capacity expansion appears to be SLOWER THAN existing substrate makers' expansion."*** ⚠️ **Read carefully: this is the substrate layer, not the fab layer. It does not raise or lower any WFE number on this page. What it does is identify a bottleneck UPSTREAM OF THE BOTTLENECK — substrate makers can fund capacity faster than the single supplier of the laminator can build the tools to fill it, which is the same shape as the sub-system-tier constraint this page logged on Aug 1.** (Nomura · Aaron Jeng / Donnie Teng / Eric Chen / Vivian Yang / Anne Lee, 2026-09-06.)
- 🔴 **AND THE SECOND LEAD-TIME NUMBER COMES WITH SOMETHING RARER — A NAMED CUSTOMER PERSONALLY RATIONING THE SLOTS. On [[INTC]]'s EMIB-T: *"the lead time of the [Toray Engineering] die bonder is around **12 months**, and the **move-in schedule is MANAGED BY INTEL** with planned shipments through **end-2027E**."*** ➤ **A customer allocating a vendor's build slots two years forward is allocation behaving like price — the pattern this page first flagged on Aug 12 — and it is a durable, dated commitment rather than a sentiment datapoint.** (Nomura · Aaron Jeng et al., 2026-09-06.)
- ⚠️ **THE COMPETITIVE COUNTER-MOVE TO THAT MONOPOLY, ALSO DATED: **ASMPT is developing a TCB tool for QUALIFICATION IN EARLY 2027**, against **Toray as the current leading TCB supplier**. Separately, **GPTC** (wet-process tools to ASE) is guided to **400+ sets in 2027, up from a prior estimate of 220** — an ~82% raise to a single vendor's unit plan inside one conference cycle.** (UBS · Sunny Lin / Randy Abrams et al., 2026-09-06.) ➤ **Note the asymmetry the two Nomura lines and this one make together: where a tool has a near-monopoly supplier (Nikko-Materials laminators, Toray TCB), lead times are blowing out; where a second source is arriving or already exists (ASMPT into TCB, GPTC in wet process), the response shows up as UNIT PLANS being raised instead. Same demand, two different pressure-release valves.**
- 🔴🔴 **THE COST PASS-THROUGH IS NOW ANNOUNCED AND DATED, WHICH IS WHAT MAKES THIS A P&L ITEM RATHER THAN AN ANECDOTE: *"we expect **foundry and OSAT to conduct price hikes of at least 10-15% and OVER 20%, respectively, in JAN 2027**"*, reflecting *"strong demand from AI leading to INSUFFICIENT CAPACITY and ELEVATED MATERIAL PRICING."*** (Morgan Stanley · Daniel Yen / Charlie Chan / Daisy Dai / Tiffany Yeh, "Aspeed Technology: Substantial Upside Seen for 4Q26; Reiterate OW", 2026-09-06. ⚠️ **The note's subject, Aspeed, is OFF-COVERAGE on this wiki — no page created; the OW and the NT$24,000 PT belong to Aspeed and are recorded here only to source the quote.**) ➤ **The OSAT hike being the LARGER of the two (>20% vs 10-15%) is the tell: back-end capacity and packaging materials are tighter than front-end wafer capacity, which is consistent with every other item in this section coming from the packaging/substrate layer rather than from litho or etch.**

### 🔴 THE SYNTHESIS — THE BINDING CONSTRAINT BEING DESCRIBED IS **TIME**, NOT MONEY

Four houses (MS, Nomura, UBS, and MS again on the Aspeed note) plus the largest buyer's own COO, all in one day, all describe the same thing from different seats: **capital budgets are not what is limiting the AI build-out right now — tool and material DELIVERY is.** The evidence is unusually concrete because it is all lead times and dates rather than forecasts: **a laminator gone from 6 months to 2 years since April/May at a near-monopoly supplier whose own capacity expansion is slower than its customers'; a die bonder at 12 months whose slots [[INTC]] is personally allocating through end-2027; TSMC's Deputy Co-COO saying WFE partners' tool delivery is *"still not enough"* while demand changes faster than he has ever seen; and announced Jan-2027 price hikes of 10-15% at foundry and over 20% at OSAT.** ➤ **The investable consequence for this page is that the marginal WFE and back-end dollar in 2027 is being rationed by VENDOR CAPACITY, not by customer willingness to spend — which is bullish for tool-vendor pricing and backlog duration, bearish for any thesis that treats a capex-budget raise as immediately convertible into shipped tools, and it puts a floor under the delivery-schedule debate that the positioning-driven ~40% two-month de-rating of [[AMAT]]/[[LRCX]]/[[KLAC]] has been ignoring.**

⚠️ **THE COUNTERPOINT THIS PAGE MUST CARRY AGAINST ITSELF, AND IT IS ON THE [[ASML]] PAGE:** lead times can only be relieved by building more of a tool that already exists. The same-week evidence on ASML says the *capability* half of this cannot be accelerated at all — **@lithos_graphein** (independent author on chip patterning; **NOT a broker — no rating, no PT, no model**), recounting a 2020 Zeiss talk, dates one lens upgrade end to end: **a prototype already DEMONSTRATED WORKING in the then-shipping NXT:2000i in 2020 still took ANOTHER FOUR YEARS to ship in a production tool (NXT:2100i)**, and it was *"only one of many tool modifications"* in that generation, because *"Zeiss only knew what to do because it had absorbed years of fab data and the exposure of MILLIONS OF FULL-STACK PRODUCT WAFERS… They are developed on the production floor"* and *"it is very difficult, if not impossible, to apply these lessons in parallel just to go faster"* (2026-09-05). ➤ **So the constraint has two layers with very different clocks: CAPACITY lead time (2 years on a laminator, 12 months on a bonder) is a factory-build problem that money and time can fix; CAPABILITY lead time (four years from working prototype to production tool, gated by accumulated production-wafer data) is not. Any bull case that assumes the 2027 shortage resolves by 2028 is implicitly betting on the first layer only.**


## 🆕🔴🔴 2026-09-05 — **[[TSM]] EQUIPMENT DEMAND REPORTEDLY *DOUBLES IN SIX MONTHS* WITH 2026 CAPEX PUSHED TOWARD $64bn AND TOOLS IN SHORTAGE — WHILE CHINESE VENDORS MOVE ON THE BACK END AND THE FLOW DATA SHOWS FUNDS SELLING SEMIS ANYWAY.**

- 🔴🔴 **THE DEMAND HEADLINE: *"TSMC Fab Equipment Demand Nearly Doubles in Six Months — AI Surge Pushes 2026 CapEx Toward $64B Amid TOOL SHORTAGES."*** (Susquehanna · Mehdi Hosseini weekly, 2026-09-05, relaying tomshardware.) ⚠️ **A relayed trade-press headline, NOT a TSMC disclosure — TSMC's own 2026 capex guide is tracked on [[TSM]] and is NOT superseded by this. "Tool shortages" is the operative word for this theme, not the dollar figure.** ➤ **If it holds, it cuts directly against the positioning story below: [[AMAT]] / [[LRCX]] / [[KLAC]] de-rated ~40% over two months on what the 09-04 ingest logged as positioning rather than fundamentals, and this is a fundamentals datapoint pointing the other way. Related, same weekly: **TSMC bonuses surged 50%, outpacing revenue growth** (per SEC filings) — a cost line, and an AI-talent-war read.**
- 🔴🔴 **THE FLOW SIDE, AND IT IS THE COUNTERWEIGHT: funds *"selling higher-beta AI pockets including GLOBAL MEMORY, SEMIS and AI Tech Beneficiaries while continuing to ADD TO HYPERSCALERS as a higher-quality AI expression."* US L/S **net leverage fell 2pts WoW to 54%** and **gross fell 3pts to 209% — the 11th PERCENTILE since 2010**; APAC shows *"little appetite to re-enter the Asian AI complex"*, with Asia gross at **124%, the 15th percentile since 2010.** (Morgan Stanley PB Content team, via MS *Global Reflections* sales commentary / Nick Savone, 2026-09-04 — SALES COMMENTARY, NOT RESEARCH.) ➤ ✅ **This is the missing evidence for the 09-04 "de-rating is positioning" call: the funds actually did sell semis, and they did it while ADDING to the hyperscalers that buy the tools. Gross at the 11th percentile is the part that matters — positioning is light, not crowded, heading into a seasonally stronger period for gross additions.**
- 🔴🔴 **CHINESE EQUIPMENT VENDORS MOVE ON THE BACK END, WITH NAMED TOOLS AND NAMED STAGES.** NAURA has launched **12-inch hybrid bonding in both WTW and DTW, with DTW *"already at the customer application stage"***, plus TSV electroplating and panel-level packaging; **AMEC's plasma dicing is at CUSTOMER QUALIFICATION** at major Chinese customers, and it targets **>70% of advanced-packaging equipment categories within five years**; **Hwatsing shipped its first 12-inch wafer-thinning system for advanced memory in H1** after supplying CMP into Chinese HBM lines. (@jukan05, corpus, 2026-09-05, relaying Korean industry sources.) ⚠️ **The source's own caveat is carried: most of these tools are still in qualification or early commercialisation, and *"competitive yields, equipment uptime, stability and productivity in actual high-volume manufacturing lines"* remain unproven. NOT ADOPTED as share loss.** ➤ **Direct read-through to [[BESI]] (hybrid bonding) and [[DISCO]] (dicing / thinning / grinding) — filed on both pages. Full account on `china-export.md`.**
- 🔭 **UBS, relayed: China remains *"10 years"* behind ASML on EUV, *"stuck at ASML's 2004 level."*** (Susquehanna weekly, 2026-09-05, relaying wccftech.) ⚠️ **Relayed headline of an unread note; logged as framing. Note it is not in tension with the item above — the front-end gap is exactly why the back-end push exists.**
- ⚠️ **CONFERENCE CALENDAR, both inside the next window: **Citi Global TMT Conference Sept 8-10** and **Goldman Sachs Communacopia + Technology Conference Sept 8-11.** (Susquehanna weekly, 2026-09-05.) GS's own preview frames the tech session as *"The next wave of AI + San Francisco Communacopia and TMT Conference Preview"* with Eric Sheridan (GS Weekend Macro Call, 2026-09-05 — agenda only, no substance carried).**


## 🆕🔴🔴 2026-09-01/04 — **[[ASML]]'s CFO SAYS PRODUCTIVITY UPGRADES ADD ~45% MORE WAFER CAPACITY OUT OF THE INSTALLED BASE — WHICH IS BULLISH FOR ITS P&L AND MECHANICALLY BEARISH FOR TOOL UNITS, AND THE NOTE ONLY ARGUES ONE SIDE. PLUS [[INTC]] PUTS A USE-OF-PROCEEDS ON THE $23bn RAISE, AND A DESK CALLS THE SEMICAP BOTTOM ON A −40% TWO-MONTH DE-RATING.**

- 🔴🔴 **THE ASML CFO MEETING (BofA Dutch Bus Tech Tour, 1-3 Sep; feedback note 2026-09-04, Didier Scemama — management feedback, no rating/estimate change; BofA is Buy).** ***"Effective EUV capacity may be MATERIALLY UNDERESTIMATED, with productivity upgrades driving c.45% MORE WAFER CAPACITY and supporting structural gross margin expansion."*** Demand: ***"2027 EUV capacity LARGELY SOLD OUT and 2028 ORDERS ALREADY BEING SECURED, leaving SUPPLY AS THE KEY CONSTRAINT."*** The meeting itself: *"**Very bullish meeting. No slowdown. Visibility extending in part to 2028.** Structural GM improvement with more software, more options, better mix and **more upgrades.**"* ➤ 🔴 **HOLD BOTH SIDES OF THE 45%, BECAUSE THE NOTE HOLDS ONLY ONE. **Bullish for ASML:** upgrades are high-margin service-annuity revenue requiring no new tool build. **Bearish for WFE UNITS at the margin:** 45% more wafer output from the existing fleet is 45% of a tool order a customer does not have to place. **For a page that models WFE in dollars of equipment, an installed-base productivity step-change is a genuine downward pressure on the unit leg that no house in this window has quantified.** It also cuts against the simple "capacity is the constraint" framing this page has been running.**
- **CHINA, FROM THE SAME MEETING, THREE CLAIMS: ① ***"China ArFi tools ARE NOT IN OPERATION and China customers 'DO NOT WANT TO USE THEM'"*** — the domestic immersion substitute is not being adopted by its intended customers. ② *"China demand to **RECOVER STRONGLY next year**."* ③ *"US admin **understands the implications of the MATCH Act**."* ⚠️ **CFO characterisations relayed by a broker. ① is falsifiable and is the most valuable; ③ is a political read with no evidence attached and is NOT a de-risking event.** **And it is contradicted in direction the same week by Digitimes: *"[[ASML]] / China EUV shortfall around 10-15 years, but **DUV GAINS TEST WESTERN EXPORT CONTROLS**"* (via MS desk, 2026-09-04).** **Also: the CFO sees *"not much business case for FEL technology"* — the incumbent dismissing an alternative EUV source class.**
- 🔴 **[[INTC]] PUTS A USE-OF-PROCEEDS ON THE AUGUST RAISE, AND IT IS A DIRECT TOOL-ORDER STATEMENT: *"Intel said its **US$23bn August equity raise FUNDED CAPACITY EXPANSION RATHER THAN ADDRESSING FINANCIAL DISTRESS.** The group plans to **EQUIP FABS IN IRELAND, ARIZONA AND OREGON ahead of 14A HIGH-VOLUME MANUFACTURING IN 2028**, and expects **data-center CPU demand to EXCEED SUPPLY THROUGH 2028**. Intel will **address DRAM requirements through EXTERNAL PARTNERS rather than building memory fabs**, supporting equipment, advanced-packaging and memory suppliers."*** (Digitimes via Jefferies Asia TMT · Conor O'Mara, 2026-09-04.) ⚠️ **Trade press relaying company statements — judge it by whether the orders appear. But "equip three fabs for 14A HVM in 2028" is exactly the 2027-28 order stream this page's 2028 terminal depends on, and it closes a speculative thread: no Intel memory fabs.** **Related: [[ASML]] is raising Japanese headcount to ~700 from 500 to serve **Rapidus and [[TSM]]** (Nikkei via Jefferies) — leading-edge service build-out is going to Japan as well.**
- ⚠️ **AND THE POSITIONING CALL, LOGGED AS A POSITIONING FACT NOT A FUNDAMENTAL ONE: *"**LRCX, AMAT, KLAC and ASML have DE-RATED BY (−40%) IN THE PAST 2 MONTHS**… These stocks — high quality, FCF generative, capex light relative to foundry/hyperscalers — were **swept up into the BottleNeck/Momentum Trade, but THE PREMISE NEVER FIT THEM**… They are all on the cusp of a very strong **2027-2029 WFE spending cycle**. [[TSM]], [[SAMSUNG]], INTC all substantially raised capex. INTC did a capital raise. In addition… the probability of upside to WFE seems to have gone up given **25% raises to Cloud Capex** over that time, [[NVDA]]'s AI guide, [[AVGO]]'s AI guide, demonstrable ROI on capex from [[MSFT]], [[AMZN]] and others. **Compute is Semis.**"*** (BofA TMT desk · Brian Fenske, **SALES COMMENTARY, explicitly *"my personal view"*, not research — no rating, PO or estimate**, 2026-09-04.) ⚠️ **BofA's fundamental analysts (Vivek Arya, Didier Scemama) are Buy on all four; all four present at GS and Citi conferences the following week.** ➤ **The useful content is not the call: it is the checkable −40% de-rating and the falsifiable claim about WHY the group fell (momentum-basket contagion, not fundamentals). If that is right, the group re-rates without a single estimate changing; if it is wrong, the estimates have to come down.**

## 🆕🔴🔴 2026-09-02 (source dated 2026-09-01) — A NEW HOUSE INITIATES FOUNDRY COVERAGE AND FRAMES THE WHOLE CHAIN AS **ONE OPERATING-LEVERAGE TRADE (VOLUME × ASP × UTILISATION)**, WITH THE FOUNDRY AS THE BOTTLENECK. THE USEFUL PART FOR THIS PAGE IS THE CAPITAL COMPARISON — **TSMC 6-YEAR CAPEX '23-'28E >$320bn, WITH ~$220bn OF IT IN 2026-28E AGAINST ~$100bn SPENT 2023-25** — AND A **WFE LAYER TRADING AT ~16x LTM SALES AGAINST FOUNDRY AT ~6x.**

**(Stifel · Sahej Singh, Associate Vice President, Applied Technology — "Foundries: The Bottleneck — Initiation of Foundry Coverage", 2026-09-01. Initiates **TSM Buy $515 · GlobalFoundries (GFS) Buy $60 · TSEM Buy $270**, with GFS and TSEM named Top Picks.)** [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/STIFEL_Initiating_Coverage_on_the_Foundries_-_TSM_Buy_GFS_Buy_TSEM_Buy.html) ⚠️ **SLIDE DECK — the text extraction is COLUMN-INTERLEAVED across the three companies. Every figure below has had its owner verified on the page it came from, and where ownership stayed ambiguous the figure was OMITTED rather than guessed (two such cases are named explicitly). Company-level detail on [[TSM]] and [[TSEM]] is another run's; GlobalFoundries has no wiki page — plain text.**

### 🔴 (1) THE SUBSECTOR THESIS, AND WHY IT IS A WFE ARGUMENT AS MUCH AS A FOUNDRY ONE

Stifel's stated macro engine: ***"SPECIALIZATION OF AI-HARDWARE MEANS OPERATING LEVERAGE LIKELY IMPROVES VIA SALES VOLUME GROWTH ON HIGHER PRICING (ASPs), WITH IMPROVING UTILIZATION RATES."*** The three levers, and the claim that they are compounding at once: **volume** — *"wafer shipments compound with capacity adds; **capacity is the binding constraint**"*; **unit economics** — *"**HPC-related ASPs run ~3–5x mature nodes**; product mix has improved this cycle"*; **utilisation** — **GlobalFoundries mid-70s% FY24 → 85% FY25, TSEM modelling 85% at built-out capacity, and a TSM rule of thumb of ~1pt utilisation ≈ ~1pt gross margin.** Stifel: *"All three levers are compounding simultaneously **for the first time since the 2020–21 upcycle** — volume from capacity adds, price from leading-edge and specialty mix, utilization from AI demand against tight supply. That is the setup where high fixed-cost economics convert **single-digit revenue beats into double-digit earnings beats**."* ➤ **Read straight through to this page's own tape: the ASP leg UBS said on 09-01 is *"not in consensus at all"* is the same leg Stifel is underwriting one layer downstream. Two houses, two layers of the chain, same variable — and neither says volume is the swing factor.**

### 🔴 (2) THE CAPITAL COMPARISON — THE CLEANEST NEW CAPEX ANCHOR IN THE DECK

- **TSMC 6-year capex '23-'28E: >$320bn**, with **consensus estimates of ~$220bn in 2026-28E against the ~$100bn spent 2023-25** — Stifel: *"TSM alone has spent ~100B over the past 3 years and is projected to spend ~$220B in the next 3 years."*
- Benchmarks it sets that spend against: **the Apollo Program (1961–72, inflation-adjusted) at ~$280bn** and **the US CHIPS Act at ~$280bn** — *"'Apollo-scale' is no longer a metaphor — it's a capex line item"*, and **spend on chip manufacturing now exceeds Apollo.**
- **Leading-edge (<7nm) concentration, sourced to TrendForce <7nm share Q4'25: TSMC 90% / Samsung + Intel + Other 10%.**
➤ **This page tracks WFE TAM in dollars per year. The >$320bn / ~$220bn-forward TSMC series is the single largest identifiable customer commitment underneath those TAMs, and it is now on the page in a form that can be checked against the WFE aggregates rather than inferred from them.** ⚠️ *These are foundry CAPEX figures, not WFE — do not net them against a shipped-WFE number; capex includes shells, land, facilities and non-WFE equipment. The same shells-vs-tools distinction this page logged from Samsung IR on 08-25 applies.*

### 🔴 (3) THE VALUATION MAP OF THE CHAIN — WFE IS CAPITALISED NEAR THE FABLESS MULTIPLE, THE FOUNDRIES AT A THIRD OF IT

Top-5-by-market-cap per sub-segment, aggregate cap and **median EV / LTM sales**: **Fabless ~$8.2trn @ ~18x · Foundries ~$2.2trn @ ~6x · Wafer Fab Equipment ~$1.8trn @ ~16x · IDMs ~$2.0trn @ ~12x · Data Center Networking ~$790bn @ ~7x · Data Center Storage ~$640bn @ ~11x · Rack-scale OEM/ODM/EMS ~$480bn @ ~2x · EDA/IP/SDA ~$275bn @ ~10x · OSAT/Packaging ~$130bn @ ~4x.** WFE constituents named: **[[ASML]] $680bn · [[LRCX]] $380bn · [[AMAT]] $370bn · [[KLAC]] $230bn · Tokyo Electron ([[TOKYOELEC]], listed by Stifel as TOELY) $160bn.** ➤ 🔴 **THE TOOL LAYER TRADES AT ~16x LTM SALES WHILE THE FOUNDRIES IT SELLS TO TRADE AT ~6x AND THE OSATs AT ~4x. The market is already paying a near-fabless multiple for the equipment layer's share of this cycle — which is the valuation counterpart of the "semicap is now the CONSENSUS LONG-ONLY expression of the memory trade" positioning item this page carries from 08-25. It is the argument for why an upside WFE revision has to be large to move the group.**

### (4) STIFEL'S MAP OF THE CHAIN — USEFUL BECAUSE IT NAMES WHO SITS WHERE

**WFE: [[ASML]] · [[AMAT]] · [[LRCX]] · [[KLAC]] · Tokyo Electron ([[TOKYOELEC]])** — ⚠️ **Stifel writes this as "TEL", which in THIS wiki is [[TEL]] = TE Connectivity, a different company. Read as Tokyo Electron.** **Foundry: [[TSM]] · GlobalFoundries (GFS) · [[TSEM]].** **Advanced packaging / OSAT: ASE (ASX) · Amkor (AMKR) · JCET · TSMC-CoWoS.** **EDA/IP/SDA: [[ARM]] · [[CDNS]] · [[SNPS]] · LSCC.** **IDMs: [[MU]] · [[INTC]] · [[TXN]] · [[ADI]] · MCHP.** **Fabless: [[NVDA]] · [[AVGO]] · [[AMD]] · [[QCOM]] · [[MRVL]] · plus AWS/[[GOOG]]/[[MSFT]] custom.** Stifel's node-cadence exhibit ties the WFE layer directly to the accelerator roadmap: **ASML NXE:3400B (7/5nm) → NXE:3600D (5/3nm) → NXE:3800E (2nm) → EXE:5200 High-NA (<2nm)** against **TSM N7 → N5 → N3 → N2 → A16 → A14**, with each NVIDIA/AMD generation hung off it — *"WFE enables Foundry capabilities and, therein, Fabless innovation roadmaps, offering multi-year visibility."*

### 🔴 (5) THE THREE-CHAPTER FRAME, AND THE LINE IN IT THAT MATTERS MOST TO THIS PAGE

**Chapter 1 (2018-22, complete): prove the scaling laws** — frontier training on monolithic leading-edge GPUs, N7→N5, one foundry winner. **Chapter 2 (2023-27, "LATE INNINGS"): scale training and reasoning** — *"the biggest printable die + HBM + CoWoS; **packaging — not wafers — starts to gate accelerator shipments**; supply chronically short"*, with **CoWoS at ~10% of TSM volume**. **Chapter 3 (circa-2027+, "STARTING"): heterogeneous compute** — GPU + custom ASIC + CPU, Hopper repricing up three years into life, and *"**power delivery, interconnect & optical I/O become the binding constraints**"*, pulling the specialty foundries into the AI bill of materials. ➤ ⚠️ **"PACKAGING, NOT WAFERS, GATES SHIPMENTS" IS A CLAIM ABOUT WHERE THE WFE DOLLAR HAS TO GO, AND IT SITS AGAINST THIS PAGE'S FRONT-END-WEIGHTED TAMs.** If Stifel is right that the binding constraint moves to packaging in Chapter 2 and to power/interconnect/optical in Chapter 3, the mix of a $225-275bn WFE year matters as much as the level — and the advanced-packaging and inspection legs this page already splits out are where the incremental dollar lands.

### ⚠️ (6) THE TERAFAB SLIDE — IT CORROBORATES THE "~70% OF TSMC'S OUTPUT" FIGURE THIS PAGE ALREADY CARRIES, AND IT FUSES TWO DIFFERENT CLAIMS INTO ONE SENTENCE

Verbatim from the deck: ***"Terafab is a $20–25B JV (Tesla + SpaceX + xAI + Intel, Apr 7, 2026) targeting 1 TW/yr compute; ~70% of TSMC's current output."*** The exhibit beside it is titled **"TERAFAB STATED ANNUAL COMPUTE DEMAND (GW)"** and shows **1 TW against ~20 GW of "Industry avail. (est.)" — a stated 50x gap** — with the **Musk framing that *"current global fabs produce ~2% of what Tesla/SpaceX will need for robotics + orbital AI."*** Stifel's own use of it is deliberately modest: ***"Terafab is a demand-robustness signal. It indicates the long-term demand backdrop stays tight even if hyperscaler capex cyclically moderates."***
- ✅ **THE CORROBORATION: this page already carries "~70% of TSMC's entire current monthly output" from the DAMNANG backfill, where it is explicitly the ratio for **1 MILLION WAFER STARTS PER MONTH** (against Terafab's 100k-wpm start point) and is sourced to Elon's own statements. Stifel reproduces the same ~70% independently.**
- ⚠️⚠️ **BUT THE SENTENCE FUSES TWO INCOMPATIBLE UNITS: "1 TW/yr compute" is a DEMAND figure from a Musk presentation; "~70% of TSMC's current output" is a WAFER-OUTPUT ratio. They are not two readings of one quantity and the ~70% is NOT the wafer equivalent of 1 TW. Do not chain them. And note the axis label — the exhibit is in GW and the bar is written "1 TW", i.e. 1,000 GW against ~20 GW available; that is a demand-side ambition, NOT a facility-GW or IT-load-GW build number, and it must never be netted against any GW basis on this wiki (see `_meta/assumptions.md` on mixing GW bases).**
- ⚠️ **A SEPARATE SLIDE, NOT THE SAME CLAIM: the deck's "2022–2032 KEY LEADING-EDGE FABS GLOBALLY" list (TSMC Fab 18 Tainan N3/N2 · TSMC Fab 20 Hsinchu A16 · TSMC Arizona N4/N3 ramping · Samsung Hwaseong SF2 · Intel Hillsboro 18A) sits in the adjacent column and carries NO compute target. In the column-interleaved extraction it reads as though the fab map is what targets 1 TW/yr. It is not. Recorded so the misread is not propagated.**
- ➤ **NET FOR THIS PAGE'S OPEN TERAFAB QUESTION (Mizuho uses it as a material WFE offset; MS models only ~$2bn of SpaceX spend in 2028): Stifel is a THIRD house and it lands with Mizuho on direction but explicitly refuses to size it — it is used as a demand-robustness argument, not as a capex line. Terafab stays logged as OPEN with no disclosed wafer volume, capex or schedule.**

### (7) THE ANCHOR CUSTOMER'S OWN MODEL, AS THE DECK STATES IT — AND THE ONE RISK THAT POINTS BACK AT THIS PAGE

**TSMC's long-term model has been raised TWICE IN TWELVE MONTHS — to a ~25% revenue CAGR with AI-accelerator revenue compounding at a mid-to-high-50s% CAGR (2024-29), a through-cycle gross-margin floor lifted to 56%+ despite 2-3pp of overseas-fab and 2-3pp of N2-ramp dilution in 2026, and 2026 revenue guided +30%+ in USD.** The buildout behind it: **the $265bn US buildout (Arizona GIGAFAB cluster)**, which Stifel expects to ease the geopolitical overhang over time — *"though mgmt frames expansion as **customer-driven**, TSMC has historically invested in PP&E during **upcycles**."* On capex behaviour: *"CapEx ebbs with the demand cycle and is deployed into every upcycle; the **invest-ahead model** is what converts that demand into operating leverage"*, and *"even during some of the largest nominal capex cadence we have seen in recent historical context, TSM employs a disciplined approach in terms of scale and timing."* ➤ **The invest-ahead characterisation is the mechanism this page needs: it says the customer's capex leads its own revenue, which is why WFE orders lead the foundry cycle rather than lagging it.** ⚠️ **Stifel's third stated risk is the one that reads straight onto this page: *"AI CapEx Digestion & Customer Concentration: a hyperscaler pause would hit the AI-accelerator CAGR."*** Its catalyst calendar makes the same link operational — ***"TSM & ASML bookings are key, alongside hyperscaler capex"***, with **ECOC the first optical read**, **OCP and SEMICON West framing CPO demand**, and **IEDM previewing the A16 / A14 and High-NA roadmap.** ⚠️ *Two figures were deliberately NOT logged from this deck because the interleaved extraction left their owner ambiguous: the per-company utilisation history chart and the FY28E street consensus line other than TSM's (~94% Buy, PT range ~$500-650, street ~$296bn revenue / 59.8% operating margin / $29.36 EPS for FY28E, which is unambiguously TSM's).*

## 🆕🔴🔴🔴 2026-09-01 — THE $200bn+ 2028 DOUBT THIS PAGE LOGGED ON 08-31 IS ANSWERED BY A RAISE, NOT A DEFENCE: **UBS TAKES 2027 WFE $198bn → $225bn AND 2028 $248bn → $275bn**, WITH 2029 BIASED ABOVE $300bn — AND SAYS THE ASP LEG IS *NOT IN CONSENSUS AT ALL*

**(UBS · Timothy Arcuri, w/ Natalia Winkler, Seth Cho, Stacy Che — relayed by UBS sector sales · Robert Ruple, 2026-09-01)**

- 🔴🔴 **THE NUMBERS, OFF A 2026 BASELINE OF ~$158bn: *"our supply chain work signals that **WFE will likely push up to ~$225B next year** with **C2028 tracking to at least ~$275B** with **further upside to $300B possible if foundry spending exceeds our expectations.**"*
- ✅ **AND IT IS CORROBORATED FROM BOTH DIRECTIONS, WHICH IS WHY IT DESERVES THE TOP OF THIS PAGE: *"In addition to our bottom-up work, we also see **TOP-DOWN evidence in the form of NEW CLEANROOM ANNOUNCEMENTS and CAPACITY EXPANSION and MESSAGING TO THE SUPPLY CHAIN from major SPE suppliers to remain AHEAD OF DEMAND.**"* ➤ **"Suppliers messaging that they intend to run ahead of demand" is the tell: it is capacity being built for a number, not a forecast of a number.**
- 🔴 **THE MIX, AND IT SAYS THE CYCLE *ROTATES* RATHER THAN COMPOUNDS — this is the actionable part for name selection:**

| Year | Total WFE | y/y | Memory | DRAM | NAND | Foundry/logic |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **2026 (base)** | **~$158bn** | — | — | — | — | — |
| **2027** | **~$225bn** | **+~43%** | **+~53%** | **+~60%** | **+~30%** | **+~34%** |
| **2028** | **~$275bn** | **+~22%** | **+~10%** (NAND > DRAM) | *< NAND* | *> DRAM* | **>+30%** |
| **2029** | **>$300bn** (bias, not a forecast) | — | — | — | — | — |

- ➤ 🔴🔴 **SO 2027 IS A MEMORY YEAR AND 2028 IS A FOUNDRY/LOGIC YEAR: *"for 2028… **foundry and logic spending becom[es] the DOMINANT DRIVER, accounting for roughly 75% of the projected $50 BILLION year-over-year increase.** Major contributors include [[TSM]], [[INTC]], **Samsung Foundry**, and notably **[[SPCX]]'s new TERAFAB, which could emerge as a MAJOR SOURCE of industry spending over the next several years.**"* ⚠️ **That is the FIRST TIME an industry WFE forecast on this wiki has built SpaceX into the denominator — from an analyst with no SPCX equity coverage. No dollar figure is attached to the SpaceX share, and *"over the next several years"* is not a 2028 commitment. Do not convert it into a capex line.**
- 🔴🔴 **THE ASP LEG IS THE PART THE STREET DOES NOT OWN, AND UBS SAYS SO EXPLICITLY: *"equipment vendors are gaining pricing power, with **industry-wide ASP INCREASES OF 5% TO 10% THROUGH 2028 potentially adding $10 BILLION TO $30 BILLION to WFE forecasts. Because this pricing leverage is NOT YET REFLECTED IN CONSENSUS ESTIMATES**, the report sees meaningful upside to both WFE forecasts, which could exceed $300 billion by 2029, and to **semiconductor equipment STOCK EXPECTATIONS.**"*
- ✅✅ **AND THE SAME ARGUMENT ARRIVES INDEPENDENTLY, BOTTOM-UP, FROM UBS'S EUROPEAN ANALYST ON ONE NAME — WHICH IS THE STRONGEST FORM OF CORROBORATION THIS PAGE CAN GET: Francois-Xavier Bouvignies on [[ASML]] (Buy, PT €2,350) has **EPS 7% and 20% ABOVE CONSENSUS in 2027E/2028E** and **€85 EPS by 2030E vs consensus €70 (a 31% EPS CAGR 2025-30E)**, explicitly *"NOT the WFE cycle"*: *"**while investors remain focused on the WFE cycle, we believe the market is overlooking a STRUCTURAL STEP-UP in ASML's earnings power driven by PRICING, RISING LITHOGRAPHY INTENSITY and INCREASING MEMORY EXPOSURE.**"* Sized: *"**EUV pricing could provide 5-10% upside to consensus 2028E assumptions, while potential IMMERSION pricing actions could add a further 10-15%**"*, driving *"**gross margins toward 60% by 2028, EARLIER than currently expected**"*, with *"**memory expected to represent roughly 45% of product sales in 2026.**"*** ⚠️ **THE UNRESOLVED TENSION INSIDE THAT NOTE, WHICH IS WHERE TO ATTACK IT: Bouvignies also has *"EUV throughput improving by roughly 45%, ahead of immersion at 30-35%"* — a 45% throughput gain is a UNIT headwind (fewer tools per wafer of output) that the pricing leg must more than offset. He presents both as bullish without bridging them.**
- **UBS'S GLOBAL PREFERENCE SET, verbatim: *"the team globally continues to prefer **[[AMAT]]/[[LRCX]] in the US, TEL/Naura/Eugene in APAC, and [[ASML]]/ASMI in EU.**"* ⚠️ **Note who is NOT on it: [[KLAC]], [[TER]] and [[ADVANTEST]].**
- ⚠️🔴 **THE TAPE IS THE COUNTERWEIGHT AND IT MATTERS MORE THAN USUAL, BECAUSE THIS RAISE WAS NOT PAID FOR: on the same morning, Barclays' momentum screens showed **relative UNDERPERFORMANCE streaks vs SMH for [[TER]] (−7 days), [[AMAT]] (−5) and [[LRCX]] (−5)** — with **no semicap name on the outperformance list** — and **RSI <40 for [[KLAC]](36), [[AMAT]](37), [[VECO]](37), UCTT(37), ACLS(37), MKSI(34), KLIC(35), PLAB(37), COHU(39), CAMT(39)**. MS's positioning note adds that August brought *"the **2nd largest week of supply from HFs in the last 12 months** (week of August 20th) with the **most net-sold areas in MEMORY, SEMI-CAPS and AI BENEFICIARIES**"*, with HF net exposure to High Momentum names down to the **19th percentile vs 12m**. ➤ **A $27bn/$27bn two-year forecast raise landed into a group being actively de-grossed. That is a setup, not a refutation — but the marginal buyer is absent.**
- **DATED ACCESS TO TEST IT: UBS *"SEMICON WEST Cocktails with [[LRCX]] and [[AMAT]] hosted by Tim Arcuri"* **2026-10-12** (San Francisco); UBS Semi & Semi Equipment Bus Tour **2026-10-08** (San Jose/Santa Clara); [[TOKYOELEC]] Albany R&D Center Tour **2026-10-01**; BofA SEMICON West group meetings **2026-10-13/14** with **[[TER]], MKSI, CAMT, PLAB, [[AEIS]], NVMI, ACLS** (Vivek Arya); Bernstein *"meetings at SEMICON West in SF"* **2026-10-13/14**; Bernstein 8th Annual Semis bus tour **2026-09-15/16** San Jose ([[ALAB]], [[AMAT]], AMBA, [[AVGO]], LSCC, MPWR, [[NVDA]], [[NXPI]]).
- **TWO SMALLER CORROBORATIONS FROM THE SAME WINDOW: (a) **Rigaku** group call with president Jun Kawakami *"emphasised STRONGER APRIL-JUNE ORDERS as **Asian customers invest in DRAM capacity and North American logic capacity**… most orders booked through 1H will contribute to F12/26 sales"* — ⚠️ but with a caution from the same call: *"**strong market expectations likely need to be corrected after a stretch of strong semi investments**"* (MS, via desk, 09-01). (b) **[[TSM]]** at SEMICON Taiwan: **A12 in mass production in 2029, 14x-reticle CoWoS in 2028, N2P-on-N3P 3D integration in 2026 → A14-on-A14 in 2029 with interconnect pitch to 4.5 microns** — the roadmap the foundry/logic leg of the 2028 step-up is being spent on. (c) *"**China Semi Equipment — Entering a new growth phase; more positive on 2027-28E outlook**"* (UBS · Jimmy Yu, 09-01) — ⚠️ no numbers in the relay as delivered; OPEN ITEM.

**🔴🔴 ADDED SAME DAY (/run-inbox, second pass) — THE HIGH-NA HVM PRIMER, AND IT OVERTURNS THE "HIGH-NA IS A ONE-CUSTOMER MARKET" ITEM THIS PAGE PUT UP ON 08-11 (Bernstein · David Dai / Carmine Milano / Juho Hwang / Jack Lin, "ASML: High-NA EUV Primer - The shrinking never stops", 45pp, 2026-09-01; [[ASML]] Outperform, PT €2,500 / ADR $2,859, top pick — reiterated, nothing superseded).**

- ⚠️⚠️ **THE CORRECTION FIRST, BECAUSE IT IS A STRUCTURAL CLAIM THIS PAGE HAS BEEN CARRYING FOR THREE WEEKS.** The Aug-11 item (4) reads *"the High-NA book is now [[INTC]] alone into ~2029–30, which makes the $20bn raise and the High-NA order book the same trade — and concentrates the risk."* **That conflated [[SAMSUNG]] LOGIC — which did push High-NA out to ~1nm/A10 ≈2030, as logged — with Samsung and [[SKHYNIX]] DRAM, which Bernstein has adopting at 1d in 2027 and calls the broadest economic case for the tool.** Named evidence: *"**SK hynix has the clearest early signal: the company announced in September 2025 that it had assembled ASML's TWINSCAN EXE:5200B, the first production-oriented HNA system, at its M16 fab for next-generation DRAM development.** Samsung is also moving quickly, with industry reports indicating that it ordered two ASML EXE:5200B tools, with the first expected by late 2025 and the second in H1 2026."* And ASML's own SEMICON Taiwan disclosure the same day: **10 EXE systems running at FOUR customers, three more shipping, 1.35mn cumulative wafer exposures, first high-volume logic product reached.** ➤ **What survives: [[INTC]] is the only early adopter in LOGIC and the only one in HVM today. What does not survive: a one-customer High-NA market into 2029-30. Re-grade both the bull and the bear reading of that bullet.**

- 🔴🔴 **THE ADOPTION MAP, IN ONE LINE — *"Our best guess of HNA adoption is: [[INTC]] in 2026 (18A), [[SAMSUNG]] / [[SKHYNIX]] DRAM in 2027 (D1d), Samsung logic and [[TSM]] in 2030 (A10)."*** [[MU]] later: *"There is no official HNA disclosure from Micron yet, but they could adopt HNA slightly later, consistent with its more conservative EUV LNA strategy in DRAM."* Intel is already in HVM — *"Specific Intel 18A layers have been DUAL-QUALIFIED on HNA with product shipping to customers at YIELDS MATCHED TO THE NXE PLATFORM"* on Panther Lake — against *"a sizable low double-digit HNA backlog supporting initial insertion, with a LARGER ORDER WAVE expected later."* **Timing tell for that second wave: *"insertion decisions and follow-on orders more likely to materialize in 2H26 and into 2027."*** ⚠️ **THE FALSIFIABLE LEG, AND IT IS BERNSTEIN AGAINST THE COMPANY: ASML's own framing puts DRAM insertion at the 0a node (*"a 35% patterning cost reduction versus low-NA EUV in HVM 0a"*), while Bernstein pulls it a generation earlier — *"We expect it could be even earlier at ~1d."* The whole "DRAM adopts first, in 2027" thesis rests on that pull-forward.**

- 🔴 **WHY DRAM AND NOT LOGIC — IT IS THE HALF-FIELD, AND IT IS ARITHMETIC, NOT PREFERENCE.** High-NA's anamorphic 0.55 NA optics halve the wafer field (**~26 × 16.5mm vs 26 × 33mm**), so large logic die must be split across two reticles (AB) and stitched. **DRAM die fit inside a half-field, so DRAM runs in AA mode with no stitching.** Litho-tool cost per exposure vs Low-NA: **2.4x (AA) / 3.1x (AB) in 2025 → 1.9x / 2.1x by 2030**; absolute High-NA cost per exposure **~€82 → ~€63 (−23%)** as availability goes ~80% → 95%. Result: *"for DRAM, HNA is almost certainly cheaper than LNA LELE double patterning… However, for large logic die requiring stitching (AB), it is only justifiable over time to replace SALELE with either 2x EUV + 2x ArFi or 3x EUV. In fact… **it will still remain cheaper to use double exposure LNA rather than single exposure HNA over the next five years**"* (1x HNA : 2x LNA = **1.6x in 2025 → 1.1x in 2030, never crossing 1.0x**).

- ⚠️ **AND A THROUGHPUT CORRECTION THAT MATTERS FOR ANY WFE MODEL USING HIGH-NA WPH: the qualified EXE:5200B figure circulating from SEMICON Taiwan (135 wph) is the STITCHED (AB) number.** The full ladder in both modes: **EXE:5200B 175 AA / 135 AB · EXE:5200C 190 / 160 · EXE:5200D ≥195 / ≥175 · EXE:5400E ≥210 / ≥180**, plus a high-productivity **EXE:5600 ≥250 wph** around the middle of the next decade. Low-NA over the same period goes to **300 wph (NXE:4200G) and 330 wph (NXE:4200H, post-2030)** on source power **500-600W → 740W demonstrated → 1,000W** — so *"HNA remains STRUCTURALLY BELOW LNA on scanner throughput"* and will while the half-field stands. ➤ **When sizing High-NA units, use AA for DRAM and AB for large logic; the two differ by ~23% today.**

- ✅✅ **THE CONCLUSION THIS PAGE SHOULD ADOPT, BECAUSE IT INVERTS HOW WE HAVE BEEN READING HIGH-NA SLIPPAGE SINCE 08-11: *"Financially, **slower HNA adoption is probably even better for [[ASML]]… at least in the near term.** As shown above, from litho tool perspective, **it's economically better to sell more LNA EUV machines than fewer HNA machines. This is especially the case when considering the MUCH HIGHER MARGIN for LNA over HNA EUV.**"*** And on why the migration still happens: *"HNA adoption will further increase litho intensity and hence ASML's market share in WFE, as more value goes from non-litho steps (etch, depo, cleaning, etc) to litho when 1 HNA replaces multiple LNA-ArFi steps."* ➤ **So a Samsung/TSMC push-out is margin-accretive near term and share-neutral long term. The genuine bear case is NEITHER tool — layer counts stopping — which is not what any roadmap on this page says.**

- 🔴 **THE LITHO-INTENSITY LADDER, NODE BY NODE — the cleanest set of numbers this page has for litho's share of WFE.** Overall litho intensity **24% → 26%**; the historical series runs **17% (2022) → 25% (2023) → 23-24% since**, with the blended forecast trending to **28%**. **DRAM: ~20% (1Y/1Z) → 26% (1c) → ~30% (1d)**, driven by EUV exposures **3-4 (1a) → 4-6 (1b) → 6-7 (1c)** and double-patterning EUV or High-NA at 1d. **Logic: ~20% (10nm) → >35% (5nm/3nm) → ~32% (2nm, GAA adds non-litho steps) → similar or higher at 1.4nm → higher again at 1nm.** ASML's own estimate for the shift: **~1.2x higher litho capex+opex for single-exposure High-NA vs Low-NA multi-patterning, with non-litho cost declining.** Franchise context: litho was the **2nd largest WFE segment in 2025 at US$29bn**, the decade's fastest-growing category at a **17% CAGR vs 14% for WFE**, with [[ASML]] **>90% litho share** and **16% (2022) → ~22% (2025) of total WFE**.

- **EXPOSURE-COUNT SIZING (the unit-demand input): ASML's Investor Day model has **DRAM EUV exposures roughly doubling from ~5 in 2025 to 7-10 by 2030, of which 2-3 on average are High-NA**; and **advanced logic at ~25-30 total EUV exposures by 2030 including ~4-6 High-NA.** Cost benefit of the substitution, per demonstrated use case: **logic P36 via — Low-NA needs 250 mJ/cm² for 52 WPH and still shows deformed holes, vs High-NA defect-free at 77 mJ/cm² and 136 WPH; P30 ~20%; P19 local interconnect (3 LNA → 1 HNA) ~35%; A14 HVM 18% → ~24% with extendibility. DRAM — 15nm contact holes needing 2 LNA + 1 DUV replaced by ONE High-NA exposure at ~30%; 0a HVM 35% → 37%; and ~50% at equalised LCDU** (High-NA 1.4-2.3nm LCDU at 45-53 mJ/cm² across P40-P28 vs Low-NA 2.4-3.6nm at 63-73, with Low-NA needing **191-238 mJ/cm²** to match). ✅ **Also, sourced to the company: ASML *"pushed back on the idea that 4F² DRAM architectures create an EUV demand cliff, arguing that 4F² requires more advanced lithography and should INCREASE both immersion and EUV intensity"* — the second independent rebuttal of the 4F² air-pocket bear case.**

- **LONG-DATED: HYPER-NA (0.75 NA) is scoped but NOT committed — IMEC puts adoption *"around A3 as metal pitch reaches 12 to 16nm"* with CFET ~2033, and *"the future high productivity common platform is designed to REUSE source, stages, interfaces and other common modules across LNA, HNA and potentially Hyper NA, with the main change being the OPTICS"* — capital-intensity relief rather than a new architecture bill. Separately, a **6" × 12" mask format** is the only path that removes stitching entirely (**[[INTC]] *"has been the strongest advocate"***), but it needs a coordinated ecosystem upgrade across blanks, pellicles, writers, inspection, pods and fab handling — which is exactly why the industry accepted the half-field instead.**

## 🆕🔴🔴 2026-08-31 — TWO CONFERENCES, ONE WEEK, AND THEY AGREE ON SENTIMENT WHILE DISAGREEING ON FUNDAMENTALS. PLUS THE FIRST NAMED DOUBT ABOUT THE $200bn+ 2028 WFE NUMBER.

**Jefferies (Chicago Semis & Hardware, 95+ companies / ~400 investors, w/c 08-24) and Deutsche Bank (2026 Technology Conference, Dana Point, same week) both published feedback on 2026-08-31. They describe the same souring sentiment and draw OPPOSITE conclusions about whether the fundamentals justify it. Carried side by side because the gap between the two IS the trade.**

- 🔴🔴 **JEFFERIES — TACTICALLY CAUTIOUS, AND THE REASON IS POSITIONING PLUS A CRACK IN THE OUT-YEAR NUMBER (Lucy Roe, Equity Sales, 2026-08-31): *"The **lack of incremental info** coming out of meetings with semicaps (which I can vouch for in **[[ASML]] and [[AMAT]]**) makes the setup from here trickier with **cons positioning being MAX LONG** — and **there are some doubts creeping in on whether HIGH 200B WFE IN 28 IS REALISTIC**. So I think there is reason to be **tactically more cautious semicaps here as sentiment sours from recent highs**."*** ➜ ⚠️⚠️ **This is the first time on this wiki that a named source reports the buy-side questioning the high-$200bn 2028 WFE figure itself, rather than the multiple. **Max-long positioning plus a contested terminal number is the classic setup for a de-rating on no news, which is exactly what August delivered ([[AMAT]] −10% on the month, an NDX laggard — GS TMT, 08-31).**
- ⚠️ **AND JEFFERIES ADDS AN EVENT RISK WITH A DATE: *"Especially with the **next Trump-Xi meeting scheduled for SEPT 24** and seemingly a **growing risk that the MATCH ACT is put in front of Trump for approval** — investors may want to **lighten their semicap load in a period where noise around export restrictions could intensify**."* (Jefferies is hosting an Andy Rothman preview call on **Sept 14**.) **Independently corroborated: UBS · Timothy Arcuri flags *"potential headline risk into the Trump-Xi meeting"* the same day, and GS's TMT catalyst list carries *"President Xi reported visit to the US — 9/24."*** ➜ **Three sources, one date. See [themes/china-export](china-export.md).**
- 🔴🔴 **DEUTSCHE BANK — THE OPPOSITE CONCLUSION FROM THE SAME SENTIMENT (Melissa Weathers / Joe Schwab / DJ Sebastian / Robert Sanders / Gianmarco Conti, 2026-08-31): *"Concerns on memory supply have trickled in to investor sentiment on WFE spending. With **higher degrees of uncertainty on total memory demand and with WFE EXPECTATIONS ALREADY SKY-HIGH**, an increasing number of investors are wondering **what is the next catalyst for the semicap group to move higher. IN STARK CONTRAST TO THIS BEARISH INVESTOR SENTIMENT, FUNDAMENTALS IN THIS GROUP APPEAR TO REMAIN ROBUST AS EVER, with NEW FAB ACTIVITY TRACKING IN-LINE WITH EXPECTATIONS and setting up for STRONG GROWTH IN 2027/2028**."*** ⚠️ **DB adds the margin frame both ways: *"Investor focus has also shifted incrementally to the MARGIN front for these names, both on the positive side (**potential for PRICING TAILWINDS**) and the negative side (**capacity expansion weighing on margins**)."***
- ➜ **HOW TO HOLD THE TWO: they are not contradictory on facts. Jefferies is reporting FLOW and a specific doubt about a 2028 number; DB is reporting CORPORATE COMMENTARY and new-fab tracking. **The reconciliation is that fundamentals are fine and the out-year number is unfalsifiable for another two years — so the stocks trade on positioning and export-control headlines in the interim. That argues for owning the fundamentals through a de-rating, not for fighting the de-rating.**
- 🔴 **DB'S BROADER FRAME, WHICH IS THE MOST USEFUL PARAGRAPH OF THE WEEK FOR THIS THEME: *"There was a notable discrepancy between **management teams' outlooks for FUNDAMENTALS** and **investors' outlooks for SHARE PRICES**… most co's in attendance signaled **steady and strong trends**… By contrast, **investor sentiment has soured MATERIALLY from the enthusiasm seen in the first half of the year, with 'PEAK CYCLE' FEARS common**. Much of this bearishness stems from ongoing concerns of **DEMAND DESTRUCTION CAUSED BY DRAM PRICING** and **technological innovation to break through the 'MEMORY WALL'**."* ➜ **Both named fears are now testable and both have evidence this week: demand destruction is REAL and visible in handsets ([[AAPL]]: units −16.7%, sub-$100 −60% y/y, on NAND/DRAM +300%+), and the memory-wall innovation is real too (SRAM-heavy inference chips, custom base dies, HBF). **Neither, so far, reduces wafer demand — they change what the wafers are used for. That is the argument the bulls have to make and DB is making it for them: *"most management teams believe the issue to be one of ECONOMICS RATHER THAN TECHNOLOGY, with aggregate DRAM demand likely to remain high."***
- 🔴 **DEMAND SIDE, FROM DB'S OWN MEETINGS AND IT IS THE BIGGEST CAPEX NUMBER OF THE WEEK: *"it's clear demand for AI compute is not slowing, with **investors NOW ASSUMING 2027 HYPERSCALER CAPEX WILL REACH $1.2–$1.5t, UP FROM ~$800b IN 2026**. In addition, **demand has broadened beyond hyperscalers alone, with NEOCLOUDS, ENTERPRISES, SOVEREIGN AI INITIATIVES, AND EMERGING AI LABS all ramping spending**… **demand is NOT the limiting factor; rather, MEMORY and, to some extent, POWER are the primary bottlenecks**."* ⚠️ **Basis note: J.P. Morgan's tech team published $1.40trn for 2027 the same day — dead centre of DB's reported buy-side range, so house and buy-side agree on 2027 capex. See [themes/hyperscaler-capex](hyperscaler-capex.md).**
- **[[ASML]]'S UNDERPERFORMANCE GETS TWO NON-FUNDAMENTAL EXPLANATIONS, WHICH IS ITSELF THE POINT: *"A lot of my ASML meeting was spent by investors **bouncing views off each other on WHY ASML HAS UNDERPERFORMED vs US semicaps on a ~1yr view DESPITE ARGUABLY THE STRONGEST FUNDAMENTALS**, geopolitical risk aside. Perhaps that is exactly it, but some other (more technical) suggestions were: **1/ it misses out on a huge amount of PASSIVE ETF FLOW in the US, and 2/ a lot of US mutual funds are ALREADY MAXED OUT on how much semis/tech they can own**"* (Jefferies, 08-31). ➜ **If the explanation is flow-structural rather than fundamental, the discount is persistent rather than closing — an important distinction for anyone holding ASML as a catch-up trade.**
- **TWO SECONDARY OBSERVATIONS WORTH KEEPING: (a) **MARGINS DOMINATED THE MEETINGS** — *"roughly **80% of the meetings I sat in on spent a BIG CHUNK of the total 40mins discussing GMs**. Felt like more of a focus than usual at a tech conference. **Factor of supply tightness limiting near-term REVENUE upside for many names?** So investors are searching for potential earnings surprises in the coming quarters via **improved costs/capacity util**"* (Jefferies). ➜ **When revenue is supply-capped, the only variable left is margin — a good explanation for why beat-and-raises are not being rewarded.** (b) **BACK-END CAPACITY OPTIONALITY: *"In my meeting with **AMKR** they were talking about how they have secured **an extra 67 acres of land in Arizona** which gives optionality for a **bigger Phase 2 than originally planned and potentially a Phase 3**. Sounds pretty bullish for back-end names geared into AMKR/OSATs"* — Jefferies also saw SUSS and C Sun, *"both strong meetings."* See [themes/cowos-packaging](cowos-packaging.md).**
- **BROAD-BASED RECOVERY, from DB, for the analog/industrial cohort: *"the broad-based semiconductor cycle is **firmly on the path to recovery**. Suppliers are increasingly **shipping to natural demand as distributor channels clear**, leading to a steady **stabilization of manufacturing capacity utilization from previous cyclical lows**… data center and power infrastructure markets remain primary growth engines… next-generation power upgrades (e.g. **800VDC ramping in late CY27**)… **automotive… a more gradual cyclical recovery with an anticipated content-driven 2H ramp**, even as **48V and SiC EV adoption is phased gradually over the next couple of years**."* See [themes/800v-dc-power](800v-dc-power.md), [[ON]], [[NXPI]], [[TXN]], [[ADI]], [[MCHP]], [[IFX]], [[POWI]], [[NVTS]], [[AEIS]].**

## 🆕 Recent signals (Aug 29, /run-inbox — **LEAD TIMES BACK THROUGH 18 MONTHS, AND THE KOREA IMPORT PRINT IS STILL *ACCELERATING* ON A 3-MONTH BASIS**)

- 🔴 **LEAD TIMES: Wells Fargo reports lead times from the **top five semi-cap equipment makers — [[AMAT]], [[LRCX]], [[TOKYOELEC]], [[KLAC]] and [[ASML]] — stretching to >18 MONTHS.** ⚠️ **Rakers frames the consequence as a question this theme should treat as a RISK, not a bull point: *"Stretching Semi Cap Lead Times Providing a Boost for China Domestic Players?"* An 18-month quoted lead time is simultaneously a demand signal and a share-donation mechanism to domestic Chinese toolmakers who can deliver sooner at the trailing edge — which connects directly to the [china-export](china-export.md) and YMTC/CXMT threads on this wiki.**
- **As a reminder, Rakers notes [[AMAT]] *"has continued to report visibility of 8 ROLLING QUARTERS"*, and that AMAT and [[LRCX]] *"have consistently expressed confidence"* that the shortage is concentrated at **leading-edge nodes requiring significantly more advanced tooling** — i.e. the tightness is a mix phenomenon, which is also why the trailing-edge share risk above is real.**
- 🔴🔴 **THE KOREA PRINT — the highest-frequency read on memory WFE available, and it is still accelerating: **July South Korea semiconductor tool imports of $2.24bn, −6% M/M but +90% Y/Y. The 3-MONTH AVERAGE IS +70% Y/Y, UP FROM +64% Y/Y IN 2Q26.**** ➤ **The sequential dip is noise; the accelerating 3-month average is the signal. A +70% and rising 3-month trend is difficult to reconcile with any 2027 memory-WFE digestion call, and it corroborates the DRAM-capex leg this theme already carries from the $69bn/$96bn 2027-28 DRAM WFE marks. Rakers tracks the series against combined Korea revenue for AMAT, ASML, Lam Research, KLA and Tokyo Electron.**
- **[[ASML]] capital returns, for the trail: **€391M repurchased in the week 8/10-8/14 (5 active days), ~flat W/W; €1.91bn QTD across 1.26M shares — the highest since 4Q25, and ~5.4% of ASML's trading volume.****
- **Also in the same weekly, filed for completeness: SYNA's CFO resigned to join Qnity; Avnet raised its dividend 5.7% (7th increase in 6 years).**

_(Wells Fargo Securities · Aaron Rakers, Technology Weekly, 2026-08-21) [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Wells_Fargo_Hynix_-_Whos_Customer_B_CPO_Roadmap_S_Korea_Memo.html)_

## 🆕 Recent signals (Aug 28, /run-inbox — **YMTC TURNS ITS NEW FABS INTO *QUALIFICATION ENVIRONMENTS* FOR DOMESTIC TOOLS, WITH A >50% LOCAL-CONTENT FLOOR ON PHASE III; PLUS A $550M UPW CONTRACT THAT PRICES A FAB COST LINE NOBODY MODELS**) — 🔴 **TWO SLOW-BURN ITEMS, NEITHER OF WHICH MOVES A 2026 WFE NUMBER, BOTH OF WHICH CHANGE WHAT A FAB DOLLAR BUYS.**

### 🔴 (1) THE CHINA LOCALISATION TRANSMISSION BELT GETS NAMED: FABS AS TOOL-QUALIFICATION SITES

- **17 semiconductor companies signed projects worth >CNY6bn** in Wuhan's Donghu zone (China Optics Valley) under YMTC's **"Wutong Tree" Plan**, first group; named equipment suppliers **AMEC and Skyverse**. YMTC is *"drawing equipment, components, inspection and R&D companies closer to its fabs to create a more concentrated domestic semiconductor supply chain centred on wafer manufacturing"* — explicitly *"rather than simply adding vendors."*
- **🔴 THE MECHANISM THIS PAGE HAS BEEN MISSING: "new production lines therefore serve two purposes: expanding capacity AND PROVIDING PRODUCTION ENVIRONMENTS IN WHICH LOCALLY MADE EQUIPMENT AND MATERIALS CAN BE QUALIFIED."** Plus a hard floor: **YMTC Phase III lines are reportedly subject to a >50% domestic-equipment requirement.**
- ➤ **Why it matters more than another China-capex headline: qualification is the gate. This page's China WFE numbers ($57 / $73 / $101bn) implicitly assume a foreign-vendor share that erodes only as domestic tools PASS QUAL — and YMTC is now industrialising the qual process itself, at scale, with state zoning behind it. Slower than an export ban, and far harder to reverse. Cross-filed to [china-export.md](china-export.md).**
- ⚠️ **Trade-press single source; CNY6bn is signed-project value NOT capex; no Phase III timeline given.**

(Digitimes "YMTC builds China chip supply chain around Wuhan fabs with 'Wutong Tree' Plan", Taipei, 2026-08-28.) [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/YMTC_builds_China_chip_supply_chain_around_Wuhan_fabs_with_Wutong_Tree_Plan.html)

### (2) A $550M ULTRAPURE-WATER CONTRACT — THE FAB COST LINE THIS PAGE HAS NEVER SIZED

Jefferies' water theme note puts hard numbers on fab water scope, which sits *outside* WFE but inside fab capex/opex and increasingly gates *where* a fab can be built at all:

- **Veolia / [[INTC]] Ohio, 2025: a 16-YEAR design-build-operate contract for UPW and wastewater treatment — $550M, "the largest single disclosed water contract for a US semiconductor facility."** Plus **Intel's Ocotillo Brine Reduction Facility (Chandler, 2023) at 96% on-site water recovery, 12.8M m³ saved**, which Jefferies calls the model for fab-to-utility partnerships. A separate **Veolia Water-as-a-Service deal for a US fab (2024) at $66M / 2.1M gal/day recycled.**
- **🔴 THE SITING CONSTRAINT IS THE REAL READ-THROUGH: "TSMC's Arizona campus and Intel's Chandler operations are served by the same water system, already under pressure from data center demand,"** while CHIPS-Act capacity concentrates in **Arizona, Texas and New York** — the same states absorbing hyperscaler DC growth. Arizona enacted a **three-year moratorium on DC tax incentives (June 2026)**; Texas's PUC began requiring DC water/cooling/power reporting in spring 2026.
- ➤ **For this page: a second, non-WFE gate on greenfield timing, alongside the cleanroom-space cap [[AMAT]] management already named as the limit on 2028 upside. Water permitting does not change tool intensity per wafer — it changes whether and when the shell that holds the tools gets approved. Detail on [ai-datacenter-water.md](ai-datacenter-water.md).**

(Jefferies "Water & AI Infrastructure: The Next 'Behind the Meter' Theme?", Stephen Volkmann, 2026-08-24.) [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Water_AI_Infrastructure__The_Next___Behind_the_Meter___Theme.html)


## 🆕 Recent signals (Aug 27, /run-inbox — **THE JEFFERIES CONFERENCE PRIMARY: [[AMAT]] SAYS DEMAND IS RUNNING *ABOVE* THE ~$150bn 2026 SHIPPED-WFE MARKET, TWO VENDORS INDEPENDENTLY RANK C27 GROWTH DRAM-FIRST, AND [[ASML]] HAS A 2029 CAPACITY DECISION DUE BEFORE DECEMBER**) — 🔴 **THIS PAGE ALREADY CARRIES THE 21h RELAY OF THIS CONFERENCE. WHAT THE FULL NOTE ADDS IS PER-COMPANY DETAIL — INCLUDING TWO DATED DECISION POINTS AND ONE CAPACITY CEILING THAT NO AGGREGATE ON THIS PAGE HAS.**

**Source: Jefferies "2026 Semiconductor Conference Recap" (Blayne Curtis, Janardan Menon, Kevin Garrigan et al.), 2026-08-27.** [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_Semiconductor_Conference_Recap.html)

- 🔴 **[[AMAT]]: *"**demand running ABOVE the ~$150bn WFE market SHIPPING this year**."*** ⚠️ **Note the basis — "shipping," not bookings, and not a calendar-year TAM. It is NOT directly comparable to the $163bn 2026 figure this page carries from Morgan Stanley or to the $150bn Lam number; each uses a different perimeter. What is usable is the DIRECTION: the largest deposition vendor says demand exceeds what is physically shipping.** **ICAPS *"likely to GROW in C27 after capacity digestion ended, but % in F/L still going DOWN as leading edge grows faster"*; JulQ new projects were **~50% DRAM**, with several NAND projects **including one in China**.**
- ✅ **TWO VENDORS INDEPENDENTLY RANK C27 THE SAME WAY, WHICH IS THE CLEANEST MIX SIGNAL IN THE NOTE: [[LRCX]] — *"**C27 WFE growth led by DRAM**, followed by F/L and NAND"* — against AMAT's ~50%-DRAM new-project mix. **NAND stays a conversion story: *"mostly CONVERSIONS in C27, with more GREENFIELD possible in C28 as [[MU]] comes online."*** ➤ **DRAM-first is now a two-source call, and it lands directly on the leg doing most of the work in the MS raise this page tracks.**
- 🔴 **[[ASML]] HAS A DATED, BINARY CAPACITY DECISION AND IT IS THE BEST FORWARD CATALYST ON THIS PAGE: low-NA EUV capacity is being expanded through **cycle time (22wks → 16-17wks)** plus R&D cabin space and **unused high-NA bays**, with *"a **2029 CLEANROOM DECISION NEEDED BY YEAR-END** and **GATED BY DEMAND DURABILITY**."* **2027 planning is at 85 low-NA systems** with a few more possible. ⚠️ **A mechanic worth knowing before reading any beat on units: *"**SMART TESTING allows SHIPMENT BEFORE ALL TESTS COMPLETE, with revenue STILL ON SHIPMENT**"* — shipments and revenue can be pulled forward without the full test cycle finishing.** **China: revenues *"picking up 2H26 vs 1H on mature logic, with further improvement in 2027 as **CXMT/YMTC cleanrooms come online**."***
- 🔴 **AND A HARD CEILING NUMBER THIS PAGE DOES NOT HAVE ANYWHERE ELSE, FROM THE COMPONENT LAYER: VAT (VACN) says its *"**fully ramped footprint supports a $225-250bn WFE market**. **ADDITIONAL CAPACITY BEYOND THAT NEEDS 15-18 MONTHS**, implying a **DECISION BY END-1H27** if higher 2028 WFE demand looks likely."*** ➤ **That is a physical supply ceiling on the whole industry from a monopoly-ish vacuum-valve supplier, and it sits right at Morgan Stanley's $223bn 2027 and BELOW its $254bn 2028. If MS is right about 2028, VAT has to commit capital by mid-2027 — and if it doesn't, the aggregate is unachievable regardless of demand. This is the most useful constraint datapoint on the page.** ⚠️ **VAT also reports *"orders expected around current levels"* with customers seeing **up to 8 quarters of visibility vs ~2 historically**, and — the cautionary note — ***"NOT YET seeing a pick-up in NAND investments."***
- **Packaging cross-checks: [[AMAT]] claims it is *"**better positioned in CoPoS/EMIB-T vs CoWoS** as customers require higher-end PVD"*, while [[INTC]] says *"**EMIB-T backlog supports IFS revenue from 2H27 into C28 and beyond**"* — two companies, one architecture, consistent timing. ⚠️ **And a growth-rate mismatch to keep un-averaged: [[LRCX]] reiterates advanced packaging **+70%+ in C26**; ONTO reiterates **+80%+** for the same year. Different served bases, not comparable.** **[[LRCX]] margin: **51-52% GM through 1H27**, then toward the mid-50% long-term model; a second Malaysia fab equal in size to the first, with **cleanroom readiness the primary bottleneck** — the same physical constraint ASML names.**
- **Two smaller marks: SUSS (SMHN) reports a **spin-coater order boom with further Taiwanese orders in Q3 after a $115m OSAT order**, for CoWoS and other AI applications, *"with the bulk of orders for 2027 (incl. late 2027) delivery, so future order levels COULD BE LOWER"* — ⚠️ an explicit warning that the order book is front-loaded. It is also **losing share at [[SAMSUNG]] on temporary bonders as Samsung de-risks a single supplier**, while remaining **dominant at [[MU]]**.**

## 🆕 Recent signals (Aug 27, /wiki-ingest — **THE LARGEST ETCH/DEP VENDOR'S OWN 2026 WFE NUMBER IS ~$10bn BELOW MORGAN STANLEY'S, THE NAND GREENFIELD THAT JUSTIFIED PART OF MS'S RAISE WON'T PRODUCE UNTIL 2029-30, AND A NEW STRUCTURAL SPEC-UPGRADE CYCLE OPENS AT CHIP PROBE**) — 🔴🔴 **THIS PAGE HAS BEEN TRACKING MS'S FIFTH WFE RAISE ($163bn 2026 / $223bn 2027 / $254bn 2028). THREE ITEMS THIS WEEK CUT INTO THE 2026 BASE AND THE 2028 TERMINAL FROM DIFFERENT DIRECTIONS — AND ONE OPENS A GENUINELY NEW, UNMODELLED CONTENT VECTOR.**

### ⚠️⚠️ HOUSE-VS-COMPANY GAP ON THE 2026 BASE: LAM'S OWN GUIDE IS *"LOW $150 BILLION RANGE"* AGAINST MS'S $163bn

**[[LRCX]] CEO Tim Archer, on the June-quarter earnings call (i.e. late July — *dated commentary, not new this week*, but it is the company's standing number and this page had not scored it against MS): *"As we enter the second half of the year, we expect **calendar 2026 wafer fab equipment spending, or WFE, to be in the LOW $150 BILLION RANGE, up from our prior outlook of $140 billion with upside bias**."*** On 2027: *"we see an **EXTRAORDINARY SETUP for WFE growth**. AI is driving record revenue and profitability for our customers who have signaled **unprecedented long-term demand visibility**. They've also announced **multiyear timelines for new fab projects** and are working with us to **secure equipment orders to fill the incremental clean room space as it comes online**."* (quoted in Semiecosystem · Mark LaPedus, 2026-08-26).
➤ 🔴 **MS's 2026 WFE of $163bn sits ~$10bn (~7%) ABOVE the largest deposition-and-etch vendor's own published view of the same calendar year. Lam sees the order book directly and raised its own number by $10bn+ this cycle; MS has raised five times. Both can be right only if MS is picking up spend Lam does not serve (litho, metrology, back-end) — which is plausible, but neither this page nor MS has shown that bridge. Logged as an open reconciliation, not smoothed.**
- **New this week from Lam: broke ground on a 120,000 sq ft R&D centre at its Tualatin, Oregon campus — expanding cleanroom lab space **>50%** at Tualatin when completed in **2028**, >400 new jobs, its **biggest Oregon investment to date**; supports advanced deposition and etch, materials science and hardware validation. It follows Lam's announcement earlier in August of **>$3bn over five years to expand its global R&D lab network, increasing R&D capacity by >50%.** Senior leaders from [[INTC]] and Micron attended the groundbreaking** (Semiecosystem, 2026-08-26).
- *For reference, Lam's June-2026 quarter as published: revenue **$6.72bn**, net income **$2.28bn** / **$1.81** diluted GAAP, versus **$5.84bn** and **$1.83bn** / **$1.45** in the March-2026 quarter.*

### ⚠️ THE NAND GREENFIELD IS CONFIRMED — AND IT PRODUCES IN 2029-2030, NOT 2028

**[[KIOXIA]] plans a third fab at Iwate (Kitakami) with partner [[SNDK]] for high-density 3D NAND for AI data centres; investment expected **>1tn yen (~$6.3bn)**. 🔴 **Production is not expected until 2029-2030.** Iwate focuses on higher-end datacentre NAND; Yokkaichi stays consumer/smartphone. Kioxia +5%.** Redburn: *"NAND supply-demand tightness is expected to continue through 2026-28, driving stronger pricing for longer. **Bit supply growth and investments near-term remain in UPGRADING EXISTING CAPACITY to taller layer counts.** New greenfield NAND capacity additions is a positive read-x to WFE growth **BEYOND 2028**, most notably for **[[LRCX]] (Buy)** given its high NAND exposure as 3D layer counts rise; a more modest read to **[[AMAT]] (Neutral)**"* (Rothschild & Co Redburn, 2026-08-27).
➤ **MS's 08-25 raise counted *"a NAND greenfield restart"* among the bull-case elements that had *materialised*. It has — as an announcement. The tool revenue lands after the 2028 window MS's own deceleration debate turns on (+37% 2027 → +14% 2028; *"WFE would need to exceed $300bn in 2028 to sustain the current growth rate"*). ⚠️ This makes the 2028 number MORE dependent on logic/DRAM and LESS on NAND than the raise implied.**

### 🔴 A NEW, UNMODELLED STRUCTURAL SPEC-UPGRADE CYCLE — THERMAL MANAGEMENT AND ACTIVE TEMPERATURE CONTROL MIGRATE FROM FINAL TEST TO **CHIP PROBE**

**JPM Asia (Jimmy Huang / Jerry Tsai / Mio Shikanai / Josie Yu / Gokul Hariharan, *"Semiconductor Testing: Advanced thermal solutions for chip probing emerging as a new spec upgrade trend"*, 2026-08-26). The house's own framing: *"the market is LESS AWARE of this technology migration roadmap."***
- **The driver, quantified: *"The thermal design power (TDP) of AI ASICs and GPUs has exceeded **1kW** and **2kW** respectively, and is expected to keep rising… **power delivery per reticle (W/reticle) during probing could be up to 5x on N2 versus on N5P**."***
- **Two independent mechanisms force the upgrade: (1) *"high-temperature test environment would reduce the **current-carrying capacity of probe cards** and increase the **risk of probe burning** when handling large currents"*; (2) *"the critical need for **KNOWN-GOOD-DIES (KGDs)** will only intensify, as early defect detection during chip probing drives cost and capacity savings… **Ensuring KGDs helps avoid wasting expensive resources amid ongoing supply tightness across the AI supply chain including HBM, CoWoS, SoIC, ABF substrates, testing**."***
- **What changes in the tools:** testers add integrated accessories enhancing thermal transfer and airflow *"by multi-folds"*, with a spec migration **from air cooling to hybrid or liquid cooling** over time — *"given IP protection, thermal dissipation structures and thermal management could be COMPETITIVE EDGES for tester companies."* Wafer probers need **ATC thermal chucks**: embedded sensors reading wafer temperature, active heating **and** cooling with fast response and high precision, **multi-zone** heating/cooling, and **power density above 200W/cm²**. Singulated-die probing carries similar requirements.
- **Named exposure: [[ADVANTEST]] (6857 JT) for testers · [[TOKYOELEC]] (8035 JT) for probers · Hon. Precision (7769 TT) for ATC thermal chucks · Chroma (already re-rated on the FT/SLT leg) · Hermes Testing Solutions (7856 TT, NC) for thermal-management integrated modules.** Hon is developing wafer-level ATC as a new initiative (flagged at its 1Q26 and 2Q26 results meetings), leveraging its cold-plate and ATC capability from FT/SLT — *"a potential TAM expansion in the mid to long term (beyond 12 months), rather than any contribution in the near-term"*, with **ATC content value once ~30% of Hon's ATC handler shipment value** as a sizing reference. Current industry thermal chucks *"either offer limited precision in temperature control or come at a high cost."* ⚠️ *Caveat JPM states itself: Hon has no wafer probers, so integration into a prober platform is an open question.*
➤ 🔴 **WHY THIS MATTERS FOR THE PAGE'S CENTRAL DEBATE: it is a back-end content vector that rises with TDP and node, and is therefore INDEPENDENT OF HBM STACK HEIGHT. It cuts directly against the 08-25 MS-desk bear case (*"if you don't scale to 16-high, you don't need hybrid bonding and it's also less process-control intensive"* → bearish [[BESI]]/CAMT/ONTO/[[TER]]) and supports BofA's side (*"migration to new architectures INCREASES WFE intensity — TSV formation, hybrid bonding, process control favorable for semicaps"*). Even in a de-spec world where stacks stay at 8-Hi, W/reticle at probe still goes up 5x from N5P to N2.**
- **Corroborating capacity signal: Chroma ATE approved a new manufacturing facility at Ciaotou Science Park, Taiwan, at an estimated **NT$4bn** (via Jefferies · O'Mara, 2026-08-26).**

### THE MASK-BLANK LEG — EUV BLANKS ACCELERATE TO A 25% CAGR *BECAUSE* OF ASIC PROLIFERATION

**Bernstein (David Dai, *"Japan Semi: Mask Blanks — The Unsung Hero Behind Proliferating AI ASIC Designs"*, 2026-08-26/27): *"We forecast the **EUV mask blank market growth to accelerate to 25% CAGR over 2025-28E**, driven by both increasing volume and continued **ASP expansion as mask complexity rises**. The proliferation of ASIC designs drives increasing usage of EUV mask blanks; **every CSP and AI company is making their own GPU, CPU, and AI accelerators, and each design needs multiple sets of expensive EUV masks and mask blanks.** The **total # of chip designs using advanced logic nodes continue to grow at 21% CAGR (2025-2028)**."*** ⚠️ **The note was distributed TRUNCATED at *"This is also evident from TSMC's announcement that its 2nm node …"* — the TSMC evidence and the stock conclusions were NOT read here and are not logged.**
➤ **A rare item that is bullish on BOTH sides of the merchant-vs-ASIC fight: mask-blank demand is a function of the NUMBER of tape-outs, so it grows whether the winning silicon is [[NVDA]]'s or a hyperscaler's. See [custom-asic-tpu.md](custom-asic-tpu.md).**

**The press framing: *"A rebound in Chinese imports of semiconductor equipment used in chip manufacturing signals that the worst of the drag tied to the Asian buyer is over for ASML Holding NV and other leading suppliers. Beijing's imports of lithography tools **climbed 7% in July, marking a SECOND straight monthly increase**, helping ASML's deep-ultraviolet business hold up better than feared amid reports that a **state-backed Chinese company has begun mass production**"* [of litho tools].**
⚠️⚠️ **JEFFERIES' PUSHBACK IS THE USEFUL PART, AND IT IS A BASIS CATCH: *"In general there is a lot of difference in this data series and the data from ASML. **ASML has reported its China sales DOWN 31% in H1-26, but this data series shows litho imports into China only DOWN 18% in the first six months of the year.** Especially on a month-to-month basis difficult to make too many conclusions from this data."*** (Jefferies Global SEMIS Daily, 2026-08-26.)

➤ **Triangulate with two items from the same 48 hours and China stops being purely a risk line for [[ASML]]: (1) MS's CXMT initiation says *"**ASML DUV lithography tools may be the MAIN EQUIPMENT BOTTLENECK** under export controls"* while CXMT adds ~100kwpm annually toward 800kwpm by 2031; (2) BofA argues the market is already pricing a total China loss (below). **The China leg is a 2027 upside vector gated by policy, not a 2026 earnings drag — with the offset that domestic vendors are displacing US suppliers step by step, and a state-backed domestic litho tool is reportedly in mass production.** See [china-export.md](china-export.md), [[ASML]], [[AMAT]], [[LRCX]], [[KLAC]].**
