Redburn names the beneficiaries: **Naura, AMEC, Piotech.***

### [[ASML]] — BofA ARGUES THE MATCH ACT IS ALREADY IN THE PRICE, AND SHOWS THE ARITHMETIC

**BofA (Didier Scemama, note addressed to the desk, 2026-08-26): *"Following recent concerns around the potential impact of the proposed **MATCH Act** and a **full DUV export ban to China**, we have conducted a **sum-of-the-parts analysis** to estimate the value the market is currently attributing to ASML's China business. Our conclusion is striking: **the market may already be discounting a COMPLETE LOSS of China revenues.** Using ASML's historical valuation framework implies a **NEGATIVE value** for the China business, while a more conservative peer-based approach suggests China is worth **c.17% of group EV**. Given ASML's historical premium valuation and unique EUV monopoly, we believe even this latter scenario is conservative… With the market appearing to price in an extreme downside China outcome, **any clarification or easing of export-control uncertainty could prove a meaningful catalyst.**"* Reiterates **Buy, Top Pick, PO €2,452.**
⚠️ **Note what the argument is and is not: it is a VALUATION-IMPLIED claim about what is discounted, not new information about MATCH Act probability or scope. It is falsifiable only against the multiple, not against the policy.**

### SUPPLY-CHAIN BOTTLENECKS THAT ARE *NOT* WFE — AND MAY NOW BE THE BINDING ONES FOR 2027

- 🔴 **SUBSTRATES AND GLASS CLOTH ARE STRANDING REVENUE TODAY: Kinsus reports ABF substrate utilisation rebounded to **85%**, expected to approach **95% by year-end** on AI-server/HPC demand — but *"the supply of **T-GLASS remains constrained**, with the company estimating that approximately **10% to 15% of potential MONTHLY REVENUE cannot currently be realized** due to material shortages. This continues to be **the key operational bottleneck**."* Kinsus plans **+25% high-end ABF capacity in 2027**, material allocations already secured, built at **Taiwan** facilities — *"dismissing market speculation that existing BT production lines could be converted to ABF."* **BT substrate utilisation ~75% → ~85% by year-end**, supported by memory demand and high-speed optical. Customer engagement on 2027-2029 capacity planning starts in 4Q; a further expansion round possible **as early as mid-2027** if optical accelerates.** (Jefferies · O'Mara, 2026-08-26.)
- ⚠️ **AND THE CORPUS PUTS IT ABOVE MEMORY AS THE 2027 CONSTRAINT: *"ABF substrates & PCB/CCL are the biggest constraints for 2027. **Nvidia or Google can reduce HBM per GPU and DRAM per server to increase shipments, but they can't work around this**"* (@zephyr_z9, corpus, 2026-08-27). ➤ **This is the most important strategic point in the run for this page: HBM has a de-spec lever, substrates do not. If the binding constraint has migrated from HBM/CoWoS to ABF/CCL/T-glass, then WFE forecasts keyed off wafer capacity are measuring the wrong thing for 2027, and the marginal upside accrues to substrate and glass-cloth capacity rather than to front-end tools.**
- **[[SKHYNIX]] says the same thing about cleanrooms rather than tools: *"limited wafer capacity expansion (for both DRAM/NAND) **even with new shell fab construction** (as **new cleanroom space remains tight**)"* (BofA · Simon Woo, 2026-08-26) — which is the demand-side echo of [[SAMSUNG]] IR's 08-25 *"aggressive capex is focused mainly on SHELL FAB / INFRASTRUCTURE CONSTRUCTION RATHER THAN EQUIPMENT."* **Two of the three memory makers now describe the constraint as shells and cleanrooms, which is capex that does NOT convert to WFE in the same year.**
- **Equipment-in dates, which is when the shells start converting: SK Hynix **Y1 receives equipment from February 2027**, two clean rooms adding capacity in 2027 in sequence; **[[SAMSUNG]] P5-1 from July 2027 onwards** (UBS Korea Summit, 2026-08-27).

### THE CONFERENCE READ — SUPPLY IS THE BINDING CONSTRAINT ACROSS THE BOARD, AND SENTIMENT IS THE GAP

**Jefferies' annual Semiconductor Conference, ~100 companies (Blayne Curtis et al., recap 2026-08-27): *"the **disconnect between the tough tape and positive underlying fundamentals** was front and center. Messaging from management teams throughout Compute, Analog, Semi-Cap, Optical, Memory, and everything in between was **almost uniformly positive**, underscoring our belief that current market dynamics have shifted away from fundamentals towards more a glass-half-empty view of AI focused on **frontier lab economics, open-source model competition, concerns of circular financing, and political pushback on AI DCs**."***
- **Compute: *"supply rather than demand is increasingly the binding constraint. [[NVDA]] appears to have moved aggressively to secure substrates, DRAM, and other critical inputs, making availability an **underappreciated competitive advantage** for Vera and its broader platform."* **[[AMD]]** *"sounding more confident on supply dynamics as they are **one of the only to lock in substrate capacity during the pandemic**"*, with *"real potential for y/y acceleration in both units and ASPs tied to the release of **Venice on N2**."* **[[INTC]]** *"faces more acute substrate constraints, but its commentary was nevertheless constructive, with **Clearwater Forest demand tracking ahead of expectations** and **Granite Rapids supporting better ASPs despite a core-count disadvantage**."***
- **Analog: DC exposure dominated conversations. Auto *"modestly improved, particularly in China and EV/ADAS applications, but **Tier 1 inventories remain lean and there are limited signs of meaningful restocking thus far**."*** (Read-through: [[ADI]], [[TXN]], [[NXPI]], [[MCHP]], [[ON]], [[IFX]].) Jefferies' preferred analog names are off-coverage here (MTSI on 1.6T demand and growing PD content; ALGM on current-sensor ramp and isolated gate drivers).

THE BODY OF THE SAME NOTE CARRIES A DIFFERENT 2nm CAPACITY NUMBER, AND THE GAP IS 155kwpm. SEPARATELY, THE COMPANY PRIMARY BEHIND THE "CAPEX IS GOING INTO SHELLS RATHER THAN EQUIPMENT" LINE TURNS OUT TO DESCRIBE AN *OPTION STRATEGY* WITH AN [[AMAT]] LONG-TERM AGREEMENT AS THE HEDGE — NOT CAUTION.**

**Source: Morgan Stanley · Shane Brett, Lee Simpson, Nigel van Putten et al., "Semiconductor Capital Equipment: 3Q'26 WFE update: 2026/27 Bull Case is the Base Case", 2026-08-25.** [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/SEMICONDUCTOR_20260825_0401.html)

- **The summary page (which is what the 21h run captured, and what this page currently carries):** *"**2nm capacity reaching 335kwpm/500kwpm in 2027/28** (vs. 77kwpm in 2025)."*
- **The body of the same note, section "Leading Logic: Two very strong years of growth":** *"**2nm: We model wafer capacity of 180kwpm in 2027 and 335kwpm in 2028**, with adds across [[TSM]], [[INTC]] and [[SAMSUNG]]."*
- ➤ 🔴 **The body figure is the 335kwpm number shifted one year later. The summary appears to have advanced the whole series by a year.** ⚠️ **This wiki's standing rule from prior runs applies: front-page summaries go one revision stale; the exhibits and the body are the more reliable layer.** ➤ **The page keeps BOTH, flagged. If the body is right, MS's 2nm capacity in 2027 is 180kwpm — roughly HALF what the summary says — which matters because 2nm is the single largest driver inside the leading-logic leg of the raise.** ⚠️ **Do not use either number without stating which layer of the note it came from.** ✅ **Both versions agree on the 2025 base of 77kwpm and on the 2028 direction.**

### THE SEGMENT DETAIL THE SUMMARY DID NOT CARRY (same note)

- **LEADING LOGIC, by year and by spender:** **2026 $51bn (+65% y/y)**, growth split roughly evenly between 3nm and 2nm with [[TSM]], [[SAMSUNG]] and [[INTC]] spending; **2027 $76bn (+50%)**, 2nm the biggest driver with initial 1.4nm adds, and **[[TSM]] at $43.3bn against [[INTC]] at $20.5bn**; **2028 $90bn (+17%)**, with TSMC and Intel decelerating and Samsung picking up. ⚠️ **MS models only ~$2bn from SpaceX in 2028 — i.e. Terafab is NOT in the base case in any size.** **Mature logic: −2%/−3% in 2026, +15%/+18% in 2027** (the note quotes both pairs in different places — a second, smaller internal inconsistency). **Leading-vs-mature mix reverses from 39%/61% in 2025 (or 35%/65% in 2024, depending on the exhibit) to 63%/37% in 2028.**
- **NODE CAPACITY LADDER:** **3nm 180kwpm (2025) → 265kwpm (2026), then "marginal increases during 2027-29"** — i.e. **3nm flat-lines from 2027**, which is the structural point behind TSMC's N3 pricing power. **1.4nm 60kwpm in 2027 and 130kwpm in 2028, led by [[TSM]] and [[INTC]].**
- **DRAM:** WFE **$53bn (+70%) in 2026 and $70bn (+33%) in 2027**; **greenfield revised from 268/395kwpm to 283/493kwpm for 2026/27** (*"2027 revision is across the board but with the biggest revisions at [[SAMSUNG]] and [[SKHYNIX]]"*); **bit supply growth revised up from 29%/33% to 32%/38%**. 🔴 **And the mix inside it: MS models 2026/2027 HBM bit supply growth of 55%/57% against conventional bit supply growth of 30%/36%, with NON-HBM wafer adds tracking AHEAD of HBM wafer adds from 2027.** ➤ **That last clause is the quantified version of the "HBM crowding out commodity DRAM ends in 2027" argument — worth holding against the DAMNANG 2-3x wafer-area estimate on [hbm-memory.md](hbm-memory.md).**
- **NAND:** WFE fine-tuned to **$13.7bn/$24.0bn for 2026/27** (from $15.6bn/$24.0bn); **bit supply growth cut from 24%/31% to 24%/26%**; **"YMTC continues to account for approx. HALF of our greenfield adds in 2026/27"**; **eSSD mix rising from 29% of NAND in 2025 to 65% in 2028.** ➤ **A single Chinese producer carrying ~50% of global NAND greenfield is the sharpest China-supply datapoint in the note — see [china-export.md](china-export.md), where YMTC's IPO prospectus now sits alongside it.**

State which one you mean.**
- **THE CROSS-CHECK MS ITSELF OFFERS, and it is the reason the debate is moving to GW:** industry growth of **$100bn+ from 2025 to 2027** *"more closely aligns with the compute (GW) deployment we're seeing (**approx. 30GW of WFE vs 2027/28 GW of 29/36**), based on our analysis of **WFE per GW** and our Internet Team's forecast of industry GW deployment."* ⚠️⚠️ **THIS IS A WFE-EQUIVALENT-GW CONSTRUCT, NOT A FACILITY-GW OR IT-LOAD-GW FIGURE. It must not enter the canonical GW ledger in `../_meta/assumptions.md`, and it must not be netted against the 30/50/70GW incremental-compute series on [hyperscaler-capex.md](hyperscaler-capex.md), which is a different quantity measured a different way.**

MS's $254bn is ~54% above Gartner's 2028 number. The dispersion between the three published series is now larger than any single house's annual revision — worth stating whenever "consensus WFE" is invoked.**

### 🔴 THE COMPANY PRIMARY BEHIND "SHELLS, NOT EQUIPMENT" — AND IT IS AN OPTION STRATEGY WITH AN [[AMAT]] LTA ATTACHED

**Source: [[SAMSUNG]] Electronics Investor Relations — group call hosted by BTG, raw notes, 2026-08-25 (⚠️ company management primary; machine transcript with word garbling).** [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Samsung_IR_call_BTG_raw_notes_25_ago_26.html)

Asked directly what Samsung can do if a demand shock arrives, the DS spokesperson: *"at this point, in the near term, we are more aggressive and proactive with infrastructure investment. And currently we are having the **share first strategy**. It means on top of actual demand, we… **prepared the INFRASTRUCTURE AHEAD OF BRINGING IN THE EQUIPMENT**. It means that **if we have more [cleanroom] spaces, we can adequately and flexibly adjust to market dynamics**… After 20[28], we fully realized that there are a lot of uncertainties and risks as well. Definitely, we have more options like **more cleanroom spaces** or technology… On top of it, as you already saw, **we also recently made a LONG TERM AGREEMENT WITH [[AMAT]] and [other equipment] companies. We think that this [L]TA would act as a SAFETY NET** for us [to] respond to this market changes as well… we are confident that, compared to the previous high[ly] volatile memory industry, **we can make the memory industry less volatile**."*

- 🔴 **THIS INVERTS THE 21h READ.** The BofA relay's *"aggressive capex is focused mainly on shell fab / infrastructure construction rather than equipment"* is a true description of the cash flow, but the company's own framing is not caution — it is **a deliberately purchased OPTION on demand: build the shell early (long lead), defer the tool (short lead), and pre-contract the tool vendor so the option can actually be exercised.** ➤ **That materially weakens the bear reading of the shells-not-tools datapoint, and it strengthens the MS raise's TIMING rather than undermining it: the shell is the leading indicator of the tool order, not a substitute for it.**
- 🔴🔴 **A SAMSUNG↔[[AMAT]] LONG-TERM AGREEMENT IS A NEW ITEM ON THIS WIKI AND IT WAS NOT PICKED UP BY THE ROUTER.** ⚠️ **UNVERIFIED SIZE, SCOPE AND TENOR — management names [[AMAT]] and refers vaguely to *"AIA companies"* (ASR garble; most plausibly "and other" equipment companies, but it is not legible). No value, no duration, no product scope is given.** ➤ **Logged as a NAMED but UNSIZED commercial relationship. It is the counterpart, from the customer side, of the LTA-ification this wiki has been tracking on the memory side — and if the tool vendors are now writing multi-year agreements with memory makers, the WFE cycle's amplitude compresses in BOTH directions. Flagged for verification against [[AMAT]]'s own disclosures.** ✅ **Consistent in direction with [[AMAT]] IR's own 08-20 line already on this page that the 2x capacity plan is "demand preparedness" rather than a booked forecast.**
- **AND THE SECOND MAKER SAYS THE SAME THING.** [[MU]] management to Mizuho (2026-08-09): **"C27E WFE could potentially focus more on building out FAB SHELLS for greenfield in US, TW, SG, and JP"**, with **F27E capex ~$50B (+67% y/y) "focused on construction of front-end capacity"** and **"MU's first greenfield bits will still be SMALL in 2028E, following line/customer qualifications."** ([Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Takeaways_-_Supply_Tight_into_2028E_80_GMs_Sustainable_T.html)) ➤ **Two of three DRAM makers, independently, describe 2027 spend as shell-weighted with bits arriving late in 2028. That is a physical constraint on how early any 2028 supply glut can actually arrive — and it is the strongest available argument that MS's $70bn 2027 DRAM WFE understates the 2028 tool tail rather than the 2027 one.**

### THE DE-SPEC LEG, FROM THE PRIMARY THE 21h RUN ONLY HAD SECOND-HAND

⚠️ **PUBLISHER CORRECTION: the file titled "SemiAnalysis: Memory De-Spec, WFE, 800V Uncertainty with Copper/Optics Upside" is a MIZUHO SECURITIES USA note (Vijay Rakesh, David Liu, Jacob Grandstaff, Rafal Tworkowski, 2026-08-24), written up from a dinner Mizuho hosted with SemiAnalysis President Doug O'Laughlin. Attribute as *Mizuho relaying SemiAnalysis*; the PTs are Mizuho's.** [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/SemiAnalysis__Memory_De-Spec_WFE_800V_Uncertainty_with_Cop.html)

- 🔴 **THE WFE TRANSMISSION MECHANISM, IN THE PRIMARY'S OWN WORDS:** *"**SA noted impact to WFE as hybrid bonding and higher layer count are uncertain** — though we highlight offsetting upside from **Terafab and multiple greenfield fabs**."* And on the size of that offset: *"we note NAND-DRAM capex and share gain in foundry key tailwind for [[LRCX]], with global WFE growth and **>100 new greenfield projects globally, upside to >$190B (up >25% y/y) in 2027E**."* ➤ ⚠️ **Mizuho's own 2027 WFE number is >$190bn against MS's $223bn and Gartner's $166bn. Three houses, three numbers, ~35% spread. Do not average.**
- **AND THE PT CONSEQUENCE, which is the honest version of the bear leg:** **[[AMAT]] $650 → $590** (~31x F27E EPS from ~34x, *"vs peers now trading at ~26x and SOX at ~18x"*), **[[LRCX]] $370 → $365** (~30x F28E), **MKSI $360 → $330** (~20x F27E). *"We modestly adjust Memory and WFE PTs lower on potential DRAM/HBM de-specing driving some **multiple compression**… as **slowing HBM layer count / hybrid bonding is a headwind, partly offset by 100 new greenfield fab projects and Terafab**."* ➤ 🔴 **Note the mechanism: MULTIPLE, NOT ESTIMATE. Mizuho cut PTs by cutting the multiple and left the numbers alone. Nobody has yet cut a back-end revenue estimate on the de-spec — which is exactly what the MS desk flagged on 08-25 and what makes the Rubin-Ultra configuration freeze a genuine back-end catalyst rather than a narrative one.**
- **One [[AMAT]]-specific number worth keeping:** *"we believe that AMAT sees **$2B of incremental TAM for GAA/BPD with every incremental 100kwpm**, driving an expanding market where we believe it could potentially capture **>50% market share**."* ➤ **Multiply against MS's 2nm ladder above and the ambiguity matters: at the body's 180kwpm in 2027 that is ~$3.6bn of incremental GAA/BPD TAM; at the summary's 335kwpm it is ~$6.7bn. The 2nm inconsistency flagged at the top of this block is not academic.**

### CHINA NAND — THE DIGITIMES PRIMARY, WHICH THIS PAGE HAD ONLY AS A HEADLINE

**Source: DIGITIMES · Sherri Wang (Taipei), "Samsung, SK Hynix step up China NAND upgrades as US tool risks grow", 2026-08-25.** [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Samsung_SK_Hynix_step_up_China_NAND_upgrades_as_US_tool_risks_grow.html)

- **[[SAMSUNG]]:** converting **Xi'an X2 to V9 NAND since 1H26** (per ZDNet Korea); **V9 stacks cells at roughly 280 layers**; converted capacity expected to reach **~40,000-50,000 wafers per month**. ⚠️ **DIGITIMES states the caveat explicitly and this page adopts it: *"Because the project involves CONVERTING AN EXISTING PRODUCTION LINE, that figure should NOT be read as an equivalent volume of entirely new wafer starts."*** **A supplementary investment strengthening V9 at Xi'an has been finalised, "centered on ADDITIONAL ETCHING EQUIPMENT," with purchase orders expected around end-2026 and the spend beginning in 2H27.**
- **[[SKHYNIX]]:** began equipment investment at **Dalian Fab 2 in 3Q26**, *"corresponds to roughly **30,000 wafers per month**, with core manufacturing equipment, **including etching tools, expected to begin arriving in greater volume from September**. Investment is planned to continue through 1H27."* Background: SKH agreed to buy Intel's NAND/SSD business in 2020 and took the Dalian assets in Dec-2021; broke ground on Fab 2 in 2022 then **delayed tooling as NAND weakened**. ⚠️ *"available information does not establish how much of the 30,000-wafer figure represents INCREMENTAL WAFER STARTS versus UPGRADED PRODUCTION."* ✅ **Corroborated the same day from the broker side by UBS on SKH: NAND capacity "could be added in Dalian on floating gate process tech, which over time may move to 240L from 144/192L."**
- 🔴 **THE STRATEGY, AND IT IS THE TOOL-DEMAND POINT:** *"the two programs point to a similar capital-allocation strategy: **extracting more value from manufacturing assets already in China through PROCESS MIGRATION and SELECTIVE EQUIPMENT UPGRADES rather than committing to entirely new NAND fabs**… higher NAND layer counts can increase the amount of data stored per wafer **without requiring a matching increase in wafer starts**."* And the tool that gates it: *"**Etching becomes increasingly demanding as NAND makers stack more memory layers because channel holes must be formed through hundreds of layers while maintaining the profile needed for reliable operation.**"* ➤ **This is bit-growth WITHOUT wafer-start growth, delivered by etch. It is a bullish read for high-aspect-ratio etch specifically ([[LRCX]], [[AMAT]]) and a bearish one for anyone modelling China NAND bit supply off wafer starts.**
- 🔴🔴 **THE EXPORT-CONTROL EXPOSURE, AND A DISPUTED CLAIM THAT MUST BE CARRIED AS DISPUTED.** Per Reuters (5 Aug) as relayed: **US Commerce designated Samsung's and SK Hynix's Chinese fabs as Validated End Users in 2023 and REVOKED that status in 2025; both then received an ANNUAL AUTHORIZATION covering equipment shipments to their China facilities for 2026.** *"Their Chinese fabs remain **heavily dependent on etching equipment from US suppliers including [[AMAT]] and [[LRCX]]**… People familiar with the matter cited in the report said the companies were concerned that **future restrictions could extend beyond purchases of new machines to SERVICING, REPAIR, AND REPLACEMENT of Western equipment already installed in China**."* ⚠️⚠️ **AND THE AMEC LEG IS DENIED BY BOTH COMPANIES.** The report said Samsung and SK Hynix had evaluated **Shanghai-based AMEC** etch tools as a hedge. *"**Both companies disputed that account. Samsung said it had NOT tested AMEC equipment for use at its China factory and had NOT considered doing so, while SK Hynix said it had NOT tested AMEC tools for use in China. There is no indication that either company has decided to deploy Chinese equipment at the sites.**"* ➤ **Carry the denial with the claim, always. This is exactly the kind of item that gets repeated as fact once the denial falls off the end of the sentence.** ➤ **The durable, undisputed question is the last line of the piece: *"how many more process generations those fabs can absorb if the equipment required for each successive migration becomes harder to buy, service, or replace."*** Policy half on [china-export.md](china-export.md).

### POSITIONING, FROM THE SPECIALTY-SALES DESK — SEMICAP IS NOW THE *CONSENSUS LONG-ONLY* EXPRESSION OF THE MEMORY TRADE

**Source: Morgan Stanley · Skyler Risom (TMT specialty sales), buy-side call, 2026-08-12.** ⚠️ **SALES COMMENTARY AND FLOW OBSERVATION, NOT RESEARCH — no rating, no estimate.** [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_08_12_skyler_risom_ms.html)

- *"**I think semi-cap for sure. There's a view that it's a LESS VOLATILE trade. There's a decent amount of LONG-ONLY enthusiasm in it. WFE numbers look quite strong for 2027. I mean, those stocks are trading on 2027 numbers at this point.** So it's TBD how much actual management commentary we get. So there is kind of the concern that stocks are getting a little hot of themselves, but I think that there's probably **more appetite to jump into semi-cap than [memory]** just because the prints have been so good this season, both of the smaller names like Onto, MKSI… but then also you saw Lam was like quite strong."*
- 🔴 **AND THE FLOW ASYMMETRY UNDERNEATH IT:** *"my view would be that **generalists have kind of made their money**… they don't have a great desire to nor a great need to jump back into a trade that feels really volatile on a headline basis. And that's what our PB book[s] show, which is that **very little engagement from the non-TMT focused funds. The TMT focused fund[s] remain convicted because they have their checks in Asia and they have their contacts and they have their actual technical thesis on this**, but the observers and the generalists and the retail don't feel [they] really need to stick their necks out again."*
- ➤ **Read against this page's own MS stock framework from 08-25 (LAM/[[AMAT]]/KLA "need 2028" and already at the high end of their 8-year ranges, vs [[AEIS]]/MKS/ONTO "2027 is the peak" and below through-cycle averages): the desk is describing long-only money crowding into precisely the "need 2028" names on a 2027 multiple. That is the positioning risk MS's own analysts are implicitly flagging, said out loud by the sales desk two weeks earlier.** ⚠️ **Flow observation, not a call. Macro/positioning half on [macro-cycle.md](macro-cycle.md).**

### AND THE UPSTREAM ARITHMETIC, FROM SEMIANALYSIS — WHAT A GIGAWATT OF COMPUTE COSTS AT THE TOOL LAYER

**Source: SemiAnalysis · Dylan Patel, in interview on the Dwarkesh Podcast, 2026-08-25.** ⚠️⚠️ **PUBLISHER/HOST CORRECTION: the transcript's first speaker label reads "Daniel Grozdea" (the forwarder). The host is DWARKESH PATEL — the text contains "janestreet.com slash dwarkesh", "Dwarkesh, you're doing a terrible job" and "Where Dwarkesh, from researching his podcast…". Attribute as *SemiAnalysis · Dylan Patel on the Dwarkesh Podcast*.** ⚠️ **Conversational estimates, spoken aloud, explicitly "vibed" in places. Not modelled figures.** [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/dylan_25_ago_26.html)

- 🔴 **THE TOOL-COST-PER-GW NUMBER, and it is startlingly small.** Restating a figure from an earlier episode: **one gigawatt of Vera Rubin needs "55,000 N3 wafers, 6K N5 wafers, and 170K DRAM wafers"** (✅ already on this page's ledger as [`wafers-per-gw`](../_meta/assumptions.md) from the 08-25 Changelog — confirmed unchanged here from the source itself). Then the new leg: *"I had an LLM run your wafer fab equipment model and figure out **how much the tooling costs to produce a gigawatt of compute basically every single year. And it was at like $3 to $4 billion.** Now, suppose you add in clean rooms and shell and everything else at the fab. So **$6 billion of fab capex produces every single year a gigawatt.** And a gigawatt produces right now **$100 billion of revenue**."* Compounded: *"**Six billion of capex at the fab level will have generated over a trillion dollars of end AI revenue**"* over five years, and even halving it for the middlemen *"still means there's a **100x discrepancy between fab CAPEX and end revenue generated**."*
  ⚠️⚠️ **HANDLE WITH CARE. (1) The $6bn is an ANNUAL-OUTPUT capex figure — the tools that produce a GW's worth of silicon EVERY year — not the cost of one GW of data centre. (2) The "$100bn of revenue per GW" is END AI REVENUE, a revenue-per-watt construct: per this wiki's standing rule it must NEVER be chained into a capex or $/GW build-cost series, and must never be netted against the facility-GW / IT-load-GW ledger in `../_meta/assumptions.md`. (3) It is a spoken estimate on a podcast, not a model output.** ➤ **Its analytic value is the RATIO and its direction of travel, not the levels: it is the cleanest available statement of why the WFE supply chain is the highest-return-on-capital layer of the AI stack and why capital keeps arriving there faster than the tool vendors can convert it.**
- **THE SUPPLY-CHAIN CEILING, named at the sub-supplier:** *"**The way the supply chain is expanding currently, the 100 is roughly still the right number [100 ASML tools per year] for 2030.** But if you said, Carl Zeiss, here's $10 billion, please just expand production, that would change things. And you would have to do this with every company in the supply chain."* And on the earlier episode: *"I think when we had our episode earlier this year, **they didn't even think they needed to make that many, enough mirrors to make 100 EUV tools a year. And so now they're like, okay, we need to do that.** But in reality, because of all the economics of what's going on, **it should be even more.**"* ✅ **Independent corroboration of the mirror/optics sub-supplier constraint this page has carried since the ASML work — from the demand side, and with a number attached.**
- **THE SCARCITY RENT, stated as a trade:** *"I think if anyone had $400 million and the ability to convince [[ASML]] to sell them an EUV tool, they should totally just go buy one and wait and then sell it for **north of a billion dollars**."* ⚠️ **Rhetorical, not a transacted secondary-market price — but it is the clearest statement on this wiki of how far above list the marginal EUV slot is now valued, and it is consistent with the 12-18 month lead times the JPM Asia Tech Tour reported on 08-25.**
- **AND THE SIZE OF THE LAYER:** *"**The wafer fabrication equipment supply chain will do something on the order of $200 billion** [next year]. The data center market supply chain will do even more. The accelerator supply chain will do even more."* ✅ **Sits between Mizuho's >$190bn and MS's $223bn for 2027 — three independent 2027 WFE marks now clustered $190-225bn against Gartner's $166bn.**

## Historical backfill (Mar-May 2026, DAMNANG Substack archive) — ⚠️ **DATED CONTEXT ONLY. SUPERSEDES NOTHING ABOVE.**

⚠️ **DAMNANG (Substack) — INDEPENDENT KOREAN SEMICONDUCTOR SUBSTACK, NOT A BROKER: no rating, no price target, no formal estimate anywhere in the corpus. Source identity recorded once, on [optical-cpo.md](optical-cpo.md).**

- 🔴 **TERAFAB, WRITTEN TWICE — AND THE SECOND PIECE IS THE ONE THAT MATTERS, BECAUSE MIZUHO NOW PUTS TERAFAB IN ITS WFE OFFSET.** **DAMNANG (Substack) — "Terafab is Bullshit", 2026-03-17**, and **"The Real Game Behind Terafab: Why Reading This as Just 'Good News for Intel' Gets It Wrong", 2026-04-08.** The confirmed facts he separates from the vision: Musk first raised Terafab at the November-2025 shareholder meeting; the Q4-2025 call (2026-01-28) had Tesla needing *"a very large fab in the US that includes logic, memory, and packaging"* with chip supply constraints hitting *"within three to four years"*; **Tesla AI4 at [[SAMSUNG]]; AI5 at both [[TSM]] and Samsung in "slightly different versions"; AI6 on Samsung SF2 under a $16.5bn long-term deal signed July 2025 at Taylor, Texas running to 2033, AI6 ~2x AI5 performance with MP around mid-2028.** The vision half, flagged as such: *"target process node reportedly 2nm, with a projected cost around $25B, though Tesla has never published detailed cost figures. The ramp from **100,000 wafer starts per month to eventually 1 million** is based on Elon's own statements. **One million wafers a month… is roughly 70% of TSMC's entire current monthly output. From a single facility.**"* Then, on 2026-04-07, **[[INTC]] joined Terafab with SpaceX, xAI and Tesla** — announced in a single line on X, *"No press release. No SEC filing."* His framing of why Intel reacted so hard: **Intel Foundry FY2025 revenue $17.8bn with a $10.3bn operating loss and EXTERNAL customer revenue of just $307mn — 1.7% of the total.** ➤ 🔴 **Held here because Mizuho's 08-24 note explicitly names "Terafab and multiple greenfield fabs" as the offset to the de-spec WFE headwind, while MS's 08-25 model puts only ~$2bn of SpaceX spend in 2028. ⚠️ ONE HOUSE IS USING TERAFAB AS A MATERIAL WFE OFFSET AND THE OTHER IS NOT MODELLING IT. This backfill supplies the fact base to adjudicate that: as of April 2026 there was a joining announcement and no disclosed wafer volume, capex or schedule. Logged as OPEN.**
- **BURN-IN AS A STRUCTURAL, NOT CYCLICAL, TEST LAYER.** **DAMNANG (Substack) — "Why AEHR Matters Right Now: The One Thing Both AI Processors and Silicon Photonics Chips Need", 2026-03-31**, written from the author's own test/yield/post-silicon-debug background. The argument: probe test is optimised for speed and electrical verification and *"is not designed to catch defects that look fine today but will fail under real-world operating stress over time"*; **latent defects (weak gate oxide, voids inside vias, electromigration-vulnerable metal) pass at room temperature and fail after hours-to-hundreds-of-hours**; and the cost of catching them late rises with package complexity, because **CoWoS, HBM stacking and chiplet integration mean a single bad die scraps an assembly with many good ones in it.** Silicon photonics adds a second driver, since optical die cannot be screened the same way. ➤ **The forward relevance is direct: this is the mechanism by which the SAME de-spec that removes hybrid-bonding steps ADDS burn-in and system-level-test steps. Aehr Test Systems has no wiki page — plain text. See [outros-asia.md](outros-asia.md) for the off-coverage treatment.**
- **WHY OPTICS BREAKS THE TEST FLOW, and who sells the fix.** **DAMNANG (Substack) — "The Real Bottleneck in the Optical Era: Test and Yield", 2026-03-21.** Five mechanisms: measurement targets double (voltage/current/jitter/timing PLUS OMA, ER, TDECQ, BER, eye diagrams, needing two toolsets and engineers who read both); **external test disappears once the optical path and coupling points move inside the package — "external equipment can't reach them anymore"**, forcing **on-chip loopback, on-chip monitoring/telemetry and end-to-end system-level validation**; and **alignment determines yield in a way electrical test cannot detect — "a device can pass all electrical tests and still fail in the field if optical coupling is bad."** Named beneficiaries (none with a wiki page — plain text): **Keysight** and **VIAVI** on optical test equipment; **Fabrinet** on precision alignment and assembly; **POET** on interposer-based integration that reduces the alignment burden. ➤ **The read-through for this page is that CPO converts test from a pass/fail gate into a design-time requirement, which raises test intensity per unit — the opposite sign to the hybrid-bonding step-count loss.** Full optical treatment on [optical-cpo.md](optical-cpo.md).
- **AND A DATED CASE STUDY IN HOW ATE STOCKS TRADE A GOOD PRINT.** **DAMNANG (Substack) — "Record Earnings, 8% Drop. Is the Market Reading Teradyne Right?", 2026-04-29.** **[[TER]] Q1 2026: revenue $1.282B (+87% y/y), non-GAAP EPS $2.56 vs $2.11 consensus (+21%), CEO Greg Smith saying ~70% of the quarter's revenue was tied to AI demand — and the stock fell ~5.4% in the session and ~8.7% after hours.** The cause was the shape, not the level: **Q2 guided to $1.15-1.25B, a midpoint of ~$1.2B — still >75% y/y growth but a SEQUENTIAL DECLINE off $1.282B**, into a stock up >400% over the prior year and nearly doubled YTD. A same-day WSJ report that OpenAI was missing revenue targets hit the whole AI-infra basket. ➤ **Filed as a base rate for the current setup, not as a forecast: MS's own 08-25 framework puts [[TER]] in the "in between" bucket at through-cycle averages, and this page's 08-01 Teradyne primary already carries memory book-to-bill >2x. The April episode is what happens when a test name at a stretched multiple guides a sequential decline off a record — worth remembering into the next print.**



## Recent signals (Aug 25, /wiki-ingest — MORGAN STANLEY'S **FIFTH** WFE RAISE OF THE YEAR) — 🔴🔴 **MS TAKES 2027 WFE TO $223bn (+37%) AND 2028 TO $254bn (+14%) AND SAYS THE DEBATE HAS CHANGED SHAPE: "FROM PACE OF GROWTH (WFE) TO END-DEMAND (COMPUTE/GW)". BUT SAMSUNG IR, THE SAME DAY, SAYS ITS OWN CAPEX IS GOING INTO SHELLS RATHER THAN EQUIPMENT — WHICH LANDS DIRECTLY ON THE DRAM LEG DOING MOST OF THE WORK IN THE RAISE.**

**Source: Morgan Stanley · Shane Brett, Lee Simpson, Nigel van Putten, Charlie Chan, Suzune Tamura, Joseph Moore +5, "Semiconductor Capital Equipment: 3Q'26 WFE update: 2026/27 Bull Case is the Base Case" (21pp) and the companion "Thoughts on stocks" (6pp), both 2026-08-25. Industry View In-Line.**

- 🔴 **THE REVISION, and it is the fifth this year.** **2026: $155bn (+32%) → $163bn (+39%). 2027: $201bn (+31%) → $223bn (+37%). 2028: $227bn (+13%) → $254bn (+14%).** *"Since Dec 1st we have raised our 2026 WFE forecast by **26%** and our 2027 forecast by **54%**."* **Largest revision is DRAM and Leading Logic — "unprecedented DRAM pull-ins into 2026, driving a $17bn (47%) increase in our DRAM WFE forecast."**
- **What materialised out of the old bull case:** **[[INTC]] spending $20bn+**, **~500kwpm of DRAM greenfield capacity**, and **a restart in NAND greenfield investment**. Quarterly, MS now models the industry hitting a **$200bn annualised run rate by DecQ 2026** — up from **~$125bn just four quarters earlier (4Q25)**.
- 🔴 **THE SUPPLY CONSEQUENCE, which is where the debate moves next:** **DRAM bit supply growth reaching 38% in 2027 (vs 24% in 2025)**, and **2nm capacity at 335kwpm/500kwpm in 2027/28 vs 77kwpm in 2025.** *"We have underestimated both the strength of demand and the pace of supply additions… We're not bearish; we're pleasantly surprised."*
- ⚠️ **THE DECELERATION IS IN THE BASE CASE, NOT A BEAR CASE:** growth falls from **+37% in 2027 to +14% in 2028**, and *"**WFE would need to exceed $300bn in 2028** to sustain the current growth rate."* MS's cross-check: **$100bn+ of industry growth 2025→2027 "more closely aligns with the compute (GW) deployment we're seeing (approx. 30GW of WFE vs 2027/28 GW of 29/36)."** And note MS's **2027 DRAM WFE of $70bn still sits BELOW what the SPE companies themselves imply ($75bn+)** — i.e. the raise is still conservative against the tool vendors' own commentary.
- 🔴🔴 **THE COUNTERWEIGHT, from the largest single DRAM spender, on the same day.** [[SAMSUNG]] IR told a BofA-hosted group meeting that *"**aggressive capex is focused mainly on SHELL FAB / INFRASTRUCTURE CONSTRUCTION RATHER THAN EQUIPMENT**"* and that *"memory wafer capacity expansion is **mainly for HBM (minimal for conventional DRAM and NAND)**"* (BofA / Simon Woo, 2026-08-25). **BofA's own overlay goes further: Samsung's "more DISCIPLINED SPE capex (particularly on conventional DRAM and NAND)" is read as a POSITIVE for memory peers via reduced price competition.** ➤ **This does not falsify the MS raise — shells precede tools, and MS's 500kwpm greenfield is a 2027 statement — but it is the sharpest available test of the DRAM leg's TIMING, and it comes from the company rather than from a channel check. Hold the two side by side rather than netting them.**
- **STOCK VIEWS (MS, "Thoughts on stocks"), and the framework is a valuation taxonomy rather than a call:** **(1) "2027 is the peak" — [[AEIS]], MKS, ONTO:** trading below through-cycle averages and toward the low end of historical ranges. **(2) "Need 2028" — LAM ([[LRCX]]), [[AMAT]], KLA ([[KLAC]]):** trading toward the high end of or above their 8-year ranges, i.e. cycle longevity and/or GM% expansion partly embedded. **(3) In between — [[TER]], NVMI, CAMT:** at through-cycle averages, with an added debate on structural growth in **advanced packaging and test** plus potential share shifts. 🔴 **"Within our OWs, we have a STRONG PREFERENCE for AEIS and MKS over LAM and KLA"** — in a **~$230bn+ CY27 overshoot, MS sees AEIS earning ~$20 and MKS ~$21, both then at ~13x.**
- **SemiAnalysis raised its own 2026-28 WFE forecast the same evening** *"to factor in stronger demand and accelerating supply signaled by 2Q26 earnings"* (@SemiAnalysis_, corpus, 2026-08-25) — ⚠️ **thread teaser only, no figures published in the corpus post; the underlying note was not read.**

**🔴 THE OTHER SIDE OF THE SAME DAY — THE DE-SPEC BEAR LANDS SQUARELY ON THE BACK END.** *"Separately, SemiAnalysis talking about [[NVDA]] Memory de-spec again which is making investors more bearish on **backend players ([[BESI]], CAMT, ONTO, [[TER]])** — rationale is that **if you don't scale to 16-high, you don't need Hybrid bonding and it's also less process-control intensive**"* (MS TMT desk · Thomas Wigg, 2026-08-25 — *sales commentary, NOT research*). ⚠️ **A transmission mechanism, not a forecast — nobody has cut a back-end number on it.** **The direct rebuttal is BofA's Hot Chips read:** *"memory (not compute) is now the key constraint… **migration to new architectures INCREASES WFE intensity. TSV formation, hybrid bonding, process control favorable for semicaps**"* (BofA / Vivek Arya, 2026-08-25). ➤ **Same tool steps, opposite sign, same day. The arbitrating variable is stack height: 16-hi needs hybrid bonding, 8-hi does not. That makes the Rubin-Ultra configuration decision — due within ~1-1.5 months per Jefferies — a direct back-end catalyst, not just a memory one.**

**SUPPLY-CHAIN CONSTRAINTS NAMED AT THE JPM ASIA TECH TOUR (JPM · Gokul Hariharan et al., 2026-08-25 — 37 companies, 115 investors):** *"Bottlenecks are now shifting to **clean-room capacity** and, soon, to **lengthening equipment delivery lead times**."* **Advanced-packaging equipment is the sharpest: CoW tool lead times UP TO 12 MONTHS**, as traditional OSATs and [[TSM]] compete for the same tool set. **Major Asian OSATs raised 2026 capex to record highs, up 80%+ y/y**; brownfield conversion still runs **6-9 months** vs **~2 years** greenfield, so *"OSAT capacity tightness [extends] at least into 2027/28."* On raw wafers, **GlobalWafers reports equipment lead times as long as 18 months** (JPM · Jimmy Huang, 2026-08-24). **China localisation datapoint: NAURA 2Q26 revenue Rmb9.84bn (−5% q/q, +24% y/y) MISSED on component-supply-driven delivery delays, GM −1.5pp to 39.3%, but contract liabilities +27% q/q to Rmb5.32bn and JPM/MS supply-chain checks put FY26 order intake above Rmb70bn, +>30% y/y** (MS · Charlie Chan, OW, PT Rmb818, 2026-08-25).

## Recent signals (Aug 24, /wiki-ingest 21h — JEFFERIES CHINA SPE JULY DATA) — 🔴🔴 **THE BASIS PUZZLE THIS PAGE FLAGGED THIS MORNING IS NOW ARITHMETICALLY CLOSED: A THIRD TRACKER PUBLISHES THE FULL CATEGORY SPLIT, AND BERNSTEIN'S "$3.9bn TOTAL WFE" = FRONT-END ($3,444mn) + INSPECTION & TESTING ($489mn) = $3,933mn, WHILE BofA'S "$5.1bn" IS THE ALL-IN IMPORT LINE ($5,012mn). THE TWO NUMBERS WERE NEVER IN CONFLICT.**

**Source: Jefferies · Masahiro Nakanomyo (Japan Technology), "China SPE Import Data – July 2026", dated 2026-08-25 JST / received 2026-08-24 — with Edison Lee, Jacky He, Nick Cheng, Blayne Curtis (Jefferies LLC) and Janardan Menon (Jefferies International) on the distribution.**

- 🔴 **THE FULL JULY SPLIT, WHICH IS WHAT CLOSES THE BASIS QUESTION.** Total China SPE imports **US$5,012mn in July (−0% y/y, +12% m/m)**, decomposed as **front-end processing US$3,444mn (+3% y/y) · inspection & testing US$489mn (+20%) · packaging US$178mn (+41%) · parts US$556mn (+2%)**.
  - **Front-end + inspection = $3,933mn ≈ Bernstein's "$3.9bn total WFE".** **All-in = $5,012mn ≈ BofA's "$5.1bn".** ➜ **This page's 08-24 AM note said "Bernstein's 'total WFE' IS BofA's FRONT-END line." That was directionally right and is now superseded by the exact identity: Bernstein's WFE basis is FRONT-END PLUS INSPECTION & TESTING, EXCLUDING packaging and parts. Use that mapping when reconciling the two trackers going forward — see Changelog.**
- **The trend, which is the actual news: the China decline has stopped.** *"Following steady declines (YoY) since **November 2025**, the July result was **roughly FLAT again** compared to the previous year, **just as in June**."* Jan-Jul cumulative **US$25,934mn (−8% y/y)** against **2025 full-year US$51,739mn (+3% y/y — the third straight annual rise from 2022's US$35,483mn)**. Jefferies' own words: *"**While they dropped 8% in Jan-Jul 2026, we are seeing SIGNS OF RECOVERY.**"* ➜ **Two consecutive flat y/y months after eight months of decline is the first turn in this series in nine months.**
- 🔴 **THE COUNTRY SPLIT CONTAINS THE MOST INTERESTING SINGLE LINE ON THE PAGE — AND IT IS ABOUT [[ASML]].** *"Imports from the **Netherlands (including ASML) were US$834mn (+7% y/y) in July, increasing in a THIRD STRAIGHT MONTH**, and US$3,735mn (−11% y/y) in Jan-Jul… **While imports were previously declining in 2026, they have EXPANDED AGAIN FROM MAY.**"* ➜ **Three consecutive months of y/y growth in Dutch tool imports into China, off a base that was down 11% for the year. That is a China-litho datapoint sourced from customs rather than from a channel check.**
- **The rest of the country table, with the US the clear loser and Singapore the clear beneficiary:** **US US$454mn (−11% y/y)** in July, *"slipping again (just as in June) after May had the first rise in eight months"*, and **US$2,243mn (−26% y/y) Jan-Jul**. **Japan US$1,304mn (+7%)** in July but **US$6,340mn (−13%) Jan-Jul**. **Singapore US$1,067mn (+8%)** in July and **US$5,521mn (+13%) Jan-Jul — "It ranks SECOND after Japan."** **Germany US$83mn (−10%)**, **US$507mn (−35%) Jan-Jul**.
- 🔴 **JEFFERIES NAMES THE RE-ROUTING EXPLICITLY, WHICH MATTERS BECAUSE IT MEANS THE "US EXPORTS TO CHINA" SERIES UNDERSTATES US-VENDOR EXPOSURE: _"We think there is also an impact from **US-based SPE companies SHIFTING EXPORTS TO CHINA, EVEN FOR PRODUCTS NOT AFFECTED BY THE REGULATIONS, FROM THE US TO SINGAPORE, MALAYSIA, AND OTHER LOCATIONS**"_ and _"We think US-based manufacturers have been INCREASING PRODUCTION IN SINGAPORE."_** ➜ **Singapore at +13% YTD and #2 overall, against the US at −26% YTD, is the same tools with a different port of departure. Do not read the US line as a demand signal for [[AMAT]] / [[LRCX]] / [[KLAC]] China revenue.**
- **Cross-check against Japan's own export statistics, offered by the author as a consistency test:** SPE exports Japan→China were **¥1,910.7bn (−12%) in 2025** and **¥784.5bn (−15%) in Jan-Jun 2026** — *"These data are reporting a downturn as well (just as Chinese import data)."* ⚠️ **Jefferies lists the reasons the two series never match exactly: recognition timing, recorded value content, USD-vs-JPY conversion, and the fact that China's import statistics INCLUDE PARTS while Japan's exports do not. Log the basis, not just the number — that is now twice in one day on this theme.**
- **Demand-side cross-read arriving the same evening (see [[SKHYNIX]] / [[SAMSUNG]]):** Korean makers are *"actively carry[ing] out **EQUIPMENT INVESTMENTS** for their NAND mass-production lines **in China** through next year"* — [[SAMSUNG]] converting Xi'an X2 to **V9 NAND (~280 layers)** and [[SKHYNIX]] adding **~30k wpm** (@jukan05, 2026-08-24, second-hand relay). ➜ **If that capex is real, part of the "recovery" in China's import series from here is KOREAN-OWNED capacity inside China, not domestic Chinese buyers — a distinction this series cannot make and every WFE model needs.**

- **Driver split, and it is a two-clock argument:** **DRAM and leading-edge foundry near term; NAND and Logic/Other medium term, "including Terafab"** — on **node transitions, migration to HBM4, and capacity additions.** Preferred Buy-rated: **[[AMAT]], [[LRCX]], [[ASML]], [[TOKYOELEC]], ASMI, [[BESI]], Lasertec, Ebara.** ⚠️ **[[KLAC]] is absent from that list** — consistent with a capacity/node-transition-led forecast being a wafer-fab-equipment story more than a process-control story.

On 2026-08-20 AMAT management explicitly disowned the ~$330-340bn 2028 WFE figure the market had backed out of its 2x-capacity plan — *"not signaling a formal WFE forecast… better understood as a CEILING of what AMAT wants to be capable of serving by 2028."* GS's new $281bn sits ~15-18% BELOW that ceiling. **Source: **ASM International** management (one speaker referred to as "Paul"), interviewed by a **BofA** analyst at a **BofA-hosted conference call, 2026-08-20** — [call](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_08_20_asm_baml_call_20_ago_26.html). ⚠️⚠️ **CRITICAL LABELLING, AND THE INGEST ROUTER GOT IT WRONG: this file was routed to [[AMAT]] on three keyword hits. "ASM" here is **ASM INTERNATIONAL** — Amsterdam-listed (ASM NA), ALD and epitaxy — NOT Applied Materials. The two are different companies and the read-throughs are almost opposite. ASM International has NO wiki page, so it is plain text throughout this block and is never linked.** ⚠️ **AND WEIGH IT CORRECTLY: this is COMPANY MANAGEMENT COMMENTARY at a broker-hosted event. Every figure below is the vendor's own, and it is talking its own book. BofA is the HOST, not the author of these views.**

- 🔴 **THE 1.4nm CLAIM IS A *SHARE* CLAIM, WHICH IS DIFFERENT FROM THE INTENSITY CLAIM THIS PAGE ALREADY CARRIES: the 2nm→1.4nm transition carries **MORE ALD CONTENT**, and ASM says it has **INCREASED ITS SHARE OF WALLET AT 1.4nm** — *"our market share in 1.4 nanometer is STRONGER THAN 2 NANOMETER."*** The reason given is a location, and it is the load-bearing detail: **the ALD intensity increase at 1.4nm is in the FRONT-END-OF-LINE**, in the transistor/performance layers where ASM is strongest. **The view was first given at BofA's TMT conference in June and is reconfirmed here.** ✅ **The INTENSITY half corroborates what is already on this page — BofA's own ASM International deep-dive (Didier Scemama, 07-31: *"semiconductor scaling is increasingly driven by MATERIALS INNOVATION rather than equipment advances alone"*, with GAA/HBM/AP raising deposition intensity and ALD adoption) and the non-litho-step work below (intensity ~32% at 2nm, *"holding or rising at 1.4nm with HNA"*). 🔴 WHAT IS NET-NEW IS THE SHARE DIRECTION AND ITS LOCATION: rising intensity is a market-SIZE statement; a rising SHARE OF WALLET at the new node, concentrated in FEOL, is a COMPETITIVE statement — and it is the first one this page has at 1.4nm from the vendor itself.**
- 🔴🔴 **AND HERE IS THE ITEM THAT TURNS A DISPUTE ALREADY ON THIS PAGE FROM ANONYMOUS INTO ATTRIBUTABLE — A NEW PRODUCT AREA IN **METAL** DEPOSITION: *"for the FIRST TIME ASM really has developed films, ALD films, in **METAL DEPOSITION**"* — winning layers at 1.4nm **OUTSIDE the transistor area, in the MIDDLE-END-OF-LINE** — and also playing in **BACKSIDE POWER DELIVERY with MOLYBDENUM film ALD.*** 🔴🔴 **READ IT AGAINST THE Jul-31 [[LRCX]] BULLET FURTHER DOWN THIS PAGE, WHICH LOGGED THE OTHER SIDE OF EXACTLY THIS ARGUMENT WITHOUT A NAME: Lam *"firmly rejects a COMPETITOR'S claim of ALD MOLY WINS IN FOUNDRY LOGIC,"* holding that NAND adopts Moly FIRST and that Lam is the largest Moly player in NAND production. ASM is now on the record making precisely that claim — moly/metal ALD wins at 1.4nm in MEOL, i.e. IN FOUNDRY LOGIC. So the unnamed competitor in that dispute is CONSISTENT WITH ASM International, and the disagreement is now TWO-SIDED AND ATTRIBUTED rather than one vendor's rebuttal of an unidentified rival. NEITHER SIDE IS ADOPTED — both are vendor claims about their own wins, and the falsifier is a customer disclosure or a layer-level teardown, not a third vendor's opinion.** ⚠️ **Two further reasons to flag rather than bank this: (1) METAL DEPOSITION IS INCUMBENT TERRITORY — [[LRCX]], [[AMAT]] and TEL ([[TOKYOELEC]]) — so this is a competitive-ENCROACHMENT datapoint on the deposition names, and it is the first on this page sourced to the encroacher; (2) the BACKSIDE-POWER leg lands on a pool this page has already sized from the incumbent's own mouth — Lam put **backside power delivery at ~$1BN PER 100K WAFERS** (07-31) — so an ASM moly-ALD claim inside BSPDN is a share claim against a quantified pool, which makes it checkable.**

It converts leading-edge WFE demand from a competitive-roadmap question ("who needs the fastest chip") into a POWER-BUDGET question ("who is constrained by megawatts") — which is the constraint this wiki tracks on [ai-datacenter-power.md](ai-datacenter-power.md) and [800v-dc-power.md](800v-dc-power.md), and the mirror image of the CLEANROOM-SPACE cap AMAT names in the block directly below. The MEMORY legs — current DRAM strength being CAPACITY not MIX, the two-to-three-year payoff on this year's DRAM ALD/epitaxy qualifications, the 4F² slip (*"some of it in 2028 but it's not going to be broad-based"*), ALD/AP intensity rising even at 6F², and the *"leading-edge memory"*-rather-than-high-bandwidth reframing — are on [hbm-memory.md](hbm-memory.md).**

_(Source: **JPM · Harlan Sur**, "Applied Materials: Takeaways From Recent Meeting With Investors" — virtual NDR for APAC investors with **Mike Sullivan, Corporate VP & Head of IR**, **2026-08-20**. AMAT price $496.17 on 08-19, JPM Reit OW.)_

It also reconciles the Aug 15 callback number (Systems ~$7bn → ~$14bn exiting 2028) with the 2x framing — the 2x is what AMAT can BUILD, cleanroom is what customers can INSTALL.**
- 🔴 **VISIBILITY IMPROVED VERSUS THREE MONTHS AGO, AND IT IS NOW QUANTIFIED: AMAT is "incrementally more confident in the 2027 WFE outlook than it was 13 weeks ago," rolling customer order forecasts extend into 1H28, some customers are discussing plans into 2030, and backlog/customer-forecast visibility is 8+ QUARTERS.**
- 🔴🔴 **THE DEMAND MIX, AND THE FAB-STARTS DATAPOINT IS THE MOST ACTIONABLE LINE IN THE NOTE: "AI-related markets (leading-edge foundry/logic, DRAM, and advanced packaging) are driving roughly 80% of WFE growth this year," with a similar mix in 2027 — and "the VAST MAJORITY of the 10 new fab projects it saw at customers over the most recent quarter were DRAM-RELATED," with packaging capacity additions also meaningful.** ➜ **DRAM, not logic, is now the marginal driver of new fab starts — read straight into [[hbm-memory]], [[MU]], [[SAMSUNG]], [[SKHYNIX]]. Any 2027-28 DRAM bit-supply model built off announced greenfield fabs is structurally too low.**
- 🔴 **VALUE-BASED PRICING GETS ITS MECHANISM AND ITS TIMING WINDOW, AND THIS IS A GROSS-MARGIN LEVER SEPARATE FROM MIX: AMAT "generally does not have much room to renegotiate once fabs start high-volume production, [but] CAPACITY EXPANSIONS create opportunities to revisit pricing across the tool stack (OLD AND NEW tools)."** The rationale management gives: **"AMAT's strategy is no longer to simply innovate how chips are made, but to innovate their customers' fab economics and share in the economics that they help create."** ➜ **So a capex up-cycle is also a repricing window on the INSTALLED base. This page has tracked "allocation starting to behave like price" (Aug 12) — this is the vendor stating the pricing mechanism directly, and it means semicap GM upside in 2027 is not purely a mix/volume story.**
- **Process-intensity read from the logic side, for the 2nm transition:** Intel is expanding its TSMC 2nm footprint (**Nova Lake on N2P alongside Intel 18A; Razor Lake rumoured on the high-performance N2X**), and the trade-press framing notes that **FinFET → nanosheet raises precision requirements on deposition, etch, clean and CMP, with CMP polish steps and high-end consumable value rising as 2nm wafer-flatness and defect-control thresholds tighten** (@dnystedt / @QQ_Timmy, 2026-08-20). ➜ **Steps-per-wafer and consumable intensity rising at N2 is the quiet part of the WFE-per-wafer story — relevant to [[LRCX]], [[AMAT]] and the CMP consumables chain.**

## Recent signals (Aug 13, companion) — 🔴 **RELIABILITY / BURN-IN TEST GETS ITS OWN INITIATION, AND WITH IT THE SHARPEST PUBLISHED CLAIM YET THAT [[TER]] AND [[ADVANTEST]] ARE *NOT* IN THE FASTEST-GROWING TEST INSERTION**

**Jefferies initiates **Aehr Test Systems (AEHR)** at Buy, PT $175** (px $129.23, **+35% upside**, $4.3bn market cap), on **20x EV/CY29E revenue of $328M discounted back to CY28 at 15%** (Jefferies · Kevin Garrigan, with Blayne Curtis / Crawford Clarke / Andrew Buller, "Feel the Burn; Initiate at Buy", 2026-08-13) [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Feel_the_Burn_Initiate_at_Buy.html). ⚠️ **Off-coverage, no wiki page — carried here for the THEME, not the name.** The theme-relevant claim is that **reliability/burn-in test intensity is becoming a structurally rising share of semicap spend**, and it is one of the few test arguments on this page that does not run through the two ATE incumbents.

- **🔴 THE COMPETITIVE CLAIM, VERBATIM, AND IT IS THE LINE TO ARGUE WITH: *"While **Teradyne and Advantest dominate functional and parametric testing, neither has established a meaningful position in high-power wafer-level burn-in**. Historically, wafer-level burn-in has been viewed as a niche market relative to traditional test, limiting investment from larger competitors."*** The capability matrix in the note is blunter still — under **"High power test"** it lists **Advantest, Teradyne** with the reason **"Focused on ATE, not thermal stress,"** and under **wafer-level burn-in at high power/multi-wafer** it lists competitor **"None."** ⚠️ **This is a SELL-SIDE ASSERTION IN AN INITIATION ON THE CHALLENGER — i.e. the most conflicted possible source for it, and neither [[TER]] nor [[ADVANTEST]] was asked. Log it as a falsifiable claim to test on the next two ATE calls, not as a finding.** ➜ **The transferable point for this page stands independent of who wins: burn-in and thermal-stress screening is a DIFFERENT insertion from functional/parametric ATE, and the WFE/test models on this page do not separate the two.** (Jefferies, 2026-08-13)
- **🔴 THE STRUCTURAL DRIVER — TEST INTENSITY RISES BECAUSE THE COST OF A LATE FAILURE RISES FASTER THAN THE COST OF PREVENTING IT.** *"As AI processors move to larger packages, higher socket power, and advanced packaging like CoWoS, each successive manufacturing step places more value behind the original die, **turning reliability screening into a gating step rather than a discretionary quality check**."* **The compound-yield arithmetic is the mechanism: eight HBM stacks plus a logic die, each at 95% individual yield, compound to ~63% at module level if components are not screened before assembly; at a 1% per-die infant-mortality rate a 32-die module suffers ~32% yield loss if burn-in is deferred to the completed module.** *"Standard functional probe cannot detect these latent defects. They surface only under sustained thermal and electrical stress."* **The unit economics: screen at the wafer level for roughly $1 per die rather than scrap a finished module worth thousands** (Jefferies, 2026-08-13). ➜ **Read directly against [cowos-packaging](cowos-packaging.md): every incremental HBM stack and every extra chiplet in the package raises the economic return on an upstream reliability screen. Rising die count is a test-intensity driver, not just a packaging-capacity driver.**
- **The power axis, which is the physical reason the incumbents' tools do not simply extend into it: *"Next generation accelerators are pulling several thousand amps and over 3,000W per wafer under stress."*** Referenced platform capability: **up to 3.5kW and 4,000A per wafer at wafer level, and 2,000W or more per packaged device at package level**, against advanced packages such as **AMD's Instinct MI455X combining multiple compute chiplets with up to 12 HBM4 stacks on a single substrate** — *"delivering and dissipating that power under stress, in parallel, is a hard physical problem that standard functional probe was never designed to solve"* (Jefferies, 2026-08-13). ⚠️ **Note the note's own framing of what it competes against: *"AEHR's primary competition is NOT another wafer-level burn-in system, but ALTERNATIVE RELIABILITY METHODOLOGIES, including package-level burn-in, system-level test, statistical sampling, and internally developed solutions."* ➜ The real bear is not a competitor entering — it is customers deciding SAMPLING is good enough.**
- **🔴 THE SIZING, WHICH IS THE ONLY NUMBER ON THIS PAGE FOR THE BURN-IN INSERTION: SAM of ~$626M in 2026 → $2.0B by 2030, a 34% CAGR, *"led by AI processors and silicon photonics, with additional opportunities across power semiconductors, memory and other high-reliability applications"*** (Jefferies, 2026-08-13). ⚠️ **Single-source, and it is the SAM of one vendor's addressable insertion rather than an industry burn-in TAM — do NOT add it to the WFE marks in `## WFE — sizing & sequential growth`. It belongs alongside the CPO-test TAM already on this page ([[TER]]'s $300-700M by 2028 with 2027 guided to the low end, 2026-07-29), and the two are the same order of magnitude, which is itself the useful check: the emerging test insertions are hundreds of millions to low single-digit billions, not a WFE-scale line item.**
- **THE MIX TRANSFORMATION, because it is the cleanest available evidence that power-semi test capital has rotated into AI: *"AEHR has undergone one of the semi equipment sector's most significant mix transformations, with **SiC >90% of FY24 revenue and now AI >90% of FY26 revenue**."* Top-customer concentration falls from **~79% of revenue in FY23 to an estimated <25% in FY27E**, with revenue now spanning *"AI accelerators, package-level burn-in, SiPho, networking, and power semis"*** (Jefferies, 2026-08-13). ➜ **A two-year, ~180-degree end-market rotation inside one equipment supplier. Anyone modelling power-semi capex recovery off this vendor's revenue line is now reading an AI number.**
- **✅ THE SECOND LEG IS THE POWER-SEMI CYCLE, AND IT IS THE PART THAT MATTERS TO THE NON-AI HALF OF THIS PAGE: *"a cyclical recovery in SiC provides a 2nd leg of growth as the EV inventory correction clears, evidenced by ~$8M of new SiC WaferPak orders and lead-customer 200mm transitions. **GaN broadens exposure beyond auto into data center power**. Importantly, the thesis no longer requires a return to peak SiC spend, as stabilization plus GaN adoption is incremental upside as customers resume qualification and capacity investment"*** (Jefferies, 2026-08-13). ⚠️ **An order datapoint ($8M) plus a 200mm transition is a stabilisation signal, not a recovery — but it is a dated, checkable one, and it arrives from a supplier with no reason to flatter SiC given the mix above. Cross-ref [800v-dc-power](800v-dc-power.md) on the GaN-into-datacenter-power leg.**
- **The model, and ⚠️ A BASIS FLAG THAT MATTERS BEFORE ANY OF IT IS QUOTED.** Jefferies runs **TWO series and the tearsheet labels are misleading**: the front-page table headed *"FY (Jun)"* is in fact the **CALENDAR** series — **CY25A $53.2M → CY26E $93.3M → CY27E $169.1M → CY28E $244.7M**, pro-forma EPS **$0.43 / $1.05 / $1.87** for CY26/27/28E, gross margin **42.5% / 44.3% / 46.3%** — while the prose runs the **FISCAL** series: revenue **$59M FY25 → $50M FY26 → $140.4M FY27E → $206.0M FY28E (~52% 3-yr CAGR)**, gross margin **38.5% FY26 → 43.3% FY27E → 45.3% FY28E**, non-GAAP EPS **$0.15 FY25 → $1.45 FY28E**. **The PT is struck off a third figure: CY29E revenue of $328M.** ➜ **The two series are NOT inconsistent — they are calendar vs fiscal — but they are trivially mixable and the tearsheet's own header invites the error. Anyone lifting these numbers must state the basis.** Coverage context: **FY27 guided by management to $130-150M (+160-200% y/y) against $100.6M of effective backlog entering the year ($80.6M reported at year-end plus ~$20M booked immediately after), i.e. FY27 ~71% covered**, and **$60.7M of bookings in Q4 FY26 alone**; balance sheet **~$116.5M cash, no debt** post the FY26 equity raise (Jefferies, 2026-08-13).
- **⚠️ WHAT THE MODEL DELIBERATELY EXCLUDES, because it is where the theme read-through would come from: *"our model excludes **memory, CPO**, conversion of the AI benchmark customer, and a return to peak SiC."*** On optics specifically, the note says silicon photonics is *"transitioning from optionality to production"* — the lead SiPho customer *"has moved beyond qualification and continues to place production orders, including fully automated FOX-XP systems integrated with WaferPak AutoAligners and factory automation,"* and *"a major networking OEM has purchased multiple FOX-XP and FOX-NP systems"* — but *"**co-packaged optics remains outside our forecast horizon**"* (Jefferies, 2026-08-13). ➜ **Consistent with, and slightly more cautious than, the CPO-test TAM [[TER]] guided to the low end for 2027 (08-01, above). Two independent test vendors now put broad CPO test revenue beyond the current forecast window.**
- **The moat argument, for whoever wants to test the incumbents' ability to enter: qualification history (*"months or years"* of reliability validation before deployment, and switching vendors *"can require requalification of the entire process"*), the proprietary WaferPak contactor (*"more than 50,000 electrical contacts… simultaneously across an entire wafer"*, customised per device design, so the consumable is qualified alongside the process), and consumables targeted by management at **30-50% of revenue over time**. Jefferies: *"even if larger vendors choose to enter the market, they would still need to replicate AEHR's qualification history, WaferPak ecosystem, and installed production base"* (2026-08-13). ⚠️ **Same conflict caveat as above; and the note's own risk list concedes customer concentration, qualification/conversion timing, order lumpiness on a small base, and package-level margin dilution as Sonoma scales.**

## Recent signals (Aug 12) — 🔴 BofA CONFIRMS CY27 WFE AT $190BN AND DOES *NOT* FOLLOW BERNSTEIN UP; A LITHO-FREE SUPPLIER PRINT CROSS-CHECKS THE NUMBER FROM UNDERNEATH; AND ALLOCATION STARTS BEHAVING LIKE PRICE

> **Backfill note (/run-inbox 2026-08-12): the four sources below are dated 07-27 → 08-06 and reached the wiki late. They are logged at their real dates. The BofA CY27 mark in particular is a CONFIRMATION of a number this page already carried under Josh Evans's name (06-29), not a new revision — which is precisely why it matters against Bernstein's 08-10/11 move.**

**(1) 🔴 CY2027 WFE — BofA CONFIRMS $190bn ON A CLIENT CALL, IT DID NOT RAISE.** The EU semis speaker, verbatim: *"currently, we're at **190 billion for 2027 WFE**."* Same deck Josh Evans published 06-29 (FY27 $190bn from $183bn, FY28 $250bn, FY30 $292bn). ➜ **Scoring note for this page's CY27 ladder: BofA $190bn = [[KLAC]]'s ">$190bn" = [[TOKYOELEC]]'s ">$190bn", while Bernstein moved to $204bn on 08-10/11. BofA has NOT followed Bernstein up.** Three houses and two vendors now cluster at ~$190bn against one sell-side mark ~$14bn above it — the same gap the Aug 11 section flagged, now with a second house declining to close it. (BofA EU Semis client call, 2026-07-27) [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_27_bofa_eu_semis_pitch.html)

**(2) ★ A SUPPLIER-TIER CROSS-CHECK ON CY27, AND IT IS THE CLEANEST ONE AVAILABLE BECAUSE IT EXCLUDES LITHO.** VAT Group — *"essentially a monopoly on the vacuum valves… **they're not exposed to litho**"* — printed **orders CHF 500m vs CHF 370m consensus (+35%)**, driven by *"the Western semicaps ramping, especially for **DEP AND ETCH**"* plus China localisation. **VAT's un-updated 2027 guide of CHF 1.4bn at the midpoint was built on a $125bn WFE assumption**; on BofA's $190bn the same ratio puts **VAT 2027 revenue ~20% above consensus**. ⚠️ **The linear extrapolation is the analyst's shortcut, not a company guide — but the ORDER print is hard, and it is a dep/etch-weighted read that cannot be explained by [[ASML]].** ➜ **Reads directly to [[AMAT]] and [[LRCX]] rather than to the litho line.** (BofA EU Semis client call, 2026-07-27)

**(3) ⚠️ THE CHINA-LOCALISATION LEG IS TWO-SIDED, STATED BY A SUPPLIER THAT SELLS TO BOTH SIDES.** *"The Chinese semicaps also all use VAT valves… and the **Chinese semicaps continue to take market share domestically in China away from the Western semicaps**."* ➜ **VAT is levered to WFE regardless of who wins China; [[AMAT]]/[[LRCX]]/[[KLAC]] are not. A WFE aggregate that is right in dollars can still be wrong for the US majors' share of it.** Named and sized: **ACM Research, AMEC and NAURA** build tools *"**not as robust** but **fairly comparable, probably two to three years behind**"* the Western dep/etch/inspection vendors — and those Western names *"**trade in line to above the multiple of ASML**."* **The argument: if the China discount is real it belongs in the US semicap multiples, not in ASML's.** (BofA · Didier Scemama, 2026-07-27)

**(4) 🔴 ALLOCATION IS BECOMING A PRICING MECHANISM — [[LRCX]] MANAGEMENT, ON THE RECORD.** A very large unnamed new entrant (*"could call them a **space company, a hyperscaler**"*) is *"so desperate to get systems… **practically offering to pay more price**"*, with other customers *"upset now because they're **missing/losing slots**."* Management: *"we only have a **finite number of manufacturing slots**… luckily in the current year it's not that big. **We'll see how 2027 plays out**."* ➜ **Cross-read [[SPCX]]/Terafab: this is the MECHANISM by which Terafab hits a vendor's P&L before it shows up in the WFE aggregate — and it arrives as price and slot displacement, not as an order line.** (LRCX management hosted by UBS · Timothy Arcuri, 2026-08-06) [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_08_06_lcrx_post_earnings_call.html)

**(5) ⚠️ [[KLAC]] DOES NOT GET PAID TWICE FOR [[INTC]] — which directly contests the "KLA is the way to play Intel" framing the Aug 11 section left standing.** KLA *"massively out-shipped WFE"* at Intel in 2026 on pulled-forward tools, and itself assumes Intel is only *"flat to up"* in 2027. Arcuri's arithmetic: **Intel capex ~$20bn (WFE $12-13bn) 2026 → ~$30bn (WFE ~$23bn) 2027, with KLA revenue ~FLAT.** ➜ **Read against item (3) of the Aug 11 section, where ASML/KLAC/LRCX all rallied on the upsized Intel raise: on this telling KLA has already been paid.** (UBS · Timothy Arcuri, 2026-07-29) [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_29_arcuri_klac_stx_ter_recap.html)

**(6b) TWO MORE HOUSE MARKS ON THE SAME LADDER, AND THEY BRACKET BofA's $190bn.** **BofA · Vivek Arya (INTC client call, 2026-07-24): 2026 WFE $145-150bn**, and on Intel specifically *"the **tools part** used to grow at 25%, then they have **now bumped it to 40%**"* — *"if there is one positive takeaway OTHER THAN INTEL from this call, **it's semi caps**."* Two levers named: **acceleration** (*"one of the few places where one can make the case for ACCELERATION… in most other places you see deceleration off very high levels"*) and **pricing** — *"**ASML also suggested almost 10% pricing, 10 to 15%**, implied in their numbers."* ⚠️ **His own standing caveat, retained: *"empirically what we see is that they are QUITE CORRELATED TO JUST VOLATILITY IN MEMORY STOCKS"* — i.e. the fundamental case and the trade are different things.** **UBS · Timothy Arcuri (2026-08-05): WFE/capex 2027 *"up AT LEAST 30%, PROBABLY MORE LIKE 35%."*** ➜ **Off a ~$150bn 2026 base that is ~$195-203bn for 2027 — i.e. UBS lands between BofA's $190bn and Bernstein's $204bn, which tightens the CY27 cluster rather than widening it.** [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_24_vivek_follow_up_on_intc.html)

**(6c) 🔴 THE BINDING INDUSTRY CONSTRAINT IS NAMED, AND IT IS NEITHER CAPITAL NOR TOOLS — it is SUBSTRATE and MEMORY.** [[INTC]] IR, on the record: *"it's **not just capital** that's constraining growth for the industry; **both SUBSTRATE and MEMORY are also constraints**."* **Prepays are now standard practice** — taken from customers and passed straight through to suppliers, alongside letters of credit. ➜ **Directly relevant to how this page reads a WFE number: if substrate and memory gate the build, WFE dollars can be spent on schedule while OUTPUT slips — which is the mechanism behind the "tools shipped ≠ wafers out" gap the page has flagged on Intel.** (Intel IR · John Pitzer, hosted by BofA · Vivek Arya, 2026-07-28) [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_28_intc_ir_post_2q_bofa.html)

**(6d) KOREAN CAPEX MARKS FROM THE ONLY HOUSE THAT RE-CUT AFTER THE PRINT, and the direction is the surprise.** **[[SKHYNIX]] W51.2tn (26F) / W71.7tn (27F)** on an accrual basis — **cut ~10% at both years** from Mirae's own 07-14 W56.9/79.7tn **even as the company guided capex UP ~80%**. **[[SAMSUNG]] W72.1tn (26F) / W96.6tn (27F)**, DS W66.3tn → W90.1tn, with the 26F split **DRAM W30.4tn / NAND W18.2tn / Foundry-LSI W17.7tn**. ⚠️ **Basis trap: Samsung capex appears as W78.6tn on the same note's shareholder-return table — a different basis. Do not mix.** ➜ **A house cutting its Korean capex forecast into an up-guided capex cycle is the kind of divergence this page exists to catch: either Mirae is modelling slippage the company is not conceding, or the accrual/cash basis is doing the work.** (Mirae Asset Securities · Young-gun Kim, 2026-08-11) [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/000660_KS_SK_Hynix_20260811.html)

**(6) PRICING ADVANCED-PACKAGING TOOLS INTO [[TSM]] HAS A HARD STRUCTURAL GATE, and it generalises past KLA.** The cost-of-ownership hurdle is fixed against the prior generation **when a node is introduced** — *"you can't come back in the middle. The **only opportunity with TSMC to raise price is when there is a new node**."* KLA *"really pushed back"* on the investor assumption that advanced-packaging mix lifts gross margin. ➜ **A caution on every "AP mix = margin accretion" bridge on this page.** (UBS · Timothy Arcuri, 2026-07-29)

## Recent signals (Aug 10, PM) — 🔴🔴 BERNSTEIN LIFTS TWO-YEAR WFE GROWTH TO **+75%** AND THE UPGRADE IS BROAD-BASED, NOT MEMORY-ONLY; UBS ARRIVES AT THE SAME 2027-28 NUMBERS BOTTOM-UP FROM A SUPPLIER PRINT; AND ADVANCED PACKAGING SPLITS THE INSPECTION NAMES

**🔴🔴 (1) THE BIGGEST WFE REVISION THIS PAGE HAS CARRIED (Bernstein · David Dai, "Equip this! Raising WFE growth to 75% in two years", 2026-08-10).** Bernstein *"significantly lifts"* its forecasts:

| Year | New WFE | Prior | New y/y | Prior y/y |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2026 | **$154bn** | $148bn | **+26.3%** | +21.4% |
| 2027 | **$204bn** | $175bn | **+32.6%** | +18.2% |
| 2028 | **$259bn** | $198bn | **+27.0%** | +13.0% |

🔴 **THE 2026 ROW WAS CORRECTED ON 2026-08-11 FROM THE PRIMARY PDF (/run-inbox) — old values ~~$148bn new / $141bn prior~~, retained here per the thesis-drift rule.** ⚠️ **The error was the NOTE'S OWN, not the desk's: Bernstein's front-page summary reads "we significantly lift our 2026 WFE to $148bn (+26.3% YoY) from 141bn (+21.4%)," but its Exhibit 1 and its DETAILS section both read **$154bn (new) vs $148bn (prior)** — "for CY26, we lift the WFE growth estimate to +26% YoY (+21% prior)… and now forecast $154bn ($148bn prior) WFE spending." **The arithmetic is decisive: Bernstein's CY2025 base is $122bn, so ×1.263 = $154bn and ×1.214 = $148bn, whereas the front-page dollar pair implies two mutually inconsistent 2025 bases (117 and 116). The 2027 and 2028 rows are internally consistent off $154bn (×1.326 = $204bn; ×1.27 = $259bn) and were not touched.** **Materiality for this page: at $148bn the 2026 mark EQUALS the prior estimate and reads as "no change to the current year" — a reading that had already propagated into a conclusion on [[LRCX]]'s page ("if anything slightly BELOW LRCX's own low-$150bn mark"), which inverts at $154bn. This is the second relay-versus-primary correction of the quarter and the first where the fault lay with the note rather than the relay.** ⚠️ **A SECOND INTERNAL INCONSISTENCY IN THE SAME NOTE, flagged and NOT resolved in either direction: a DETAILS bullet states logic/foundry WFE is "largely unchanged at $89bn (Exhibit 7), and see +8% YoY (+7% prior)" — but Exhibit 7's own caption and the front-page growth rates give **2027/2028 logic-foundry WFE of $104bn / $124bn (from $89bn / $93bn), i.e. +20% / +19% off an ~$87bn 2026 base.** The $89bn in that bullet is the PRIOR 2027 value quoted as if current. This page carries **$104bn / $124bn**.

**The full application split from the primary's Exhibits 6-7, which the relay only gave as growth rates:** memory WFE **$89bn in 2027E (from $75bn prior)**, of which **DRAM $69bn / $96bn (2027E/2028E, from $57bn / $71bn)** and **NAND $20bn / $29bn (from $18bn / $23bn)**; logic-foundry **$104bn / $124bn (from $89bn / $93bn)**. Regionally: **China WFE $57bn / $73bn / $101bn for 2026-28E** (2026 cut by $1.1bn, 2027 raised $6.3bn, 2028 raised $24.0bn), **ex-China +36% in 2026 and +37% in 2027** (2026 revised up $7bn — largest delta $3.4bn foundry then $2.6bn DRAM; 2027 up $22.8bn — DRAM $10.7bn then foundry/logic $9.8bn; 2028 up $36.8bn — DRAM $18bn, foundry/logic $15bn).

- **The two-year (2027-28) compounding is +75%**, which Bernstein says *"strengthens our WFE upcycle call."*
- **🔴 THE COMPOSITION IS THE NEWS, NOT THE HEADLINE.** *"The uplift is **across all applications**, but slightly more from **non-China memory**, and **China foundry/logic**."* By application, 2027/2028 growth: **DRAM +53% / +37%; NAND +58% / +42%; Logic-Foundry +20% / +19%.**
- **⚠️ Two things to hold against it.** (a) **NAND is being forecast to grow FASTER than DRAM in both years** — which is the same equipment-layer signal Jefferies used on 08-07 to build the *bearish* LTA argument (*"[[LRCX]]'s record NAND revenue indicates **someone's adding capacity in NAND, likely the Koreans, earlier than previously anticipated**"*). **The identical observation is being read as an upcycle by one house and as the seed of the next down-leg by another.** That contradiction is now explicit and unresolved on this wiki. (b) A **+27% year in 2028** is directly opposed to the standing **"2028: DRAM −8% / NAND −14%"** down-leg call already on [hbm-memory.md](hbm-memory.md) — the two cannot both be right; the equipment layer leads the device layer by 2-4 quarters, so a 2028 WFE up-year and a 2028 device down-year are only reconcilable if the capacity being bought in 2028 ships into 2029.

**🔴 (2) UBS GETS TO THE SAME PLACE FROM A COMPLETELY DIFFERENT DIRECTION — READ OFF A SUPPLIER'S PRINT (UBS · Timothy Arcuri, [[AMAT]] preview, 2026-08-10; Buy, PT $705).** *"**[[LRCX]]'s print made it increasingly clear that WFE will be run-rating ~$170-175B in CQ4**, and that **WFE is likely to exceed (and maybe materially) $200B next year**, with **$250B becoming an increasingly pedestrian number for C2028**."* ⚠️ **This matters because it is METHODOLOGICALLY INDEPENDENT of Bernstein's top-down build and lands within a few billion of it at both 2027 ($200B+ vs $204bn) and 2028 ($250B vs $259bn).** Two independent methods converging is the strongest evidence this page has had that the ~$200bn 2027 number is not a single house's enthusiasm.
- **The share consequence UBS attaches:** AMAT is *"on track to **gain >250bps of WFE share this year** (PF for EPG resegmentation) — **together with LRCX, the big share gainers this year**"*, with AMAT's full-year semi-systems guide expected to go from *"up more than 30% y/y"* to *"up 35-40% y/y (or more)"*; UBSe **+38% y/y**. Cross-ref [../AMAT.md](../AMAT.md), [../LRCX.md](../LRCX.md).

**(3) ADVANCED PACKAGING IS NOW SPLITTING THE INSPECTION/METROLOGY NAMES, AND CAMTEK IS THE TEST CASE — TWO HOUSES, OPPOSITE CONCLUSIONS, SAME DAY.**
- **Jefferies · Blayne Curtis — BUY, PT $200 (+29%), 2026-08-10.** *"Commentary around C27 echoed the strong AP trends that peers saw with improving visibility tied to **$600M of total orders YTD**"* against **revenue of $255M**. June revenue **$133.2M (+10% q/q, vs St. $130.2M)**, **AP ~75% of revenue (+17% q/q, $99.9M vs St. $93.0M)**, HPC mix ~55%. **September guided to $159M (+19% q/q) vs St. $148.8M**, with **2H26 revenue growth raised to >30% h/h (from >25%) and AP growing +45% h/h**; **GM to 52.5-53% exiting C26, OM 30-32%.** Order strength *"broad-based across **HBM4, CoWoS and CoWoS-like packaging, fan-out, hybrid bonding and large OSAT capacity expansions**,"* with **silicon photonics and compound semiconductors "beginning to contribute meaningfully."** **Eagle G5 + Hawk now ~half of systems revenue**, driving higher ASPs and opening **hybrid bonding** process steps. Jefferies raises C26/C27 revenue **+4%/+7%** and EPS **+5%/+9%**, sitting **+19%/+35% on revenue and +12%/+45% on EPS vs consensus**. **Investor breakfast at SEMICON West, October 14.**
- **⚠️ Morgan Stanley · Shane Brett / Joseph Moore — EQUAL-WEIGHT, PT $167 → $165, same day.** The bear is **relative growth, not absolute**: *"**CAMT's Advanced Packaging revenue is UNDER-growing peers (CAMT 39% vs ONTO 80%, LAM/KLA 70%, NVMI 50%)**, but total revenue underperformance is magnified by **revenue ex-advanced-packaging declining 8% y/y**."* 🔴 **The diagnosis is the useful part and it is a CUSTOMER-MIX point: "CAMT's under-growth in CoWoS/Chiplets comes from its LOWER EXPOSURE TO TSMC, which should set the company up for a better 2027 once CoWoS builds BROADEN TO ASE, SPIL, AMKR."** MS raises CY27 to **$788mn / $5.01** (from $731mn / $4.82) and **cuts the target multiple two turns, 28x → 26x**. Valuation is the whole disagreement: **CAMT at 31x 2027 MSe, "the high end of our US SPE coverage with TER & KLA, and significantly above our coverage average of 26x."**
- ⚠️ **The read-through worth carrying off this pair: within AP, the winners are the names levered to the OSAT/HBM broadening, not the TSMC-CoWoS concentration.** Corroborating datapoint the same day: **AllRing Tech 2Q "strong beat" with a NEW US ADVANCED PACKAGING ORDER SECURED** (Morgan Stanley / Tiffany Yeh, OW, 2026-08-10), and **ACM Research** *"on track to a WFE platform via ECP and AP; cleaning recovery from 2H26"* (Morgan Stanley / Jimmy Huang, OW, 2026-08-10).
