If this situation persists, then we may see a **widening gap between winners and losers** — winners will be those able to **secure enough parts to churn out equipment**, losers being those with a **growing backlog of orders that they struggle to turn into revenue**"* (Culpium / Tim Culpan, CASCI July report, 2026-08-10). ⚠️ Same failure mode this page logged on Aug 1 at the **sub-system tier** — now visible one layer up, in the equipment makers' own ability to convert backlog. ## Recent signals (Aug 7) — 🔴 THE DEMAND INFLECTION GETS A NUMBER (SEMI +52% Y/Y IN SEPQ VS WFE ~+30%), A GREENFIELD COMMITMENT LANDS WITH A 2031 WFE DATE, AND THE EQUIPMENT LAYER BECOMES THE LEADING INDICATOR FOR A *NAND OVERSUPPLY* CALL

**🔴 (1) THE SUBSYSTEM LAYER IS OUTGROWING WFE BY MORE THAN 20 POINTS, AND THE VISIBILITY EXTENDS THROUGH CY27 (BofA · Michael Mani / Vivek Arya on MKS Instruments, Buy, ⚠️ PO $500 → $450, 2026-08-07).** The header is "DEMAND INFLECTION HAS ARRIVED; WFE/PCB MOMENTUM DURABLE THROUGH CY27," and the numbers behind it are the most quantified statement of the semicap upcycle this page has carried:
- **"SEMI MARKET IS ACCELERATING TO +52% Y/Y GROWTH IN SEPQ, ON PACE TO OUTGROW CY26 WFE GROWTH OF ~30% Y/Y BY OVER 20PPTS."** ⚠️ **Note the two different aggregates — "semi market" here is MKSI's own semiconductor segment, not industry WFE. The 20pt gap IS the outgrowth claim, and BofA attributes it to "HIGHER EXPOSURE TO ETCH/DEP INTENSIVE SPENDING ACROSS DRAM AND LOGIC," with "the prospect of GREENFIELD NAND WFE" as unmodelled upside.**
- **"E&P ON TRACK TO GROW >30% Y/Y IN CY26 AS CHEMISTRY EQUIPMENT DEMAND IS AT UNPRECEDENTED STRENGTH LED BY AI-RELATED PCB DEMAND WITH VISIBILITY EXTENDING THROUGH CY27. High-GM chemistry sales tend to follow equipment, of which UP TO 15-20% IS ATTRIBUTABLE TO AI, UP FROM JUST 5% IN CY24."** 🔴 **A 3-4x increase in the AI share of an industrial chemistry business in two years is the cleanest read-through on this page from AI datacenter build into the PCB/substrate layer.**
- **Capacity being added, and the sizing of the environment it is being built for: "new MALAYSIA facility can support Semi market demand INSIDE A $200-$250BN WFE ENVIRONMENT, and CAPACITY IS BEING DOUBLED AT THE MSD CHEMISTRY EQUIPMENT FACTORY IN CHINA."** ⚠️ **A $200-250bn WFE reference frame is well above the ~$130-145bn range this page carries for CY26 — it is a CAPACITY-DESIGN envelope, not a forecast. Do not read it as a WFE estimate.**
- **Estimates: CY26/27/28E sales +8%/+12%/+12%, pf-EPS +15%/+16%/+12%; "+30% EPS CAGR CY25-28E and POSSIBLE EPS POWER OF $20 BY CY28E."** ⚠️ **The PO CUT to $450 is a multiple/method change, not a downgrade: "we lower our PO to $450 (from $500) NOW ON 17x CY28E PE (VS 22x CY28E EV/EBITDA PRIOR) DUE TO RECENT SECTOR MULTIPLE CONTRACTION," still "4x below the peer median CY28E PE of 21x."**
- **Risks BofA names itself: "CONSUMER REMAINS A LARGE PART OF E&P and chemistry seasonality could be a drag on growth in Q4 while demand for NON-AI MARKETS REMAINS MIXED"; and GM pressure as equipment/VSD becomes a larger mix.**

**🔴 (2) THE EQUIPMENT LAYER IS NOW BEING USED TO CALL A *NAND OVERSUPPLY*, WHICH IS THE OPPOSITE OF HOW IT HAS BEEN USED ON THIS PAGE ALL CYCLE (Jefferies Semis EPS Debrief · Blayne Curtis, 2026-08-07).** **"[[LRCX]]'s strong performance and RECORD NAND REVENUE indicate that 'SOMEONE'S ADDING CAPACITY' IN NAND, LIKELY THE KOREANS, EARLIER THAN PREVIOUSLY ANTICIPATED. THIS RAISES CONCERNS ABOUT POTENTIAL FUTURE OVERSUPPLY."** ⚠️ **Same inference route this page has run since July — read the capacity decision off the equipment order, not the company guide — but pointing the other way for the first time. For semicap names this is unambiguously GOOD news (revenue now); for [[SNDK]] / [[KIOXIA]] / [[WDC]] it is the bear. Logged on both.** Jefferies also flags **"ADVANCED PACKAGING AND SELECT EQUIPMENT STRONG — companies like ONTO INNOVATION are benefiting from advanced packaging trends and [[TSM]]'S OUTSOURCING."**

**(3) The greenfield commitments landing this week are all LONG-DATED, which is why they do not relieve the 2026-28 shortage.** **[[SKHYNIX]]: ~US$38-39BN for Korean expansion — "YONGIN Y2 WILL BE THE SECOND OF FOUR FABS planned at the Yongin cluster… BREAK GROUND IN JULY NEXT YEAR, TARGETING JUNE 2029 FOR OPENING THE FIRST CLEANROOM," budget also covering M17 (DRAM and NAND). JPM's Jay Kwon: in line, "consistent with the plan to FILL ITS ENTIRE WSPM AT Y1 BY 2030 (NEAR 400K WSPM). They need to start investing now for the NEXT LEG OF WFE INSTALLATION (AT Y2) IN 2031."** ✅ **This is the numerical form of [[LRCX]] CFO Doug Bettinger's constraint framing logged on 08-06 — "GREENFIELD CLEAN ROOM CONSTRUCTION TAKES ~3 YEARS REGARDLESS OF GEOGRAPHY, WHILE BROWNFIELD EXPANSIONS ARE GATED BY EQUIPMENT LEAD TIMES." Ground-break 2027 → cleanroom 2029 → WFE 2031 is a 4-year fuse. It is ALSO why the WFE cycle has a long tail even if memory pricing rolls over first.**
- **Bernstein's desk reads the combination as the setup: "ELON'S TERAFAB AMBITIONS + SK HYNIX BUILD DISCLOSURE CREATES A POSITIVE SETUP FOR SEMICAPS IN 2H/2027 (+[[LRCX]]/[[AMAT]]/[[KLAC]])"** (Bernstein TMT · Tyler Seidman, 2026-08-07). ⚠️ **The "terafab" reference is to [[SPCX]]'s reported US$16.8bn in-house chip ambition (DIGITIMES, "Inside Terafab: Musk's US$16.8B bet to turn AI chip shortages into a homegrown supply chain," 2026-08-07) — logged as a press relay, NOT company-confirmed, and NOT sized into any WFE forecast on this page.**

**(4) The demand backdrop under all of it, from the SIA data: "Global total semiconductor sales reached a RECORD $147.8B IN JUNE, up 10.8% M/M… CQ2 total semiconductor sales grew a remarkable 36.6% Q/Q, ~2,880BPS ABOVE the 10-yr seasonal average," with full-year 2026E at $1,637.9B (+119.6% y/y) and 2027E $2,397.5B (+46.4%)** (UBS · Timothy Arcuri, 2026-08-07). ⚠️ **Memory is ~2/3 of the growth (Bernstein WSTS: memory +379% y/y in June, "memory pricing alone driving ~2/3 of overall industry growth since the beginning of the year"), so a WFE forecast anchored on total semi revenue growth is anchored on DRAM/NAND ASP. Ex-memory sales were still +38% y/y — use that as the broad-based check.**

## Recent signals (Aug 4) — the China-DUV selloff gets its buy-side verdict: real phase transition, ~25 years behind, "the market massively overreacts"

**The July semicap flush's proximate catalyst, graded by Gavin Baker** (Invest Like the Best podcast · Atreides, ~2026-08-04; [transcript archived](../../_equity_calls/Overall/2026-08-04_GavinBaker_ILTB-AI-repricing.md)). ⚠️ *Informal podcast commentary.* **"Then China has a DUV machine and — everybody's in these baskets — this causes a huge selloff in semicap equipment."** His two-sided read, kept whole: **(a) it IS a phase transition — "they didn't have it before and now they allegedly do… like you've gone from liquid to solid. If you're China this is really important to you… they're really smart, they work brutally hard"; (b) it is ~25 YEARS BEHIND and the gap is learning-by-doing — "a DUV machine is like a propeller plane, EUV is like a jet turbine… you can't accelerate the doing. You can't teleport into the future — you have to go through those learning cycles."** Market-impact verdict: **"if this ever hits [[ASML]]'s orders, maybe it hits in 5 YEARS, and the market has forgotten about it and re-worried multiple times along the way. Did the market overreact? Probably. Is it significant? Yes."** The EUV-smuggling report is flagged as unverified noise ("what a feat of espionage — those things are giant"). Decoupling framing: "self-reinforcing on each side… they're not going to stop. Neither are we." Full row on the [[ASML]] page; cross-ref [china-export](china-export.md), [[SMIC]].

## Recent signals (Aug 2) — ★ SERVICE revenue is outgrowing EQUIPMENT by 13pp across the big four, which is not what a cycle top looks like — set against an explicit "this rolls over in 12-15 months" call

**★ ① THE SERVICE DATAPOINT, AND IT IS THE BEST LEADING INDICATOR THIS THEME HAS ACQUIRED IN MONTHS (@SKundojjala, semicap analyst, 2026-08-02; relayed in Chinese by @QQ_Timmy the same day).** **"While the market is focused on WFE, SERVICE REVENUE at the semicaps is accelerating faster. COMBINED SERVICE REVENUE ACROSS [[ASML]], LAM, TEL AND KLA GREW 33% YEAR ON YEAR IN THE JUNE QUARTER AGAINST 20% FOR EQUIPMENT."**
- **THE ANALYTICAL CLAIM, which is why it belongs at the top of this page rather than in a company note: "SERVICE BUSINESS IS THE REAL-TIME SIGNAL as its inflection is reflected in INDUSTRY UTILIZATION RATES AHEAD OF EQUIPMENT. Service business help logic/memory customers STAY ON TOP OF YIELDS AND SQUEEZE MORE WAFER CAPACITY, which is valuable in current situation."** ⚠️ **Service attaches to the INSTALLED BASE and scales with how hard it is being run; equipment attaches to NEW capacity decisions. Service outgrowing equipment by 13pp says utilisation is still tightening — which is the opposite of the inventory-build/oversupply pattern that precedes a WFE downcycle. It is also a direct empirical confirmation of the structural argument [[KLAC]] made on its 07-28 call: process control as the tool for extracting output from existing capacity rather than adding new capacity.**
- **THE LONG-TERM TARGETS, RANKED, with the revision direction flagged: "Long-term service targets now run from LAM's 10%+ and ASM's 12%+ up to KLA's 13-15% and [[AMAT]]'s MID-TEENS, with THE TWO MOST RECENT UPDATES BOTH REVISED UPWARD." Near-term guides across the group: 12-17%.** ⚠️ **Note every vendor is guiding service growth ABOVE its own equipment CAGR, and that [[LRCX]] carries the LOWEST published target in the peer group (10%+) — worth testing whether that is conservatism or a genuinely lower service attach on its installed base. Closing point for income-oriented holders: "service business FUNDS DIVIDENDS at major semicaps," i.e. it is the least cyclical dollar in these models.**

**★ ② THE OPPOSING CALL, PUBLISHED THE SAME MORNING, AND IT IS SPECIFIC ENOUGH TO BE SCORED (Fubon/Jefferies desk via William Beavington's Global SEMIS Daily, "Semis Downside? — ASMI", 2026-08-02).** ⚠️ **"Is There One Stock In Semis That We Don't Want To Own? YES THERE IS — ASMI — CYCLICAL DE-RATING EXPECTED TO CONTINUE… The greater probability is that the inherently deep cyclicality of the semiconductor sector is likely to bring in SOME WEAKNESS OVER THE NEXT 12-15 MONTHS."** **The named triggers: "weakness in DDR5 SPOT AND CONTRACT PRICING IN 2027, hyperscaler capex spend coming IN-LINE OR SLIGHTLY BELOW expectations, signs of INVENTORY BUILD, or DELAYS IN FAB CONSTRUCTION."**
- ⚠️ **THE MECHANISM IS THE MOST IMPORTANT PARAGRAPH ON THIS THEME THIS WEEK BECAUSE IT DOES NOT REQUIRE DEMAND TO FALL: "in an environment of SEMICONDUCTOR SHORTAGE AND EXCESSIVE ORDERING, EVEN IF HYPERSCALER CAPEX GROWTH STOPS EXCEEDING EXPECTATIONS AND COMES IN AT PREDICTED STRONG GROWTH RATES, IT COULD SEND THE SEMICONDUCTOR INDUSTRY INTO OVERSUPPLY AND A DOWNCYCLE WITHIN A FEW QUARTERS." Expanded elsewhere in the same note: "semiconductor upcycles usually PEAK WHEN DEMAND STOPS ACCELERATING and starts to be IN LINE OR BELOW expectations, EVEN IF DEMAND IS STILL SHOWING STRONG GROWTH. In a capacity-constrained environment, customers are INVARIABLY ORDERING EXCESSIVELY. This does not cause an inventory build AS LONG AS DEMAND CONSISTENTLY EXCEEDS EXPECTATIONS, but it COULD RESULT IN AN INVENTORY BUILD OVER 3-4 QUARTERS IF DEMAND STARTS TO BE IN LINE with expected growth rates. Semiconductor valuations tend to be VERY FORWARD-LOOKING and usually PREEMPTIVELY DERATE in such situations."**
- **This is the sharpest statement of the second-derivative argument this wiki has logged, and it is the reason the group can de-rate on capex RAISES: the FY26 hyperscaler capex raise across all four was only ~5% and "MOST OF IT ESTIMATED TO BE COMING FROM MEMORY COST INCREASES," putting the four "heading for a YoY CAPEX GROWTH OF AROUND 109% THIS YEAR." Chris Wood's framing in the same note: "the market is CLEARLY NOW RESPONDING NEGATIVELY TO INCREASES IN CAPEX WHICH IS A WARNING SIGNAL," while noting the unwind took semis "close to their 200-day moving averages" and that "if this is JUST A TECHNICAL FLUSHING OUT of leveraged positions… this should be more or less the LIMIT OF THE CORRECTION."**
- ⚠️ **HOW TO HOLD ① AND ② TOGETHER — they are not the same disagreement restated. ① is an observation about CURRENT installed-base utilisation (service +33% y/y, tightening). ② is a forecast about the SECOND DERIVATIVE of order growth 12-15 months out. Both can be right: utilisation peaks late in a cycle, and a de-rating typically precedes the utilisation peak. The falsifiable discriminator ② itself supplies is DDR5 spot/contract pricing in 2027 and evidence of inventory build; ① supplies service growth decelerating toward the equipment rate. Track both, and note GS's macro nowcast now shows "a MODERATE SLOWDOWN in [AI] investment (albeit from a very robust pace) IN JUNE AND JULY" (GS Global Economics, 2026-08-02) — the first hard-data deceleration signal on this wiki, covering exactly the months of the equity unwind.**

**③ Two supply-side items, filed briefly.** ⚠️ **CHINA DOMESTIC LITHO SUBSTITUTION MOVES FROM CLAIM TO ORDER: "ASML's share price came under pressure early in the week, following PRESS REPORTS THAT CXMT, [[SMIC]] AND HUA HONG HAD PLACED ORDERS FOR 25 IMMERSION LITHOGRAPHY SYSTEMS FROM A DOMESTIC SUPPLIER, putting pressure on the OVERALL SEMICAP EQUIPMENT SECTOR." Small in units, large in signal — the three largest domestic buyers ordering simultaneously is the leading indicator for the mature/mid-node China revenue line. Press-sourced, no vendor named, no ASPs, NOT ADOPTED. And the funding side just loosened: CXMT's IPO printed +500% ON DAY ONE. See [themes/china-export](china-export.md), [[ASML]], [[SMIC]].** **A FAB INCIDENT IS PUSHING OUT REVENUE ACROSS TWO SUPPLY CHAINS: Logitech guided FQ2 below consensus because "IN LATE JUNE ONE OF ITS SUPPLIERS OF SEMICONDUCTORS EXPERIENCED A SERIOUS INCIDENT IN THEIR MANUFACTURING FACILITY THAT LED TO ITS CLOSURE. THE FACILITY REMAINS CLOSED TODAY… and a DEFINITIVE DATE FOR THE OPENING OF THE FACILITY HAS NOT YET BEEN PROVIDED." The desk's inference: "we are not sure which fab this is referring to, but we note that STM HAD A RECENT INCIDENT AT ONE OF THEIR FABS THAT IS RESULTING IN OVER $100M OF REVENUE BEING PUSHED OUT FROM Q3 TO Q4."** ⚠️ **Worth tracking as a re-tooling/repair demand item and as a reminder that in a shortage a single fab outage propagates to end-customer guidance within one quarter.**

## Recent signals (Aug 1, PRIMARY SOURCES) — the two earnings calls this theme had been reading second-hand, and both change a number the page carries
_⚠️ Recovery ingest: the [[KLAC]] (2026-07-28) and [[TER]] (2026-07-29) call transcripts were archived unrouted on 07-31 and restored on 08-01. Everything below is from the Bloomberg FINAL TRANSCRIPTs, not from desk first-takes._
- **★ THE CY27 WFE NUMBER NOW HAS A MANAGEMENT-SOURCED CONSENSUS MARK, AND IT COMES WITH A GROWTH-RATE DOWNGRADE NOBODY REPORTED.** KLA's CFO on the CY27 view: ***"there's a consensus view out there… somewhere in and around the $190 billion range, there are some upside or more bullish views of that. There's not too many bearish views."*** ⚠️ **But the second half of the sentence is the one that matters and it did not make a single first-take: *"more or less, you're in and around $190 billion or so, translates into a mid-20 tech growth rate, WHICH IS SIMILAR TO THE GROWTH RATE OF 2026."*** **KLA has been the one large-cap publicly committed to "CY27 growth > CY26" since April — the stake JPM repeatedly cited as differentiating it. On the call that became "similar to."** Against the page's other CY27 marks (MS $191B, UBS ~$145B, JEF $165bn, Bernstein's implied), $190B is now the management-endorsed centre of gravity — but the *rate of change* is flat, not accelerating (KLA Corp Q4 FY26 call, 2026-07-28).
- **The $300B WFE question was asked directly, and the answer relocates the constraint to optical components on a 2029 planning horizon.** Jefferies asked KLA how long it takes to prepare for a $300B WFE market. Bren Higgins: KLA has spent *"the last few months… meeting with some of our critical suppliers and planning and doing **capacity agreements for our needs, we believe, in the 2029 and beyond timeframe**,"* and *"we can usually do whatever we need to from our own capacity point of view… really where the gauging item is for us… is **long lead time, because it can take 12 to 24 months to put new capacity in place for those types of components**"* — specifically **optical components**. ⚠️ **This is the same structural finding as the UCTT/Zeiss/sub-tier material below, arriving independently from a different OEM: the OEMs are not the constraint; their component suppliers are, on 12-24 month clocks.** Rick Wallace's cross-check is worth keeping: *"because of the overlap and the knowledge that suppliers have of each other, if you really did have a number like that, **we would see the signals**"* (KLA Corp Q4 FY26 call, 2026-07-28).
- **Lead times, quantified: ~12 months blended, 18-24 months on certain products, and *"all our conversations today with customers are about deliveries really in the second half of '27."*** Backlog/RPO re-disclosed at **~$12.5B**, *"grown pretty consistently in the last couple of quarters."* ⚠️ **This is the concrete rebuttal to the "semicap backlog has normalised, so the OEMs now depend on new orders like everyone else" bear framing (UBS's standing leg on KLAC) — a book with 2H27 delivery dates is not a normalised book** (KLA Corp Q4 FY26 call, 2026-07-28).
- **⚠️ PRICING POWER IS NOT UNIFORM ACROSS THE GROUP, AND THIS THEME HAS BEEN TREATING IT AS IF IT WERE.** The page carries "semicap pricing power is becoming increasingly apparent" as a sector conclusion (UBS · Ruple, from the LRCX call). **KLA's management explicitly declined to take it.** Asked by Jefferies why it cannot do what ASML is doing on EUV pricing — *"it seems like you're struggling to do that. Is there something unique?"* — Bren Higgins: ***"it's pretty hard to go back to your customers after you've taken orders and start to change prices on those orders,"*** with resets deferred to new-product introductions; Rick Wallace offers only *"conversations about… how KLA will help to capture some of that value."* **So the correct sector statement is: [[ASML]] and [[LRCX]] are monetising the cycle through price; [[KLAC]] is not, and says so** — which is precisely the UBS call (Arcuri, 2026-07-25) that KLAC is the least likely of the Big Three to pursue pricing, now confirmed by management. ⚠️ Note the customer-side corroboration on the same call: KLA cites *"one of the large foundry customers last week"* attributing part of a capex raise to **equipment prices going up** (KLA Corp Q4 FY26 call, 2026-07-28).
- **★ THE TEST LAYER NOW HAS ITS OWN INTENSITY RATIO, MANAGEMENT-SOURCED, AND IT IS RISING FASTER THAN WFE.** Teradyne quantified test equipment as a share of **total semi-capex: ~4% (2023) → 7% (2025) → 8% (first five months of 2026)** — *"kind of this rocket trajectory"* — expected to **settle at 7-9%, most likely 7-8%**. On **WFE approaching ~$250B by end of the decade**, that implies a **total ATE TAM of ≥$20B**. The structural claim: ***"after more than a decade of semi-cap equipment outgrowing the test TAM, the trend reversed in 2024, and test is now outpacing fab equipment."*** Underlying drivers given as **5-10% annual 300mm wafer growth** and **15-20% CAGRs in both transistor production and memory bits**. ⚠️ **This matches BofA's independently-derived "7-8% of WFE → $14bn+ ATE at $200bn WFE" (Arya, 2026-07-30) — two derivations, same band — but management alone supplies the caveat: the ratio *"could revert down to 6%, 7% or so"* in 2027, because WFE→ATE runs with a ~1-year lag (money changes hands a year before wafers emerge) plus ~16-week tester lead times.** ⚠️ **And note the denominator trap now live on this theme: TER's ratio is test ÷ semi-CAPEX (7-8%); UBS's long-standing ~1% figure is test ÷ semiconductor REVENUE. Both circulate; they are not interchangeable** (Teradyne Q2 CY26 call, 2026-07-29).
  - **➕ UPDATED 2026-08-03 — the ratio is now framed as a PLANNING RANGE against WFE, with the denominator ambiguity partly resolved and the mid-term narrowing quietly dropped** (UBS fireside — Teradyne post-2Q26 callback, w/ IR Amy McAndrews, 2026-08-03; ⚠️ machine transcript — ASR: `Arcuri`←"Mercuri"/"Al Curry", `ATE`←"ATTM"/"ATTE"). **Teradyne's IR, asked directly whether 8% is "the right number to use, back of the envelope" against WFE: ATE has *"kind of historically been 8%"*, *"got down below that in the past couple years"*, and has *"now snapped back to be something actually higher than eight here recently"* — specifically **8% for the first five months of the year** *"from the data we have."* The planning statement: *"in our models, we're kind of assuming that the ATE will be in the range of like **7 to 9% over the mid-term**, that's the range we're planning… this is a directional sort of metric, so it could move around year to year."* And the honest opener, worth keeping: ***"the real answer is we don't know."*** ⚠️ **Two changes versus the 07-29 call version above, both softening: (a) the analyst's question is explicitly framed against WFE (not total semi-capex), and IR answers in that frame without correcting it — so the 7-9% band is now in circulation on BOTH denominators, which makes the *interpretation* looser, not tighter; (b) IR does NOT repeat either the "most likely 7-8%" narrowing or the "could revert down to 6%, 7% or so in 2027" caveat Greg Smith gave nine days earlier. Carry the caveat from the CEO, not from IR.** **THE MECHANISM, stated in one line and reusable for this theme: *"more WFE means more wafers, more advanced packaging means more test per wafer"*** — i.e. test rides WFE twice, once through wafer volume and once through packaging complexity, which is the structural reason the test ratio rises rather than holds. **THE BRIDGE TO $20BN, and note whose numbers they are: the WFE inputs are the ANALYST's, not the company's.** Arcuri: *"we all see it's going to be **quite a bit more than $200 billion next year** and potentially **quite a bit more than 250 in 2028**… those numbers would suggest that your TAM in 2028 ought to be like, you know, **20 plus**."* IR neither confirms nor disputes the WFE figures — the company's only commitment is the 7-9% ratio. ⚠️ **Set that against the CY27 marks this section already carries: KLA's management-endorsed ~$190B consensus (with growth "similar to 2026"), MS $191B, JEF $165bn, UBS ~$145B. Arcuri's ">$200bn next year" sits ABOVE the management-endorsed centre of gravity, so the $20bn+ 2028 ATE TAM inherits that optimism — the ratio is the company's, the WFE is the bull's.** Finally, the venue is now dated: the callbacks after the print clustered on *"how quickly can the ATE TAM get to $20 billion and what do we look like when it's at that level"*, and TER says it **plans to frame exactly that at its Q4 (February) earnings call** — the moment the ≥$20B number either does or does not enter a company-published model. See [[TER]], [[ADVANTEST]]. [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_08_03_ter_ubs_3_ago_26.html)
- **Two demand-side marks that cut against the "buyside is already above management" framing this theme adopted after the KLAC print.** ① **Advanced packaging process control raised again**, to ~**$1.1B CY26, +>70% y/y** — and disclosed as being *"above our prior expectations of **high 50% growth**"*, with the **AP market itself re-marked from ~20% at the start of the year to a mid-to-high-30s growth rate**. ② **KLA is sizing supply for scenarios 10-20% ABOVE the ~$190B CY27 base**, and says legacy/mature customers are **not modelled at all** — *"potentially… an upside factor, although not a big one."* ⚠️ **Also logged as contested: KLA expects "some greenfield investment in flash" in 2027, which cuts against the carried JPM view that the first NAND greenfield does not arrive until CY28** (KLA Corp Q4 FY26 call, 2026-07-28).

## Recent signals (Aug 1) — ★ the binding constraint on 2026-27 WFE shipments is named, and it is the SUB-SYSTEM tier, not demand and not the OEMs
- **★ THE SENTENCE THAT REFRAMES THE WHOLE WFE DEBATE, and it appears inside a UCTT preview rather than an OEM note: "major SPE suppliers like [[LRCX]] and [[AMAT]] are set to GROW SHIPMENTS ~10% Q/Q FOR THE FORESEEABLE FUTURE, LIMITED LARGELY BY THE CAPABILITIES OF SUB-SYSTEM SUPPLIERS LIKE UCTT"** (UBS · Timothy Arcuri, via Robert Ruple "Tech Musings", 2026-08-01). ⚠️ **This theme has spent the last month arguing about the SIZE of the TAM — LRCX's low-$150Bs, UBS's $170-175bn CQ4 run-rate, MS's $160bn 2026 / "$220bn+ 2027", the buy-side's implied $250bn 2028, and management conceding a third manufacturing site would be needed above $250bn. Arcuri is now saying that for the next several quarters the number that gets SHIPPED is set by the tier BELOW the OEMs — gas and fluid delivery, frames, modules, weldments — regardless of where the TAM lands. The demand question and the revenue question have come apart.**
- **And it has a date. Arcuri's UCTT model: existing Malaysia capacity "FULLY UTILIZED BY C4Q26"; the company grows into its entire currently-available footprint (which "can sustain up to ~$4B") by C1H27, against CEO commentary that today's capacity "can really run at a $3 BILLION ANNUALIZED RUN RATE." With a typical 12-16 MONTH GREENFIELD BUILD-OUT, "management would effectively need to PULL THE TRIGGER RIGHT AROUND NOW to support continued growth."** ⚠️ **THE CHECK, and it is imminent: UCTT reports 08-03 AMC. If it does NOT announce greenfield capacity, the ~10% q/q shipment cadence at LRCX and AMAT has a hard ceiling arriving in mid-2027 that is invisible in every WFE-TAM forecast on this page. Arcuri makes the priority explicit: "the MOST IMPORTANT THING THIS EARNINGS SEASON will be SIGNS OF CAPACITY EXPANSION ACROSS THE SUPPLY CHAIN and at major OEMs like LRCX and AMAT themselves."**
- **How far behind the Street the sub-tier is modelled: UCTT C2026/27/28E EPS revised to $3.46 / $8.89 / $12.53 (from $3.55 / $8.45 / $11.12), PT $145 from $130, Buy — with "consensus estimates here are, we think, FAR BEHIND REALITY with Street ONLY AT ~$5 IN C2027," a ~78% gap.** ⚠️ **If Arcuri is right about the constraint, the sub-system tier is where the operating leverage and the pricing power actually sit in 2027, and it is the least-covered layer of the stack.**
- **THE SAME PATTERN, THREE MORE TIMES IN ONE WEEK — the 2026 tool cycle keeps resolving to a sub-tier's capacity rather than to end demand, and that is now a reusable principle rather than an anecdote:** ① **Zeiss opened its first new Oberkochen building four years after groundbreaking — the site that "CAPS [[ASML]]'S EUV SCANNER OUTPUT"** (Tom's Hardware, via Susquehanna · Hosseini weekly, 2026-08-01). ② **[[AIXA]]: demand outstrips supply with CUSTOMERS RESERVING MOCVD SLOTS INTO NEXT YEAR, i.e. optical-component makers are short DEPOSITION TOOLS, not substrate** (@crux_capital_, 2026-07-31 — see [[AXTI]]). ③ **300mm wafers heading into shortage with SPOT ABOVE LTA pricing** (DB's Siltronic upgrade, already logged 07-31). **Four distinct layers — reticle optics, deposition tools, sub-systems, raw wafers — all binding at once. The corollary for the TAM debate: WFE revenue can under-run WFE demand for several quarters without demand ever weakening, which is a very different risk profile than the cyclical one the multiple usually prices.**
- **Consumables corroborate that the cycle itself is inflecting, in case the constraint framing reads as bearish: "commentary across the supply chain has INFLECTED this quarter" — Pall's Applied Filtration +10% q/q, Shin-Etsu Electronics Materials +16% q/q, plus continued capex/WFE revisions. [[ENTG]]-equivalent read: management now refers to a potential "DOUBLING" of the business, which is informing its footprint consolidation; UBS models 2026 revenue growth "fairly gradual" (+8% y/y) before ACCELERATING in 2028 to ~$4.5B/yr at ~52% gross margin (~500bps above current) and >$7.50 EPS. Entegris also "highlighted [[INTC]] FOUNDRY AS AN INCREMENTAL OPPORTUNITY, noting INTC's TAM AND ECOSYSTEM ARE COMPARABLE IN SCALE TO THOSE OF [[TSM]]"** — a materials supplier, paid per wafer start, treating Intel Foundry as a TSMC-scale second ecosystem. ⚠️ **Supplier positioning statement with an obvious interest, not an audited forecast — but it is a claim about expected WAFER STARTS, which is the metric the Intel-external-customer debate has lacked.**
- **Pricing power, restated by a second house from the LRCX call: "SEMICAP PRICING POWER IS BECOMING INCREASINGLY APPARENT based on what we heard from LRCX this week, where they struck a bullish tone calling for GMs IN THE MID-50s RANGE and OPM IN THE MID-40% OVER THE NEXT SEVERAL YEARS — consistent with Tim Arcuri's view that PRICING LEVERAGE IS STILL UNDERAPPRECIATED for semicaps"** (UBS · Ruple, 2026-08-01). **On the China DUV scare, UBS's own position is now on the record: Francois Bouvignies "concludes that [[ASML]] WILL REMAIN THE PREFERRED SUPPLIER given its significant performance advantage" and is a BUYER ON WEAKNESS.** Technical corroboration of why unit counts mislead, from the corpus: **@lithos_graphein — immersion plus self-aligned quadruple patterning (SAQP) "can actually ACHIEVE BETTER RESOLUTION THAN LOW-NA EUV," with the real limiter being SEED PATTERN LINE-WIDTH VARIATION producing PITCH WALKING; even with the extra deposition/etch steps, EUV's cost keeps the approach competitive** (2026-07-31). ⚠️ **That is a double-edged datapoint and should be carried as such: it supports the "China can do more with DUV than tool counts imply" bear case for ASML, while also explaining why the deposition/etch intensity that [[LRCX]] and [[AMAT]] sell RISES when litho is substituted away — the WFE dollars move between vendors rather than leaving the industry.**
- **Positioning (UBS desk, 2026-08-01): Semis/Semicap LONG-BIASED — [[MU]], [[LRCX]], [[KLAC]], [[ASML]], [[TER]], [[INTC]], [[MRVL]], [[SNDK]], [[TSM]], [[AMD]], [[TXN]], [[ADI]], [[MCHP]], ENTG, SMTC, [[ARM]], [[WDC]], [[STX]]. SHORT-BIASED — [[QCOM]], [[ARM]], WOLF, SWKS, [[NVDA]], [[CEREBRAS]], [[AVGO]], QRVO. DEBATED — [[AVGO]], [[ARM]], [[MRVL]], ENTG, [[ALAB]], [[INTC]].** ⚠️ **[[ARM]], [[AVGO]], [[MRVL]] and [[INTC]] appear in two buckets each — genuine two-way books, not a tagging error. Note [[NVDA]] sitting in the SHORT-biased column while the semicap complex is long-biased: the desk is long the picks-and-shovels and short the marquee name, which is the positioning expression of the "capacity is the constraint, not demand" argument above.**

## Recent signals (Jul 31) — intensity is inflecting across litho, deposition AND test at once; and the input side (wafers) is about to tighten
- **★ [[ASML]] NAMED BERNSTEIN'S BEST IDEA FOR 3Q26 (Outperform, PT €2,500) — two days after Bernstein's own emergency client call on the China-DUV competition scare and after an ~8% drawdown, so this is highest conviction placed INTO the dip** (David Dai / Carmine Milano / Juho Hwang, 2026-07-30). ⚠️ **The reusable number: LITHO INTENSITY IS "RISING STEADILY FROM 20% TO CLOSE TO 30% as DRAM migrates to more EUV layers, and STAYS ELEVATED ABOVE 30% FOR ADVANCED LOGIC."** That recasts ASML as a **share gainer WITHIN WFE**, not WFE beta. Plus **High-NA EUV adoption "gradually [by] virtually all leading edge players in DRAM and logic BY 2030"** — modelled as an industry standard, not an option.
- **⬆ THE FULL 29-PAGE NOTE IS NOW ON DISK, and the quantified build supplies three reusable cross-company numbers this theme did not have** (Bernstein · Dai/Milano/Hwang, 2026-07-30) [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/BERN_258544.html). **① THE WFE DECOMPOSITION FOR 2026 — the canonical litho-share frame: litho is ~24% OF TOTAL WFE blended, split 52% DUV / 48% EUV. By segment: ADVANCED LOGIC 30% litho (of which 80% EUV / 20% DUV), DRAM 25% litho (48% DUV / 52% EUV), and MATURE LOGIC AND NAND 100% DUV, 0% EUV. Segment weights: Logic 60% of WFE (advanced 30 / mature 30), Memory 40% (DRAM 32 / NAND 8).** That is the table to use whenever a WFE dollar needs splitting between the litho and non-litho complexes. **② THE BLENDED-INTENSITY PATH, more precise than the summary's "20%→30%": 24% → 28% BY 2028**, driven by mix (DRAM and advanced logic growing faster than mature logic and NAND). Per segment: **DRAM <20% at 1Y/1Z → 26% at 1c → ~30% at 1d; LOGIC ~20% at 10nm → peak >35% at 5nm/3nm → ~32% at 2nm** (GAA added non-litho steps), holding or rising at 1.4nm with HNA. **③ DRAM OVERTAKES LOGIC AS THE SECTOR'S GROWTH ENGINE, quantified: total DRAM EUV exposures +4.5x in five years, 3.4 MWPM (2025) → 26.3 MWPM (2030), lifting DRAM from 26% → 44% of total EUV exposures, vs logic "just over 3x." EUV layers per DRAM node ~2 at 1a → 3 at 1b → 5 at 1c → 8 at 1d, into the low teens at 0b/0c.** ⚠️ **The 4F² rebuttal matters for the whole memory-WFE debate and should be logged as contested: Bernstein reads the 6F²→4F² transition around 0a as "a TEMPORARY EASING of scaling requirements rather than a departure from the long-term trend" — i.e. NOT the 2028 air-pocket the bear case has used.**
- **⚠️ THE HNA MECHANISM IS A TRANSFER FROM NON-LITHO TO LITHO, and non-litho vendors should read it that way. Bernstein: HNA single-exposure replaces LNA multi-patterning flows (SALELE), "eliminating multiple masks, exposures, litho-etch loops, and cut steps" — with ASML's own estimate that LITHO CAPEX AND OPEX EACH RISE ~1.2x WHILE NON-LITHO COSTS DECLINE.** Total process cost can fall even as the litho bill rises — which is the mechanical reason litho intensity steps up, and the structural offset to the deposition/etch intensity story in the ASMI and [[LRCX]] bullets above. Adoption order (Exhibit 10): **[[INTC]] validated at 18A TODAY → DRAM at [[SKHYNIX]]/[[SAMSUNG]] 1d → broader logic at Intel A14 and Samsung SF1.4 → [[TSM]] only at ~A10 at the end of the decade.** Why DRAM leads logic: **"DRAM dies are smaller than logic dies and therefore do not require stitching"** → higher throughput, lower cost per exposure.
- **THE CHINA-EXPOSURE LEAGUE TABLE (1H CY26 revenue), which is the frame for every Match-Act / domestic-substitution headline: [[AMAT]] 30% (⚠️ CYQ1 only — had not reported CYQ2) > [[LRCX]] 29% > TEL/[[TOKYOELEC]] 27% > [[KLAC]] 25% >> [[ASML]] 16%, the group LOW** (down from 32% in 2025, guided ~20% FY26). ⚠️ **The asymmetry BofA also reached from the competitive angle on 07-28: the market is applying its largest China discount to the least China-exposed name, while the four names that already face domestic competitors (AMEC, Naura, ACM) carry the real exposure.** Offsetting the exposure: **Bernstein has CHINA WFE COMPOUNDING >15% CAGR TO ~US$77BN BY 2028 (from ~$45bn '25)** — with no EUV access forcing extra multi-patterning steps that *raise* DUV and process intensity per wafer. **Relative growth on BBG consensus (CY-adjusted): revenue CAGR CY25-28 — ASML 30% > LAM 28% > AMAT 25% > TEL 23% > KLA 22%; EPS CAGR — ASML 45% > LAM 38% > AMAT 36% > TEL 32% > KLA 28%.** Valuation: ASML at **31.0x 1-yr fwd / 23.5x 2-yr fwd vs a WFE group average of 31.3x / 24.9x** — below peers on both, against a ~60% average historical premium.
- **DUV IS A GROWTH LEG FOR THE COMPLEX, NOT A DRAG — the ratio to remember: the blended DUV:EUV capex ratio is ~1:1, so every incremental EUV dollar pulls a corresponding DUV dollar. In DRAM, DUV is still ~50% of litho spend even at the 1c node; in advanced logic the ratio is ~1:4 but absolute DUV spend is resilient on wafer-capacity growth and richer ArFi mix.** Bernstein's DUV revenue forecasts sit **above street across all five years** despite the post-Q2 consensus revision.
- **★ THE PRICING VARIANT NOW HAS A FALSIFIABLE FORM, and it is the sharpest thing on this theme this week. Bernstein splits ASML pricing into (i) ~20% EUV PRICE INCREASE OVER TWO YEARS from the 3800E→F migration — mechanical, already contracted — and (ii) POTENTIAL LIKE-FOR-LIKE PRICE HIKES, the discretionary lever. Chipstrat makes the checkable claim: "the way the street has 2027 modeled, ASML's customers END UP PAYING LESS PER WAFER OF CAPACITY THAN THEY DO TODAY. THAT HAS NEVER HAPPENED"** (Austin Lyons, 2026-07-31). ⚠️ **If consensus implicitly assumes ASML does NOT charge for value, then flat like-for-like pricing is upside and hikes are large upside.** Management's language moved between calls: **April "not the way we do business" → July "more flexibility for pricing."** ⚠️ **The constraint both sources name: ASML "measures that value with a much narrower yardstick" than [[TSM]] — throughput/overlay economics rather than end-product value — and over-taking "anger[s] customers and incentivize[s] competition. And regulators start paying attention," which two days after the China-DUV scare is not hypothetical.**
- **The [[TSM]] comparison is a useful calibration for the whole equipment complex, because it shows what "selling your value" looks like once it works: TSMC calls it "selling our value" and lists it among its six profitability factors, but "prefers not to explicitly talk about raising prices… the last call pointed to improved utilization and reduced costs for margin gains." ⚠️ THE DECOMPOSITION: "GROSS MARGIN ROSE 9.1 POINTS, AND MANUFACTURING PERFORMANCE EXPLAINED 3.2 OF THOSE POINTS. THE OTHER 5.9 POINTS? PRICING." Roughly two-thirds price, one-third cost — against management framing that points to utilisation** (Chipstrat, 2026-07-31).
- **★ INTENSITY IS RISING IN DEPOSITION TOO, which makes this a broad-based intensity story rather than a litho story: BofA's ASM International deep-dive argues "semiconductor scaling is increasingly driven by MATERIALS INNOVATION rather than equipment advances alone" — GAA, HBM, advanced packaging and AI architectures are "increasing DEPOSITION INTENSITY and accelerating adoption of ATOMIC LAYER DEPOSITION." Buy, PO €1,457; CY27-28E EPS raised 4-5%; 2030 EPS power framed at €52-66** (Didier Scemama, 2026-07-31). The differentiated leg is **PRECURSOR IP** — ASM combining ALD leadership with materials discovery to "co-optimise reactor design, process recipes and precursor chemistry," which BofA argues deepens customer lock-in even though "precursors are unlikely to become a separate revenue stream." ⚠️ **Worth carrying because it identifies the NEXT bottleneck: "as dimensions approach atomic scales, SPECIALISED PRECURSOR MOLECULES are becoming critical to yield, performance and power efficiency," with high qualification barriers and early visibility into customer roadmaps.**
- **★ AND IN TEST: BofA puts test intensity "SUSTAINING AT 7-8% OF WFE, implying a $14BN+ ATE OPPORTUNITY AT $200BN WFE," derived from [[TER]]'s improving AI test outlook** (Vivek Arya, 2026-07-30). Corroborated from three peer P&Ls — **Cohu (Buy, PT $60 → $74; Sept revenue guided $170M vs Street $146M; COMPUTE SYSTEMS ORDERS +150% Y/Y; CY26 HPC revenue $90M → $105M; long-term HPC pipeline $750M → $850M); Chroma ATE (OW, PT NT$3,000 → NT$3,160; >50% share in CPO test Insertion 3 and 100% in Insertion 4.0; 2027 optical-comm revenue NT$7bn → NT$10bn); and Hon Precision (OW, PT NT$8,000 → NT$8,350; large-package ATC handlers at a 20-25% PRICE PREMIUM from Oct 2026; customer thermal roadmaps at ~10KW in 2027-28).** ⚠️ **The single best per-unit multiplier available: "the TESTING TIME of [[AMD]]'s GPU could be 2-3X THAT OF NVIDIA'S GPU, with TDP largely on par or slightly higher."**
- **[[LRCX]] follow-up call — the segment ranking and one quantified new opportunity: DRAM is expected to be the FASTEST-GROWING WFE segment in FY26, OUTGROWING leading-edge foundry logic, with Lam expecting to OUTGROW overall DRAM WFE (conductor-etch leadership, Akara adoption doubling y/y since launch, >70% diffusion-barrier share, plus dry photoresist/HBM/TSV kickers). FY27 has DRAM and foundry logic "a lot closer," NAND "a distant third." BACKSIDE POWER DELIVERY sized at ~$1BN PER 100K WAFERS. Gross-margin target ~52% → mid-50s over "several years."** ⚠️ **A DIRECT COMPETITIVE REBUTTAL logged as contested, not settled: Lam "firmly rejects a competitor's claim of ALD MOLY WINS IN FOUNDRY LOGIC," holding that NAND adopts Moly FIRST and that Lam is the largest Moly player in NAND production** (2026-07-31).
- **⚠️ TWO QUALITY CAVEATS on the Lam print that anyone quoting it needs: (a) THE CSBG BEAT WAS LOW-QUALITY — +43% and 15-16% above expectations, but driven by SPARES (customer pre-buying amid back-end constraints) and Reliant upside rather than upgrades, with CSBG guided FLATTISH for the next two quarters and September's uptick coming from systems instead; (b) NAND WFE IS DOWN 1H'26 vs 1H'25 IN DOLLARS — the structural SLC/TLC-for-AI-memory-stack demand is teed up for '28-'30, NOT 2026-27.**
- **Independent supply-chain corroboration, which is better evidence than a repeat of the guide: LRCX INVENTORY DAYS ARE AT A 19-QUARTER LOW — demand is drawing down inventory, not building it — with "visibility into new fab builds significantly higher than normal" and momentum into 2028. Both KLAC and LRCX raised 2026 ADVANCED-PACKAGING expectations, with momentum building into 2027 as packaging architectures get more complex** (DB · Melissa Weathers, 2026-07-30/31). **MKSI: Buy, PT CUT to $360 from $380 on a lower target multiple despite HIGHER EPS estimates; Semiconductor segment expected to grow at least in line with WFE (>30%) this year; named DB's top SMID-cap pick into earnings; reports 08-05. ONTO: Buy, PT $350 — advanced packaging is a larger % of sales than at large-cap peers, so A/P momentum should drive "outsized positive estimate revisions"; DB argues share-loss fears are overblown; reports 08-06.**
- **Sector marks for calibration: KLAC raised its 2026 WFE outlook to the LOW-$150BN RANGE with BACKLOG +59% Y/Y; LRCX guided materially above expectations with 2027 likely a FOURTH CONSECUTIVE YEAR of WFE outgrowth on NAND upgrades, HBM-related DRAM investment and rising etch/deposition intensity. Equipment demand is broadening across memory, leading-edge logic, foundry AND advanced packaging** (BofA · Arya, 2026-07-30). ⚠️ **The offsets named: wafer and substrate limitations are BOUNDING AI-compute shipments, and China competition partly offsets memory/foundry/AP growth through CY27-28.**
- **★ THE INPUT SIDE IS ABOUT TO TIGHTEN, and this is a new constraint for the theme: DB UPGRADED SILTRONIC TO BUY, PT €100 (from €67.95) — "After years of struggle, Siltronic emerges as a classic 'LATE CYCLE' play." The argument: "the AI spending boom has pretty much PASSED [the wafer industry] BY so far given LOW WAFER AREA CONSUMED BY MARKET LEADERS SUCH AS NVIDIA. However… the wave of demand from AGENTIC AI is now set to TIP THE 300MM WAFER INDUSTRY INTO SHORTAGE CONDITIONS, STARTING WITH EPITAXIAL WAFERS, BUT WITH POLISHED WAFERS SET TO FOLLOW"** (Robert Sanders, 2026-07-30). ⚠️ **Quantified: 300mm industry utilisation heading to the MID-90s % IN H2-26, which "points to SPOT PRICING HEADING ABOVE LTA-AGREED PRICING on 300mm over the next 12 months," making 2027 LTA renewals a positive driver for producers. SPOT-ABOVE-LTA IS THE INVERSION SIGNAL — bargaining power returns to wafer producers. Note the mechanism: it is agentic-AI VOLUME, not accelerator die area, that tightens the market — which is why wafers lagged the rest of the complex.**
- **Positioning: buy-side re-entry into the AI trade is reported as "limited to higher quality, lower-vol names, i.e. Nvidia, Hyperscalers, and SEMICAPS (LAM RESEARCH, APPLIED MATERIALS, ASML)," with a second wave of names still 30-50% OFF HIGHS expected to follow — "Corning, Marvell, SanDisk, KLA Corp, Intel and Lumentum"** (BofA · Fenske/Liu via Villani, 2026-07-30/31). **LRCX +18% and ADVANTEST +16% on 07-30.**
- **Advanced-packaging capacity, for the WFE-dollar implication: [[TSM]]'s CoWoS is being outsourced at pace — the OUTSOURCED SHARE goes 8% (2024) → 46% (2025) → 75% (2026E) → 85% (2027E) → 90% (2028E), and ASE raised FY26 capex a SECOND time by $2bn to US$10.5BN across 20+ facility projects (13 greenfield, 8 brownfield), with 2027 capex expected to stay elevated** (JPM · Hariharan, 2026-07-30). See [themes/cowos-packaging](cowos-packaging.md).

## Recent signals (Jul 30) — [[LRCX]] raises WFE to low-$150B, and the Street immediately goes past it
- **★ THE COMPANY NUMBER: [[LRCX]] raised its CY26 WFE call from ~$140B to the low-$150B range (+36-38% y/y), and raised its advanced-packaging growth guide from 50%+ to 70%+** (LRCX F4Q26 call, 2026-07-29). Attribution matters: the uptick is "mainly driven by **pull-ins of new cleanroom buildouts**" and, per DB, "**the resolution of certain bottleneck tools**" — i.e. partly a supply unlock rather than pure incremental demand. DB also endorsed an **"extraordinary set-up" for CY27 WFE growth (DBe >30%)**.
- **★ THE STREET IS ALREADY ABOVE IT, ON THREE DIFFERENT MEASURES:**
  - **Exit rate:** UBS · Timothy Arcuri — "**the industry now looks to be run-rating ~$170-175B in WFE in CQ4**" (2026-07-30). The calendar-year low-$150B figure therefore understates where the industry finishes the year.
  - **2026 and 2027 totals:** Morgan Stanley — "We're likely headed to **$160bn of WFE in 2026** (+37% y/y, 2025 MSe $117bn) and if we assume a 'pretty darn good year' is of a similar growth rate to 2026, then we're headed to **$220bn+ in 2027**." MS raised CY27 LRCX to $39.6bn/$11.26 EPS (from $37.1bn/$10.14) and now models CY26/27 growth of **+87%/+46% DRAM, +56%/+40% NAND, +33%/+46% Foundry Logic** — adding that LRCX's DRAM commentary means "we have to **revisit and redo** some of our analysis" (2026-07-30).
  - **Capacity ceiling:** in the UBS callback, **LRCX management acknowledged that supporting $250B+ of WFE "would likely require a third manufacturing site"** (2026-07-30). That is the first time a major toolmaker has put a physical-footprint constraint on the upside case; read with UBS/Arcuri's prior framing of capacity actions pointing to WFE toward $250B in CY28.
- **Base-year caveat — semicap growth rates are not comparable without it (Barclays · Tom O'Malley, 2026-07-30).** "WFE is up 36% y/y on [LRCX's] metrics but by our mark likely grows **Systems closer to 50%** this year. **[[KLAC]] earlier this week described a similar $ WFE market for this year, but off a HIGHER BASE and is growing revenue in line with this at ~28%. The divergence in absolute growth is largely a function of [the base].**" Apply this before comparing LRCX's ~50% to KLAC's ~28%.
- **Margin structure re-rated across the group.** LRCX formally raised long-term targets to **mid-50% GM / mid-40% OM** (from low-50%/40%), on "outsized cost efficiency gains and **value-based pricing**" (BofA · Vivek Arya, who raised CY26/27E EPS by **16%/12%** and sees LRCX WFE share going **9% in CY24 → 14%+ in CY27, ~500bp in four years**, 2026-07-30). Susquehanna: both gross and operating margin came in above the **upper end** of guidance. The technology drivers cited for further share gain: **GAA, CFET, HBM, 4F² DRAM cell structures and panel-level advanced packaging**.
- **Litho intensity — the ASML-specific version of the same trend (Bernstein · David Dai, ASML named Best Idea 3Q26, OP PT €2,500, 2026-07-30).** "**Litho intensity is rising steadily from 20% to close to 30% as DRAM migrates to more EUV layers, and stays elevated above 30% for advanced logic.** We also expect **HNA EUV to be adopted gradually [by] virtually all leading edge players in DRAM and logic.**" Capacity math from the JPM desk: **[[ASML]]'s 85 EUV tools in 2027 "reflects today's supply-demand balance rather than a hard production ceiling," with a pathway to ~110 EUV tools in 2028 within the existing footprint** — and "many investors already modelling closer to **120-125 tools**"; buy-side 2028 EPS converging on **€75, bulls at €80**, stock at **22x 2028** (JPM · Scott Silver, 2026-07-30).
- **Japan/ATE leg confirms: [[ADVANTEST]] delivered a beat-and-raise, RAISED its TAM forecast, and is PULLING IN capacity build-out** (Bernstein · David Dai, ▲PT to ¥45,800, 2026-07-29). Jefferies: this "reinforced confidence in AI infrastructure demand" and drove a Japan SPE relief rally that also lifted **[[TOKYOELEC]], Rigaku and Tokyo Seimitsu** — notable because Samsung, SEMCO and Kioxia all faded hard the same session. Also in the window: **[[AIXA]] 2Q26 order intake €215m vs MSe €200m / consensus €184m (75% Opto), 3Q guide ahead, FY26 unchanged, and 1H26 operating cash flow €173m vs €85m on customer advance payments** to fund the coming ramp (MS · Nigel van Putten, EW PT €45, 2026-07-30).
- **Positioning is the near-term driver, not fundamentals — and the desk says so explicitly (JPM · Scott Silver, 2026-07-30).** "A number of companies have delivered objectively strong results, including **[[ASML]], ASM International, BESI, VAT Group and Nokia, yet were met with disappointing share price reactions as positioning overwhelmed fundamentals.**" On the 07-30 reversal: "**EU semis leading higher and the AI momentum trade firmly back in favour** … we've been better buyers across EU semis, particularly ASML, ASM International, [[IFX]], [[AIXA]] and Siltronic. Today alone we've been around **2.5x better to buy in Infineon**, while notably we have seen **virtually no selling in ASML**." The caution: "the speed of today's move does make me slightly cautious. It reinforces **how fragile and positioning-driven this market has become** … even modest re-risking can generate outsized price moves."
- **China DUV — the concrete progress is on the DUV, not EUV, end of the moat (2026-07-30).** Three items in one day: **"China's DUV shock: the secretive Aishengna rattles [[ASML]] with US$1B state backing"** (DIGITIMES) on Shanghai Aishengna Electronic Technology; **"China's 28nm DUV lithography enters fab validation, reportedly guided by State Council-level experts"** (DIGITIMES); and **"China Begins Mass Production of Homegrown DUV Chip Tools"** (The Information). Counterweight from the memory side: a former SK hynix executive argues Chinese makers remain **effectively blocked from advanced DRAM/HBM by equipment export controls** (BTG, 2026-07-30).
- **Advanced packaging is now a two-horse race at the leading edge.** **The Information (exclusive, 2026-07-30): "[[TSM]] is developing an advanced chip-packaging technology similar to what [[INTC]] already offers … a sign that the world's leading chip manufacturer is worried about competition from a distant rival."** Published takeaways: "TSMC develops **EMIB-like** chip packaging to counter Intel's offering; **Intel's EMIB packaging attracts [[NVDA]], [[GOOG]] for AI chip designs**; TSMC's packaging capacity constraints limit customer growth for AI chips." Substrate-side confirmation that EMIB-T is scaling: **Unimicron raised 2026 capex to NT$53.7bn (US$1.66B) from NT$34.1bn** for long-lead-time equipment with 2Q net profit **+441x y/y**, and JEF/Fubon expects **EMIB-T substrate mass production from 2027, profitable even at an initial 50% yield target because there is no interposer** — shifting value to the substrate supplier (BUY, TP TWD1,100; FY27 EPS +18.5% to NT$37.34; ABF GPM target 50-60%).

## What it is / why it matters
WFE is the toolset that builds the fabs — litho, deposition, etch, metrology/inspection, advanced packaging and test. We are in the early-to-mid innings of a multi-year up-cycle whose ceiling keeps getting raised: the bull-case WFE TAM is now ~$200B for CY27 and ~$250B for CY28 (Arcuri/UBS, 2026-05-15), i.e. roughly +$50B added each of the next two years off a base the Street still models at high-$160s/$170s. The driver is memory capex (DRAM, HBM) plus a foundry/logic node-transition wave (3nm/2nm and the first sub-2nm) and an Intel/Terafab greenfield leg layered on top. For a buy-sider the read is simple: order visibility is unprecedented (Applied cited eight quarters of customer visibility — Sur/JPM, 2026-05-15), positioning is *less* crowded than the tape implies, and the equipment names have barely flexed pricing/margin upside.

## State of play (latest)
- **NEW (2026-08-25) — A THIRD GW→WAFER CARD ARRIVES, THIS ONE SPLIT BY NODE — AND IT SITS BELOW BERNSTEIN ON TOTAL WAFERS WHILE SITTING *ABOVE* IT ON THE LOGIC SHARE.** **Dylan Patel (SemiAnalysis)** gives **55,000 N3 + 6,000 N5 + 170,000 DRAM wafers per GW of Vera Rubin ≈ 231k 300mm wafers/GW**. **Source: Dylan Patel (SemiAnalysis), in interview — relayed by Felipe, 2026-08-25.** ⚠️ **VINTAGE, not attribution, is the open item: Patel dates the figures to an interview “a few months” earlier, so carry them as ~2Q26 vintage rather than a fresh mark — which matters because the SOCAMM spec has been halved since (see the sensitivity below).** **Like-for-like against the two cards already on this page:** Bernstein's rack physics implies **~295k rack wafers/GW** (65 wafers/rack × 4,545 racks/GW) and the AlphaSense wafer-supply expert says **~350k wafer starts/GW, HBM 60-70%** (2026-07-17, on [hbm-memory.md](hbm-memory.md)) — so the three bracket **231-350k**, with Patel's card lowest because it carries **no NAND and no non-rack DDR leg** (Bernstein's 46-50k WSPM/GW is ~53% DRAM / 20% NAND / 16% HBM / 11% logic and explicitly includes agentic CPUs at ~1.4M units/GW-yr with 0.5TB DDR each). ⚠️ **But the LOGIC shares are inconsistent and that is the finding:** Patel's card is **26% logic** (61k of 231k) versus Bernstein's **~14% logic ex-NAND** — and a die-area bottom-up of the VR200 NVL144 BOM gives only **~25-30k foundry wafers/GW (5.5-6.5 wafers/rack)**, against **Fubon's 4.6 TSMC wafers/rack** on Blackwell (33k/GW at 7,200 racks, on [../TSM.md](../TSM.md)). So on the wafer TOTAL the three sources are converging; on **how much of it is leading-edge foundry, they differ by ~2x** — which matters here because the tool-only intensities are **$3.4B per 10k WSPM for advanced logic vs $1.4B for DRAM/HBM**, i.e. mis-splitting the logic share by 2x moves the WFE-per-GW card by roughly **$0.5-0.9bn/GW** on a ~$7.5-8B base (⚠️ stock-vs-flow: the 32k-wafer/GW logic delta is ~2.7k WSPM annualised, worth ~$0.9bn at $3.4B/10k WSPM, ~$0.5bn net of the DRAM capacity it displaces at $1.4B/10k). ⚠️ **Basis discipline:** this is a one-time wafer BILL per GW built; Bernstein's 46-50k WSPM is STANDING CAPACITY per GW/yr of additions — never substitute one for the other. Ledger: [`wafers-per-gw`](../_meta/assumptions.md).

- **NEW (2026-07-27) — the PRIMARY source behind the bullet above is now on file, and it corrects the maker's identity, sizes the hole in the WFE pool properly, and names the remaining chokepoint (The Information (Staff) "China Starts Mass-Producing Homegrown DUV Chipmaking Tools", 2026-07-27).** ⚠️ **Attribution correction first, because the sell-side relay got it wrong: The Information does NOT name the manufacturer.** It reports that **an unnamed SHANGHAI-BASED STATE-BACKED COMPANY has begun making immersion DUV, having "brought together immersion DUV development teams from other Chinese companies, including a state-backed chip equipment startup called SHANGHAI YULIANGSHENG TECHNOLOGY"** — i.e. Yuliangsheng is one of the absorbed teams, not the builder, and the article's spelling is *Yuliangsheng* (the Jefferies relay and the bullet above carry "Yuliansheng" as the maker). Both sourced to **"two people familiar with the situation"**, who asked that the company not be identified — **single-outlet, anonymously sourced, no rating and no PT; treat as reported, not established.** **The sizing, which is the part that matters for the WFE pool: ~5 machines in 2026 and ~20 in 2027, against ASML's 131 IMMERSION DUV SYSTEMS SHIPPED LAST YEAR** — roughly **4% and 15% of a single year of ASML immersion output**, and that is unit substitution at the *low* end of the performance curve, not incremental WFE. **The correct read for this page is therefore STRUCTURAL, NOT NEAR-TERM: "ASML therefore faces little immediate threat. But rising domestic output could gradually erode its market position in the country, particularly if tighter Western restrictions accelerate Chinese chipmakers' shift to local equipment."** Three gates on even that: the tools **"still trail ASML's products in performance and build quality"**; moving them onto mass-production lines **"could take many months or longer as chipmakers test their accuracy, reliability and compatibility with other equipment"**; and 2026 deliveries are **slated to [[SMIC]], Hua Hong and CXMT** — the same three names in the Jefferies relay — off **prototypes shipped to some chipmakers last year with "encouraging test results."** ⚠️ **The chokepoint that survives, and it is the single most useful line in the piece for a WFE model: the Chinese tool "uses mostly domestic components, though SOME KEY PARTS COME FROM JAPAN," and "DELAYS FROM LOCAL SUPPLIERS HAVE HELD BACK PRODUCTION THIS YEAR."** So the binding constraint on the domestic-substitution curve has already moved from the scanner OEM to the sub-supplier base, and part of it sits in an allied jurisdiction — which is where the MATCH Act's component/servicing scope (see [china-export.md](china-export.md)) actually bites. **Demand-side counterweight in the same piece, and it cuts the other way for the litho pool: "the global AI boom is straining chip supply, pushing manufacturers to race to expand capacity — and increasing demand for ASML's products," with SMIC CEO ZHAO HAIJUN saying on a MAY EARNINGS CALL that OVERSEAS CUSTOMERS WERE LOOKING TO MAKE CHIPS IN CHINA BECAUSE CAPACITY WAS TIGHT ELSEWHERE.** Where the tools land: **mature nodes first — "power-management components essential to AI servers and data centers… shortages can still hold up the construction of entire computing systems"** — plus the DUV-multi-patterning path already on this page (**SMIC has used multiple patterning on immersion DUV "to produce advanced chips for smartphones and AI systems despite lacking access to EUV equipment"**). (The Information (Staff) "China Starts Mass-Producing Homegrown DUV Chipmaking Tools", 2026-07-27; see [../ASML.md](../ASML.md), [../SMIC.md](../SMIC.md); the policy leg on [china-export.md](china-export.md)) [source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/China_Starts_Mass-Producing_Homegrown_DUV_Chipmaking_Tools_An_Advance_for_Local_Chip_Industry_The_Information.html)
