The company-level guide bogeys for this week: **LRCX expected to guide C26 WFE to $145B vs the $140B (+27% y/y) offered on the April call**, and **KLAC has been the conservative voice at "high-teens WFE growth to $140B in C26,"** which Jefferies argues is precisely why the stock has lagged (+78% YTD but behind large-cap peers). BofA separately frames the structural case: **WFE spending "projected to grow at 20% CAGR, 2025-2030, well above the 13% CAGR in the prior 6 years."**
- **NEW (2026-07-26) — the customer-capex evidence accumulated in a single week, and it is the actual bull case (BofA · Vivek Arya / Brian Fenske; Jefferies · Curtis/Menon):** **TSM raised 2026 capex to $60-64B from $52-56B — "this 15% raise at the midpoint was generally not expected by the Street"**; **INTC raised 2026 capex to $20B+ with 2027 "significantly" higher and colour pointing to more in 2028**, Vivek modelling **gross capex $20.2B ('26) / $27B ('27) / $32B ('28)** off the decision to "fully commit to high-volume ramps in 2028" for 14A after 2H27 internal risk production; and **the reported $200bn AVGO-Samsung supply deal (memory + foundry + advanced packaging) "bodes well for Samsung capex as well"** — a third leg that was not in anyone's model a week ago. Jefferies adds the equipment-specific reads: **Intel is "aggressively locking in tool purchase orders and accelerating cleanroom buildouts," 14A is "likely to be a material user of high-NA systems," and ASML's Intel revenue is "subdued this year but likely to accelerate in 2027"** (positive for ASML, ASM, VAT). ⚠️ **The gate on the whole leading-edge leg is unchanged and worth stating plainly: "Leading Edge will step meaningfully higher IF INTC has in fact secured a client for 14A, but official guidance for C27 remains 6 months out"** — so the upside is contingent on an announcement that will not arrive at these prints. **Terafab remains "a material swing factor but timing and total $ opportunity remain uncertain and we don't expect answers by next week."**
- **NEW (2026-07-26) — NAND conversion pulls revenue FORWARD, which resolves the "no greenfield until CY28" objection (Jefferies, LRCX preview):** "In Memory, conversion activity for NAND is accelerating with management now expecting **the majority of the $40B+ upgrade opportunity to be completed before the end of C27. Critically, layer upgrades do not require new cleanroom space and given the heavy $ skew towards HAR etch, LRCX captures the bulk of the economics.**" A $40B+ pool arriving a year earlier than the greenfield cycle, concentrated in the highest-margin content. See [[LRCX]].
- **NEW (2026-07-26) — the sector derating, quantified by sub-sector since the SOX peaked on June 22 (Jefferies Global SEMIS Daily): memory -24% share price / -36% P/E · CPUs -21% · semicap -18% / -20% P/E · optical -15% · analog -14% (the smallest).** Jefferies' conclusion is the one to carry: **"the Global Semis Sector has entered a cyclical de-rating phase where multiple expansion ceases to be a material driver of share prices after 9 months of sharp re-rating, and has now become a headwind. Share price performance is, therefore, likely to depend on the extent of earnings upgrades."** ⚠️ **And the flows say the semicap complex is being sold into the upgrades: Jefferies' desk has "net sold Semicaps amongst both HFs and LOs for 4 weeks in a row now."** Positioning is contradictory across houses and left that way — BofA's chart work shows **LOs far LESS overweight semicaps than in early 2023, with the flow/positioning inflection only beginning mid-2025**, while JPM/Schlegel has **global semis at the 98th %ile on both net exposure and L/S ratios** on long-term metrics (12m readings easing to ~81st %ile). Different lookbacks, opposite conclusions.
- **NEW (2026-07-26) — a second capex cycle from packaging, sized: advanced-packaging TAM US$19bn (2026E) → US$122bn (2030E), a 59% CAGR, and "CoPoS and EMIB-T should drive another capex cycle, benefiting equipment suppliers" (UBS · Sunny Lin / Randy Abrams / Nicolas Gaudois / Timothy Arcuri, 2026-07-26).** UBS's named beneficiaries are mostly off-coverage here (initiates All Ring at Buy NT$1,550; likes GPTC, Chroma, ASMPT, Gudeng; substrate top picks Ibiden and Unimicron) but the read-through to [[KLAC]] is direct — **KLAC raised its C26 Advanced Packaging SPC revenue forecast to $1B on the April call, +57% y/y**, and **TSMC announced two ADDITIONAL advanced-packaging fabs on its 2Q26 call.** See [cowos-packaging](cowos-packaging.md).
- **NEW (2026-07-25) — a pricing HIERARCHY emerged this week, and it is the first time the Street has ranked the Big Three on who actually gets the pricing kicker (UBS · Tim Arcuri previews via Ruple's "Tech Musings", 2026-07-25; Deutsche Bank · Melissa Weathers, 2026-07-23).** Post-ASML, "semicap pricing" has been treated as a group-wide lever. Two houses now say it is not. **DB (07-23): pricing is "largely ASML-specific"** (ASML the most supply-constrained), and "pricing has rarely been an upside lever for the broader semicaps historically." **UBS (07-25) ranks it explicitly:** ASML sets the price; **LRCX** will be "more assertive on pricing — albeit likely **to a lesser extent than ASML (or AMAT)**," taking "a more measured approach"; and **KLAC is "the least likely in the group to pursue pricing given its already industry-leading margin profile,"** with a tone expected to be "less forceful than that of ASML and AMAT." **So the ordering is ASML > AMAT > LRCX > KLAC**, and the pricing narrative that lifted the whole group is really an ASML story with diminishing echoes. Corroborating on the ASML side: UBS hosted the **ASML CFO for a virtual roadshow (07-25)** — "demand continues to exceed supply" and **end-market visibility is extending beyond 2027, "a rare positive signal this early in the year."** Cross-ref [../ASML.md](../ASML.md), [../LRCX.md](../LRCX.md), [../KLAC.md](../KLAC.md), [../AMAT.md](../AMAT.md).
- **NEW (2026-07-25) — in-quarter checks show simultaneous pull-ins across ALL THREE segments, and SPE is asking suppliers for ±10% q/q shipment flex (UBS · Tim Arcuri, LRCX preview, 2026-07-25).** "Our in-quarter checks again flagged **very strong pull-in requests across the board (F/L, DRAM, NAND)** that are consequently driving SPE companies to ask sub-system suppliers to be **ready for +/- 10% Q/Q in WFE shipments over the next quarter**." The gating variable is no longer demand but **fab readiness** — what LRCX ships in September "will depend almost entirely on fab readiness." UBS expects LRCX and KLAC management to **walk CY2026 WFE up to ~$150B** on their calls (KLAC's last public number was >$140B; Jefferies has C28 WFE **above $250B**). **The differentiated leg UBS flags as underappreciated is NAND:** suppliers are pushing for **faster equipment upgrades to push higher-layer-count bits into the market at current pricing** — an upgrade cycle rather than a greenfield one. UBS sizes LRCX at **~$18.50 EPS in a $300B WFE scenario**, before any industry-wide pricing increase.
- **NEW (2026-07-25) — Intel's equipment orders reportedly revised UP 30-40%, and the leading-edge step-up is explicitly gated on a 14A customer (KeyBanc · John Vinh via Semiecosystem/LaPedus, 2026-07-24; Jefferies · Blayne Curtis, 2026-07-24).** Vinh: "due to surging demand for server CPU for agentic AI workloads, we believe INTC plans to **significantly expand capacity at 18A and on Intel 3 (Granite Rapids)**. More recently, we believe INTC has **upwardly revised some of its foundry equipment orders by 30-40%**." That is a harder number than the capex guide itself. But Curtis states the condition the whole group is waiting on: "**Leading Edge will step meaningfully higher IF INTC has in fact secured a client for 14A** — but official guidance for C27 remains 6 months out." His read on why the group has not been paid for any of this: TSMC raised C26 capex to **$60-64B**, ASML raised FY26 to **€43-45B from €36-40B**, INTC stepped up C26 and finally committed to a significant C27 raise — "and even this offered little help." For the stocks to work, Curtis wants **incremental information on leading-edge spend (INTC/TSMC C27), improved pricing dynamics beyond what's anticipated, or hard figures on the pending Terafab buildout.** BofA's desk (Villani/Fenske/Liu, 07-24) is pitching **semicaps as clear winners here/now, with ASML and KLA benefiting most on direct Intel reads**, and points to **Nova and Camtek** for more torque — smaller-cap, cheaper than large-cap peers. Cross-ref [../INTC.md](../INTC.md).
- **NEW (2026-07-25) — the counterweight: TSMC's capex intensity is ~18 points below its own last supercycle (Chipstrat · Austin Lyons, 2026-07-24).** In the pandemic-era supercycle TSMC spent **53% of revenue on capex**; in the AI supercycle, on ~**$100bn/yr of operating cash flow**, it is spending **34-36%**. "Matching the 2021 playbook today would mean **another $30 billion a year**." Since fabs are 10-year+ assets, "**today's CapEx is TSMC's opinion on demand for the next decade**." Worth holding against the uniformly bullish WFE flow above: the industry's largest spender is still well below its own precedent, which is either post-2021-2023 discipline or a quiet demand signal. See [../TSM.md](../TSM.md).
- **NEW (2026-07-24) — Intel's capex raise is now a first-order WFE datapoint, and MS translated it into tool counts (Morgan Stanley Europe · van Putten / Simpson / Kim, "Implication of Intel's capex raise for ASML and ASMI", 2026-07-24).** On its Q2'26 call [../INTC.md](../INTC.md) raised **FY26 capex to >$20B (from $18B)** and guided **CY27 "significantly above" that** (MS's Joe Moore forecasts **$30bn**; JPM **$26bn, "may still prove light"**; UBS **~$30bn**). The composition is what makes it WFE-relevant: **space/clean-room capacity is already in place from prior years' investment, so "the bulk of incremental capex is being directed toward tooling"** — **equipment spend +40% y/y in 2026**, concentrated in front-end tools for **Intel 3, 18A and 18A-P**, where Intel is "**aggressively locking in tool purchase orders**," plus stepped-up 14A investment for **risk reduction in 2027 and committed HVM in 2028**. Intel has historically been a **>10% customer of equipment revenue at both [../ASML.md](../ASML.md) and ASMI**. **The two concrete revisions:** (1) **ASML — MS had modelled just 1 Low-NA EUV shipment to Intel in FY26 ramping to 6 in FY27, and now sees Intel demand at 2-4 Low-NA EUV tools this year.** MS is **not** raising estimates, and the reason is the bullish part: *"we think this is now **pushing against what ASML may be able to deliver**"* — so it reads the excess demand as **reaffirming ASML's pricing power**, with volume-ramp efficiency plus pricing supporting near-term margin improvement. (2) **ASMI — the 14A addressable market per equivalent 1k-wpm of capacity is ~40% higher than Intel 3** (analogous to TSMC 3nm vs A14), putting "significant upward pressure" on MS estimates currently at +15% y/y in '26 then +35% in '27. **Corroborating same-day marks:** **BofA (Arya)** expects Intel capex growth "**at least in line with ASML's recent 30%+ annual industry WFE growth**"; **UBS (Arcuri)** calls it "**positive for Semicaps/KLAC**," with 14A HVM in C28 "further support for WFE spend"; and on the print **ASML traded +2.4% and KLAC +2.8%** (JPM · Meyers). **Note this supersedes the practical relevance of the 04-15 BofA datapoint** that Intel's CFO "shot down" ordering more EUV tools saying they had enough capacity — now ~3 months stale and contradicted by Intel's own guide and MS's revised tool count. **Funding caveat that belongs on this page, not just the INTC page:** management conceded **2027 positive FCF is "more challenged"** and that it **"may need to tap the capital markets"** — i.e. the incremental WFE demand carries financing risk. Also relevant: **TSMC's own capital discipline is being questioned from the other direction** — "TSMC could spend $10B more on N2 capacity. Why won't they?" (Chipstrat · Austin Lyons, 2026-07-24), with N2 super-hot-run mix at **20-30% (from 10-20%)** at ~+50% ASP per tier, i.e. the constraint on leading-edge capacity is increasingly the incumbents' *choice* rather than the order book. See [../TSM.md](../TSM.md).
- **NEW (2026-07-22) — Morgan Stanley initiates AEIS at OW and prints fresh WFE growth marks: WFE +32%/+31% (26/27), AMAT+LAM system shipments +41%/+33% (above WFE) (Morgan Stanley "No slowdown to WFE or Data Center", 2026-07-22).** In its Advanced Energy initiation (OW, PT $421), MS's semicap desk (Shane Brett / Joseph Moore) lays down fresh WFE-growth marks: **WFE +32% (2026) / +31% (2027)**, with **AMAT + LAM combined system shipments +41%/+33%** — i.e. AMAT/LAM outgrow WFE (combined **+210 bps** of WFE share in 2026, **+60 bps** in 2027, with no aggressive inventory build assumed). AEIS's own semi biz modeled **+30%/+33%** (in line with WFE, below AMAT/LAM), and **AMAT + LAM = 75% of AEIS' 2025 semi revenue** (record). These growth marks are broadly consistent with — and sit slightly above — MS's standing WFE **$149B (2026) / $191B (2027)** dollar levels already on this page (line: MS "On Memory and Semicap," 06-22), and corroborate the critical-subsystem read-through leg. (see [../AEIS.md](../AEIS.md), [../AMAT.md](../AMAT.md), [../LRCX.md](../LRCX.md)) [source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/ADVANCED_20260722_0401.html)
- **NEW (2026-07-21) — Arete bottom-up EUV model lands in-line with ASML guidance (100+ units by '28) and, via litho intensity, implies ~$240-254bn WFE for '28 — Street already at $250bn, buyside $270-300bn; top-5 equipment-revenue consensus lags a flat-share $250bn-'28 by ~$11bn/6.5%, but the market is "already pricing it" (Arete Research "Real-Time Views #68", 2026-07-21).** Arete's capacity-led EUV build takes units from **44+4 ('25) → 64+4 ('26) → 84+5 ('27) → 100+5 ('28)** (low-NA + high-NA) — "surging towards 100+ by '28" after 5 years stuck at 40-50 — **in line with ASML's ~30% unit-capacity-growth guidance through '27/'28** (with ArFi units growing in-line with EUV per ASML's 2Q26 call). TSMC stays the dominant EUV buyer (~40% of low-NA tools '26-28); Intel+Samsung combined ~48 tools '26-28 (half of TSMC). Extending to WFE via **litho intensity** (litho rev ÷ WFE; long-run avg **19.2%**, last decade **21.2%**, last 3 years '23-25 **25.2%**, 2025 at **27%**), Arete's Table implies **'28 WFE of $237 (@27%) / $254 (@25.2%) / $305bn (@21.2%)** — it deems the 25.2%/27% levels most likely → **~$240-254bn '28**, noting **the Street is already at $250bn and the buyside likely $270-300bn**. The kicker: current **consensus top-5 equipment revenue** (ASML/AMAT/LAM/KLAC/TEL) totals **$109.6/$141.8/$168.6bn (CY26/27/28)** — but a $250bn '28 WFE at the flat '24-25 top-5 share (71.7%) implies **~$179bn** aggregate, i.e. a further **~$11bn / 6.5% upgrade vs consensus**. Arete's conclusion: WFE-company estimates *lag* the WFE-TAM view, but the market **already prices it** (WFE names at **~30x FY2 EV/EBIT** vs a 3-yr avg of **20.5x**), so with '28 WFE spend preceding '29 wafer output by ~9 months, the **risk/reward now favors compute + memory over WFE** given the valuation gap. (Corroborates the existing UBS $248B / house $260B / Cowen $250B '28-WFE cluster and the "Street ~$250bn" convergence; adds an independent litho-intensity-derived cross-check.) (see [../ASML.md](../ASML.md), [../AMAT.md](../AMAT.md), [../LRCX.md](../LRCX.md), [../KLAC.md](../KLAC.md); ledger: [wfe-tam](../_meta/assumptions.md)) [source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Semis_Hardware_Real_Time_Views_July_26_Edition_1.html)
- **NEW (2026-07-20) — Bernstein asks "how much WFE do we need to reach AI nirvana?" — the GW→WFE conversion card: ~46-50k WSPM ≈ ~$7.5-8B WFE per incremental GW/yr; 50GW scenario ⇒ $200/245/291B CY27-29 and "$300B+ seemingly doable" (Bernstein · Rasgon/Dai/Lin/Newman et al., 2026-07-20).** Bernstein translates DC-GW buildout scenarios into required WFE: each incremental GW/yr of additions capability needs **~46k WSPM** (DRAM 53% / NAND 20% / HBM 16% / adv-logic 11%; physics: VR200 rack = 220kW compute-TDP → 4,545 racks/GW × ~65 wafers/rack, plus non-rack legs — agentic CPUs ~1.4M units/GW-yr, DDR 0.5TB/CPU, DC-NAND 30% bit CAGR) × tool-only capital intensity (**adv logic $3.4B / DRAM-HBM $1.4B / NAND $1.3B per 10k WSPM**) ⇒ **~$7.5-8B WFE per incremental GW/yr**. Their published base model stays the **street LOW ($148/175/198B CY26-28E, incl WLP ~$9-11B/yr; segments 28E: logic 93 / DRAM 71 / NAND 23 / WLP 11)** — but the 50GW scenario (their self-described "baseline": 2030 additions ~70GW/yr vs ~20GW/yr 2026 run-rate; 27-29 cumulative adds ~130-140GW) requires **$200/245/291B CY27-29 (cum $736B, incl $120B/yr non-AI baseline)**, and 100GW/yr ⇒ **$542B CY29**. Caveats that matter: the scenario path is **back-loaded** (2027 capped at $200B in ALL scenarios — a near-term ramp cap; 2029 carries the differentiation, printing ABOVE Redburn's bull), GW = **compute-TDP basis** (excl cooling — don't mix with facility-GW), **replacement demand excluded** (real need higher), 0.5TB/CPU DDR attach is their low end, and their US team calls the Asia-team intensities "low/conservative". Company math at 50GW: **EPS vs consensus +9.8/+24.1/+59.5% AMAT, +9.2/+25.6/+54.4% LRCX, +6.1/+21.6/+37.1% KLAC (27/28/29)** → scenario P/FE-29 of 14.9x/18.4x/21.2x; at 100GW: +142-181% EPS-29, ≤12x P/FE. Pick = **AMAT** (DRAM/HBM = 69% of incremental wafers). This is the most explicit sell-side challenge yet to the ex-Samsung-EVP **"never $300B" ceiling**, sitting between UBS ($250B '28, "$300s" '29) and TD Cowen ($300B '29 / $400B '30). **Folded into the Semicap MAP** (`P:\Felipe Monteiro\Semis MAP\Semicap_MAP_v1.xlsx` + dashboard/Artifact): scenario 6 "Bernstein 50GW" + an implied-GW cross-walk — on Bernstein physics, (Σ WFE 27-29 − $360B non-AI) ÷ $7.5B/GW says our paths fund: **engine ~37 GW/yr incremental (33-41 band), UBS ~42, house-260 ~47, bull Redburn ~53, bear ~26** — i.e. **house-260 is nearly Bernstein's 50GW base-case world**, arrived at via EUV slots instead of GW physics; and the MAP's engine intensities independently land within ~4-8% of Bernstein's (N2 3.6 vs 3.4; DRAM 1.35 vs 1.4 — theirs bundles HBM; NAND 1.2 vs 1.3). (see [../AMAT.md](../AMAT.md), [../LRCX.md](../LRCX.md), [../KLAC.md](../KLAC.md), [../ASML.md](../ASML.md); ledger: [wfe-tam](../_meta/assumptions.md))
  - **Ratings (same Bernstein 20-Jul-26 WFE note):** [../AMAT.md](../AMAT.md) OP $525 (top pick, DRAM/HBM leverage), [../LRCX.md](../LRCX.md) OP $340, [../KLAC.md](../KLAC.md) OP $197.50, [../ASML.md](../ASML.md) OP €2,500/$2,859, [../SNDK.md](../SNDK.md) OP $3,000, [../TOKYOELEC.md](../TOKYOELEC.md) OP ¥59,200, [../ADVANTEST.md](../ADVANTEST.md) OP ¥39,200, [../DISCO.md](../DISCO.md) OP ¥85,000, [../BESI.md](../BESI.md) OP €280; plus (no wiki page — plain text) Screen (Market-Perform ¥12,600), Kokusai (OP ¥8,240, batch-ALD/NAND GAA), Lasertec (OP ¥50,000, actinic mask inspection ~50% share). (Bernstein · Rasgon "WFE / AI GW note", 2026-07-20) [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Bernstein_on_WFE.html)
- **NEW (2026-07-15/2026-07-20) — Didier ASML post-2Q expert call + MS ASML note frame the litho-capacity ceiling: Low-NA EUV 85 (FY27) / 110 (FY28), High-NA 4-5 rev-rec in FY26 on top; Brainport (BIC) operational 2028 = capacity optionality above 110 (Didier "ASML post-2Q" call, 2026-07-15; Morgan Stanley "ASML", 2026-07-20).** [../ASML.md](../ASML.md) Low-NA EUV capacity of **85 tools (FY27) → 110 (FY28)**, with **4-5 High-NA EUV rev-rec in FY26 on top of Low-NA**; the new **Brainport Industries Campus (BIC) becoming operational in 2028 = capacity optionality above the 110 Low-NA level**. Corroborates the +30%/yr Low-NA ramp and the "break the 90-EUV ceiling → 110+" leg already on this page. [Source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_15_didier_asml_post2q.html) · [MS note](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/ASML_20260720_0400.html)
- **NEW (2026-07-17) — Citi reads the ASML GM beat as mostly volume/mix, not pricing → only modest read-through to the US Big Three; KLAC has the most Street GM upside (Citi · Atif Malik "Weekly Investor Pulse," 2026-07-17).** Fielding investor questions on how much of ASML's GM strength is pricing vs a read-through to **AMAT/LRCX/KLAC**, Citi's ASML analyst attributes ASML's near-term GM more to **volume + mix (maybe some pricing on higher input costs)** than a structural price hike, implying only **~10-20bps GM upside to the US Big Three**. Per-name: **AMAT is the most vocal on "value-based pricing"** (offsetting input-cost headwinds via cost innovation); **LRCX GM upside is execution/efficiency-driven**; **KLAC took a GM hit from memory-pricing increases this year** but has the **most Street-modeled upside (30-50bps from Q2)** on value-based pricing + LT customer relationships. Also notes some investors think **TSMC's capex raise is partly tool price increases + prepayments**. Tempers the "ASML pricing = semicap-wide margin windfall" read into the AMAT/LRCX/KLAC prints. (Citi · Atif Malik, 2026-07-17; see [../AMAT.md](../AMAT.md), [../LRCX.md](../LRCX.md), [../KLAC.md](../KLAC.md))
- **NEW (2026-07-17) — ASML London Roadshow reaffirms the +30%/yr litho-capacity ramp and adds three fresh WFE levers: cycle-time compression, value-based pricing, and a 50/50 DRAM/logic order book (ASML mgmt "2Q26 London Roadshow", 2026-07-17).** On the MS-hosted corporate-access call ASML guided **Low-NA EUV *and* ArF-immersion capacity +30% each in both 2027 and 2028** — implying EUV capacity ~85 (2027) → ~110 (2028) on Low-NA alone (High-NA on top) — and is **evaluating a further +30% for 2028**. Big productivity lever: cutting **EUV assembly cycle-time from 22–23 weeks → 15–16 weeks** (also using AI to optimize test sequencing); the binding bottleneck is cleanroom space + assembly cabinets (freeing cabinets occupied by proto/R&D tools; Zeiss also adding cleanroom). **Gross margin 54% in 1H26, implied ~56% 2H26, target 56–60% by 2030** (drivers: install-base/upgrades, volume, mix toward EUV+immersion, higher E-tool ASPs — E-tool 230 wph vs D-tool 160 wph). **Value-based pricing is now an active lever** — ASML negotiating to capture more of the incremental value it delivers, with TSMC's CC Wei publicly agreeing there is a pricing runway (won't show immediately given 1–2yr order lead times). Order book: **2027 low-NA EUV is nearly fully booked (~85 units, all DRAM + logic players); significant 2028 orders already accepted**; the DRAM/logic demand mix is now **~50/50 (was ~70/30 logic)**. On multi-beam e-beam inspection (HMI): the current 1100 tool = **25 beams / 1–2% wafer coverage per hour**; **next-gen ~3,000 beams → >5% coverage/hr** — the threshold to move e-beam from R&D into HVM. China ~20% of business, mostly mainstream logic this year. (see [../ASML.md](../ASML.md), [../KLAC.md](../KLAC.md)) [source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_17_asml_2q26_london_roadshow_virtual_group_meeting.html)
- **NEW (2026-07-17) — Jefferies/Fubon post-TSMC 2Q: TSMC 2026 capex raised to $60–64B; Jefferies models $80B (2027) / $95B (2028) + a further $100B US leg; tool inflation ~10%; installed-base EUV being upgraded (Jefferies · Sherman Chang "Update with Fubon post-TSMC 2Q earnings", 2026-07-17).** TSMC **raised 2026 capex to $60–64B** (from the $56B guided last quarter); Jefferies estimates **$80B in 2027 and $95B in 2028**, plus an additional **$100B of US capex** (~4 new fabs: 2 foundry + 2 advanced-packaging). Jefferies links TSMC's capex raise to **ASML's earlier FY guidance raise ($36–40B → $43–45B)**. **Tool inflation running ~10%** (TSMC flagged tool inflation; Jefferies checks put the price increase ~10% — corroborating ASML's value-based-pricing lever above). TSMC is **upgrading its installed-base EUV scanners** for higher throughput in current fabs. **Advanced packaging ≈ 20% of TSMC capex → ~$16B (2027), ~$20B (2028)** back-end investment. (see [../TSM.md](../TSM.md), [../ASML.md](../ASML.md); advanced-packaging detail on [cowos-packaging.md](cowos-packaging.md)) [source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_17_jefferies_update_with_fubon_post_tsmc_2q_earnings.html)
- **NEW (2026-07-15/16) — ASML 2Q26 print resets the litho-capacity ceiling higher; DUV immersion the surprise WFE leg (Bernstein "Quick color" 2026-07-15; Redburn "Visibility extending, margins expanding" 2026-07-16; didier expert call 2026-07-15).** ASML guided **Low-NA EUV + ArF-immersion capacity +30%/yr for BOTH 2027 and 2028** — EUV units **65 ('26) → ~85 ('27) → ~110 ('28)** plus **~20 High-NA EUV capacity for '27/'28** (on top of Low-NA), and DUV **ArF-immersion 130 → 170 → 220** (far above the prior +11%/+6% cons — the under-modeled WFE vector). FY26 revenue guide raised **€36-40B → €43-45B** (mid €44B, +35% y/y): EUV +45% / DUV+M&I +25% / IBM +30%; **Memory +75% y/y** = rising litho intensity. Redburn frames FY30 at **litho intensity climbing toward ~25% as High-NA mixes in ('28-'30)**, ASML system sales scaling to ~€77B by FY30, and lifts FY26-30 WFE-name EPS +10-26%. The didier expert caps the litho gate: ASML **~85 Low-NA '27 is "best deliverable"** (~88-90 w/ fast shipments), 2028 ~110 stretchable to **120-125** and a theoretical **~170 Low-NA by 2030** once the new Brainport cleanroom (~50 tools) is built. **Intel now running High-NA in production on 18A** — the first HVM adoption milestone. (see [../ASML.md](../ASML.md), [../AMAT.md](../AMAT.md), [../LRCX.md](../LRCX.md), [../KLAC.md](../KLAC.md)) [source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Comment__Quick_color_on_ASML_Q2_results_and_guidance.html) · [Redburn](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Redburn_Research_4.html)
- **NEW (2026-07-15) — UBS ASML callback adds the EUV-pricing arithmetic + the "ASML outgrows memory-WFE" framing: EUV ≈50% of 2026 revenue → a +10% EUV price flows to ~60% of the P&L; H2 EUV ASP ~$240m (vs ~$230m H1); memory +75% vs WFE-memory +40-50% (UBS ASML callback, 2026-07-15).** A UBS post-2Q ASML callback quantifies the pricing lever that the roadshow bullets above only qualify: **EUV is ~50% of 2026 revenue and >half in '27/'28, so a ~+10% EUV price increase moves ~60% of the P&L** (~70% including immersion) — "ASML, if they increase price, it has much more impact than any other semicap." H2'26 **EUV ASP ~$240m (vs ~$230m in H1)**, and the 110-vs-85-unit '28 step comes with a **further +20-30% ASP/ISP** on top. Two more incremental reads: (i) **ASML is outgrowing memory-WFE by a wide margin — memory WFE +40-50% this year vs ASML memory +75% y/y** — expected to persist the next two years (litho intensity rising, memory the main '26 driver); (ii) **immersion attaches ~1:1 to EUV in logic**, so blended immersion growth tracks EUV closely — the market "was way too conservative" on immersion (the under-modeled DUV leg). Corroborates/quantifies the ASML-2Q cluster below (85→110 EUV, memory +75%, value-based pricing); incremental = the EUV-ASP pricing math and the ASML-vs-memory-WFE outgrowth. (UBS ASML callback, 2026-07-15; see [../ASML.md](../ASML.md)) [source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_15_ubs_asml_call_back_15_jul_26.html)

- **NEW (2026-07-14) — Redburn strategy note keeps ASML a European top Buy; frames the capex-durability debate (Rothschild/Redburn · George Karamanos "AI Monetisation & Sector Rotation", 2026-07-14).** Redburn's sector-rotation strategy note reads the recent market rotation as investors worrying about **AI-capex monetisation**, but sizes **AI revenues reaching $2trn by 2028 (from $789bn in 2025)** as justifying **~100GW of capacity (~$4-5.5trn of capex, ≈ the AI-11's 2024-32 spend)**. Its cross-sector screen (c.2,500 DM companies over the past 6 months) shows **Semiconductors as the standout — a high capex increase that drove sales-growth + RoCE upgrades on modest extra leverage** (vs Software: capex up, RoCE cut, leverage up, flat sales revisions) — i.e. semis are being *rewarded* for capex where software is being penalised, supporting the WFE-durability leg. **ASML is named a European top Buy** (with Lam Research, Coherent, Lumentum, Amphenol, BE Semiconductor, VAT). The bear cut: **hyperscalers could pull capex back**, lifting their own FCF/shares while "spooking the data-centre supply chain" that demand was overestimated — the central risk to the WFE bull case this page tracks. (Rothschild/Redburn · George Karamanos, 2026-07-14; see [../ASML.md](../ASML.md), [../LRCX.md](../LRCX.md)) [source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Redburn_Research_3.html)
- **NEW (2026-07-12) — Bernstein debunks the "semicap = memory beta" worry, and the Taiwan tracker corroborates the foundry pull (Bernstein · David Dai, 2026-07-12).** Two same-day Bernstein notes. (1) **"Can semi cap work if memory doesn't?"** — reviews the share-price history to rebut the fear that SPE names are just correlated to memory: memory↔SPE correlation was only **0.4 in 2012-18 and ~0.6 since 2019**, whereas **SPE↔SOX correlation is consistently 0.8-0.9** — i.e. SPE and memory prices can and do diverge, so the long-semicap call can work even if memory equities wobble. (2) **Taiwan SPE Import Tracker (Jun 2026): equipment imports +58% YoY / +46% MoM globally (+44% YoY / +67% MoM from Japan); 3-mo MA +8% global / +6% Japan** — "strong capex from Taiwan foundry continues to drive," a hard read confirming TSMC's accelerating tool pull into the 07-16 print (see [ASML.md](../ASML.md), [AMAT.md](../AMAT.md), [LRCX.md](../LRCX.md), [KLAC.md](../KLAC.md), [TSM.md](../TSM.md)).
- **NEW (2026-07-09) — TD Cowen (Krish Sankar) prints the most aggressive WFE curve on the Street: ~$150/$200/$250/$300/$400bn for CY26-30; DRAM-fab payback ~3 months (TD Cowen "Earnings Preview: Puts & Takes", 2026-07-09).** Cowen models WFE of **~$150B/$200B/$250B/$300B/$400B for CY26/27/28/29/30** — entered the year at a ~$120B run-rate, might **exit ~$170B run-rate (vs consensus' ~$157B)** — a **2026-'30 WFE CAGR of 25-30%**. The framework: peak WFE = peak-CapEx/peak-EBITDA. TSMC+Samsung+Hynix+Micron (~70% of WFE) spent ~40% of peak EBITDA on capex in 2018 and ~70% at the 2022 peak; Cowen's $200B 2027 WFE implies only a **20% capex/EBITDA ratio on $1T+ of EBITDA**, so reversion toward historical reinvestment rates → a **peak WFE range of ~$265-420B**; Cowen leans upper end (**~$400B by 2030**). The engine is memory reinvestment moving from low-20s% next year toward mid-40s% (supports ~$250B DRAM/NAND WFE by 2030), justified by **DRAM-fab payback of only ~3 months at current pricing after full production — 3x better vs 2018, 5x vs 2022** even at floor LTA pricing, plus 3-5yr LTAs + prepayments. Post-GenAI, incremental **leading-edge foundry WFE ≈ 50% of DRAM/NAND WFE** (vs ~100% pre-GenAI): $250B memory WFE by 2030 → ~$120B leading-edge + ~$30B trailing ≈ $400B total (excl. Terafab upside). **Pricing:** front-end semicaps expected to raise prices **10-20% by year-end** (extended visibility as customers fear a 2027 equipment/materials shortage); **back-end (TER) is NOT raising prices** but EPS is de-risked. **MKSI can support $170B WFE with 25% surge capacity**; component suppliers expected to prep for $200B+ WFE later this year; **Semicon West (October) = catalyst.** Top Longs into the print: **ASML, AMAT, LRCX, KLAC, ICHR, UCTT, MKSI**. Scenario EPS: **AMAT ~$25 @ $250B WFE** (≈1.9x CY26E $13.35), **LRCX ~$9-10 @ $250B**. Main pushback = valuation (semicaps at 40x+ NTM P/E) + memory demand-destruction risk if scarcity/high pricing bites. This sits at/above the top of the range this page tracks (Capstone house $260B 2028E is close to Cowen's $250B '28 / vs UBS $248B / cons $202B; Cowen's $400B-by-2030 is more aggressive than Redburn's $272B-by-2030). (see [AMAT.md](../AMAT.md), [LRCX.md](../LRCX.md), [KLAC.md](../KLAC.md), [ASML.md](../ASML.md), [TER.md](../TER.md), [AEIS.md](../AEIS.md)) [source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Cowen_Preview_Semicap_1.html)

- **NEW (2026-07-07) — UBS: SpaceX's Terafab could spend ~$50B on WFE in 2030/31 alone ≈ "adding another TSMC" (UBS · Timothy Arcuri, "SpaceX's Terafab — Putting Things In Perspective", 2026-07-07).** Arcuri sizes the Terafab (SpaceX/Tesla/Intel) greenfield chip-fab program as a potentially massive incremental WFE leg — **~$50B of equipment spend in 2030/2031 alone**, which he frames as "basically like adding another TSMC" of demand. If real, it materially raises the CY29+ WFE-TAM ceiling this page already tracks (the "Terafab/Intel greenfield leg" is currently a small add) and is a direct positive for AMAT/LRCX/KLAC/ASML. Aggressive/early — Terafab is pre-production and the timeline is uncertain (see [SPCX.md](../SPCX.md), [INTC.md](../INTC.md)) — but a concrete quantification of the upside vector. **Full note now on disk (2026-07-07 inbox)** adds the sizing chain: SpaceX AI capex ~$1.1T over 5yrs, ~20% (~$225B) to Terafab, ~60% capex→WFE → **~$135B WFE over 5yrs ≈ the entire global WFE TAM this year**; ramps from a pilot line ~$5B in C2027 to ~$10B in C2028 (already in UBS's ~$250B '28 WFE) then above ~$50B/yr by C2030/31. Initial fab investment ~$55B, expandable to **$119B** across all phases (Grimes County TX), plus an advanced-tech fab in Austin. Reinforces UBS's long-held conviction that **industry WFE could approach ~$300B by C2029**; UBS checks suggest **INTC may be helping Terafab secure tool slots at toolmakers** (see [INTC.md](../INTC.md)). Overall "supportive of SPE suppliers." ([UBS · Timothy Arcuri "SpaceX's Terafab — Putting Things In Perspective", 2026-07-07](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/ued46938.html); corroborated @jukan05, 2026-07-07)

- **NEW (2026-07-02) — UBS foundry-demand read: N2 ~220kwpm by 2028, server-CPU advanced capacity to 200kwpm by 2030, disciplined mature foundry — all WFE-additive (UBS "Asia Semiconductors — Sector Keys", 2026-07-02).** UBS (Sunny Lin) reiterates Buy on TSMC and expects **N2 foundry demand to reach ~220kwpm by 2028 — larger than N3's foundry demand in its 3rd year of mass production** — a direct leading-edge WFE pull. On the agentic-AI CPU leg: UBS forecasts global server-CPU shipments **23m (2025) → 63-70m units (2030, ~30% CAGR)**, requiring **~200kwpm of leading-edge foundry capacity by 2030** (of which TSMC ~122kwpm), with N2/A14 node TAM scaling to **300-350kwpm** — corroborating (and slightly re-cutting) the existing UBS "CPU TAM" leg on this page (2026-05-21) with the fresh July note. On the **mature/trailing-edge foundry**, UBS is "anti-consensus, positive": improving supply/demand over the next 2-3 years on **more disciplined China competition** (China refocusing on leading edge), TSMC optimizing mature capacity for advanced packaging, and demand recovery — UMC can raise prices **5-10% in H2'26** + another ~10% early 2027 (UMC Buy, PT NT$230); Vanguard/PSMC also flagged. On **silicon wafers**, UBS sees "the worst behind" for 12″ and 8″ demand picking up in 2026E — a supply/demand improvement read across the base. The read-through for WFE: every leg (leading-edge node migration + server-CPU capacity + mature-foundry discipline + wafer recovery) points to sustained equipment demand, consistent with the $200B/$250B CY27/28 bull-case TAM already on this page (UBS "Asia Semiconductors — Sector Keys", 2026-07-02). _(Advanced-packaging equipment names — GPTC, Gudeng — folded to [cowos-packaging.md](cowos-packaging.md).)_
- **NEW (2026-06-25) — Cantor/AMAT "Master Class": Applied's DRAM + advanced-packaging roadmap is the "outgrow-WFE" case; DRAM SAM +10% (4F2) then +30% (3D DRAM); $10B incremental over 5 years; AP revenue $500M→$1.7B→>$2B (Cantor · C.J. Muse, AMAT OW/PT $650 TOP PICK, 2026-06-25).** Applied hosted a DRAM + Advanced-Packaging Master Class that Cantor reads as the basis for AMAT **outgrowing WFE in CY26 "and beyond."** DRAM: **5 emerging inflections** (more EUV at 6F2; advanced wiring/transistors — new Epi, more copper interconnect favoring PVD/CMP, FinFET peripheral logic; **CMOS-Bonded Array = memory/logic built separately + hybrid bonding**; **4F2** vertical-cell transistors; and **3D DRAM = +30% to AMAT's SAM, etch/deposition-led**) — with SAM **+10% moving 6F2→4F2** and a **$10B incremental opportunity over 5 years ($6B periphery-transistor scaling + $4B advanced capacitors)**. Advanced packaging: AP revenue **$500M (CY20) → $1.7B (CY25) → >$2B (CY26)**, with larger accelerators driving **panels (>7x usable interposer area vs 300mm wafers)** and AMAT citing **~70% SEMVision eBeam share** + a new AP-specific inspection tool. Reinforces the DRAM-node-migration + advanced-packaging legs of the WFE super-cycle from the leading equipment supplier's own roadmap. (Cantor · C.J. Muse, 2026-06-25; see [../AMAT.md](../AMAT.md); DRAM/AP detail on [hbm-memory.md](hbm-memory.md) / [cowos-packaging.md](cowos-packaging.md)) [source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_06_25_FW_CantorAMAT_Todays_Master_Class_Focus_on_DRAM_and_Advanced_Packaging_email.html)
- **EXTENDS the 2026-06-14 Kirin bullet below (dedup pass, 2026-07-24) — the three sentences that bound the China DUV story, plus the CGP wall that binds even WITH EUV (SemiAnalysis / STEEL Team, "Is SMIC N+3's Metal Pitch Smaller than Intel 18A's?", 2026-06-14).** The teardown's DUV/multi-patterning-intensity read is already on this page; three specific limits were not, and they are the ones that matter for sizing the China DUV tail. **(1) The diminishing-returns statement, verbatim:** *"Future nodes will be tougher. N+3 still had room to tighten local metals and reduce cell height and CGP. **Further scaling without EUV leaves fewer levers. More aggressive multi-patterning adds masks and overlay error. SMIC can keep pushing DUV, but each step will get more expensive and less forgiving.**"* Read for WFE: the China DUV/deposition/etch intensity tailwind is **real but self-limiting on cost and overlay**, not an indefinite mask-count escalator. **(2) The N+5 process-flow bill (a new-tool-category read, not just more of the same):** a theoretical N+5 using **backside contacts** would additionally require **"new process flows for backside alignment, wafer thinning, contact reveal, and backside metallization"** — i.e. a step change in tool categories (bonders/thinning/backside metal), not incremental litho passes. **(3) The physical wall that applies to everyone: "CGP is unlikely to shrink much further. Even with EUV, 48 nm has been the practical limit for yield and process control."** That is the cleanest single-line justification on this page for why scaling value is migrating to packaging and 3D integration rather than gate-pitch shrink (see [cowos-packaging.md](cowos-packaging.md)). Forward relaxation SemiAnalysis expects if SMIC trades density for manufacturability: **M0 relaxing slightly to ~34nm**, with M2/M4 relaxing further, to cut process complexity. (SemiAnalysis / STEEL Team, 2026-06-14; see [../ASML.md](../ASML.md), [../AMAT.md](../AMAT.md), [../LRCX.md](../LRCX.md), [../KLAC.md](../KLAC.md), [../SMIC.md](../SMIC.md); the policy/diffusion leg is on [china-export.md](china-export.md)) [source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_06_14_ENC_Is_SMIC_N3s_Metal_Pitch_Smaller_than_Intel_18As_email.html)
- **NEW (2026-05-21, one line — a competitive-moat framing worth having on the page in someone else's words) — SemiAnalysis ranks the semiconductor moats and puts litho below software: "EDA is the most defensible business model in semiconductors. ASML faces High-NA adoption risk, and TSMC faces geopolitical concentration." (SemiAnalysis "EDA Market Primer", 2026-05-21).** Logged because it is the bear framing this page's ASML thesis has to answer, from a house that is otherwise constructive on the AI capex chain: SemiAnalysis's argument is that **EDA's software moats "widen with each node transition"** — every year of accumulated switching costs raises the next year's barrier — whereas **[../ASML.md](../ASML.md) carries adoption risk on High-NA specifically** (as opposed to Low-NA EUV, where its monopoly is uncontested) and **[../TSM.md](../TSM.md) carries geopolitical concentration risk**. No sizing or timing attached; it is a qualitative ranking, not a forecast. **Hold it against the corroborating and contradicting marks already on this page**: ASML's own corporate-access day (2026-07-17) had **High-NA "too early for '28"** with ~110 Low-NA systems by '28 and Intel 18A as the High-NA adopter, and SemiAnalysis itself notes elsewhere that **"a slower increase in EUV spend is still an increase and great for ASML"** — i.e. the High-NA *adoption-timing* risk is well documented, but nothing on the page supports a High-NA *displacement* risk. (SemiAnalysis "EDA Market Primer", 2026-05-21; off-coverage SNPS/CDNS — no wiki pages) [source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_05_21_ENC_EDA_Market_Primer_email.html)
- **NEW (2026-06-14) — SemiAnalysis Kirin teardown = a China WFE-mix read: the no-EUV path forces heavier DUV multi-patterning (SAQP), skewing China leading-edge equipment demand to DUV/deposition/etch (SemiAnalysis "Is SMIC N+3's Metal Pitch Smaller than Intel 18A's?", 2026-06-14).** SemiAnalysis's Kirin 9030 / SMIC N+3 teardown shows the WFE consequence of the EUV export ban: to hit a **32.5nm M0 pitch without EUV, SMIC cascades a second spacer step (SADP→SAQP)** and leans on DUV immersion + DTCO — i.e. **more mask, deposition and etch passes per layer (higher DUV/deposition/etch tool intensity per wafer)** but at higher cost/complexity and lower maturity/yield (SMIC N+3 ~114 MTr/mm², ~38% below Intel 18A HD; no EUV, no backside power). Read for WFE: China's leading-edge equipment demand structurally over-indexes to **DUV/multi-patterning-adjacent tools** rather than EUV — a China-WFE-mix datapoint (the process/China detail sits on [china-export.md](china-export.md); packaging angle on [cowos-packaging.md](cowos-packaging.md)). (SemiAnalysis, 2026-06-14; see [../SMIC.md](../SMIC.md)) [source](../../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_06_14_ENC_Is_SMIC_N3s_Metal_Pitch_Smaller_than_Intel_18As_email.html)
- **Critical-subsystem read confirms the 2H26 acceleration (AEIS ingest, 2026-07-02).** AEIS — RF/DC power into AMAT/LRCX etch/dep, ~30% share — said customer forecasts "strengthened considerably" during 1Q26 and guided semi revenue +>30% y/y in 2H26 (Citi/BofA 2026-05-05); Wells Fargo models AMAT/LRCX systems up mid-30s% in C2026 and upgraded AEIS to OW $465 as a 3Q26 tactical idea on accelerating semi + DC demand, flagging subsystem inventory builds as supportive of WFE outperformance (Quatrochi 2026-07-01; 2026-05-05). Full ratings wave on the new [AEIS page](../AEIS.md).
- **WFE TAM marching up.** UBS holds $200B CY27 / $250B CY28, going higher again in CY29; Arcuri stresses the buy side is "not there" — he says these numbers in every meeting and people "look at me like I have two heads" (Arcuri/UBS, 2026-05-15). More conservative house views have ratcheted up too: JPM/Tokyo desk now hears CY26 +25–30% and CY27 +20–25%, up from prior 21%/18% (Mio Shikona, JPM memory call, 2026-05-28).
- **The large caps raised guides this print.** AMAT took systems growth from ~20% to 30%+ for the year; all three US names (AMAT/LRCX/KLAC) saw a step-up in orders over the prior 90 days. KLA is the only one to commit publicly that CY27 WFE > CY26; AMAT now flags CY28 as another growth year — a notable stake in the ground from a conservative company (Sur/JPM, 2026-05-15).
- **Arcuri thinks AMAT is sandbagging H2.** AMAT's "up 30%" implies shipments barely grow in Oct/Jan quarters after +$900M sequential steps; Arcuri sees "zero evidence" of a decel in the slots and models shipments +40%, reading the guide as conservatism (Arcuri/UBS, 2026-05-15).
- **Segment leadership rotates by year.** CY26 favors the deposition/etch-intensive names (LRCX, AMAT) as DRAM/HBM bulk capacity ramps; CY27 shifts toward the new-node leaders (KLAC, ASML) as sub-2nm programs fire — TSMC A14, Intel 14A, plus 2nm expansion at Samsung/Rapidus and initial Terafab orders (Sur/JPM, 2026-05-15).
- **Advanced packaging re-accelerating.** After a soft 2025 (tough HBM-WFE compare from Q4-2024), LRCX and AMAT both guide advanced packaging +50%+ this year, KLA ~60%; AMAT advanced packaging becomes a ~$2B business, nearing 10% of systems (Sur/JPM, 2026-05-15). AMAT's EnexX (panel-level plating, ex-ASMPT) and earlier Tango (panel PVD) buys are CoPoS plays for 510–600mm panels (Arcuri/UBS, 2026-05-15).
- **Litho is the choke point.** ASML EUV capacity of ~90 tools by end-2027 looks insufficient against Samsung/Hynix/Micron each adding ~100k wfr/mo from 2028 (needing 20–25 tools each, Micron 10–15) plus TSMC already near 40 tools — EUV is "really, really the bottleneck" (Jay/JPM memory call, 2026-05-28). UBS counters that ASML will "march to that drum" and add capacity rather than cap the cycle (Arcuri/UBS, 2026-05-15).
- **ASML can break its own 90-EUV ceiling — and the Street is way behind.** JPM (Deshpande) reports ASML's message has shifted from a "≥80 Low-NA EUV" floor to *capacity*: it can lift EUV output **without new clean rooms** (cycle time 20→14 weeks by 2H27, fewer R&D cabins, repurposing High-NA capacity for Low-NA, Covid-style "fast ship") — JPM thinks **110+ Low-NA tools** are feasible. JPM models **90/105 EUV units in '27/'28** and a big DUV-Immersion call (**200 units '27** vs cons 137, as China reverts to '25 levels + non-China ramps). Result: FY27/28 revenue €55.4B/€63.8B (+16.7%/+21.8% vs cons), EPS +31.7%/+35.4% vs cons; PT €1,900/$2,200 (29.4x '28 EPS) (JPM "ASML", 2026-06-03). Redburn independently puts ASML FY30 litho sales at $54B (24.9% litho intensity, **stable 91% share**), raises FY27-30 EPS 9-12% to 14% above cons, PT €1,900 — ASML its top semicap pick (Redburn, 2026-06-17).
- **Bernstein High-NA deep dive quantifies the litho leg (David Dai, 2026-07-06).** Bernstein's follow-up models EUV (incl. High-NA) shipments of **91 systems in 2027 (from 86), 113 in 2028 (from 87), and ~125 by 2030**, with **EUV revenue ~€42.7B by 2030** (30% CAGR, ~+30% vs Street) and **litho intensity rising 24% ('25) → 26% ('28)** as the High-NA mix climbs (ASML gaining WFE share as value shifts from etch/depo/clean to litho). High-NA adoption is **DRAM-first in '27** (1d node — smaller die needs only 1 mask/AA exposure), then logic — **Intel ~'28 (A14), Samsung logic ~'29, TSMC ~'30 (A10)**; High-NA equipment cost per exposure forecast to fall from **~3.1x Low-NA (2-mask AB) / ~2.4x (1-mask AA) today toward ~2.1x/~1.9x by 2030** as tool availability climbs 80% (2025) → ~95%. DRAM litho intensity seen rising **~20% (1z) → ~30% (1d)**; logic stable ~32% at N2/A14. Bernstein lifts 2025-28 EPS to **€56.7 (from €49.2, +15%)**, raises target multiple **35x → 40x**, PT **€1,700 → €2,300** (Street-high). Underpins the "ASML raises EUV capacity guidance = catalyst" call (Bernstein "ASML: High-NA EUV cost deep dive", 2026-07-06). See [../ASML.md](../ASML.md).
- **Goldman 2Q preview — raises WFE-name estimates/PTs on pull-ins + 2028 visibility; DRAM-skew makes KLAC the laggard (James Schneider, 2026-07-05).** GS sees upside to estimates across SemiCap heading into 2Q, driven by WFE pull-ins and long-term visibility into 2028, and raises PTs: **AMAT Buy PT $645 (from $520)**, **LRCX Buy $380 (from $290)**, **TER Buy $465 (from $350)**, **KLAC Neutral $230 (from $155)**. The structural cut: WFE spend is skewed to DRAM, which favors deposition/etch (AMAT, LRCX) over inspection/metrology — so **KLAC is expected to lag peers** ("WFE spending is skewed toward DRAM, where inspection/metrology intensity is lower"), while AMAT gets "best-in-class growth in 2026" on DRAM strength + potential pricing tailwinds. GS tactical: long AMAT, short KLAC into the prints (Goldman Sachs "2Q Preview", 2026-07-05). _(GS also flags advanced packaging as a secular growth driver for AMAT — folded to [cowos-packaging.md](cowos-packaging.md).)_
- **Redburn lifts the whole WFE curve: $272B by 2030.** Redburn's Silicon Valley bus tour ("demand parabolic") drives global semi revenue to **$1.67T by 2030 (16% CAGR)** and WFE to **$272B by 2030 (18% CAGR, ~16% intensity)** — depo $59B, etch&clean $73B, litho $67B, process control $32B. Raised estimates across the group (LRCX/VAT/ASMI Buys alongside AMAT/KLAC Neutral); LRCX preferred depo/etch pick on logic-foundry share wins, PT $420; AMAT PT $560 (15% discount on China risk); KLAC PT $260 (Redburn, 2026-06-17).
- **TSMC capex/ASP — the foundry swing factor inflecting up.** Fubon raises TSMC FY27 revenue to a Street-high NT$7,057bn (+37% y/y, vs cons +26%) on a **15-20% supply-demand gap** and wafer pricing turning aggressive: FY27 increases "start at 10%" including Apple, blended ASP +≥10% on N2/N3; super-hot-run orders carry 15-20% premium; N2/N3 utilization 110-120% in FY27; GM to 68-70%. TSMC capex (UBS) ramps $40.7B (2025) → $56B (2026) → $75B (2027) → $85B (2028) (UBS, 2026-05-21; Fubon, 2026-06-05). UBS adds the server-CPU leg: agentic AI drives server-CPU units 23M (2025) → 63-70M (2030, 30% CAGR), N2/A14 node TAM scaling to 300-350kwpm vs 120-130kwpm at prior good nodes — a direct WFE pull (UBS "CPU TAM", 2026-05-21).
- **Intel WFE — a real greenfield leg (older note).** SemiAnalysis pegs Intel's full multi-phase leading-edge buildout at **up to $119B**, with the committed near-term plan alone calling for ~$33B of leading-edge tooling (credible case toward $50B) against a ~$5B/qtr base capex run-rate — a "$20-35B financing hole." If the agentic-CPU TAM is ~$200B, that maps to ~100k WSPM of incremental 18A/14A capacity (initial 14A target 100k WSPM scaling to 1M); the first capex guide-*up* of the whole saga. Bullish-on-the-margin for WFE if Intel funds it (SemiAnalysis "INTC SemiCap", 2024-12-09).
- **UBS pushes the ceiling again: $250B CY28, into the $300s CY29 (Arcuri, 2026-06-15).** On a Jun-15 call Arcuri took 2028 WFE to **$250B** and said "it's not inconceivable you get into the threes [$300B+] for 2029" — at which point an A-rated semicap (e.g. AMAT) "would earn close to $50/sh." Litho is *not* the bottleneck until ~**110 EUV slots ≈ ~$225B WFE**; ASML "will march to that drum" and add capacity rather than cap the cycle. He ranks DRAM-dollar leverage **ASML ~30% → AMAT ~25% → (gap) → LRCX → KLAC** (LRCX has gained DRAM share but trails; KLAC "fundamentally not that exposed"). Memory overcapacity not seen until "the middle of 2028 at the earliest," so the first low-multiple year for the group would be ~2029 (UBS "Arcuri", 2026-06-15).
- **MS WFE: $149B / $191B / +mid-teens 2026/27/28 (Moore-Shane, 2026-06-22).** MS revised WFE up in mid-May; DRAM-WFE ~60% growth in 2026 decelerating to mid-to-high-20s% in 2027; NAND-WFE "resurrection" a 2027 story (capital discipline still "remarkable"); Intel a 2027 WFE driver (~$25B to supply +20M CPUs). Reads the buy-side as ~$150B (2026) / $200B (2027) / $250B (2028) — i.e. converging on the bull case. Shane prefers **LAM + KLA over AMAT** (LAM = NAND + share-gain; KLA = the Intel play) (MS "On Memory and Semicap", 2026-06-22).
- **JPM WFE: ~$140B (2026) → $171B (2027) → $200B (2030); KLA flags $215B by 2030.** JPM models WFE +20-25% in 2026, +20%+ in 2027 (chip-design software +10-15%); 2030 base $200B, with KLA itself signaling WFE could reach **$215B by 2030** and JPM's own global-semi forecast implying upside "well into the mid-$200Bs." Cloud DC capex +80% in 2026, +50% in 2027 (top-4 hyperscalers ~$575B in 2026, +$250B incremental); 2027 hyperscaler capex growth raised to +50% from +40% (JPM "Semis Spring Series", 2026-05-29).
- **EUV is now a multi-buyer scramble — DRAM makers competing with foundries for litho (Conor + Avril, 2026-06-23).** Two 2026-06-23 sources independently flag EUV/advanced-litho as *the* 2027 constraint, and crucially that **memory makers now compete with TSMC and foundries for the same tools**. Conor's checks: **TSMC has already booked 40–45 EUV units for 2027** and finds it "difficult to get more" because "the memory guys also need it" — capping TSMC capex upside beyond ~$70–75B (vs bull talk of $80–85B) (Redburn "Conor O'Mara Asia Trip Takeaways", 2026-06-23). TrendForce frames the same dynamic from the DRAM side: 2026's binding limit is clean-room space, but **2027 adds EUV/high-end litho as a second constraint — DRAM makers "have to fight for EUV with TSMC and a lot of foundry companies"** even after placing orders (StoneX/TrendForce "Memory expert call — Avril", 2026-06-23). This is the same ~90-tool ASML ceiling the JPM/UBS desks debate, now corroborated by primary supply-chain checks on both the foundry and memory sides.
- **Wafer shortage emerging — TSMC at its prior peak (Conor, 2026-06-23).** A new takeaway from the Asia trip: the industry is "probably going into a wafer shortage." **TSMC is back to ~8M wafers/month — its prior peak — with limited new capacity added**, and Samsung "acknowledged wafer-shortage risk" (Conor's group was the first to ask Samsung about it). For memory the implication is mix optimization: a wafer shortage + the TSV/backend penalty on HBM pushes makers to **favor conventional DRAM over HBM**, tightening HBM further (Redburn "Conor O'Mara Asia Trip Takeaways", 2026-06-23). TSMC's supply/demand gap is seen widening to **15–20% in 2027**; 2027 wafer pricing "starts at 10% including Apple" (vs 2026 blended ASP ex-2nm only +3–4%: AI/HPC +10%, consumer +5%, Apple LSD), pushing GM toward high-60s/70% (Redburn, 2026-06-23 — Sherman).
- **Ferrotec semi-cap consumables +35%; Hamamatsu/M.A.Tech lab equipment +51% (Conor, 2026-06-23).** Two new off-coverage Asian semi-cap reads from the trip: **Ferrotec**'s semi-cap consumables business is growing **~35% vs an expected 25%** (its customers for the new double-capacity Malaysia plant are laminate makers and AMAT), with capex tapering and an activist (Hong Kong) involved; and lab-equipment house **M.A.Tech** — which counts "just about everybody" in its top-10 customers — is growing **+51%**, the lead that surfaced IR/CCD detector maker **Hamamatsu Photonics** as a probe/metrology supply-chain idea (Conor hasn't met them yet) (Redburn "Conor O'Mara Asia Trip Takeaways", 2026-06-23). Daifuku (Conor's lead indicator) shows orders confirming the WFE-to-2028 runway: **Taiwan/TSMC ~NT$103bn (+30% vs 73), Hynix ~NT$74bn (+40%), China ~NT$100bn (vs 90), Intel only ~NT$5bn** — Daifuku visibility now out to mid-2028 (Redburn, 2026-06-23).
- **TEL: WFE ~$150B 2026 / ~$170B 2027 base case, credible path to $200B (SIG "Susq Asia tour", 2026-06-18).** On SIG's 2Q26 Asia tour Tokyo Electron guided current-industry WFE to **~$150B (2026) with a ~$170B base case for 2027**, staying constructive on a multi-year cycle led by DRAM + advanced logic/foundry and more cautious on NAND. Key net-new: (1) **DRAM technology migration yields only ~10% bit-growth vs mid-teens–20% demand, so capacity adds are required** — the mechanical underpinning of the DRAM-WFE call through 2027; (2) NAND migration alone yields ~30% bit-growth (less capacity-hungry), but TEL is **now seeing real NAND capacity adds — ≥2 NAND customers currently increasing capacity** (vs "no meaningful expansion expected" four months prior), though not yet called a sustained multi-year cycle; (3) **3D-NAND layer counts moving from high-200s toward ~300 in 2027 (early deployments approaching 400)**, raising etch intensity/content-per-wafer; (4) **China WFE ~low-$50B 2026, up from mid-$40B**, but no longer the primary growth engine and Chinese-memory/post-IPO investment is *not* in the $170B forecast (= upside); (5) getting from $170B → **$200B** needs faster NAND + faster advanced-logic + more memory capacity (DRAM already robust). Order visibility runs on 5–6-month lead times + customers signing 2–3-yr agreements into 2028 (SIG "Susq Asia tour", 2026-06-18).
- **Cryo-Etch — a new NAND etch TAM (SIG "Susq Asia tour", 2026-06-18).** TEL sizes the **cryogenic-etch long-term addressable market at ~$1.5–2.0B, targeting ~50% share (~$0.8–1.0B revenue)**, with modest 2026 contribution but ≥1 customer beginning HVM adoption in 2027 around 400-layer NAND (TEL working with all major NAND makers ex-China). A concrete new depo/etch content leg on the NAND-layer-count ramp (SIG "Susq Asia tour", 2026-06-18).
- **Contrarian ceiling: WFE passes $200B but "never $300B"; ASML capped at ~140–150 machines/yr (former Samsung foundry EVP, 2026-06-30).** A 30-yr-industry operator who ran Samsung's foundry ($8–9B rev) argues the $300B-by-2029/30 bull case is "completely unrealistic" — nobody is building enough fab to justify it, and over-building would collapse pricing that the few remaining players are deliberately protecting. He does see WFE **passing $200B/yr**. On litho as the hard gate: **ASML cannot exceed ~140–150 EUV machines/yr — not a will but a can't**, because Zeiss can only make so many mirrors (each <10 pm surface variation, ~9 months to produce) and other components are equally supply-locked (he "begged Zeiss twice" for more mirrors at Samsung). Unit economics he cites: each EUV ≈ **$400M incl. 5–10yr maintenance, ~100 wafers/hr, ~10–12 machines per leading-edge fab**; **litho ≈ 35–40% of leading-edge *equipment* capex**, AMAT/KLA/TEL/LRCX combined another ~40–45%. Fastest-growing intensity is **metrology** (now near every step), then **etch/deposition** (atomic-level precision). Also: **LTAs are "worth nothing"** — unenforceable if the market cracks (he renegotiated many post-2022 and "lost all") — and of the ~145 GW of announced US data-center power, he thinks only **~15–20 GW actually gets built by 2030**. Samsung foundry ~$20B this year vs TSMC $120–140B; Intel "no chance" at leading edge (former Samsung foundry EVP call, 2026-06-30).
- **Cycle not over — component supply-mismatch worsening into 2027 (Nomura, 2026-06-30).** Nomura's "Is the cycle over?" argues the recent SOX pullback (after +85% since March, +211% since May-2025) is healthy digestion, not the peak: hyperscaler spend has upside into 2027F despite insufficient FCF, and their proprietary data-center build-tracking + "two years of greenfield build from late-2025" imply **insufficient supply into 2027F**, with an "unprecedented component supply-mismatch from 2H26F worsening into 2027F." Price hikes + earnings revisions the key catalysts (Nomura "Asia AI Semi & Server — Is the cycle over?", 2026-06-30).
- **CY26 WFE consensus consolidating around ~$140B.** LRCX raised CY26 WFE to ~$140B (from $135B); KLA to >$140B; Citi/Citi-house and the AlphaSense primers all converge on ~$140B for CY26 (+~27% y/y off ~$110B CY25) (AlphaSense "DR LRCX primer", 2026-06-22; Citi "C1Q26 recap", 2026-04-30).
- **DRAM WFE capex growth by company, CY26 (SemiAnalysis "Memory Mania", 2026-02-06).** SemiAnalysis's Feb-2026 estimate for the memory-cycle-driven WFE spend by memory maker: **Samsung DRAM WFE capex +26%, SK Hynix +34%, Micron +20%** in 2026 (p.34) — the earliest on-disk company-level breakdown of the memory-driven WFE ramp this page otherwise tracks via the Capstone bottoms-up model and the UBS/MS/JPM house-view marks. The mechanism: **EUV layer-count increases across all three memory makers**, tied to the **1b/1c node migration and HBM4 scaling** (p.31-33) — the same 1b/1c-migration EUV-layer-count driver that ASML and Lam Research management independently confirmed on their most recent earnings calls (p.30). ASML management is quoted describing a **"very strong memory outlook for EUV"**, driven by the combination of HBM wafer-capacity expansion, rising EUV layer counts, and 1b/1c node-migration demand (p.31). SK Hynix separately flagged that its own EUV spend growth will **"level off" longer-term** as MOR (multiple-optimization/throughput) adoption reduces tool intensity per wafer — but SemiAnalysis notes "a slower increase in EUV spend is still an increase and great for ASML" (p.33), i.e. even the most EUV-intensity-cautious of the three memory makers still adds EUV dollars, just at a decelerating rate. Net: a February-2026 primary-source confirmation, by name and by percentage, of the DRAM-capex-driven WFE growth this page's June desks (UBS/MS/JPM) later converge on independently.
- **Citi's relative-growth read (2026-04-30):** AMAT/LAM growing total sales "more in the 20s" in 2026 vs **KLA "high teens"** — KLA's lower growth an artifact of tougher 2025 advanced-packaging comps + a higher-DRAM 2026 WFE mix that "impacts them negatively versus peers." Citi in-house 2027 WFE ~$180B (vs Street ~$200B) (Citi "C1Q26 recap", 2026-04-30).
- **Peer valuation snapshot (AlphaSense primers, late-May/Jun 2026):** Mizuho CY27E P/E — LRCX 36.6x, AMAT 26.8x, ASML 39.2x, KLAC 35.6x, TER 38.2x, TEL 29.3x; Mizuho frames cumulative CY24→27 revenue growth LRCX 128% > WFE 85% > AMAT 65%. Bernstein reported FY27E P/E — LRCX 34.8x, AMAT 26.6x, ASML 26.9x (post multiple-compression, now "aligned with AMAT"), KLAC 34.3x — Bernstein views KLAC as a "premium vehicle" on process-control growth + lower China replacement risk (AlphaSense "DR LRCX/ASML primers", 2026-06-22).

## WFE — sizing & sequential growth (the numbers)

The qualitative "$200B/$250B" bull case resolves to a concrete, sequential ramp. After a two-year digestion (2023–24), WFE re-accelerates hard from 2026 with **memory (DRAM/HBM/NAND) the dollar driver** and a foundry/logic node leg on top. The **Capstone model sits materially above the Street** in the out-years — house WFE **$260B by 2028E vs consensus ~$202B** (a ~$58B gap), and above UBS's $247.5B.

### …and where it lands by company

The Top-5 equipment makers' **systems revenue roughly doubles 2024→2028E ($73B → $146B)** — LRCX grows fastest (depo/etch into the memory ramp + logic-foundry share gains), ASML stays the largest absolute dollar, and the whole basket re-rates as memory mix richens:

These systems-revenue ramps drive the house EPS folded into each page: ASML $40/$57/$69 (26-28E), AMAT $13/$19/$23, LRCX $6/$8/$12, TER $5.5/$6.9 (below Street). KLAC's model EPS line is flagged inconsistent (formula error) — excluded pending a fix._

## Key debates
- **Bull / where the money is:** memory is the engine. DRAM ASP +50–60% Q/Q in 2Q26, +20% Q/Q in 3Q26; NAND +75–100% Q/Q in 2Q (briefings, 2026-05-29). Agentic AI lifts the GPU:CPU ratio and DRAM content — AI-CPU demand alone (Vera 500k units CY26 → 3M CY27, Gokul/JPM) could add 15–20%+ to server bit demand (Jay/JPM memory call, 2026-05-28). CPU is far more WFE-intensive than GPU, so a rotation toward inference/CPU racks is *more* WFE per dollar (Arcuri/UBS, 2026-05-15). DRAM is the dollar driver of the incremental $50B/yr, favoring high-DRAM-share toolmakers (AMAT ~25% of every DRAM WFE dollar; AMAT PVD ~80%+ share at ~70% gross margin). **GW-translation (Bernstein, 2026-07-20):** each incremental GW/yr of DC additions capability ≈ ~46-50k WSPM ≈ **~$7.5-8B of WFE** (compute-TDP GW; DRAM+HBM = 69% of the wafers) — the conversion card that turns any GW buildout view into a WFE number, and the direct arithmetic rebuttal to the "never $300B" ceiling ("$300B+ seemingly doable" at 50GW/yr incremental).
- **KV-cache → NAND, the new structural leg:** agentic inference grows context ~30x/yr, inflating the KV-cache that must be stored and served across a memory hierarchy — HBM → DRAM/CXL expansion → and crucially a *new* NAND-flash "context memory" tier (tens-to-hundreds of TB), an entirely new data-center use case for NAND. SSD data-center demand is growing faster than HDD (Sathya Kumar, Arcuri-Micron memory call, 2026-05-15). Supportive of NAND-WFE durability even though Applied still sees little greenfield NAND.
- **Bear / what could break it:** (1) buy-side bars are already very high — AMAT's guide-up "wasn't enough" to clear them and the stock sold off on the print despite a strong number (Sur/JPM, 2026-05-15); (2) the H2 decel AMAT itself guided to, if real rather than conservatism, would dent the slope; (3) China ICAP overcapacity caps mature-node tool demand (AMAT: don't expect China growth); (4) NAND greenfield is still absent — first greenfield NAND wafer-outs not until CY28 (Micron Singapore), so the NAND leg is technology-migration-only near term (Sur/JPM, 2026-05-15); (5) a China equipment-export ban under US discussion would hit CXMT's expansion (Jay/JPM, 2026-05-28) — bearish for tool sales into China, marginally bullish for ex-China supply tightness; (6) **a hard ASML ceiling ~140–150 EUV machines/yr** (Zeiss-mirror + component supply, not will but *can't*) plus deliberate memory/foundry capex discipline could **cap WFE below the $250–300B bull case** — a former Samsung foundry EVP sees WFE passing $200B but "never $300B," and warns LTAs are unenforceable and only ~15–20 of the announced ~145 GW of US data-center power actually gets built by 2030 (former Samsung foundry EVP call, 2026-06-30). This is the sharpest primary-source pushback yet on both the litho-can-scale (UBS "ASML will march to that drum") and the $300B-by-2029 lines.
- **Timeline / inflection points:** CY26 = depo/etch + DRAM bulk capacity; H2-26 = memory mix richens, EUV-capacity confirmation from ASML is the catalyst the Street is waiting on; CY27 = sub-2nm node wave + first Terafab/Intel orders; CY28 = first greenfield NAND, +$50B WFE; CY29 = UBS sees the TAM higher again. Near-term datapoint: MU reports F3Q26 on 2026-06-24 (briefings, 2026-06-12).
- **🆕 High-NA: the debate this page had backwards (Bernstein "ASML: High-NA EUV Primer - The shrinking never stops", 2026-09-01).** This page logged Samsung's push to ~1nm and TSMC's A10 timing on 08-11 as a negative (*"High-NA has quietly become a one-customer market"*). Bernstein's cost work says the opposite on both legs. **(a) Who buys it: DRAM adopts FIRST, at 1d in 2027, led by [[SAMSUNG]] and [[SKHYNIX]] — the 08-11 item conflated Samsung LOGIC (~2030/A10) with Samsung DRAM; SK hynix already has an EXE:5200B installed at M16 (Sep-2025) and ASML disclosed 10 EXE systems at four customers on 09-01. [[INTC]] is the only early adopter in LOGIC (18A, in HVM now, broadening at A14); [[TSM]] and Samsung logic ~2030 at A10; [[MU]] later. (b) What slippage means for the vendor: *"slower HNA adoption is probably even better for [[ASML]]… it's economically better to sell more LNA EUV machines than fewer HNA machines… much higher margin for LNA over HNA EUV"* — because for large stitched logic die 1x High-NA never beats 2x Low-NA on litho-tool cost through 2030 (1.6x → 1.1x). The value transfer runs through LITHO INTENSITY (~1.2x for HNA single-exposure vs LNA multi-patterning; blended to 28%, DRAM ~30% at 1d) rather than through High-NA units.** ⚠️ **Falsifiable: ASML puts DRAM insertion at 0a; Bernstein pulls it to ~1d (2027). Watch 2H26 insertion decisions and the second High-NA order wave.**

## Who's exposed (companies)
| Ticker | Angle | Read |
|---|---|---|
| [ASML](../ASML.md) | Litho near-monopoly; EUV/High-NA + immersion DUV. The cycle's choke point. | Net positive, capacity-gated. **Bernstein (David Dai) Street-high, PT €1,700→€2,300** (40x P/E, EPS €56.7 25-28E from €49.2): EUV 91/113 units '27/'28 (from 86/87), €42.7B EUV rev by 2030 (30% CAGR), litho intensity 24%→26% '25-28, High-NA DRAM-first (2026-07-06). JPM (Deshpande) FY27/28 rev EUR55.4B/63.8B (+17%/+22% vs cons), PT EUR1,900; 90 EUV units 2027, 200 immersion DUV (+35% vs cons) (JPM "ASML", 2026-06-03 — capacity > 90-EUV ceiling, 110+ Low-NA feasible w/o new clean rooms, China DUV reverts to '25 levels in '27). Redburn corroborates: $54B litho sales / 91% share by 2030, PT €1,900, top semicap pick (Redburn, 2026-06-17). Terafab + High-NA + DRAM capex the upside; capacity/Europe-China discount the pushback. |
| [MU](../MU.md) | Both a memory-capex *spender* (drives WFE) and the demand-side beneficiary (DRAM/HBM/NAND pricing + KV-cache NAND tier). | Strongly positive. UBS (Arcuri) PT $1,625, path to >$100 EPS; ~$400bn FCF C27–29E; LTAs lock ~30% of industry DDR bits (briefings, 2026-05-26/05-31). DRAM tightness extends to 2028+. EUV access (post-Idaho fabs) a watch item. |
| [TSM](../TSM.md) | Foundry capex — the logic leg of WFE; ~40 EUV tools and rising; A14 in the sub-2nm wave. | Positive. Anchor customer for the CY27 node transition; capex/EUV pull a primary WFE swing factor (Jay/JPM, 2026-05-28; Sur/JPM, 2026-05-15). Capex (UBS) $40.7B→$56B→$75B→$85B (2025-28); 15-20% supply-demand gap, FY27 wafer pricing +≥10% incl. Apple, N2/N3 util 110-120%, server-CPU TAM (23M→63-70M units by 2030) a fresh N2/A14 WFE pull (UBS "CPU TAM", 2026-05-21; Fubon, 2026-06-05). |

**Not in the universe but central to the theme (prose):** **AMAT** (Applied) — depo/PVD/CMP leader, ~25% DRAM WFE share, ~80%+ PVD share at ~70% GM, advanced packaging ~$2B; systems guide raised to +30%, UBS/JPM both OW. **Goldman's top SemiCap tactical long — Buy, PT $520→$645** (32x on normalized EPS $16.10→$20) on DRAM-led best-in-class 2026 growth + visibility to 2028 + potential pricing tailwinds (GS "2Q Preview", 2026-07-05). Redburn Neutral, PT $560 (FY26-30 ests +4-9%, but 15% peer discount on China-share risk to Naura/Piotech; partnered with BESI on hybrid bonding) (Redburn, 2026-06-17). **LRCX** (Lam) — etch/deposition leader, the highest-conviction WFE share-gainer; UBS pairs LRCX + AMAT as "the two stocks you want to own." **Goldman Buy, PT $290→$380** (35x on EPS $9.00→$10.75) on WFE upside into CY27, HBM4 ramp + NAND-cycle timing (GS "2Q Preview", 2026-07-05). Redburn's preferred depo/etch pick (Buy, PT $420, FY27-30 EBIT +10-12% vs cons) on logic-foundry share wins away from memory (Redburn, 2026-06-17). **KLAC** (KLA) — metrology/inspection, levered to early-stage new-node ramps; only large-cap to commit CY27 WFE > CY26, advanced packaging +~60%. **Goldman's tactical *short* into the print — Neutral, PT $155→$230** (35x on EPS $4.85→$6.50): expected to lag peers because the current WFE cycle is DRAM-skewed, where inspection/metrology dollar-content-per-wafer is lower; the process-control-intensity inflection waits for the foundry/logic mix shift in CY27 (GS "2Q Preview", 2026-07-05). Redburn Neutral, PT $260 (well-reflected at 37x 2yr-fwd EV/EBIT) (Redburn, 2026-06-17). **TER** (Teradyne) — test, with AI/compute and CPO leverage. **Goldman Buy, PT $350→$465** (GS "2Q Preview", 2026-07-05). Read-across from **Advantest** (SoC-test peer, SIG "Susq Asia tour", 2026-06-18): capacity roadmap 5,000 SoC testers/yr (FY26) → 7,500 (subsequent) → **10,000 by CY28/29**, accelerated on customer fears of AI-driven test-capacity shortage; ~**30% SoC-tester demand growth**, ~85% of SoC demand from compute/comms, GPUs the largest driver (more volume, complexity, longer test times) with growing custom-ASIC leg; HBM3E→HBM4 raising test insertions; a silicon-photonics production PO booked (early-stage) — all corroborating structural test-intensity growth. **VAT Group** & **ASMI** — Redburn covers both (VAT Buy PT CHF750 on China-semicap exposure + 17% WFE growth; ASMI Neutral on GAA/2nm ALD leadership) (Redburn, 2026-06-17). On the memory-maker side, **SK Hynix / Samsung / Kioxia / SanDisk / CXMT** are the capex spenders setting DRAM/NAND tool demand.

## Management commentary — cross-company evolution (4–6 quarters)

How the semicap names have talked about the key themes across their last 4–6 earnings calls (arc shown start→latest; ↑ rising, ↓ falling, → stable). Per-company quarter-by-quarter tables live on each name's page. _Source: company earnings calls; management commentary, paraphrased. For toolmakers the first column is WFE TAM; for MU/TSM it is their own capex._

| Company | WFE TAM / own capex | Memory (DRAM/HBM/NAND) | Nodes & adv. packaging | China | AI demand / visibility | Margins / pricing |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| [ASML](../ASML.md) | FY26 guide €34–39B → **€36–40B** raised, H2-wtd; 2030 €44–60B held ↑ | "strong" → **sold out, tight beyond 2026** ↑ | High-NA >500k wafers (1 ≈ 3–4 Low-NA); first adv-pkg tool ↑ | >25% → **~20%** FY26, guide absorbs controls ↓→ | "key driver" → **demand outpaces supply foreseeable future** ↑ | GM ~51–53% flat; ASP/wph-led → |
| [AMAT](../AMAT.md) | own equip growth nil → >20% → **>30% CY** ↑ | DRAM record (LE spend +50%), **~80% of WFE growth**; NAND <10% ↑ | GAA $5→4.5B then "LE full, prices up"; adv-pkg >$3B → **+50%** ↑ | 35% → 28% → 27% → **24%**, stabilizing ↓→ | 1–2yr → **rolling 8-qtr forecasts "unprecedented"** ↑ | GM 48.8% → **50.0% record**; LE prices up ↑ |
| [LRCX](../LRCX.md) | CY26 ~$135B → **~$140B**, all segments ↑ | DRAM 14% → **27%** of systems; **NAND inflecting**, ~$40B conversion pulled fwd ↑ | etch/depo intensity; first dielectric-etch win; adv-pkg >40% → **>50%** ↑ | 35% → 43% → **34%**, June-qtr to decline ↓ | AI engine; **cleanroom-space constrained**; 2nd Malaysia fab ↑ | GM ~50% → **50.5%**; EPS record $1.47 ↑ |
| [KLAC](../KLAC.md) | CY26 mid-SD → low-$120B → **>$140B**; own PC >20% ↑ | PC mix 25% → 41% → **~18% CY26** (foundry/logic wins); NAND marginal ↑ | "all WFE"; **~82% foundry/logic** sub-2nm; adv-pkg +70%/yr → ~$1B ↑ | ~30% → mid-20s ($300–350M loss) → **"immaterial"** ↓↓ | 6–8mo lead → **"as fast as we can support"**, CY27>CY26 ↑ | GM ~63% → **~61.75%**, ~100bps DRAM-cost drag ↓ |
| [TER](../TER.md) | test rev $652M → **$1.28B** record; 2026 H1-wtd, ~2-cust ↑ | memory test $61M → **$206M** record; HBM3E/HBM4 ↑ | test intensity ↑ (chiplets); **SiPh/CPO $300–700M TAM** ↑ | — (not discussed) | AI mix 50% → **~70%**; candid lead-time caution ↑ | GM 57% → **60.9% record**; evergreen 59–61% ↑ |
| [MU](../MU.md) | capex ~$14B → $20B → **>$25B** FY26; FY27 higher ↑ | DRAM **+mid-60s% q/q**, NAND +high-70s%; fills only 50–66% ↑ | — | legacy DDR4/LP4 only (Q1FY25); ~n/a after | DC mix >50% → **DC bits >50% of TAM**; "strategic asset" ↑ | GM 37.9% → **75%** → guide ~81% ↑ |
| [TSM](../TSM.md) | capex $38–42B → **$52–56B** FY26; 3yrs "significantly higher" ↑ | n/a (high-end skew, "no impact") | N2 HVM 4Q25; **first-ever N3 expansion**; CoWoS perpetually tight ↑ | episodic; mid-40s+ AI CAGR holds even if restricted → | "robust" → **"insane"**; AI-accel CAGR mid-40s → **mid/high-50s** ↑ | GM floor 53% → **56%+**; reported 66.2% ↑ |

**Read:** every name's narrative inflected *up and to the right* over the last year — the debate is no longer *whether* the up-cycle is real but *how high* (WFE TAM, capex, memory pricing) and *what gates it* (litho/EUV capacity, cleanroom space, lead times). The two clear de-rating lines are **China** (managed down across AMAT/KLAC/LRCX/ASML) and, at the equipment names, a modest **gross-margin give-back** from DRAM-component cost inflation — even as the demand lines accelerate.

Getting stronger"*, broad-based across silicon and silicon carbide into 2027**; 🔴 **and a China view that does NOT confirm a peer's improvement — 2H26 lower than 1H26, unchanged for 2027, with China exposure limited by export control to power analog and mature nodes** (⚠️ the peer is UNNAMED in the transcript; the identity is CONSISTENT WITH [[AMAT]]'s revision to *"flat to slightly higher"*, not confirmed). Growth framing: *"if the market is going to grow by X, we at least going to grow by that X"* — a relative commitment against a market ASM also declines to size. ⚠️ **Analyst-asserted and unverified in the same call: a garbled *"Terrafab"* new-factory announcement (🔴 possible match to the **TeraFab** name already on this page in KLA's broadening leading-edge list — NOT confirmed, deliberately not mapped), "[[INTC]] raised their capex guidance recently" (✅ already on this page via Intel's Q2'26 raise and MS 07-24), and "[[SAMSUNG]] signed a massive contract with [[AVGO]] on leading edge logic foundry" (⚠️ possibly an imprecise restatement of the >$200bn Samsung–Broadcom LTA this wiki already holds).** The advanced-packaging leg is on [cowos-packaging.md](cowos-packaging.md); the DRAM / 4F² / leading-edge-memory legs on [hbm-memory.md](hbm-memory.md).

- **2026-09-20 (/wiki-ingest, 21h window) — 4 sources: UBS research (Jimmy Yu / Nicolas Gaudois / Timothy Arcuri et al., China SPE import analysis, published 21-Sep, received 09-20); Jefferies' US Semis Silicon Valley Bus Tour (⚠️ desk relay of private meetings); J.P. Morgan TMT spec sales (Scott Silver, ⚠️ not research); UBS Sector Sales (Robert Ruple, ⚠️ not research).** ⚠️ **NOTHING SUPERSEDED — no WFE series, capacity ladder or house forecast on this page was replaced.** 🔴🔴 **NET-NEW #1 — THE CHINA IMPORT SERIES INFLECTED AND THE INFLECTION IS IN THE DERIVATIVE: August SPE imports $3.3bn (+24% y/y), with **8M26 cumulative at −4% y/y, NARROWING from −8% in 7M26, and +28% y/y EXCLUDING GUANGDONG.** Litho $989mn (+32% y/y, 30% of total) is at its highest level of the year and has grown four consecutive months; non-litho $2.3bn (+21%), dry etch $721mn (22%), deposition $741mn (23%). Netherlands $919mn is the largest exporter, then Japan $723mn (−7%), Singapore $502mn (+74%), Malaysia $417mn (+17%), Taiwan $281mn (+175%) — the last three read through to US vendors.** 🔴🔴🔴 **NET-NEW #2 — THE SINGLE MOST INFORMATIVE NUMBER IS A PER-UNIT ASP OUTSIDE THE TRADITIONAL CLUSTERS: **Sichuan took THREE litho units at a US$100mn ASP each** (*"likely indicating a capacity expansion at relatively advanced nodes"*), Jilin one at $63mn, and six other provinces eight units for $114mn combined. Shanghai $1.4bn (+71% y/y, 44% of the month) on both memory and leading logic. Geographic broadening with an immersion-class ASP is a materially different signal from aggregate growth.** ⚠⚠ **AND THE SHARE RISK INSIDE IT: muted Hubei/Anhui imports against active YMTC/CXMT tenders lead UBS to infer *"an accelerating pace of equipment LOCALIZATION"*; UBS's top picks in the note are **Naura and AMEC, not the Western names**, and it raised **China WFE to US$49bn / 63bn / 70bn for 2026-28E (+9% / +29% / +11%)**. CXMT's G5 entering mass production the same day — 11.95nm active-area half-pitch, ≥50% gross-DPW gain, *"ahead of our previous expectation"* — says the localisation is producing WORKING NODES, not just tool orders.** 🔴🔴🔴 **NET-NEW #3 — THE CY28 WFE BOGEY HAS CONVERGED, AND CONVERGENCE IS ITSELF THE FINDING: JPM's Asia marketing found ~$260-270bn for 2027 and **~$300bn for 2028**; UBS's desk reports investors *"firmly anchored to $300b in WFE"*; both sit on the Barclays desk survey logged 09-16 (modal CY28 bucket $290-300bn, up from $240-250bn in mid-June). **Three independent buy-side surveys in five weeks on the same number means the ~$35-40bn one-quarter re-rating this wiki flagged is now CONSENSUS rather than a survey artifact — so the bogey is no longer a source of upside surprise.** Jefferies draws exactly that conclusion: *"the Semi-caps look like a FUNDING SHORT tactically, given the lack of potential upside on '28 WFE right now."*** ⚠️ **JPM's note prints a euro
