- **2026-07-28** — **/wiki-ingest. 5 State-of-play bullets. The [[KLAC]] FQ4 print (07-28 AMC) is the sector event of the week and it resolved the 07-27 positioning warning on this page in the bearish direction.** KLA beat, guided Sept above Street on revenue/GM/EPS, and **raised CY26 WFE to the “low $150B range”** — and fell 7-9%. **Barclays supplies the generalisable mechanism: buyside WFE numbers now sit ABOVE management's raised numbers (“buyside is already there and higher”), so a beat-and-raise is the base case, not the upside case — and “earnings season could be tough for ALL of semicap given how high buyside numbers already are for the next few years.”** Two corroborating facts: the Sept guide **cleared UBS's explicit “>$4b to rally” bogey and the stock still fell** (the bogey framework failed), and **JPM's pre-print net-short→neutral short-covering reversed within a session** (“folks looking to revisit the short”). Tape: **SOX -4.5% on the day, -25% from the June 22 high**, HF positioning **not yet neutral**, after four straight weeks of desk-level net selling and an **-18% sub-sector derating (-20% P/E)**. **Two important non-uniformities logged rather than smoothed over.** (1) **KLAC's beat came from PCB/Display & Component inspection (~7% of revenue), not core process control** — a mix-quality question — while **GM guided to 62.5%, ABOVE the 62% bogey**, suggesting the 100bps DRAM-cost drag on systems GM is easing (the standing Citi critique). (2) **The ATE sub-segment went the opposite way: [[TER]] beat by 9.2% on revenue and 21% on EPS and guided Sept ~21%/~37% above Street, killing the -15% q/q “air pocket” consensus had modelled — with BofA arguing the ATE buy rate is structurally INFLECTING UP from ~1% of semiconductor revenue on HBM/CoWoS/advanced-packaging test intensity.** So the de-rating is bar-specific, not sector-wide. Also folded: **[[ASML]] -8% on the day (~-13% over two sessions) on the China domestic-ArFi report, and BofA's first quantified rebuttal — ~€1.4bn / 2.4% of group revenue even if all ~20 of the 2027 tools displace ASML**, plus the comparative that **AMAT/LRCX/KLAC already face AMEC/Naura/ACM in China today yet trade at or above ASML's multiple.** [[LRCX]] (July 29 AMC) is the immediate test of GS's “modestly negative” read-through.

- **2026-07-27 (b)** — **/run-inbox (theme-dossier pass). 1 State-of-play bullet + 1 Sources entry from the PRIMARY source behind the China immersion-DUV story this page already carried via the Jefferies relay. Additive except for one attribution correction, logged below.** ⚠️ **THESIS-DRIFT / CORRECTION — the maker's identity.** The 2026-07-27 (a) bullet and Changelog entry below state that **"SHANGHAI YULIANSHENG TECHNOLOGY, a Chinese state-backed company, HAS STARTED MANUFACTURING IMMERSION LITHOGRAPHY MACHINES"** (Jefferies · William Beavington relaying The Information, 2026-07-27). **The primary text says something materially different: the manufacturer is an UNNAMED Shanghai-based state-backed company which "brought together immersion DUV development teams from other Chinese companies, INCLUDING a state-backed chip equipment startup called Shanghai YULIANGSHENG Technology"** — Yuliangsheng is one of the absorbed teams, not the builder, and the spelling differs. **The prior wording is retained above with its own date and attribution; the corrected version is carried in the new bullet.** Nothing else on the page was overwritten. **Net-new substance, in order of usefulness to a WFE model:** (1) **the correct scale — ~5 machines in 2026 and ~20 in 2027 against ASML's 131 IMMERSION DUV SYSTEMS SHIPPED LAST YEAR, i.e. ~4% and ~15% of a single year of ASML immersion output, and unit substitution at the low end rather than incremental WFE** — which supports reading domestic substitution as a STRUCTURAL, not near-term, headwind ("ASML therefore faces little immediate threat. But rising domestic output could gradually erode its market position in the country"). (2) ⚠️ **the surviving chokepoint has moved from the scanner OEM to the sub-supplier base, and part of it is Japanese: the tool "uses mostly domestic components, though SOME KEY PARTS COME FROM JAPAN," and "DELAYS FROM LOCAL SUPPLIERS HAVE HELD BACK PRODUCTION THIS YEAR"** — this is why the MATCH Act's component/servicing scope matters more than its sales ban. (3) **qualification lag** — tools "still trail ASML's products in performance and build quality" and moving them to mass-production lines "could take many months or longer." (4) **the demand counterweight in the same piece: the AI boom is INCREASING demand for ASML, with SMIC CEO Zhao Haijun saying on a MAY earnings call that overseas customers want to make chips in China because capacity is tight elsewhere.** (5) **placement — mature nodes first, incl. power-management parts essential to AI servers, plus SMIC's existing immersion-DUV multiple-patterning path for advanced smartphone/AI chips.** **Provenance flag attached to all of it: single-outlet, sourced to "two people familiar with the situation," no rating and no PT — reported, not established.** Policy legs (MATCH Act scope + servicing, the ~10% ASML China DUV price increase, domestic EUV still at prototype stage) routed to [china-export.md](china-export.md), not duplicated here. Cross-filed [[ASML]], [[SMIC]].
- **2026-07-27** — **/wiki-ingest (Outlook, into the [[KLAC]] 7/28, [[TER]] 7/28 and [[LRCX]] 7/29 prints). 4 State-of-play bullets. No canonical WFE number superseded** — the ~$150B C2026 figure is logged as an EXPECTED walk-up on this week's calls, not yet a company number. **The most useful reframe is UBS's: this earnings season is 'less about quarterly results and more focused on CAPACITY EXPANSIONS AND MARGIN TRAJECTORY' — and UBS ranks the group on pricing posture, which is now the differentiator: [[ASML]] and [[AMAT]] most forceful, [[LRCX]] measured, [[KLAC]] least likely to pursue pricing 'given its already industry-leading margin profile.' That ranking cuts directly against the sector-wide buy-side hope for 'unprecedented like-for-like ASP hikes' — the vendor with the best margins has the least incentive to lead on price.** **Operationally the most important datapoint is that the constraint has moved from orders to FAB READINESS: UBS's checks flagged very strong pull-in requests across foundry/logic, DRAM AND NAND, enough that SPE companies are telling sub-system suppliers to be ready for +/- 10% q/q in shipments — and 'what LRCX will ship in Sep quarter will still depend ALMOST ENTIRELY ON FAB READINESS.'** **Two under-covered legs named: NAND, where 'suppliers push for faster equipment upgrades to then push higher layer count bits into the market at current pricing' (DRAM and leading-edge foundry now well understood post TSMC's out-year capex); and TEST, where MS's Shane Brett models 2026-27 SoC Tester TAM +48%/+30% y/y against WFE +32%/+31% — yet has revised WFE up FIVE TIMES this year versus TWICE for Testing TAM, a process observation that partly explains the multiple gap between front-end and test names.** Scenario markers logged: **LRCX ~$18.50 EPS in a $300B WFE world before pricing (vs UBS C2028E $16.47); [[TER]] approaching ~$8-9B revenue and ~$19 EPS on a ~$19-20B C28 Test TAM at ~40% share; and investors possibly already pricing 2028 WFE of $250bn (+24% vs Brett's +13%).** **Positioning is now the risk and JPM says it directly: 'there has been no tech sub-sector where investors seem more ebullient than WFE… buyside TAM whispers make me very nervous.' The trade is LONG LRCX (long-onlys, +3 score) and AMAT (hedge funds), FUNDED BY SHORTING KLAC — but KLAC has swung FROM NET SHORT TO NEUTRAL on short covering, as investors position for [[INTC]] and Terafab shifting orders toward long-lead-time providers like KLAC and [[CDNS]]. The funding leg of the crowded trade has been removed while the crowded leg remains.** **Finally, a China datapoint with a measured market reaction: Shanghai Yuliansheng has STARTED MANUFACTURING IMMERSION LITHO for [[SMIC]], Hua Hong and CXMT — five systems this year, ~20 in 2027 — and ASML fell as much as 6.6%, closing -5%. Exposure bounded at a LOW-TEENS PERCENTAGE of ASML system sales, and logged with both hedges: prior false reports of Chinese immersion progress exist, and the MATCH Act would ban those sales anyway if passed.** Cross-filed [[LRCX]], [[KLAC]], [[AMAT]], [[ASML]], [[TER]], [[ADVANTEST]], [[INTC]].
- **2026-07-26 (/wiki-ingest — Outlook, weekend batch into KLAC 7/28 AMC and LRCX 7/29 AMC). 6 State-of-play bullets. Nothing superseded.** **⚠️ The headline is that the pricing question became a DIRECT two-house disagreement four days after the hierarchy was established, and it is deliberately left unresolved: BofA/Arya — "pricing is mostly an untapped gross margin expansion source"; Jefferies/Curtis — "we expect commentary from LRCX/AMAT/KLAC management to be more TEMPERED."** Do not average them. The **ASML > AMAT > LRCX > KLAC** hierarchy (07-25), with **KLAC "the least likely in the group to pursue pricing,"** means the disagreement bites unevenly — hardest to apply to KLAC, easiest to AMAT — and **the two prints this week settle it.** Menon's analog analogue: price increases "not yet… a driver of revenue and margin broadly in the sector, though we expect a stronger impact at [[IFX]]." **Second, the ceiling: Jefferies now has WFE "Above $250B in C28" and BofA/Arya carries $144B/$190B/$250B for CY26-28 — with BofA's C26 figure ABOVE the ~$140B base, i.e. underwriting the raise.** Company bogeys: **LRCX expected to guide C26 WFE to $145B (from $140B, +27% y/y)**; **KLAC the conservative voice at "high-teens… to $140B in C26,"** which Jefferies argues is exactly why the stock lags at +78% YTD. BofA's structural frame: **WFE at a 20% CAGR 2025-2030 vs 13% in the prior 6 years.** **Third, the actual bull case is customer capex and it accumulated in one week: TSM 2026 capex to $60-64B from $52-56B (a 15% midpoint raise "generally not expected by the Street"); INTC to $20B+ with 2027 "significantly" higher (BofA modelling $20.2/$27/$32bn '26-28) plus "aggressively locking in tool purchase orders and accelerating cleanroom buildouts," 14A "likely to be a material user of high-NA," and ASML's Intel revenue "subdued this year but likely to accelerate in 2027"; and the reported $200bn AVGO-Samsung deal reading through to Samsung capex — a third leg that was in nobody's model a week ago.** ⚠️ **The gate is unchanged: "Leading Edge will step meaningfully higher IF INTC has in fact secured a client for 14A, but official guidance for C27 remains 6 months out"** — the upside is contingent on an announcement these prints will not deliver, and **Terafab timing/size stays unknown.** **Fourth: NAND conversion pulls the $40B+ upgrade pool FORWARD — "the majority… completed before the end of C27" with "layer upgrades do not require new cleanroom space" and a heavy $ skew to HAR etch — which resolves this page's "no greenfield until CY28" objection.** **Fifth, the derating quantified by sub-sector since the June 22 SOX peak: memory -24% (-36% P/E) · CPUs -21% · semicap -18% (-20% P/E) · optical -15% · analog -14%**, with Jefferies' conclusion that the sector "has entered a cyclical de-rating phase where multiple expansion… has now become a headwind" and performance "likely to depend on the extent of earnings upgrades." ⚠️ **And the flows are selling into the upgrades: Jefferies has "net sold Semicaps amongst both HFs and LOs for 4 weeks in a row."** Positioning contradictory and left that way (BofA: LOs far less overweight than early 2023, inflection only from mid-2025; JPM: global semis at the 98th %ile on long-term net-exposure and L/S metrics). **Sixth: a second capex cycle from packaging — advanced-packaging TAM US$19bn (2026E) → US$122bn (2030E), 59% CAGR, "CoPoS and EMIB-T should drive another capex cycle, benefiting equipment suppliers"** (UBS), with the direct read to **KLAC's C26 Advanced Packaging SPC forecast of $1B, +57% y/y** and **TSMC's two additional advanced-packaging fabs.** Cross-filed [[KLAC]], [[LRCX]], [[TSM]], [[INTC]], [[ASML]], [[SAMSUNG]], [cowos-packaging](cowos-packaging.md).
- 2026-07-25 — /wiki-ingest (Outlook). Added 4 State-of-play bullets; additive, no prior number superseded. **The material new structure is a PRICING HIERARCHY** — until now "semicap pricing" has been carried as a group-wide post-ASML lever, and two houses now rank it: DB (07-23) calls pricing "**largely ASML-specific**," and UBS (07-25) orders it **ASML > AMAT > LRCX > KLAC**, with LRCX "more assertive... but to a lesser extent than ASML (or AMAT)" and **KLAC "the least likely in the group to pursue pricing"** given industry-leading margins. Also net-new: UBS's in-quarter checks showing **simultaneous pull-ins across foundry/logic, DRAM and NAND**, with **SPE asking sub-system suppliers to be ready for ±10% q/q WFE shipment swings** and **fab readiness — not demand — as the gating variable**; the expectation that **LRCX and KLAC walk CY26 WFE to ~$150B** on their calls; UBS's **~$18.50 LRCX EPS at $300B WFE** scenario; and the **NAND upgrade cycle** (faster equipment upgrades to push higher-layer-count bits at current pricing) as the underappreciated leg. On Intel: **KeyBanc reports foundry equipment orders revised UP 30-40%** with 18A and Intel 3 capacity expanding — a harder datapoint than the capex guide — while **Jefferies states the gate explicitly: "Leading Edge will step meaningfully higher if INTC has in fact secured a client for 14A,"** with C27 guidance still six months out, and notes the group got "little help" from TSMC's $60-64B, ASML's €43-45B and Intel's own raises. Finally, logged the **counterweight from Chipstrat (07-24): TSMC's capex intensity is 34-36% of revenue vs 53% in the pandemic supercycle** — ~$30bn/yr below its own precedent — with the observation that today's capex is TSMC's opinion on the next decade of demand.
- 2026-07-24 (b) — /run-inbox SemiAnalysis batch (theme-dossier pass). **Added two State-of-play bullets + a Sources entry; additive only, nothing superseded, and the existing 2026-06-14 China-WFE-mix bullet was left intact and NOT duplicated** (it already carried the SAQP/DUV-immersion/DTCO multi-patterning-intensity read, the 32.5nm M0 and the ~114 MTr/mm² / 38%-below-18A-HD normalised density). (1) **S5 SMIC/Kirin teardown (2026-06-14) — DEDUP-PLUS, existing bullet EXTENDED:** added the three limits that bound the China DUV story and were missing — the diminishing-returns statement (*"further scaling without EUV leaves fewer levers… each step will get more expensive and less forgiving"*), so the China DUV/dep/etch intensity tailwind is **real but self-limiting on cost and overlay** rather than an indefinite mask-count escalator; the **N+5 backside-contacts process-flow bill (backside alignment, wafer thinning, contact reveal, backside metallisation)** as a step change in *tool categories*, not more litho passes; and the universal physical wall — **"even with EUV, 48 nm has been the practical limit for yield and process control"** on CGP, which is the cleanest one-line justification on this page for scaling value migrating to packaging/3D (cross-ref [cowos-packaging.md](cowos-packaging.md)). Plus the forward relaxation SemiAnalysis expects if SMIC trades density for manufacturability (M0 → ~34nm, M2/M4 further). (2) **S4 "EDA Market Primer" (2026-05-21), one line — the moat ranking, added because it is the bear framing this page's ASML thesis must answer:** "EDA is the most defensible business model in semiconductors. **ASML faces High-NA adoption risk**, and TSMC faces geopolitical concentration." Logged with the balancing marks already on the page — ASML's own 2026-07-17 corporate-access day (**High-NA "too early for '28,"** ~110 Low-NA by '28, Intel 18A the adopter) and SemiAnalysis's own "a slower increase in EUV spend is still an increase and great for ASML" — i.e. **High-NA adoption-TIMING risk is well documented, High-NA displacement risk is not supported by anything on the page.** No sizing attached; qualitative only.
- 2026-07-24 — /wiki-ingest. Added the top State-of-play bullet: **Intel's Q2'26 capex raise translated into tool counts (Morgan Stanley Europe · van Putten / Simpson / Kim, "Implication of Intel's capex raise for ASML and ASMI", 2026-07-24).** [../INTC.md](../INTC.md) raised **FY26 capex to >$20B (from $18B)** with **CY27 "significantly above"** (Street: MS $30bn / JPM $26bn "may still prove light" / UBS ~$30bn). Composition is what makes it WFE-relevant: **shells/clean-room already built, so the bulk of incremental capex is tooling** — **equipment spend +40% y/y in 2026** on front-end tools for **Intel 3 / 18A / 18A-P**, with Intel "aggressively locking in tool purchase orders," plus stepped-up 14A spend for **risk reduction 2027 / committed HVM 2028**. Intel historically **>10% of equipment revenue at both [../ASML.md](../ASML.md) and ASMI**. **Two concrete revisions: (a) ASML — MS's modelled Low-NA EUV shipments to Intel go from 1 in FY26 (ramping to 6 in FY27) to 2-4 tools THIS year**, and MS pointedly **declines to raise estimates** because "this is now **pushing against what ASML may be able to deliver**" — reading the excess demand as **reaffirming pricing power** and supporting near-term margin improvement; **(b) ASMI — 14A addressable market per equivalent 1k-wpm is ~40% higher than Intel 3** (analogous to TSMC 3nm vs A14), pressuring MS estimates currently at +15% y/y '26 then +35% '27. Corroborating same-day: **BofA (Arya)** expects Intel capex growth "at least in line with ASML's recent 30%+ annual industry WFE growth"; **UBS (Arcuri)** "positive for Semicaps/KLAC"; **ASML +2.4% / KLAC +2.8%** on the print (JPM · Meyers). **Logged as superseding the practical relevance of the 04-15 BofA datapoint** that Intel's CFO "shot down" ordering more EUV tools claiming sufficient capacity — ~3 months stale and now contradicted by Intel's own guide and MS's revised tool count (old datapoint retained on [../ASML.md](../ASML.md) for the record). **Financing caveat carried onto this page deliberately:** management conceded **2027 positive FCF is "more challenged"** and it **"may need to tap the capital markets"** — the incremental WFE demand is real but not yet funded. Also added: the mirror-image question at TSMC — **"TSMC could spend $10B more on N2 capacity. Why won't they?"** (Chipstrat · Austin Lyons, 07-24), with N2 super-hot-run mix at **20-30% (from 10-20%)** at ~+50% ASP per tier, i.e. **leading-edge capacity is increasingly gated by incumbents' choice rather than the order book** — which simultaneously supports the WFE-durability bull case and the N3-overflow-to-Intel thesis. Additive; no WFE figure on the page superseded.
